## Reviewer slice summaries

- Read cover to cover as a first-time handling editor, this is a technically careful, unusually honest and unusually exhausting manuscript. The central object (a route dictionary A_u c_u whose columns are morphology-constrained delivery profiles) is clean, the exact E/I factorization is elegant, and every headline number I recomputed from Source Data matched. But the paper never states its contribution in one sentence: the abstract is a twelve-result inventory, the Results are ten sections of roughly equal weight with a disclaimer at the end of nearly every paragraph, and the Discussion restates each section rather than synthesizing. The decisive evidence is (i) the cut-rank theorem and estimate-then-learn pipeline (Fig 6), (ii) the sparse coarse anatomical dictionaries (Fig 7), and (iii) three well-controlled boundary results (one signal per neuron suffices on images, shunt transport vanishes at standard passive calibration, no endogenous alignment in measured responses). The signal-noise screens (Fig 3B-F,H), the 1.27-pp ancestry effect (Fig 5), the optimizer-dependent depth section (R6, no figure), and the two self-described conceptual panels (3H, 9F) read as filler that dilutes the advance. At ~8,400 main-text words plus ~8,100 Methods words, 9 figures and 35 SI items cited from the Results, I would return it for focus and length before sending it to referees.
  - Readiness: Not ready for Nature Communications in this form: as an editor I would return it for focus and length before sending it out, and if it went to referees they would struggle to name the advance. Before submission it needs (1) a one-sentence thesis that the abstract, Introduction and Discussion are built around, with Fig 6 and Fig 7 presented as the constructive core and the image, shunting and measured-response results as its boundary; (2) a cut to about 5,500-6,000 main-text words and 7 figures following the plan above (R3 screens, R6, half of R5 and the Fig 3H/9F panels to SI); (3) a rewritten Discussion that synthesizes rather than recites, and removal of the 'historical'/directory-name language and the per-paragraph disclaimers. The science underneath, including every number I checked, is sound and should be protected during the cut.
- The theoretical framework (S1–S4 and the corresponding main-text/Methods equations) is mathematically sound: I re-derived the directed path-gain product and adjoint form, the quotient-rule eligibility, the weighted-CV cosine identity, the moment bound and its optimized-step utility, the projected-stochastic crossover at unit and general step, the interior-optimum marginal condition, the fixed-step reliability optimum, the Sherman–Morrison no-sign-reversal result, the Ky–Fan/laminar proposition and shortfall decomposition, the finite-time coefficient risk, and the mean-field branch-compatibility threshold; all check out, and the 21 theory tests in tests/ pass. Reported numbers that I recomputed from Source Data (Fig 1E capture 52/48/72/63%, Fig 3C/E/F cell values, 214/540 zero-utility fits, Spearman 0.951/0.924 with intervals, 13/20 and 8/20 seed matches, regrets 1.94/2.75) reproduce exactly. The main weakness is presentational rather than mathematical: the only test of the "signal–noise" utility on trained models (Fig 3G, Methods, S4) uses Σ_route = 0, so the score reduces exactly to ||μ||²[cos(μ,m)]₊²/(2L), a per-seed monotone transform of the routed-update cosine, and the bare cosine correlates better with both endpoints than the utility does; the section therefore validates initial alignment, not noise rejection. A few prose claims are slightly stronger than what is proved (laminar optimality "realized" by a screen whose aligned endpoint violates the ordering hypothesis at K=1), and one legacy Source Data directory contradicts the current text.
  - Readiness: Mathematically the framework is submission-ready: I found no incorrect equation or proof in S1–S4 or the corresponding main-text/Methods material, the numerical claims reproduce from Source Data, and the theory tests pass. Before submission the authors should (i) reframe the factorial reanalysis (main 192–194, Fig 3G, Methods, S4) as an initial-alignment check, stating that the deterministic score is ‖μ‖²cos₊²/(2L) and reporting the cosine baseline that outperforms it; (ii) reconcile or annotate the superseded source_data/interior_optimum directory and fix the S16 caption attribution; (iii) qualify the laminar-optimality prose for K=1 in the spectral screen and align Fig 3F/3H captions with what is plotted. These are wording and provenance fixes, not new experiments.
- The image-task feedback ladder (§R2, Fig. 2, S30, S4D/E, Tables S16/S17) is numerically sound: I recomputed every accuracy, contrast, seed count, cosine, RMS ratio and path-specific energy fraction quoted in the main text and caption from the source-data CSVs and they all match (11.2/7.8 pp, <0.2 pp path gain, 10.8/11.0 pp under DFA, ≤0.09 pp path gain under DFA, ≤0.2 pp readout-minus-DFA, 0.57/0.51/0.38/0.39 pp ownership with 10/10 seeds, 18/32/53 % residual energy with 15/15 seeds, 0.71/0.63 vs 0.01/0.00 cosines). The DFA factorial is a genuinely strong control and the D/E "large distal path-specific fraction, no accuracy benefit" result is an honest boundary. The weaknesses are in framing and provenance rather than data: the gradient-cosine panel silently reuses checkpoints from the cohort the SI says is "not used in the article"; the ladder is really a rank-1 versus rank-10 comparison and the DFA/ownership results together show that any fixed rank-10 map suffices, which the "neuron identity" wording glosses over; the between-neuron factor is invisible in the figure's schematic; and "fallback"/"per layer" wording is ambiguous. These are fixable before submission without new training, except the optional rank-k ladder.
  - Readiness: The data behind §R2 and Fig. 2 are reproducible and correctly reported; nothing here would fail on numbers. Before submission: fix the provenance contradiction for panel C (either recompute on the ladder checkpoints or correct the 'not used' sentence and Table S16 caption), make the between-neuron DFA factor visible in the artwork, report the strict-scalar substitution rate and disambiguate 'fallback', and either add a rank-k feedback ladder or soften 'neuron identity' to 'feedback rank above one with a fixed map'. All but the optional rank ladder are text/figure edits without new training.
- The two controlled-task sections (§R4 branch selection, §R5 ancestry/subtree factorial) are numerically sound: I recomputed every headline number from source_data (35–58 pp full-conflict gaps, 20/20 positive interaction slopes, the 1.27 pp [0.59, 1.95] K=4 contrast with 15/20 positive seeds, the exact sign-flip P=0.00259 and both Holm families, Table S2 entries, the 23.15/5.02 pp rewiring contrasts with 20/20 seeds) and found no discrepancies; the forward/eligibility/selector logic and the χ_c=B/[2(B−1)] derivation are correct and the text now correctly says unselected exact derivatives are zero. The weaknesses are interpretive. The K-profile of the ancestry-minus-control contrast is fixed by the generator (net coefficients −3.05, −0.05, +0.85, 1 at K=1,2,4,8; K=8 tied by construction), so a positive contrast could only appear at K=4, yet neither the text nor the figure tells the reader this or explains the below-chance accuracies that follow from the sign inversions. The 'utility predicts the K=4 contrast' claim rests on a score that outputs K=4 for every seed (so 13/20 is the base rate) and that was redefined after an earlier archived version predicted K=8. The gradient-cosine diagnostic quoted in Methods uses the common-exact-state cosine, which is negative where the exact gradient is near zero, while the shared rule's own-state cosine at the same dose is +0.78; and the deranged control is described as failing 'at high conflict' though it is 25 pp below correct already at χ=0. Figures 4 and 5 are clear and the decisive contrast has its own axis with seeds.
  - Readiness: The numbers in this slice are solid and reproduce exactly; nothing needs re-running. Before submission, (i) reword the utility-prediction sentences in the Fig. 5 caption and SI S16 to reflect that the deterministic score is a constant K=4 predictor and was redefined after an earlier version chose K=8; (ii) add the four net route coefficients (−3.05, −0.05, +0.85, 1) and the sign-inversion explanation of below-chance accuracies so the K=4 location is presented as designed rather than discovered; (iii) replace the common-exact-state cosine in Methods with the own-state values and correct the 'deranged failed at high conflict' sentence; (iv) consolidate the three P values for the K=4 contrast into one primary test. These are text and caption edits of a few sentences each.
- Section R6 (main.tex 262–277) and its Methods (862–917) and SI material (supplementary.tex 2424–2840, Figs S15/S18/S28/S31) are numerically faithful: every main-text number (30.9, 10.96, 0.64, 13.47, −0.06, 22.26/30.41, −11.68, 25.63/−1.46) reproduces from the Source Data CSVs, the hierarchical_gain_load generator in synthetic_datasets.py matches the stated equations (noise added, floored at 1e-4, then lognormal gains with −kσ²/2 mean correction, α-mixed sensors, final floor 1e-5), and the resource-identical grouped-point control and the placement/shuffle/zero-alignment triple are well designed. The optimizer interaction is presented with the actual numbers and appropriately hedged. The most serious problem I found is undisclosed: in every aligned-D3 fit I could inspect (serial BP, exact-path LocalCA, shared-soma LocalCA, soma-broadcast BP; task-family and rerun-H3 cohorts) the run reached the 180-epoch cap with validation loss still falling ~0.01 per 10 epochs, so validation stopping never triggered at D3 while it did at D1/D2. All headline D3 contrasts are therefore fixed-budget numbers, and the optimizer "interaction" is at least partly a training-speed interaction between a 0.003 and a 0.0008 learning-rate recipe. Presentation-wise, the section is five paragraphs of numbers spanning four cohorts with no main figure; the argument is hard to follow without S31.
  - Readiness: The slice is numerically honest and well controlled, but it cannot go out as written: the manuscript must disclose that every aligned-D3 fit ran to the 180-epoch budget with validation loss still falling, and ideally verify with a modest longer-budget rerun (about 50 fits) that the 30.9-, 10.96- and 0.64-point contrasts survive, since the optimizer interaction is otherwise indistinguishable from a learning-rate speed effect. The 2×2 optimizer-by-credit cell means and the generator baselines (5.0/0.02/2.0, noise 0.08) should be stated in the main text and Methods, and the local-ratio family's extra differences from nested should be acknowledged or removed. Decide deliberately whether R6 gets a compact main figure; in its current text-only form the section is the hardest part of the paper to read.
- Section R7 (main.tex 278-314, Fig 6, Methods 817-861, SI S35-S44) replaces a failed prospective morphology-selection test with a new body of work on a scalar multi-affine tree model: an exact centered-cut representability theorem, a constructive dynamic program, Lasso-based estimation of interaction structure from noisy queries, gradient learning on the estimated trees, a positive-conductance grouping bridge, and a four-input Boolean study. The mathematics is correct and the numbers are reproducible: I re-derived the 8/15 crossed-grouping bound, the 49/105 compatible Boolean pairs, the nested balanced bound (22-2*sqrt(57))/55, the 1,407/1,680 attained-bound count, the 319/420 balanced-vs-comb count, and every headline NMSE/regret/CI in the text from the source_data CSVs. The protocol discipline (sealed choices, adjusted intervals, margins, retained failures) is exemplary. The scientific problems are of framing rather than computation: the section is a forward-representability result in a signed algebraic model that sits awkwardly inside a paper about feedback route dictionaries over conductance trees; the theorem is a special case of the hierarchical-Tucker/tree-tensor-network rank characterization but is presented as new; several 'minimum depth' statements are vacuous; the paper's own moment-score selector failed prospectively and that failure is relabeled a 'linear control' with no numbers in the main text; and at 1,186 words plus ten SI figures the section is the longest in the paper despite resting on 8-input and 4-input toy algebra.
  - Readiness: The computations behind R7 are sound and fully reproducible, so nothing here needs rerunning except the optional converged-pilot control. Before submission the section needs a framing pass: disclose the prospective failure of the R3 utility score with numbers and drop the 'linear control' label; cite the hierarchical-Tucker/tree-tensor-network origin of the cut theorem and claim only the centered refinement; remove the vacuous depth-2/depth-3 statements; state the sign-varying-multiplier mechanism that unifies 6E and 6F and acknowledge the two broadcast rules differ; and cut the section to roughly 700 words with the Boolean and fixed-pool material moved to SI. With those changes the section becomes a defensible forward-capacity complement to the route-dictionary thesis rather than a second paper embedded in the first.
- I audited every number in §R7 (main.tex 278–306), the Fig. 6 caption (311), Methods 817–859, the six morphology/Boolean SI fragments, SI Figs. S35–S44 captions and the underlying Source Data. All headline values trace to CSV/JSON files and match to the stated precision: the 1,407/1,680 bound attainment, 319/420 balanced-vs-comb wins (with zero reversals), regrets 0.0274/0.1561/0.0872 and intervals [0.0826,0.1771]/[0.0268,0.0939], the end-to-end 0.1246/0.5837/0.7321 with 0.4591 [0.3849,0.5323] and 0.6075 [0.5692,0.6461], conductance 0.000150/0.028921 and 0.02877 [0.02692,0.03073], broadcast excess 0.000059 [−0.000008,0.000154], Boolean 0.5632 [0.5491,0.5827], 0.00095/0.00345/0.00251, parity 0.0008–0.0010 vs 0.89–0.98, and the depth census (105+35 at depth 3, 24 at depth 4, max reconstruction NMSE 6.9e-30). I independently recomputed the Boolean centered-cut bounds from truth tables (8/15 crossed XOR-of-AND, nested balanced minimum (22−2√57)/55 = 0.12546) and they agree with source data. Freeze/seal timestamps are consistent with the prespecification claims (Boolean protocol 20:59:58Z, selection 21:02:56Z, first fresh outputs 21:03:34Z; calibration and end-to-end seals record zero completed fits), and no failed primary comparison was dropped. The remaining problems are presentation-level: one Methods sentence conflates the original 12,800-fit cohort with the new 25,600-fit cohort, the conductance experiment reports its two declared primaries with unadjusted 95% intervals unlike the sibling experiments, NMSE denominators and noise floors differ across Fig. 6 panels without a single clear statement, and the "require depth three" phrasing is vacuous for eight-leaf binary trees.
  - Readiness: Numerically, this slice is submission-ready: every reported value, interval, count and prespecification claim in §R7, the Fig. 6 caption and the Boolean/morphology Methods is reproducible from Source Data, and the key analytic bounds recompute exactly. Before submission, fix the cohort conflation in Methods 857, state the multiplicity policy for the conductance primaries (or adjust to 97.5%), add a single sentence clarifying the per-panel NMSE denominators and noise floors in Fig. 6, soften 'require depth three' to reflect the leaf-count floor, and unify quartet/quartic. None of these change a conclusion, but the first two would be flagged by a careful statistical reviewer.
- The reconstructed-arbor section is carefully bounded (modeled fields, capacity not use, every caption says so) and its headline numbers reproduce from the frozen source tables to the last digit: 85.0% of oracle at 6.92% coefficients, 14.18 and 2.82 capture-per-wiring ratios, the panel-C means (subtree 0.458 vs depth 0.445 at K=8), 47/47 and 9/10 sign agreement, 4.71% and 73.0% label figures. Main Fig. 7 separates the independent focal-shunt target (C) from the route-generated positive control (D) in-panel and in text, which is the right structure. Two problems remain. First, the second-animal and 47-cell replications, the SI text, and Supplementary Fig. S27 report a K=4 "capture" that is actually 1 minus the weighted residual norm (Fig. 7G says so; the SI and its figure do not), so the same quantity carries two names and the Pinky "capture" values (0.902 etc.) are not the Eq. (reconstructioncapture) capture. Second, the independent-field test that the text calls "the stronger structural test" exists only in the eight-cell proxy-labeled pilot, where subtrees tie depth bins at K=8 and lose at K<=4; both replications and the efficiency panels E/F rest on the deliberately favorable positive control. The proxy label's inhibitory precision in the pilot is probably around 50%, which the text does not quantify.
  - Readiness: Before submission, unify the anatomical estimand: relabel the SI Pinky paragraph and Supplementary Fig. S27C as residual-norm quantities (or recompute as energy capture) so they match Fig. 7G and Eq. (reconstructioncapture). Then decide whether to run the independent focal-shunt operator on the 47-cell cohort; without it, the strongest structural test and the efficiency headline both sit on the eight-cell proxy-labeled pilot, and main.tex 333 and 421 should be reworded to say the replications cover only the positive control. Quantify the proxy's inhibitory precision and fix the 73.0% sentence. The remaining items are polish.
- The focal-shunting slice (§R9, Fig. 8, Methods 1002-1052, SI S9, Figs S11/S13/S24, Tables S4/S5/S7/S25/S26) is numerically solid: every headline number I recomputed from the frozen source tables (panel B relation means 0.110 vs 0.035; panel C dose contrast 0.018→0.128, 8/8 at every dose; panel D driving-force-only 0.026 vs full 0.104; panel F -0.0019/+0.0024 at Rm=15,000; active contrasts 0.119/0.382/0.873, 8/8; Shapley 0.087/-0.017) matches the text and the builder to the printed precision, the current-matched control and soma-restoring construction are correctly described, and the reliability-shunting experiments are reported without overclaim. The scientific framing, however, has a structural problem that I verified analytically and numerically: in a passive tree the shunt's effect on every descendant adjoint is exactly the common scalar 1/(1+kappa_k r_k), and its effect on every other primary subtree is another common scalar, so at the adjoint level a focal shunt is precisely a diagonal gain on the ancestry-route partition of the site -- the opposite of the paper's repeated claim that a physical shunt "reshapes the transfer rather than multiplying a fixed dictionary column by a gain". The localization index (descendant vs other-stem contrast) cannot distinguish these cases, and the same identity means the "electrotonic boundary" is not a loss of the effect but a loss of selectivity: at standard calibration the shunt still halves descendant credit (log change -0.55 at unit input-conductance dose) while also attenuating the rest of the cell almost as much (-0.35). The active-channel ensemble, presented as robustness to Na/K/Ca/HCN/NMDA, uses median channel densities of 0.025-0.10 x leak in a membrane with ~30x standard leak and reproduces the passive curve to 0.7%, so it does not test what the text implies. These are fixable by reframing, and the reframing would in fact strengthen the route-dictionary thesis.
  - Readiness: The data, controls and statistics in this slice are ready; the interpretation is not. Before submission, rewrite §R9 (and the two 'diagonal gain vs reshaping' sentences at 353/521 and SI 518/1046) around the exact passive identity that a focal shunt is a diagonal gain on the ancestry-route partition with descendant factor 1/(1+kappa r_k), reframe the electrotonic boundary as a loss of selectivity rather than of effect (report descendant and off-route signed changes together), state the physical dose behind Fig. 8F, and either strengthen or honestly downscope the active-channel ensemble (densities and active/passive Jacobian ratio in the SI). Disclose the channel parameter table and the negative dose pilot. None of this requires new simulations except, optionally, an active ensemble at physiologically meaningful densities.
- Section R10 and its SI back-end are numerically consistent: every count and interval I recomputed from source_data (69 partners, 13 scans/7 targets, 464 trials and 280 stimulus identities per scan, 520 fits, the 0.788/0.832/0.833/0.839 NMSE means with builder-seed bootstrap intervals, the 0.232/0.244/0.385/0.468/0.976 oracle values, the -0.141 and -0.224 paired contrasts, the -0.0152 ridge contrast, the -0.069/-0.0485 structure-function partials and the six-animal Francioni table) reproduces exactly, and the text states clearly that the recordings were not taken during learning. The problem is what the full-tree test can logically show. Reading the training script and the oracle outputs, the exact input-conductance field in this passive model is essentially scalar error times the fixed soma-to-site transfer vector (the unprojected baseline profile matches it at 0.976-0.986, per-run minimum 0.953), so the 'subtree vs random vs shuffle' comparison in Fig 9B and the update-match rows of 9C reduces to how much of that fixed vector each four-route span retains, a task-independent geometric quantity (Spearman 0.91-0.95 between retained energy and update match). Worse, the 'subtree' dictionary is mostly single-site distal addresses (mean 1.31 sites per route, one-hot in 6 of 13 scans, ~58% site coverage), which Fig 9A does not show. Only the structure-function correlation rows are a genuine measured-response test of route alignment, and they are underpowered. The section is honest about the frozen rule but needs reframing before submission.
  - Readiness: The numbers are reproducible and the caveats are honest, but section R10 currently sells a geometric comparison as a biological null: in the passive model the exact field is ~98% a fixed transfer profile, the 'subtree' dictionary is mostly one-site distal addresses covering ~58% of sites, and the dictionary ranking follows retained transfer energy almost perfectly. Before submission, reframe the section around the structure-function test, report route support/coverage and retained-energy fractions, redraw Fig 9A, state that all frozen rules lose to treeless ridge, move the imposed-alignment panels to SI and replace them with a same-pipeline positive control (or an explicit minimum detectable effect), and strip the post-hoc p-values from the Francioni table. With these changes the section becomes a defensible boundary statement rather than an apparent measured-response result.
- I built a claim-by-claim ledger for the title, abstract, all ten Results headings and the Discussion, and recomputed the headline numbers from the live source_data folders. Numerically the manuscript is in very good shape: every figure-level number I rechecked (MNIST ladder, DFA factorial, Fig 1E capture, Boolean and conductance contrasts, calibration regrets, within-span oracle, physical-depth effects, focal calibration, bound attainment counts) matches the CSV/JSON summaries, and the text is unusually careful about labelling nulls and negative results. The mismatches that remain are framing mismatches: the title and Discussion present "morphology as a dictionary" as the unifying object, but the strongest new results (R7) are forward-representability results that involve no route dictionary, and the within-arbor route-dictionary experiments are, in aggregate, mostly null or reversed (exact path adds <0.2 pp on MNIST and is reliably worse than neuron-specific on CIFAR-10; two-subtree oracle profiles do not beat shuffled profiles; measured-response subtree update match is below both controls; depth bins match or beat subtrees on the only independent anatomical target). The Discussion's "second mouse supporting a structural check" refers only to the route-generated positive control, which is not stated there. These are fixable with wording changes; no new experiments are needed for the claims to be sound.
  - Readiness: The quantitative content is submission-ready: every number I rechecked reproduces from source_data, and the hedging is careful. Before submission the framing needs one pass: (i) state in the Discussion and Fig 7G that the disjoint-cohort and second-mouse checks use the route-generated positive control only, (ii) state the CIFAR-10 exact-below-neuron reversal in the main text, (iii) disentangle 'route dictionary' from the Fourier feature basis and reconcile Table S14 with line 133, and (iv) settle on one prespecified test for the K=4 ancestry effect. Consider a title that asserts a constraint rather than an identity, since the within-arbor dictionary evidence is conditional and modest.
- The nine main figures are unusually disciplined for a computational-neuroscience manuscript: one 12-column canvas, a fixed type scale, consistent marker/interval conventions, all captions under 350 words, and every panel letter reconciled between artwork, caption and text callouts. The weakness is allocation rather than craft. Roughly a third of the total figure area is schematic or "roadmap" material (Fig 1A–D, Fig 2A/F, Fig 3A/B/H, Fig 5B–D, Fig 9D–F), and the decisive comparisons the paper actually stands on — the 1.27-pp K=4 effect (Fig 5H), the electrotonic boundary (Fig 8F), the measured-response null (Fig 9B/C) — are drawn at 1.9–3 in width with 6-pt labels while conceptual thumbnails get equal or more space. A handful of correctable issues would mislead a reader at a glance: inverted red/blue semantics between adjacent heatmaps in Fig 3, two stacked forest strips in Fig 2G whose x-scales differ 15-fold, a Fig 3 caption that mislabels bootstrap ribbons as "analytic construction", a P value printed in Fig 5H that is a different test from the one the text reports, and subscript glyphs that print at 4.3–4.6 pt. None of these require new data; all are builder-script edits.
  - Readiness: The figure set is close: typography, captions and panel bookkeeping already pass the checks a Nature Communications art editor would run, and no finding requires new experiments. Before submission fix the four items that would mislead at a glance (Fig 3 C/E color inversion, Fig 2G unequal strips, Fig 3 ribbon definition, Fig 5H P-value mismatch) and the sub-5-pt subscripts, since those are one-line builder edits. Then reclaim area from the roadmap/schematic panels (Fig 3H, Fig 5B–D, Fig 9E/F) to enlarge the decisive data panels; that is the change most likely to improve how referees weigh the modest effect sizes.
- Supplement sections S1–S5 are mathematically sound and unusually candid: I recomputed the key identities (weighted-CV cosine, lognormal depth penalty, projected stochastic crossover, marginal-utility condition, fixed-step reliability optimum, mean-field conflict boundary) and the internal bookkeeping (2,413,312 core parameters, Fashion-MNIST/CIFAR contrasts, the 135/200 interior-optimum heatmap) and found no errors. Figures S1–S22 are legible at text width, use a consistent native style, and nearly every caption names the inferential unit and interval type. Of the nine defects flagged by the prior audit, all are fixed in the live repository (S7, S10, S16, S17, S23, S28, S29, S31), but the frozen snapshot still carries the stale overlay version of S21 whose artwork contradicts its caption, and 12 of the 26 figure files I checked differ between snapshot and live repo, so the compiled supplementary PDF must be rebuilt from the final artwork. Two new problems need attention: S8F is misregistered by one depth on the x-axis (identical in snapshot and live), and the S2 caption has a literal tab that renders as "extbfE". Organizationally, S4 has become a 25-subsection grab-bag with the morphology/Boolean methods spliced into the middle of the signal–noise theory, and all figures S5–S44 sit in one block on pages 69–107, numbered by accretion history rather than by order of first citation.
  - Readiness: The scientific content of S1–S5 is submission-grade; the blocking items are production, not science. Before submission: fix the S8F x-axis registration, rebuild the supplementary PDF from the final (22:56–22:57) figure set so the stale S21 overlay and the 11 other differing files cannot ship, repair the S2 caption tab/quote, and renumber/relocate the figures by first citation with the morphology methods promoted out of S4. Those four changes are a day's work and would remove every reader-visible inconsistency I found in this half of the supplement.
- Cross-referencing in this manuscript is in unusually good shape for a 42+128-page package: both read-only tests pass (10 passed), every main-figure panel A–I referenced in Results exists in the caption and in the artwork and is cited in first-use order, all 44 supplementary figures and 35 tables are numbered monotonically in file order, the compiled PDFs contain no unresolved references, and roughly 40 headline numbers I recomputed from Source Data (Fig 1E 72/63%, Fig 2 11.2/7.8/<0.2 pp and 32/53% with 15/15 seeds, Fig 3G rho 0.924/0.951 and 214/540, Fig 4 35–58 pp, Fig 5 1.27 [0.59,1.95] 15/20 P=0.00259/0.00519/0.0101, Fig 6 1,407/1,680, 319/420, 0.369/0.443, 0.4591/0.6075, 0.02877, 0.5632, Fig 7 85%/6.9%/14.2/2.8 and Pinky 9/10 tie, Fig 8 −0.0019/+0.0024 and P=0.0078, Fig 9 0.232/0.244/0.385/0.468/0.976 and −0.0152, S32 7.16/54.47/0.31/64.03/80.29, R6 30.9 pp) all match to the stated precision. The remaining problems are about synchronisation and presentation rather than wrong numbers: the compiled supplement embeds different builds of Figs S19 and S21 than the figure files in the snapshot (and the S21 figure file contradicts its own caption); six supplementary figures are never cited from the main text; the K=4 ancestry contrast is reported under three different multiplicity treatments; and the update-moment notation in the main text (mu, g-tilde, Sigma_route) does not match the supplement's proposition and notation table (g, d, V). Several stale panel-range filenames and a symbol drift for the anatomical route matrix (A_K / Phi / A / Gamma) round out the polish list.
  - Readiness: Numerically and structurally this slice is close to submission-ready: every number I recomputed matches Source Data and every main panel and SI reference resolves. Before submission the authors must (1) regenerate the S19/S21 figure files (S21's file contradicts its caption), rerun `make combined`, and add a check that embedded figures equal the files to be uploaded; (2) cite Figs S6, S7, S18, S19, S21 and S26 from the main text; (3) settle on one multiplicity treatment for the K=4 ancestry contrast and one symbol set for the update-moment bound across main text, Fig 5H and SI. The filename, terminology and citation-target items are quick polish that the existing test suite could be extended to enforce.
- The statistical reporting in this manuscript is unusually careful for the genre: paired designs with the seed/cell/target as the declared unit, sign counts alongside intervals, explicit nesting statements, prespecified margins, and machine-readable per-panel source tables. I recomputed the headline contrasts behind Figs 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 and 9 from journal/source_data and every number I checked (11.2/7.8 pp; 1.27 pp [0.59,1.95], 15/20; rho 0.924/0.951; 35-58 pp; 0.5632; 0.4591/0.6075; 0.02877; 85%/7%/14.2/2.8; 9/10 Pinky; 0.232/0.244/0.385/0.468/0.976; 0.015 [0.004,0.027]; -0.0019/+0.0024; 30.9/10.96/0.64; 7.16/54.47) reproduces. The problems are not in the arithmetic but in presentation: the same K=4 ancestry contrast carries three different adjusted P values across text, caption and SI, with the post hoc sign-flip test foregrounded; the Fig 3G pooled correlation is dominated by bandwidth and its seed-block interval conveys false precision; the main text omits that exact-path transport is reliably worse than neuron-specific feedback on CIFAR-10 (-0.86 pp, 2/20 seeds) and Fashion shunting; and the Fig 8F "small, opposite-signed" calibration contrasts are in fact systematic in both cohorts (45/45 and 6/8), which is a cohort discrepancy rather than a null. Interval and test conventions vary across figures (Student-t, percentile bootstrap at 5k/10k/20k/50k draws, paired-t, 97.5% Bonferroni, TOST, sign-flip, Wilcoxon, BH vs Holm); all are declared but a consolidated table is needed.
  - Readiness: Statistically this slice is close to submission-ready: units, nesting, prespecification and margins are handled better than most computational-neuroscience papers, and every headline number recomputes from the released source tables. Before submission the authors should (1) settle on one prespecified test and P value for the K=4 ancestry contrast across text, caption and SI; (2) rewrite the Fig 3G correlation statement to give condition-level and within-budget correlations and describe what the seed-block interval does and does not cover; (3) disclose in the main text the CIFAR-10/Fashion reversal of the exact-path rung and the systematic opposite-sign Fig 8F contrasts with intervals; and (4) add a single statistics table (unit, n, estimator, interval, draws, test, multiplicity family per panel) so the six conventions in use can be checked in one place.
- Supplement sections 6–14 and the 35 tables are in good shape: every table has a prose callout, units are stated in captions (fractions vs. percentage points vs. percent, log-unit localization, capture fractions), and the three distinct NMSE denominators (population/target variance, split-variance test error, training-mean predictor) are named where they are used. I recomputed the headline numbers I could reach: Table S24 sums/means (28,669 contacts, mean depth 8.75, mean map pass 0.980), the Table S2 Holm/BH adjustments against review_evidence_reanalysis/ancestry_control_contrasts.csv, the Table S25/S26 capacity values against capture_per_wire/summary.csv and reciprocal_routing/summary.json, the S36 bound-vs-fit relation (0 of 1,680 fits below the centered-cut bound), and the S43/S44/S40/S41/S42/S39 caption counts (49 exact pairs, 4/√15, 1/5 projection energy, 3,600/1,440/6,720/25,600 fits) — all check out. The new figures S32–S44 are legible at text width and their captions match the artwork; the defects that remain are presentational: mis-rendered negative-number interval ranges in three tables, a legend that sits on top of data in S25B, a missing colour key in S36/S37, stale or mislabeled figure files, and a handful of caption/artwork mismatches on summary marks.
  - Readiness: This half of the supplement is substantively ready: the numbers I could recompute agree with the source data, the new S32–S44 figures match their captions, and the NMSE conventions are stated and consistent. Before submission, fix the typeset interval ranges for negative endpoints in Tables S4–S6 (and the footnotes of S26/S28), move the S25B legend off the data, add a family colour key to S36/S37, and rename or purge the mismatched/stale figure files (S27 A-C vs A-D, S29, S31). The remaining items are caption wording and column-format harmonization that can be done in a single editing pass.
- The Introduction and Discussion position the work carefully against the circuit-level dendritic credit literature (Urbanczik/Senn, Guerguiev, Sacramento, Payeur, Ellenberger, Richards/Lillicrap) and against feedback alignment, DFA and e-prop, and the earlier "one coefficient per compartment" reduction of Bicknell & Häusser is gone. The remaining problems are of two kinds. First, the single-neuron dendritic-computation and branch-specific-plasticity lineage that most directly anticipates Results sections R4, R6 and R7 is absent: Jones & Kording 2021 (binary trees of units over Fashion-MNIST inputs), Legenstein & Maass 2011 (branch-specific plasticity), Moldwin et al. 2021, Poirazi & Mel 2001, Körding & König 2001 and Beniaguev et al. 2021 are cited nowhere in main text, supplement or bibliography. Second, the paper's own direct predecessor preprint (arXiv:2607.03556, still under NeurIPS consideration) is cited only in the supplement, even though Supplementary Figs S1–S4 reproduce analyses from it and the authors' own reference audit says it must be cited and disclosed. The bibliography is mostly verified against Crossref, but one DOI is wrong (Greedy 2026), two preprint entries render without any DOI or URL, and one title has a case-mangled ion name. The novelty claims (route dictionary, utility bound) are defensible as framing, but the utility bound is the textbook descent lemma and should be labelled and cited as such in the main text.
  - Readiness: Before submission the main text must cite and disclose the direct predecessor preprint (arXiv:2607.03556) and its pending NeurIPS status, and the Introduction/§R4/§R6/§R7 need the single-neuron dendritic-computation and branch-specific-plasticity lineage (Jones & Kording 2021, Legenstein & Maass 2011, Moldwin et al. 2021, Poirazi & Mel 2001, Körding & König 2001, Beniaguev et al. 2021, Gidon et al. 2020) with one explicit delta sentence each. Schiess 2016 and Bicknell & Häusser 2021 should be cited at Eqs. (2)–(3) and in §R2, the utility bound labelled as the standard descent lemma, and the Lv 2025/Chavlis 2025 grouping corrected. Fix the Greedy DOI and the @misc/DOI rendering; these are quick, but a wrong DOI and locator-less preprints in a Nature Communications reference list will be flagged at production.
- The Methods section is unusually thorough in intent — every cohort is indexed in Table 1, prespecification and exclusion rules are stated, and every headline number I recomputed from Source Data (11.2/7.8 pp, 72/63 % capture, 18/32/53 % path-specific energy, ownership 0.57/0.51/0.38/0.39, D3-D1 30.9 pp, DFA 10.8 pp, fit-count arithmetic for 6,720/3,600/1,440/2,240/12,800/520) matched. But as a Methods section it is mis-shaped: at ~8,000 de-LaTeXed words it is roughly 2.7x the Nature Communications guidance, and about 1,700 of those words are restated Results, post-hoc score comparisons and implementation chronology (matched-width fallback history, S1/S3 aggregate exclusions, optimizer discovered post hoc). Meanwhile the primary Fig. 2 MNIST experiment has no optimizer, batch, epoch, contact-count or nonlinearity specification in Methods (only CIFAR-10 gets them), the synthetic noise-resilience task used in several cohorts is never defined anywhere in main text or SI, and the physical-depth LocalCA optimizer is misreported as a single rate. The section needs restructuring more than rewriting.
  - Readiness: Numerically the Methods are sound — every value I recomputed matched Source Data and the fit-count arithmetic is internally consistent — so the section is not scientifically wrong, but it is not yet a Nature Communications Methods. Before submission it needs (i) a structural cut from ~8,000 to roughly 3,000-4,000 words by removing restated Results, post-hoc score narratives and implementation chronology (all of which already exist in the SI), and (ii) filling three reproducibility holes that the cut must not obscure: the Fig. 2 MNIST training recipe, the definition of the synthetic noise-resilience task, and the correct split LocalCA optimizer plus generator constants for the physical-depth cohort. The remaining items are polish (duplicate fallback paragraph, undefined terms, which K=4 test is primary).
- The release machinery is unusually thorough for an academic submission: explicit allowlists, SHA-256 manifests for every bundled file, deterministic ZIPs, a figure-allowlist check against both manuscripts' includegraphics calls, private-path scanning, and audit scripts that pass cleanly on the current tree (submission audit 0 errors on main and SI, format audit within guidance, reproducibility audit passed, 28 read-only tests pass, 156 tests collect). The rebuilt bundles agree with the snapshot on structure (42/128/170 pages, 9 main + S1-S44 figures; second-mouse and 44-figure language in README, cover letter, checklist and completion answers). However, the provenance story told by the Code availability statement and the software README does not hold: the reviewer software archive records a paper-repository commit that does not exist in the repository, the journal worktree was dirty (206 entries) when every archive was built, and 89% of the 211 MB software archive is a stray 187 MB PowerPoint plus several hundred files of unrelated LLM transformer-replacement work that the manuscript promises to release publicly under MIT. The consolidation plan is followed on the big items (journal is canonical, no sequel language, prior dissemination disclosed outside the narrative, no TMCR/phase-theory branding), but the preprint whose derivations are integrated is never cited in the main text, and the terminology hierarchy for the three depths and for 'reactivation' is only partly adopted. The 44 untracked/modified files that carry S32-S44 are mostly outside the test and Makefile audit chain.
  - Readiness: Before submission: (1) commit the working tree and rebuild every archive from a reachable commit so METADATA.json/bundle_metadata.json stop pointing at a phantom hash and a dirty tree; (2) strip LLR_n.pptx, compiled PDFs and the unrelated transformer-replacement/compression material from the software archive and decide what the MIT public release actually covers; (3) cite the arXiv preprint once in Methods and fix the '34 supplementary figures' line. Then wire rebuild_final_publication_figures.py and the new review scripts into `make audit`/tests and re-snapshot for a final panel pass. The editorial documents, Source Data and manifests are otherwise in good shape.

## Findings (158 merged; by verified severity: {'high': 13, 'medium': 66, 'low': 79})

### Severity: high

#### M003 — Direct predecessor preprint (arXiv:2607.03556) is cited only in the supplement, never in the main text
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:55-70, 1170-1220; supplementary.tex:2853
- **Claim:** The same-author predecessor preprint (Safaai, Richards & Sabatini, arXiv:2607.03556), from which the Article inherits the conductance-tree equations, eligibility×transport factorization, LocalCA rule, Fig. 1A-C artwork and Supplementary Figs. S1-S3 byte-for-byte, is never cited in the main text or its compiled reference list; the only citation is one sentence in the supplement (supplementary.tex:2853) in the artificial-tree section, not at the derivation (§S1) where the reuse begins. Disclosure otherwise lives only in the cover letter/extension statement, so readers and referees cannot tell what is new. The authors' own reference_audit.md and text-reuse audit already flag this as required. Fix: one attributing sentence in the Introduction (after main.tex:67) or Methods (~main.tex:1163) plus a citation at SI §S1, and list both arXiv records as related manuscripts at submission.
- **Evidence:** grep safaai2026localcredit: main.tex 0, supplementary.tex 1 (line 2853, lineage note). analysis/reference_audit.md says it 'must be cited and disclosed'; audit_neurips_text_overlap.py prints 'Earlier work cited: NO'. NeurIPS decision pending.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.93):** (1) grep "safaai2026localcredit|2607.03556|preprint" over snapshot main.tex: 0 hits (only safaai2026gainload at main.tex:421). pdftotext of main.pdf reference list: only arXiv:2607.24990 (gain-load) appears; 2607.03556 is absent, so the compiled Article never cites the predecessor. (2) supplementary.tex:2853 is the single citation: "Some of these analyses were also reported in the associated preprint \citep{safaai2026localcredit}" — placed in the artificial-tree section (§"Regular-tree foundation and boundary experiments", line 2845), not at §S1 "Exact gradients in a directed conductance tree" (line 58) where the reused derivation begins. (3) references.bib:356-365 defines the entry (Safaai, Richards, Sabatini, arXiv:2607.03556, 2026), so the key exists and is simply unused in the main text. (4) Reuse is real, per the authors' own records: analysis/NEURIPS_FIGURE_LINEAGE_AUDIT_20260804.md states "Figure 1 panels a-c use the original Figure 1 functions byte for byte" and "Supplementary Figures S1-S3 are the unchanged final NeurIPS/arXiv Figures 3-5 ... byte-identical"; analysis/NEURIPS_TEXT_REUSE_AUDIT.md lists "Earlier work cited in the journal manuscript: NO" and names the "shared conductance equations, exact eligibility--error factorization, path-gain definition and regular-tree controls" as the inherited foundation; scripts/audit_neurips_text_overlap.py:91,116 computes that flag from the main text. main.tex:454-470 (Eq. directedcurrentbalance/directedvoltage) and main.tex:106 (LocalCA rule) present that foundation with no attribution. (5) analysis/reference_audit.md:45 (authors' own audit) says the preprint "must be cited in the manuscript and disclosed in the cover letter"; submission/extension_statement.md discloses it only outside the manuscript and records the NeurIPS decision as pending. (6) I could not independently fetch the Nature Portfolio preprint/duplicate-publication policy page (nature.com redirected to an auth wall), so the exact policy wording in the claim is taken from the authors' own audit rather than verified by me; the factual core (no citation in the Article, one weak citation in SI) is fully verified.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.92):** Checked in the snapshot: (1) grep of main.tex for "safaai2026localcredit|2607.03556|preprint|arXiv" -> 0 hits; the only self-citation in the main text is \citep{safaai2026gainload} at main.tex:421 (the forward gain-load preprint, arXiv:2607.24990). pdftotext of main.pdf: "2607.03556" 0 hits; the compiled reference list contains only arXiv:2607.24990. (2) supplementary.tex:2853 is the sole citation: "Some of these analyses were also reported in the associated preprint \citep{safaai2026localcredit}", scoped to Supplementary Figs. S1-S4 (regular-tree controls), rendered as [6] in supplementary/supplementary.pdf; the theory sections (SI 58-714) and main §R1/§R2 and Methods carry no such note. (3) references.bib:356-365 has the entry (Safaai, Richards, Sabatini, arXiv:2607.03556, q-bio.NC). (4) Overlap is real: neurips/local_credit_assignment_body.tex lines 2-35 and 84-132 present the exact "local eligibility x compartment error" factorization, path gain, LocalCA rule, 3F/4F/5F rules, matched-width/scalar-fallback and neuron-wise feedback ladder on MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/figure-ground MNIST; main.tex:82-106 (§R1 factorization, LocalCA), 160 (Fig 2 ladder caption) and 477 present the same objects without attribution. (5) scripts/audit_neurips_text_overlap.py:101-108 flags cited=False and its comment states that "a main-text self-citation ... [is] not [required]"; analysis/NEURIPS_TEXT_REUSE_AUDIT.md reports "Earlier work cited in the journal manuscript: NO" -- so the omission is a deliberate authorial choice, not an oversight. (6) submission/extension_statement.md discloses both arXiv records and a "pending official conference decision", and analysis/reference_audit.md:45 states the preprint "must be cited in the manuscript". Correction to the reviewer's framing: the disclosure is not only in the cover letter/extension statement (it exists once in the SI), and Nature Portfolio's preprint policy permits rather than mandates citing preprints; the applicable expectation is the duplicate/prior-publication policy for expanded conference/preprint work, under which the earlier record should be cited in the Article and the extension stated. Could not verify the current NeurIPS decision status beyond the extension statement.
- **Suggestion:** Add one sentence in Introduction (after 67) or Methods (~1163): 'The conductance-tree gradient factorization and regular-tree feedback ladder were introduced in our preprint [safaai2026localcredit]; here we add ...'. Cite again at SI S1-S4; list both arXiv records as related manuscripts; update audit expectation.

#### M004 — Fig 3G 'utility' validation on 540 fits is a scaled cosine and a bandwidth-ordering; it does not test noise rejection
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:192-194, Fig 3G caption (201); Methods 757-762, 787-800; supplementary.tex ~1290-1300, Fig S16D
- **Claim:** Fig 3G and the R3 sentence at main.tex 190-193 present Spearman rho=0.951 (P1) / 0.924 (accuracy) over 540 fits as a test of the update-utility account, but with Sigma_route=0 the deterministic score equals ||mu||^2 [cos(mu,m)]_+^2/(2L) exactly (reproduced to 6e-12), i.e. a per-seed monotone transform of the clipped initial cosine, and the norm-matched progress endpoint P1 is itself the initial cosine to first order (Pearson 0.999 across the 540 fits), so the 0.951 is near-circular; the bare cosine predicts both endpoints better (0.991/0.960; clipped 0.967/0.943). The pooled n=540 also contains 205 rows that are exact duplicates of other rows (identical full-rank fits at K=8 and identical K=1 routes under different family names), which is not stated for the pooled correlations, and the panel contains no noise term so it cannot bear on noise rejection. The manuscript already concedes that the correlations 'mainly reflected the benefit of larger bandwidth' and are retrospective (main 195-198), and the interval is correctly labeled as seed-block; the fix is disclosure and reframing: state the cosine reduction and P1~cos relation, report the cosine baseline and the n=27 condition-mean rho (0.976/0.974) and within-budget rho per K (0.76/0.86/0.90/0.17), note the duplicate rows, and describe G as an initial-alignment consistency check rather than a test of 'this account'.
- **Evidence:** Reconstructed U_det=g2·max(cos,0)²/(2L) from moment_scores.csv, max |diff| 6e-12; cosine Spearman 0.967 (P₁) / 0.943 (acc) vs 0.951/0.924. Mean cos at K=4: ancestry 0.976, random-rank 0.860, derangement −0.927. Within-budget ρ vs accuracy 0.76/0.86/0.90 but 0.17 at K=8; 205/540 rows duplicate; ρ on 27 condition means 0.974/0.976.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked main.tex 164-201 (R3 text, Eq. phaseutility, Fig 3G caption), Methods 748-770, supplementary.tex 1315-1370 and Table S3 (4380-4395), scripts/analyze_review_evidence.py 83-166, and recomputed everything from source_data/review_evidence_reanalysis/moment_scores.csv (2700 rows; 540 dendritic_tree rows = 27 condition_ids x 20 seeds) and pooled_correlations.csv.

Reduction: U_det = [r]_+^2/(2 L ||m||^2) reproduces the stored deterministic_maximum_bound_decrease to 6.1e-12. Taking ||mu||^2 per seed from the exact_compartment_transport row (there r == ||m||^2 exactly) and cos(mu,m) = r/(||mu|| ||m||), U_det = ||mu||^2 [cos]_+^2/(2L) also to 6.1e-12. So within a seed the score is a monotone transform of the clipped initial cosine; confirmed. (Note: the CSV column initial_gradient_cosine is a different quantity — e.g. 0.362 at K=1 for all families, 0.0 for derangement — and does NOT reproduce the reviewer's numbers; the reviewer's numbers come from the derived cos(mu,m).)

Cosine baselines: Spearman of [cos]_+ with P1 = 0.9666, with heldout accuracy = 0.943 (reviewer's 0.967/0.943 confirmed); unclipped cos does even better: 0.9914 (P1) and 0.9596 (acc), vs U_det 0.9509/0.9242 (stored values match Table S3). Mean cos(mu,m) at K=4: ancestry 0.976, random_rank_k 0.860, derangement -0.927 (confirmed exactly). Crucially, P1 (Eq. normmatchedprogress, Methods ~776) is essentially the cosine itself: Pearson(P1, cos) = 0.9991, Spearman 0.9914 across the 540 fits, so the Fig 3G x- and y-axes are two functions of the same initial-alignment quantity; the 0.951 is close to circular.

Within-budget Spearman U vs accuracy: K=1 0.760 (n=140), K=2 0.861, K=4 0.902, K=8 0.169 (n=160) — confirmed. rho(K, acc)=0.758, rho(K, P1)=0.797, rho(U,K)=0.734. Condition-mean (n=27) rho: U-P1 0.9764, U-acc 0.9739 — confirmed. Duplicates: 205 of 540 rows share (seed, U, accuracy) with another row — confirmed; origin: at K=8 seven families (backpropagation, exact_compartment_transport, ancestry, depth_interleaved, learned_rank, random_rank, random_sparse) are the identical full-rank fit (20 seeds x 6 duplicate rows = 120), and at K=1 five families (ancestry, depth_interleaved, neuron_indexed_shared, random_sparse, derangement) are identical (80), plus a few K=2 coincidences. This is not disclosed for the pooled n=540 correlations (the 'equivalent representations' caveat at main 767 / supp 1367 refers to architecture cells and selection curves, and the script docstring's 'duplicate architecture implementations excluded' only drops the other four architectures).

Points where the finding overstates: (i) 'driven by the K ordering' — within-budget rho at K=1,2,4 is 0.76-0.90, so route-alignment differences within a budget carry much of the correlation, and the manuscript already says at main 195 'The correlations mainly reflected the benefit of larger bandwidth: always choosing K=8 matched the score's selection success' and at 197 'explanatory consistency'. (ii) 'false precision' — the interval is explicitly labeled as a seed-block interval (main 192, caption 201 '5,000 bootstrap draws over 20 seed blocks', Methods 761 'whole-seed bootstrap draws'), so its resampling unit is disclosed; the residual issue is only that a reader may not realize the 27 conditions are fixed and 205 rows are duplicates. What the text does not disclose anywhere: that with Sigma_route=0 the score collapses to a scaled clipped cosine, that P1 is itself ~cos, and that the bare cosine outperforms U on both endpoints. Main 190 'We tested this account' (the signal/noise trade-off account) with a zero-noise score is therefore a framing overreach, though Methods 757 does state Sigma_route=0.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.92):** Text checked (snapshot): main.tex 192-194 ("We tested this account against the eight-context routing task ... 540 existing fits ... rho=0.924 (0.911-0.940) ... rho=0.951 (0.949-0.954; Fig 3G)"), Fig 3G caption at 201 ("Deterministic moment utility ... correlates with norm-matched one-step progress at Spearman rho=0.951, 95% seed-block interval 0.949-0.954. Nonpositive alignment gives zero utility in 214 fits"), Eq. phaseutility at 173-179 (U=[mu^T m]_+^2/(2L(||m||^2+tr Sigma_route))), Methods 757 ("The deterministic score used Sigma_route=0"), P1 definition at 787-793; supplementary.tex 1339-1345 and Table S3 (tab:credit_phase, lines 4381-4396), which report only the four utility correlations and no alignment/cosine/capture baseline (grep for 0.877/0.838 finds nothing in main or supplement). Fig 3G artwork viewed (figure_03.pdf): U-vs-P1 scatter, 214 points on the U=0 wall spanning P1 from about -1.1 to +0.3.

Code checked: scripts/analyze_review_evidence.py lines 83-104 compute deterministic_maximum_bound_decrease = max(r,0)^2/(2 L v) with r = retained_signal_inner_product (= mu.m, validated against the exact_compartment_transport gradient at lines 107-135) and v = operator_gradient_norm_sq (= ||m||^2); pooled_correlations() lines 139-170 uses the 540 dendritic_tree rows and resamples 20 seed blocks only.

Recomputed from source_data/review_evidence_reanalysis/moment_scores.csv (540 dendritic_tree rows, 27 conditions x 20 seeds), with cos = r/sqrt(v ||mu||^2) and ||mu||^2 taken from each seed's exact_compartment_transport row:
- Reduction: max |U_det - ||mu||^2 [cos]_+^2/(2L)| = 6.1e-12. ||mu||^2/(2L) is constant within a seed (range across seeds 1.449-1.857), so U_det is a monotone transform of the initial cosine within each seed.
- Baselines: Spearman signed cosine vs P1 = 0.991, vs accuracy = 0.960 (seed-block CIs 0.988-0.995 and 0.952-0.967); positive-part cosine [cos]_+ = 0.967 / 0.943 (this is the reviewer's quoted number); utility = 0.951 / 0.924 (matches pooled_correlations.csv and text). The bare cosine beats the utility on both endpoints.
- Near-identity with P1: P1 is an affine function of the same initial cosine, per-seed linear-fit R^2 0.9955-0.9997 (pooled Pearson R^2 0.998), so the U-P1 correlation is essentially built in.
- Within-budget Spearman U vs accuracy: K=1 0.760, K=2 0.861, K=4 0.902, K=8 0.169 (U vs P1: 0.773/0.880/0.917/0.572); at K=8 seven of eight families have identical U=1.6596, P1=1.000 in every seed (only derangement differs). K alone: 0.758 (acc) / 0.797 (P1).
- Mean cos at K=4: ancestry 0.976, random-sparse 0.854, random-rank 0.860, depth-interleaved 0.768, derangement -0.927 (all match the reviewer).
- Duplicates: 202/540 rows repeat another condition's (seed, moments, P1, accuracy) exactly (reviewer said 205); only 10-12 distinct U values per seed.
- Condition-mean (n=27) Spearman: U vs P1 0.976, vs accuracy 0.974.
- Bootstrap: seed-block interval reproduced exactly (0.9486-0.9542 for P1; 0.9108-0.9401 for accuracy); a condition-block bootstrap over the 27 conditions gives 0.885-0.970 (P1) and 0.834-0.946 (accuracy), so the narrow interval only reflects seed resampling with the 27 conditions fixed. Zero-utility count 214 reproduced (cos<=0 in 60 rows; the rest have r<=0 with clamped U).

Mitigations present in the text: Methods 757 states Sigma_route=0; main 194 says correlations "mainly reflected the benefit of larger bandwidth"; 196 calls it "explanatory consistency". The main text and caption never state the reduction to a scaled squared cosine, never report any alignment baseline, and place the analysis under the heading "Restricting noisy feedback can improve expected progress" as the test of "this account".
- **Suggestion:** State the reduction at main 192 and Methods 757; report the cosine baseline (0.967/0.943) beside the utility; report condition-mean ρ (n=27) and within-budget ρ per K; describe the interval as seed-resampling conditional on fixed conditions; reword Fig 3G and the R3 sentence as an initial-alignment consistency check; reserve 'noise' claims for screens B-F.

#### M007 — All aligned-D3 fits hit the 180-epoch cap with validation loss still falling; headline D3 contrasts are undisclosed fixed-budget numbers
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:272-274, 884-886, 900; supplementary.tex:2497-2520, 2565-2590; Figs S28, S31C/H
- **Claim:** Every aligned-D3 fit behind the headline depth and credit contrasts (full BP, soma-broadcast BP, matched-optimizer broadcast, exact-path and shared-soma LocalCA; also the H3 rerun and H4 D2/D4 serial arms) ran to the 180-epoch cap with validation loss still falling (0.03-0.04 per 30 epochs for full BP/path/broadcast; accelerating 0.014→0.028 per 30 epochs for the LR-0.0008 shared-soma and matched-optimizer arms), while the text (main.tex:885) says only "validation stopping within 180 epochs" and no cohort's Source Data records best_epoch. D1 aligned arms also mostly reached the cap but were flat, so the 30.9-pt depth effect is a robust-sign fixed-budget lower bound; the 10.96-pt path-vs-shared and especially the 13.47-pt "optimizer cost" contrasts (main.tex:274, 900; SI 2577-2586; Figs S28B, S31H) compare unconverged arms trained at different learning rates for the same epoch budget, so their magnitudes and the "arise from the optimization settings" interpretation are confounded with budget. Disclose the cap binding, add best_epoch/late-slope to Source Data, and either qualify these as fixed-180-epoch quantities or run a longer-budget check for the five aligned-D3 arms.
- **Evidence:** training_summary.json: nested family α=1 D3 best_epoch 180,180,178,180,180,179,180,180,180,180, last-10 val drop 0.011-0.015; serial BP D3 179-180 in 10/10; α=0 arms stopped at 27-107; point-credit, H3 rerun LocalCA path/shared and BP D3 10/10 at cap; D1 1-2/10. Nowhere reported.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Snapshot text checked: main.tex:885-886 ("Adam used validation stopping within 180 epochs, step 0.003 for BP and 0.0008 for LocalCA"), main.tex:274 and 900 (10.96 / 0.64 / 13.47 contrasts, "different endpoints under BP arise from the optimization settings"), supplementary.tex:2497-2520 (30.86-pt depth effect) and 2565-2590 (credit/optimizer contrasts). Neither main nor SI states that the 180-epoch cap was reached; grep for epoch/budget/stopping in SI S5 lines 2400-2919 finds no disclosure, and Source Data for these cohorts (nonlinear_physical_depth_confirmatory, point_dendrite_credit_controls, physical_depth_clean_source_replication, physical_depth_h4_factorial, task_family_alignment) carry no best_epoch column (grep -rl best_epoch source_data hits only unrelated cohorts). Config: journal/configs/nonlinear_physical_depth/confirmatory/aligned_shunting_bp.yaml lines 165-170 (epochs 180, early_stopping true, patience 30).

Run directories located and re-read (training_summary.json, fields best_epoch / valid_losses): drafts/dendritic-local-learning-worktree-archive-20260817/journal/{nonlinear_physical_depth_runs, point_dendrite_credit_runs}, Code/.dendritic-modeling-journal-runtimes/h4-a99c3a7/{nonlinear_physical_depth_runs, physical_depth_h4_runs}, and /n/holylfs06/LABS/kempner_project_b/Lab/dendritic/HS/LOCAL_LEARNING/journal_extension_20260820/sweep_runs/task_family_alignment. Joined to seed_outcomes via run_dir/config_index.

Recomputed (confirmatory cohort, seeds 10200-10209): aligned shunting BP D3 best_epoch 180,180,180,180,179,179,180,180,180,180, all 180 epochs run, last-10-epoch validation-loss drop 0.004-0.013; aligned LocalCA path D3 and shared D3 20/20 at cap (best 178-180), drops 0.007-0.015. Point-credit cohort D3: soma-broadcast BP 10/10 at cap (best 179-180, drops 0.010-0.015), matched-optimizer broadcast 10/10 at cap (drops 0.007-0.014). Clean-source 430-fit rerun: aligned shunting D3 37/40 at cap (the 3 early stops are D1-reference rows folded into that group). H4 cohort: aligned serial shunting D2/D4 BP, path, shared 10/10 at cap. Zero-alignment/shuffled arms stopped at 46-95 epochs (best 16-65). Mean validation-loss trajectories at epochs 90/120/150/180: full BP D3 0.495/0.435/0.398/0.367 (still falling 0.030 per 30 epochs, decelerating); LocalCA path D3 0.604/0.566/0.525/0.487 (0.039/30 ep, not decelerating); shared-soma LocalCA D3 0.622/0.607/0.587/0.559 (drops 0.014, 0.021, 0.028: accelerating); matched-optimizer broadcast identical to shared (0.5585 final); soma-broadcast BP D3 0.573/0.523/0.479/0.439 (0.040/30 ep).

Refuted detail: the finding's "D1 stopped early" is wrong. Aligned shunting BP D1 ran to 180 epochs in 8/10 seeds (best_epoch 175-180; two seeds stopped at 110/118) and LocalCA D1 in 19/20; but D1 validation loss was flat (0.6571 at epoch 60 to 0.6556 at 180, per-10-epoch drops within ±0.002), i.e. D1 plateaued near its floor while D3 was still descending. So the budget confound does not run through "D1 stopped early"; it runs through D3 arms being unconverged at the cap. Consequence: a longer budget would likely enlarge, not shrink, the 30.9-pt depth effect (sign robust), whereas the 13.47-pt "optimizer cost" and the 10.96-pt path-vs-shared contrast compare arms whose slower (LR 0.0008) members are in the steepest, still-accelerating part of their curves at epoch 180, so those magnitudes and the main-text attribution to "optimization settings" (main.tex:900) are genuinely budget-dependent. Also note the manuscript already labels 10.96 as conditional on the split optimizer (supplementary.tex:2583-2586), which softens but does not disclose the cap.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** TEXT CHECKED. main.tex:885-886 says only "Adam used validation stopping within 180 epochs, step 0.003 for BP and 0.0008 for LocalCA"; main.tex:274 and 900 attribute the different endpoints to "the optimization settings"; supplementary.tex:2497-2590 gives the 30.86, 0.64, 13.47 and 10.96 contrasts and lines 2540/2600/2657/2685 say only that the "stopping rule" was unchanged; captions S28 (supp line 4129) and S31 (line 4150) never mention epochs, patience or the cap. Nowhere is patience=30 or the fact that the cap bound stated. Source Data CSVs (nonlinear_physical_depth_confirmatory/seed_outcomes.csv, point_dendrite_credit_controls/*.csv, physical_alignment_dose/*.csv, task_family_alignment/*.csv) carry no epoch/best_epoch columns.

CONFIG/CODE CHECKED. .dendritic-modeling-journal-runtimes/h4-a99c3a7/nonlinear_physical_depth_runs/*/original_config.yaml: epochs 180, early_stopping true, patience 30, load_best_state_dict true, lr 0.003 (BP) / 0.0008 (LocalCA). src/dendritic_modeling/training/strategies/standard.py:1013-1032, 1091-1092: best_epoch = 1-indexed epoch_counter; patience counter resets on improvement; stop fires after 30 non-improving epochs. So a fit that ran 180 epochs with best_epoch >= 151 never triggered stopping.

I therefore recomputed on (i) the same-seed 430-fit H3 rerun (seeds 10200-10209, 2026-08-19; the paper's "same-seed 430-fit rerun", main.tex:906) and (ii) the accessible point-credit runs at /n/holylabs/kempner_dev/Users/hsafaai/Code/dendritic-modeling/point_dendrite_credit_runs/ (2026-08-13, the actual source of the 0.64 contrast).

D1 [8]: best_epoch = [179,175,73,175,178,88,180,169,166,178]; stopping actually fired in only 2/10 (ran 103 and 118 epochs), last-10 drop within +/-0.0017 (plateau). D2: 10/10 ran 180, best >= 179 in 7/10, drops <= 0.004.

ACCURACY TRAJECTORIES (performance/epochs/epochN.json, mean valid/test acc at epochs 140/160/170/180):
- Aligned D3 full BP: ~0.90/0.91/0.91/0.915; already 0.89-0.92 at epoch 140, final 0.908-0.931. D1 flat at 0.60-0.62 throughout. The 30.9-pt depth effect is therefore NOT materially budget-dependent (D3 was already >= 28 pts above D1 by epoch 140 and had plateaued).

Side note from the same check: physical_depth_clean_source_replication/historical_concordance.csv shows 13/430 rerun fits differing from the historical value by > 0.5 pt (max 15.0 pts), so the rerun is not bit-identical to the 2026-08-12 originals, but the stopping behaviour above depends only on the shared config and code.
