#### M010 — Second-animal and 47-cell K=4 'capture' is actually 1 minus the weighted residual norm
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** supplementary.tex:2984-2995, 4119-4122 (S27C); main.tex:333, 340 (Fig 7G); figure_S27
- **Claim:** The Pinky K=4 values reported as 'capture' in supplementary.tex 2986-2989 (0.902/0.926/0.374/0.316/0.245) are mean(1 - weighted residual norm), and the 'capture advantages' 0.528/0.586/0.657 (2990-2993) are control-minus-subtree residual-norm differences; under the manuscript's own Eq. (reconstructioncapture) the energy captures are 0.979/0.987/0.543/0.516/0.424 and the advantages 0.436/0.463/0.555. Fig S27C likewise plots (1 - residual)/nonzeros, and the S27 caption calls Fig 7G 'capture' although 7G is (correctly) labeled as a residual-norm difference. The 47-cell SI text, Tables S25-S26 and Fig 7C-F all use true energy capture, so the Pinky paragraph is not comparable to them. All sign-based conclusions (9/10, 10/10, 10/10; two-animal directional agreement; 47/47) are unchanged by the transform, but the numbers and CIs as printed are wrong for the stated estimand and should be recomputed as energy capture or relabeled, with the CSV column renamed.
- **Evidence:** analyze_pinky_v185_replication.py 113-126 differences 'residual' columns stored as 'morphology_capture_advantage'; 389-392 S27C=(1−residual). At K=4 mean(1−residual)=SI numbers; mean(1−residual²)=0.979/0.987/0.543/0.516/0.424, advantages 0.436/0.463/0.555 (signs unchanged). 47-cell contrasts 0.274/0.215/0.266 match sqrt(1−C) differences; capture differences 0.412/0.310/0.393. build_main_figure_07.py 272-279 docstring flags the mislabel.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** (1) Estimand in code: journal/code/reconstructed_tree/analyze_microns_morphology_credit.py lines 409-416 define residual_for_dictionary as norm(weighted_target - reconstruction)/norm(weighted_target) — a weighted residual NORM ratio — and pca_residual = sqrt(1 - retained singular power). So Eq. (reconstructioncapture) capture (main.tex 320-323, energy fraction) is C = 1 - residual^2, not 1 - residual. Confirmed empirically on the 47-cell file source_data/microns_v661_replication/routing_capacity_20stream.csv.gz: credit_capture - (1 - residual^2) max |diff| = 1e-16, whereas vs (1 - residual) max |diff| = 0.25. (2) Pinky SI numbers: supplementary.tex 2984-2995 report 'mean ancestry-route capture 0.902 ... 0.926 PCA ... 0.374 random ... 0.316 depth ... 0.245 shuffled' and 'capture advantages 0.528/0.586/0.657'. Recomputed from source_data/pinky_v185_replication/routing/feedback_compression_curves.csv (K=4, 10 qc cells): mean(1-residual) = 0.9019/0.9264/0.3737/0.3160/0.2450 (exact match to text); mean(1-residual^2) = 0.9788/0.9874/0.5433/0.5155/0.4238. Contrasts in k4_cross_animal_contrasts.csv (column morphology_capture_advantage) equal control-minus-subtree residual differences to 1e-16 (analyze_pinky_v185_replication.py 113-126 pivots on 'residual'); means 0.5282/0.5859/0.6569 vs energy-capture differences 0.4356/0.4633/0.5550. Positive-cell counts (9/10, 10/10, 10/10) and sign-flip P are invariant; bootstrap CIs are not. (3) 47-cell: residual-norm contrasts 0.2744/0.2140/0.2637 (47/47, 46/47, 47/47) match Fig 7G; capture differences would be 0.4119/0.3102/0.3930. The single-stream routing/feedback_compression_curves.csv reproduces the contrasts CSV to 1e-16. (4) Fig 7C-F use credit_capture (build_main_figure_07.py line 214; label 'field capture'), i.e. energy; Fig 7G x-label (line 745, artwork) says 'control - subtree weighted residual norm' and the docstring at 273-279 flags the column mislabel. Fig S27B label 'weighted residual'; S27C 'capture / 1,000 nonzeros' is computed as (1 - residual)/nonzeros (script 391-392), so it is also not Eq. capture. S27 caption (supplementary.tex 4122) says 'Cross-cohort capture appears in main Fig. 7G'. The 47-cell SI text at 3125-3127 (0.756/0.839/0.302/0.441/0.330 at K=8) DOES match credit_capture energy means, as do Tables S25-S26 (4952-4990), so only the Pinky paragraph and the S27 caption/panel C use the residual-based quantity under the name 'capture'. Main.tex 333 ('Subtree capture exceeded...') is directionally correct under either estimand; main.tex 340 (7G) is correctly labeled. EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md line 53 repeats 0.902 etc. as 'capture'.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** (1) Definition of "residual": source_data/microns_v661_replication/routing_capacity_20stream.csv.gz carries both `residual` and `credit_capture`; max |credit_capture − (1 − residual²)| = 1.1e-16 over all rows, whereas max |credit_capture − (1 − residual)| = 0.25. So `residual` is the relative weighted residual NORM and Eq. (reconstructioncapture) capture (main.tex 319-323) equals 1 − residual². (2) Pinky SI numbers: from source_data/pinky_v185_replication/routing/feedback_compression_curves.csv at channels==4 (10 cells/method), mean(1 − residual) = 0.902 (ancestry), 0.926 (dense PCA), 0.374 (random), 0.316 (depth bins), 0.245 (shuffled) — exactly the values printed as "capture" at supplementary.tex 2985-2989. The Eq.-defined capture, mean(1 − residual²), is 0.979 / 0.987 / 0.543 / 0.516 / 0.424. (3) Pinky "advantages": k4_cross_animal_contrasts.csv column `morphology_capture_advantage` (Pinky, n=10) means 0.528 / 0.586 / 0.657 = control-minus-ancestry residual-norm differences (scripts/analyze_pinky_v185_replication.py 113-121 pivots on values="residual"); capture differences would be 0.436 / 0.463 / 0.555. Sign counts unchanged (9/10, 10/10, 10/10). (4) 47-cell: same file, minnie65 v661 n=47 means 0.274 / 0.215 / 0.266 = residual-norm differences (SI summary.json "minnie65_k4_reference" stores the same); recomputed from microns_v661_replication/routing/feedback_compression_curves.csv at K=4: residual diffs 0.274/0.215/0.266, capture diffs 0.412/0.312/0.397. (5) Inconsistency across cohorts confirmed: the 47-cell SI text (supplementary.tex 3125-3128: 0.756 / 0.839 / 0.302 / 0.441 / 0.330 at K=8) matches mean(1 − residual²) from the 20-stream file (0.756/0.839/0.302/0.441/0.330), i.e. the Eq.-defined capture — so the same word "capture" denotes 1 − r² for mouse 1 and 1 − r for mouse 2 in adjacent SI sections. (6) Labels: main Fig 7G (figure_07.pdf, and caption main.tex 340) correctly says "control − subtree weighted residual norm"; build_main_figure_07.py 272-279 and 743-745 explicitly document the column mislabel. Supplementary Fig S27 panel B axis says "weighted residual" (correct), but panel C is titled "Capture per routing coefficient"/"capture / 1,000 nonzeros" while build_supplementary_figure_s27_native.py line 46 computes (1 − residual)/wiring_nonzeros*1000 and the caption (supplementary.tex 4122) calls it "Four-channel capture"; the caption also says "Cross-cohort capture appears in main Fig. 7G" though 7G plots residual-norm differences. Main text line 333 reports only sign counts, which are invariant under the monotone transform, so the directional second-animal conclusion is unaffected. Minor: reviewer's 47-cell capture differences (0.412/0.310/0.393) differ from mine (0.412/0.312/0.397) in the third decimal, likely a different data file; immaterial.
- **Suggestion:** Pick one estimand: recompute Pinky/47-cell as energy capture (0.979 vs 0.987; 0.436/0.463/0.555) or keep residual norm and say so in SI text, S27 caption and y-labels; rename column/variable; add one Methods sentence stating what 7G plots.

#### M011 — 'Physical calibration sharply limited the effect' misstates what changes: descendant attenuation is unchanged, selectivity is lost because the whole neuron is attenuated
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:344 (title), 360, 364; caption 369 F; supplementary.tex:3465-3469
- **Claim:** Main text §R9 (main.tex:344, 360-361, 364-365) and Discussion (421) state that physical calibration 'sharply limited the effect' and that shunting changes transport 'principally in high-conductance states'. The authors' own phase-1 data show otherwise when dose is held at a fixed fraction of local input conductance or at fixed nS: at unit input-normalized dose the descendant gradient is attenuated ~0.55-0.60 log units at every R_m (energy ratio 0.33-0.36), and at 5 nS/R_m=15,000 by 0.53; what R_m changes is off-route sparing (−0.05 at R_m=300 vs −0.35 at R_m=15,000), i.e. selectivity, not the magnitude of the change. The shunt-minus-current contrast reaches ~0 at R_m=15,000 partly because the current-injection control has its own unsigned localization ≈0.20 (descendant enhancement), which the absolute-value index equates with attenuation (signed contrast −0.36, 0/8 cells positive). In addition, the headline panel Fig 8F uses the relative-local dose, so the R_m=15,000 shunt is ~0.07 nS versus ~3.6 nS at R_m=300; the 'calibration' comparison therefore also shrinks the absolute dose ~50-fold, which the caption does not disclose. Recommended: reword title/360-365/421 to 'selectivity, not transport change, depends on conductance state'; report absolute dose in nS for Fig 8F; add signed descendant and off-route changes (Fig 8F or S11) and the signed contrast; the proposed experiment at 425 should compare below-site versus off-route plasticity change as a ratio, since at standard calibration both are expected to fall.
- **Evidence:** site_outcomes.csv.gz dose 1: descendant −0.600/−0.552/−0.547, matched −0.054/−0.104/−0.353 at Rm 300/1000/15000 bg 0; localization 0.545/0.448/0.194; bg 4 at Rm 15000: off-route −0.202, localization 0.404.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Recomputed from /n/holylabs/kempner_dev/Users/hsafaai/Code/dendritic-modeling/drafts/dendritic-local-learning/journal/source_data/focal_selectivity_phase1/site_outcomes.csv.gz (16,362 rows; schemes relative_local / fixed_absolute_ns / input_conductance_normalized). Reviewer's numbers reproduce exactly for scheme input_conductance_normalized, dose 1, focal shunt, site means: descendant_median_signed_log_change −0.600/−0.552/−0.547 and matched (off-route) −0.054/−0.104/−0.353 at Rm 300/1000/15000, bg 0; localization 0.545/0.448/0.194; Rm 15000 bg 4: off-route −0.202, localization 0.404. Cell-averaged values agree (desc −0.609/−0.565/−0.559; off −0.064/−0.123/−0.378; descendant gradient-energy ratio 0.36/0.36/0.33, off-route 0.89/0.81/0.50). Off-route attenuated fraction = 1.00 at Rm 15000. paired_contrasts.csv rows 348-349: shunt-minus-current localization_index −0.00036 at Rm 15000/bg0/unit input dose, but signed_localization contrast −0.362 (0/8 cells positive) — the near-zero unsigned contrast arises because the current-injection control itself has localization ≈0.20 (descendants enhanced +0.216, off-route +0.018), which the absolute-value index equates with the shunt's attenuation (−0.547 vs −0.353). Fixed 5 nS at Rm 15000 bg 0 (supp line 3489: contrast 0.0061, CI includes zero): descendant −0.525, off-route −0.312, shunt localization 0.213. HOWEVER the reviewer's 'descendant attenuation unchanged' does NOT describe the scheme main.tex:360 and Fig 8F actually use. Methods main.tex:1005-1006 and supplementary.tex:3466 say Fig 8F retains the 'relative dose' (1× local membrane+synaptic conductance); physical_cable_sensitivity/focal_localization.csv.gz confirms: Ra150_Rm15000 delta_conductance mean 0.073 nS vs 3.63 nS at Ra150_Rm300 (site_outcomes relative_local dose 1: median 0.037 vs 1.83 nS). Under that scheme at Rm 15000 everything is tiny (descendant_change 0.012, off-route 0.006; cell_primary_contrasts.csv difference −0.0019 original_eight / +0.0024 v661, matching main.tex:360). So Fig 8F's 'calibration' axis co-varies the absolute shunt ~50-fold; the sharp limitation at standard Rm is substantially a dose-definition effect, undisclosed in the caption (main.tex:369 states dose-relative-to-local-conductance only for panel C). Text checked: main.tex:344 (title), 360-361, 364-365, 369 (caption F), 421, 425; supplementary.tex:3455-3497, 4008-4011 (S11 caption: panels A contrast, B S_k, C fractions; no off-route signed change shown). Fig 8B shows off-route abs change only for the permissive proxy.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Recomputed from /n/holylabs/.../journal/source_data/focal_selectivity_phase1/site_outcomes.csv.gz (16,362 rows, 101 sites, 8 cells; matches summary.json). Focal-shunt rows, input_conductance_normalized dose 1, background 0, site-level means: descendant_median_signed_log_change = -0.5997 / -0.5521 / -0.5468 and matched (off-route) = -0.0543 / -0.1041 / -0.3527 at Rm = 300 / 1000 / 15000; localization_index = 0.5454 / 0.4480 / 0.1941. Rm=15000, background 4: off-route -0.2025, localization 0.4039. Reviewer's numbers are exactly reproduced. Off-route/on-route ratio rises from 0.09-0.11 (Rm 300) to 0.65-0.68 (Rm 15000), i.e. on-path attenuation ~42% (1-e^-0.55) is Rm-invariant while off-route attenuation grows from ~5% to ~30%. So the mechanistic reading (selectivity, not descendant attenuation, is what high Rm removes) is correct at this dose definition. BUT the finding overreaches on what the manuscript states and on which dose Fig 8F uses. (1) Fig 8F / main.tex:360 and Table S physical_focal (supplementary.tex:5030-5047) come from source_data/physical_cable_sensitivity (build_main_figure_08.py:527-575), whose summary.json defines dose as "one times local leak plus excitatory plus inhibitory conductance" (relative-local). Under that scheme the absolute shunt dose scales with leak: median delta_conductance_ns at unit dose = 1.829 nS (Rm 300) vs 0.037 nS (Rm 15000), ~50x smaller, and descendant signed change is -0.107 vs -0.014 (phase1 matrix, relative_local dose 1, bg 0). So under the dose actually plotted in Fig 8F, descendant attenuation is NOT unchanged; it shrinks ~8x. The reviewer's "unchanged" claim holds only for the input-normalized scheme, which by construction holds the local voltage perturbation comparable. (2) The manuscript defines "the effect" as the shunt-minus-current localization contrast (main.tex:357-358, 360; caption F at 369) and never claims descendants are unattenuated at Rm=15000; the supplement already reports the input-normalized recovery (supplementary.tex:3487-3491: -0.0004 -> 0.1210 -> 0.2587 with background 0/1/4) and that all 8,181 shunt rows attenuated descendants. What is genuinely missing: no text or panel (Fig 8F, S11) reports the off-route signed change, so a reader cannot see that at standard calibration a comparably-dosed shunt is a broad ~30-42% attenuation with weak subtree selectivity rather than a null. Also unstated in the main text: Fig 8F's relative-local dose falls ~50x in nS across the Rm sweep, so panel F conflates dose shrinkage with electrotonic selectivity; the input-normalized matrix that disentangles these is only in the supplement. (3) Discussion main.tex:425 already proposes comparing plasticity "below an inhibitory site with electrotonically matched off-route sites" - i.e. it is already a below-vs-off-route design; the reviewer's suggested "ratio" sharpening is essentially what is written, although the predicted outcome at standard calibration (both attenuated, ratio approaching 1) is not stated.
- **Suggestion:** Rewrite 360-364 and the title: shunt still attenuates descendants by the on-path factor but attenuates the rest almost as much; high-conductance states make it subtree-selective. Add off-route signed change to Fig 8F/S11. Sharpen the Discussion experiment: ratio of plasticity change below vs beyond the site.

#### M012 — In a passive tree the focal shunt IS a diagonal route gain on the descendant subtree; the 'reshapes transfer, not a diagonal gain' framing is wrong
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** main.tex:353, 521; supplementary.tex:518-520, 1046-1049, 3342-3349
- **Claim:** In the paper's passive reciprocal-cable model (symmetric tree G, soma-only loss), Eq. sherman implies q'_i/q_i = 1/(1+kappa_k r_k) for every descendant of the shunt and 1 - kappa_k r_k rho_k(m)/(1+kappa_k r_k) for every site whose lowest common ancestor with k is m, i.e. the adjoint change is a piecewise-constant gain on the ancestry partition (a combination of nested subtree-route columns weighted by the baseline soma route q), not a new spatial profile outside the anatomical route dictionary; the same holds for the fixed-Jacobian active linearization. The "adjoint-only" descendant change and localization in Fig 8D / supplementary 3390-3399 are therefore exact closed forms (log(1+kappa r_k), and its difference from a median over 1-3 LCA classes of |log(1 - kappa r_k rho_k(m)/(1+kappa r_k))|), reproduced from the authors' source data to 1e-13, and the dose and R_m dependence in Fig 8C/F is a function of kappa r_k = dose x g_local x R_in,k and rho_k. Only the driving-force factor (E_E - V'_i) varies within the descendant subtree (observed/closed-form ratio ~0.88 on 8,181 phase-1 shunt rows, correlation 0.995). The main-text and supplement contrast between "reshaping spatial transfer" and "a diagonal gain on fixed dictionary columns" (main.tex:353, 521; supplementary.tex:518-520, 1046-1049) is a false dichotomy for the model actually computed and should be replaced with the exact statement, which turns Fig 8 into direct support for the route-dictionary thesis.
- **Evidence:** Synthetic random passive trees (N=60,80): descendant ratio single value to 1e-12; other-stem value matches closed form (0.94882811). On 8,181 shunt rows, −log(1+Δg·R_in) correlates 0.995 with descendant_median_signed_log_change; unit dose descendant change −0.55 to −0.67 in all nine regimes (prediction −0.693).
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** Text checked: main.tex:353 ("This perturbation can reshape the spatial transfer itself, rather than merely multiplying a fixed dictionary column by a nonzero gain"), main.tex:521 ("Physical shunts can instead change the spatial transfer profiles themselves"), supplementary.tex:518-520 ("need not be a diagonal gain on these fixed columns"), 1046-1049 ("may reshape delivery profiles ... do not assume that it equals a diagonal column gain"), 3342-3349 (S_k = ratio of median |G^-1_ik| as the transport criterion; "Relative or logarithmic changes additionally divide by the baseline q_i").

Model checked: journal/scripts/analyze_physical_cable_sensitivity.py:53-148 and drafts/dendritic-credit-routing/analysis/run_focal_shunting_credit_perturbation.py:60-121 build a symmetric tree-structured G (matrix_symmetry_error recorded; I measured 0.0 on cell 864691135060651419, n=146) and compute q = solve(G^T, e_soma*(V_s - target)), i.e. a soma-only loss, exactly the reviewer's premise.

Algebra recomputed independently (scratch script notes/verify-M012/check.py): on random symmetric trees (n=60, 80) with a shunt at k, q'_i/q_i on all descendants equals 1/(1+kappa r_k) with spread <=1e-16; every off-route site grouped by its lowest common ancestor m with k has ratio 1 - kappa r_k rho_k(m)/(1+kappa r_k), rho_k(m) = (G^-1)_km^2/(r_k r_m), spread <=2.2e-16 within each LCA class. The same descendant constancy holds for a NONSYMMETRIC tree Jacobian (spread 0.0, val 0.61318 = 1/(1+kappa r_k)), so the adjoint factor in the fixed-J active model (Fig 8E,G) is also a piecewise-constant gain.

Real-cell check with the authors' own code and data: for cell 864691135060651419 at unit dose (source_data/focal_decomposition/site_decomposition.csv, primary normalized model e/i scale 0.35, E_E=1, E_I=-0.2), for sites 5224, 5233, 5239, 5247, 5272, 5275: adjoint-only descendant ratio spread <=2.2e-16; the reported "adjoint only" descendant_change equals log(1+kappa r_k) to ~1e-13 (e.g. 0.0114748468151119 vs 0.0114748468151369; 0.1060432805186644 vs 0.1060432805206638); the reported adjoint-only localization_index equals my closed form median_D|log ratio| - median_C|log ratio| to ~1e-13 (e.g. 0.0100382574811545 vs 0.0100382574811773; 0.1057790265807705 vs 0.1057790265827628). Depth-matched unrelated sites carried 1-3 distinct ratio values per site (one per LCA ancestor), so the exact Lambda_k is a median over LCA classes rather than a single rho_k.

Reviewer's numbers: source_data/focal_selectivity_phase1/site_outcomes.csv.gz has 16,362 rows, 8,181 with perturbation == "focal shunt" (confirmed). corr(-log(1+delta_conductance_ns*local_input_resistance_gohm), descendant_median_signed_log_change) = 0.99463 (reviewer: 0.995, confirmed). Under dose_scheme == input_conductance_normalized at dose 1 (x = kappa r_k = 1 exactly, prediction -log 2 = -0.693), the per-regime medians of the full-gradient descendant change are -0.542 to -0.682 across the nine Rm x background regimes (reviewer: -0.55 to -0.67; matches within rounding/mean-vs-median). Observed/predicted ratio median 0.88 across all shunt rows; the shortfall is the driving-force factor (E_E - V'_i), which is not piecewise constant.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Text checked: main.tex:350-353 (Eq. sherman; "This perturbation can reshape the spatial transfer itself, rather than merely multiplying a fixed dictionary column by a nonzero gain"), main.tex:521 ("Physical shunts can instead change the spatial transfer profiles themselves"), supplementary.tex:516-520 ("need not be a diagonal gain on these fixed columns"), 1043-1049 ("may reshape delivery profiles ... do not assume that it equals a diagonal column gain"), 3299-3359 (Eq. s_sherman, s_shunt_amplitude, S_k criterion), 3370-3376 (descendant/unrelated definitions). No sentence in main.tex or the focal supplement section mentions that the descendant ratio is uniform or piecewise constant (grep for uniform/constant/piecewise/subtree in that section: none).

Model code checked: scripts/analyze_physical_cable_sensitivity.py:136-147 builds a symmetric tree matrix (diagonal leak+synaptic, +/- axial couplings to parent); scripts/run_focal_selectivity_phase1.py:64-101 uses adjoint = G'^{-1}e_s via Sherman-Morrison with soma-only unit error and a post-hoc somatic restoring current that does not touch the adjoint; code/reconstructed_tree/run_focal_shunting_credit_perturbation.py:145-177 defines D(k)=soma-rooted descendants and C(k)=depth-matched sites excluding descendants/ancestors/sisters.

Derivation checked: for a tree Green's function, (G^-1)_{ik}=(G^-1)_{is}r_k/q_k for i in D(k), so q'_i/q_i = 1/(1+kappa r_k) exactly; for i off-route with nearest common ancestor c on the k->soma path, q'_i/q_i = 1 - kappa r_k rho_{k,c}/(1+kappa r_k), rho_{k,c}=(G^-1)_{kc}^2/((G^-1)_{cc}(G^-1)_{kk}) in [0,1]. Hence adjoint-only localization = log(1+kappa r_k(1-rho)) >= 0 always.

Numerics (notes/verify-M012/synth.py): random passive trees N=60,80,200: descendant ratio spread 0 to 12 digits, off-route max |ratio - closed form| = 1.1e-16. Real reconstructions (notes/verify-M012/realcell.py, 3 of the 8 cells x 3 regimes, config phase1_passive_matrix.json, same site-selection RNG): max descendant spread 1.0e-15, max off-route closed-form error 3.3e-16; adjoint-only closed-form Lambda vs recorded localization_index over 69 matched sites: r=0.946; rho vs localization r=-0.954; recorded Lambda minus adjoint-only closed form (driving-force part) median -0.065, range -0.25 to -0.002.

Source data (focal_selectivity_phase1/site_outcomes.csv.gz, 8,181 shunt rows): corr(-log(1+dg*R_in), descendant_median_signed_log_change)=0.9946; descendant_attenuated_fraction==1 in 100% of rows; localization_index>0 in all 8,181 rows (min 3.2e-7); input_conductance_normalized unit dose (kappa r_k=1): per-regime mean descendant change -0.547 to -0.668 across all nine (R_m, background) regimes vs prediction -0.693, row range -0.692 to -0.396 (residual = driving force). transport_selectivity S_k<1 in 0.33% of rows (min 0.906) while localization stays positive, i.e. S_k is not the operative criterion for the log-change endpoint.

Limits: the identity requires a symmetric tree and a soma-only loss; it does not cover the active-model results (Fig 8E,G, nonsymmetric Jacobian). The driving-force factor is not negligible (up to 0.25 log units) and is what produces the small/opposite-signed shunt-minus-current contrasts at R_m=15,000. The paper's sentences are hedged ("can", "need not") and literally true for a single fixed column, so "framing is wrong" is slightly overstated; but for every passive result reported the change IS a per-subtree gain on the ancestry partition.
- **Suggestion:** Replace the contrast with the exact statement; give closed-form Λ_k ≈ log(1+κr_k) − |log(1−κr_kρ_k/(1+κr_k))| + driving-force correction; use ρ_k as the x-axis of Fig 8F/S11B. This makes Fig 8 direct support for the route-dictionary thesis.

#### M013 — The active-conductance ensemble does not test active conductances: channel densities ~0.03-0.10× leak, result is the passive curve to 0.7%
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:362, caption 369 E,G, 1040-1051; supplementary.tex:3497-3545; Table S7
- **Claim:** The "active steady-state ensemble" (main.tex:362, Fig. 8E,G, Methods 1040-1051, SI S9 3499-3545, Table S7) uses prespecified median maximal conductances of 0.06/0.10/0.025/0.05x the R_m=1000 leak (NMDA 0.15x excitatory, ~0.07x leak) in a membrane whose passive conductance is 30x the standard R_m=15,000 leak; at the solved states (V~0.07-0.23) the open active conductance is ~2% of leak and the voltage-gated contribution to the baseline Jacobian diagonal is only ~0.1-1% of the passive diagonal. Consequently the active shunt/injection localizations reproduce the passive values at the same calibration to +0.7%/+5-6% (Table S7 vs phase-1 rows 78-83), and Fig. 8E's dashed passive curve is visually indistinguishable from the active one. Stating "we also tested models with Na, K, Ca, HCN and NMDA conductances; the qualitative effect persisted" and "survives exact local linearization at 512 accepted active-conductance steady states" (SI 5216) without reporting these densities or the ~1% active share of the operator overstates what was tested. Note the densities are not reported anywhere in the snapshot although main.tex:1051 says SI S9 gives them. A quick rerun shows the ordering persists at 10x densities (shunt localization -8%, injection +33%) while 30x fails the acceptance gates, so the fix is disclosure and reframing (report densities, the open-active/leak and active/passive Jacobian-diagonal fractions, and describe the result as "weak steady-state channel perturbations of a few per cent of the passive operator do not change the passive result"), or a rerun at higher densities within what the solver admits.
- **Evidence:** active_linearization_ensemble.json; condition_summary.csv shunt localization active vs passive 0.1587/0.5287/1.3387 vs 0.1577/0.5248/1.3292 (+0.7%); injection +5-6%; per-cell SD across 64 draws 0.0007-0.0020.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.88):** TEXT CHECKED. main.tex:362 ("We also tested models with sodium (Na), potassium (K), calcium (Ca), HCN and NMDA receptor conductances. The qualitative effect persisted at R_m=1,000 ... (Fig. 8E,G)"); main.tex:369 caption E ("dashed curves show the corresponding passive model") and G; main.tex:1040-1051 ("Channel parameters were fixed in the protocol; lognormal global and segment multipliers varied conductance densities ... SI S9 gives channel equations, density distributions and acceptance criteria"); supplementary.tex:3499-3545 (S9 active ensemble) and 3511 ("drawn independently from prespecified log-normal distributions"); Table S7 at supplementary.tex:4486-4498 (shunt localization 0.1587/0.5287/1.3387; injection 0.0398/0.1470/0.4657); claim-boundaries row supplementary.tex:5216 ("Descendant-enriched attenuation survives exact local linearization at 512 accepted active-conductance steady states"). No numeric channel density appears anywhere in main.tex, supplementary.tex or the six *_methods.tex fragments (grep for 0.06/0.025/median scale/log_sd returned nothing relevant), so the promise at main.tex:1051 that "SI S9 gives ... density distributions" is not kept.
CONFIG CHECKED. journal/configs/focal_selectivity/active_linearization_ensemble.json: median_scale Na 0.06, K 0.10, Ca 0.025, HCN 0.05 (reference "leak"), NMDA 0.15 (reference "excitatory"); channel_global_log_sd 0.35, channel_spatial_log_sd 0.25; R_m=1000, background_leak_multiplier 1. In scripts/run_focal_selectivity_active_ensemble.py:117-139 (sample_conductances) the "leak" reference is electrical.g_leak from physical_conductance_system at R_m=1000, i.e. 15x the R_m=15,000 leak; with the unit background the passive membrane is 30x standard leak (recomputed: median leak/leak@15k = 15.0, (leak+background)/leak@15k = 30.0). Median g_e/leak = 0.48, so NMDA median ~0.07x leak.
DATA CHECKED. source_data/focal_selectivity_phase1/condition_summary.csv rows 78-83 (R_m=1000, background 1, input_conductance_normalized): passive shunt 0.157657/0.524849/1.329235, injection 0.037645/0.139269/0.443215. Active condition_summary.csv: shunt 0.158728/0.528699/1.338660, injection 0.039814/0.147026/0.465690. Ratios: shunt +0.7%/+0.7%/+0.7%; injection +5.8%/+5.5%/+5.1% — reviewer's numbers are exact. Per-cell SD of shunt localization across 64 draws (draw_condition_metrics.csv): 0.00024-0.0030 (reviewer said 0.0007-0.0020; same order). build_main_figure_08.py:402-416 comments confirm the dashed overlay is the phase-1 passive table at the same calibration; Fig. 8E shows the two curves visually coincident.
RECOMPUTATION (scratch script notes/verify-M013/recompute.py, using the repo's own physical_conductance_system, sample_conductances, solve_active_state on cells 864691135060651419 and 864691135196576298). At the frozen densities the solved voltages lie at V~0.07-0.23 (normalized), far below the Na/K/Ca half-activations (0.43/0.50/0.57), so the activation-weighted (open) active conductance is only ~2.1-2.6% of leak and the active term in the Jacobian diagonal (gbar[h+h'(V-E)], i.e. including gate derivatives) is -0.1% to -1% of the passive diagonal. Rerunning the full focal protocol (run_cell) with all median_scales multiplied by 0 vs 1 gives identical localization to <1% (cell 1 unit dose: 0.5499 vs 0.5463); x3: 0.5384; x10: 0.5054 shunt / 0.2737 injection (qualitative ordering preserved, shunt -8%, injection +33%); x30: only 1/4 and 0/4 draws pass the solver/acceptance criteria. So the ensemble is passive-dominated by construction at the frozen densities, the effect does persist at 10x densities, and the protocol cannot reach open-conductance-of-order-leak without failing its own acceptance gates.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.93):** TEXT CHECKED. main.tex:362 "We also tested models with sodium (Na), potassium (K), calcium (Ca), ... (HCN) and ... (NMDA) receptor conductances. The qualitative effect persisted at R_m=1,000 ... (Fig. 8E,G)"; caption 369 "E, Active steady-state sensitivity in the same arbors; dashed curves show the corresponding passive model"; Methods 1040-1051 give R_a=150, R_m=1,000, background = 1x leak and say "Channel parameters were fixed in the protocol; lognormal global and segment multipliers varied conductance densities ... SI S9 gives channel equations, density distributions and acceptance criteria." supplementary.tex:3499-3545 (S9 active subsection) and Table S7 (tab:active_focal, lines 4486-4497): shunt localization 0.1587/0.5287/1.3387 at doses 0.25/1/4; no density values, no gate equations or v_half/slope values anywhere in the SI -- line 3510 defers to "the predefined parameter file". Claim-boundary table row 5216 labels it "Descendant-enriched attenuation survives exact local linearization at 512 accepted active-conductance steady states."

CONFIG CHECKED. journal/configs/focal_selectivity/active_linearization_ensemble.json: median_scale Na 0.06, K 0.10, Ca 0.025, HCN 0.05 (all x leak), NMDA 0.15 x excitatory; channel_global_log_sd 0.35, channel_spatial_log_sd 0.25; v_half Na 0.43, K 0.50, Ca 0.57, HCN -0.21, NMDA 0.20 (normalized units, E_E=1). Rm=1,000 vs standard 15,000 plus 1x background = 30x standard leak, as the finding says.

SOURCE DATA RECOMPUTED. source_data/focal_selectivity_active_ensemble/condition_summary.csv vs source_data/focal_selectivity_phase1/condition_summary.csv rows (Rm=1000, background 1, input_conductance_normalized -- exactly the rows build_main_figure_08.py load_passive_reference() draws the dashed curve from): shunt localization active/passive = 0.15873/0.15766, 0.52870/0.52485, 1.33866/1.32924 (ratios 1.0068, 1.0073, 1.0071, i.e. +0.7%); current injection 0.03981/0.03765, 0.14703/0.13927, 0.46570/0.44322 (+5.1 to +5.8%). draw_condition_metrics.csv: SD of localization across the 64 draws within a cell is 0.00024-0.0030 for shunt and 0.00013-0.0081 for injection, i.e. the channel ensemble barely moves the statistic.

INDEPENDENT RECOMPUTATION (notes/verify-M013/jac_ratio.py, importing the authors' own run_focal_selectivity_active_ensemble.py functions, 8 draws x 8 cells with the frozen seed stream). Solved active equilibria have median V = 0.14 (range 0.07-0.29 normalized), well below the Na/K/Ca half-activations (0.43/0.50/0.57), so those gates are effectively closed: median per-channel Jacobian-diagonal contributions relative to membrane conductance (leak+background) are Na -6e-5, K +2e-5, Ca -1e-4, HCN +0.45%, NMDA -2.4%. Total active diagonal / full passive diagonal (incl. axial): median -0.4%, max |.| 2.5%. Active-minus-passive voltage shift median 0.008 (max 0.025) normalized units. The linearized operator used for every reported number is therefore the passive operator to within about 1-2%, which fully accounts for the +0.7% localization difference. Fig 8E (figure_08.pdf) visibly shows the active symbols sitting exactly on the dashed passive lines.

Revision logs (EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md:63, AUGUST_2026_CONFIRMATORY_RESULTS.md:246, JOURNAL_READINESS.md:202) treat the ensemble as positive robustness evidence without noting the operating regime.
- **Suggestion:** Either report active/passive Jacobian diagonal ratios and rerun at densities of order the leak and at R_m=15,000, or describe honestly as 'weak active perturbations (a few per cent of leak) do not change the result' and drop 'qualitative effect persisted'; state densities in Methods.

#### M014 — The full-tree exact field is essentially rank-one, so the dictionary comparison cannot test alignment of measured responses with routes
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:373-396 (title, Fig 9B, update-match rows of 9C); supplementary.tex:3721-3796; Methods 1075-1096
- **Claim:** In the complete-tree measured-response model, the exact compartment-error field at the evaluated exact-learning checkpoints is 98.4% (run min 95.3%) explained by scalar output error times the fixed pre-training soma-to-site transfer profile v0 (update match 0.976), so Fig 9B and the update-match rows of Fig 9C mainly measure how much of that anatomical vector each four-route span retains (anatomy-only retained energy 0.30/0.41/0.46 target-nested for ancestry/random/shuffle; pooled Spearman 0.94 with update capture, 0.98 with field capture) rather than any response-driven spatial credit structure. The paper reports the 0.976 figure but does not state this implication, and main.tex 394 attributes a nearly rank-one field only to the major-branch analysis. The section title remains partly supported by the separate ancestry-response partial-correlation test (Fig 9C top rows), but the update-match and NMSE comparisons should be described as anatomical-span retention of a fixed profile, with retained-energy fractions reported beside Table S9, and the section title/claim narrowed to the structure-function test.
- **Evidence:** fulltree_within_span_oracle/condition_summary.csv: unprojected baseline transfer update match 0.9756, field match 0.984 (min 0.953). Retained v0 energy: ancestry 0.288 (min 0.012), random 0.357, shuffle 0.416; Spearman with update capture 0.936 pooled.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Numbers verified in source_data/fulltree_within_span_oracle/condition_summary.csv: "unprojected baseline transfer" update match 0.97556 (CI 0.9641-0.9858), field match 0.98390; run_metrics.csv (n=130) field-match min 0.95261, mean 0.98602; cell_metrics.csv target-level field-match min 0.9755. Script scripts/analyze_fulltree_within_span_oracle.py lines 70 and 96-99: the baseline field is cache["error"][:,None]*state.readout*tree.root_to_sites, i.e. scalar trial error x readout gain x the PRE-TRAINING transfer vector root_to_sites (an attribute of build_passive_tree, fixed before training); base.capture(exact, field) = 0.984 means 98.4% of the trialwise exact error field is this rank-one anatomical product, leaving ~1.6% trial-dependent spatial structure. The dictionaries (line 74-77) are anatomy-only, so ||P_D v0||^2/||v0||^2 is computable with no responses. I recomputed that retained-energy fraction from fulltree_boundary/output/dictionary_and_validation_metadata.jsonl (fixed_feedback_norms/baseline_transfer_norm)^2, 130 rows: pooled run means 0.2879 (ancestry), 0.3566 (random), 0.4159 (shuffle) - the reviewer's numbers; target-nested means are 0.304/0.410/0.465; run minimum 0.0115/0.0110/0.0110. Pooled Spearman across 390 run x dictionary pairs between this anatomical retained energy and runs.csv common_checkpoint_update_capture = 0.936 (reviewer's figure reproduced exactly) and with common_checkpoint_error_field_capture = 0.982. So the update-match contrasts in Fig 9C (bottom rows; nested means 0.2322/0.3584/0.4392 in Table S9, supplementary.tex 4534-4557) and the frozen-profile 9B learning comparison are, to first order, comparisons of how much of the fixed anatomical transfer vector each span retains. Text check: main.tex 396 and 1096 and supplementary.tex 3784-3786 report the 0.976 baseline match but interpret it only as "freezing transport alone explained little of the loss"; nowhere is the implication stated that the exact field is >95% a trial-invariant profile, and main.tex 394 attributes a "nearly rank-one response-derived field" only to the coarser major-branch analysis (S22), which implies the full-tree field is not so limited - contradicted by 0.984. Partial refutation: the section (main.tex 373-394, Fig 9C top rows; supplementary.tex 3546-3604, Table tab:functional_all_scans) also contains a structure-function test (partial Spearman ancestry-response correlation -0.0485, CI -0.224 to 0.118, 69 partners / 13 scans) that uses measured responses directly and is not a span-geometry fact; and the paper already hedges the frozen-profile rule (main.tex 386: "constrains how strongly a negative result can speak"; 1075-1082). The title is therefore weakly supported by the partial-correlation rows, not solely by span geometry. Reviewer's 0.288/0.357/0.416 are pooled (scan-level) rather than target-nested means, but the ordering and conclusion are unaffected.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked: main.tex lines 373-408 (section title, eq. at 379-384, lines 391-392 "unprojected baseline profile reached 0.976, so freezing transport alone explained little of the loss", Fig 9 caption), Methods 1075-1096, supplementary.tex 3721-3796 and Table S9 footnote at 4553-4556; figure_09.pdf (panel F labels "endogenous task use B,C null / no detected alignment"); scripts/run_reconstructed_tree_task_learning.py lines 396-476 (exact_error = error[:,None]*readout*soma_to_site; soma_to_site is the trial-specific adjoint that differs from the fixed root_to_sites only through input shunting h = x*g); scripts/analyze_fulltree_within_span_oracle.py lines 60-90 (unprojected field = error*readout*root_to_sites). Data: source_data/fulltree_within_span_oracle/condition_summary.csv row "unprojected baseline transfer": mean_update_match 0.97556, mean_field_match 0.98390; cell_metrics.csv per-target field_match 0.9755-0.9927 (update_match min 0.9511 for target 864691135409937097); run_metrics.csv unprojected field_match min 0.9526, median 0.9884. So the exact trial-specific field is 98.4% (energy) a scalar multiple of one fixed anatomical vector v0 — rank-one claim CONFIRMED. Retained-energy recomputation: from source_data/fulltree_boundary/output/dictionary_and_validation_metadata.jsonl, (fixed_feedback_norm/baseline_transfer_norm)^2 gives ||P_D v0||^2/||v0||^2 with no response data; run-level pooled means topology 0.2879, random 0.3566, shuffle 0.4159 (run min 0.0115) — matches the reviewer's 0.288/0.357/0.416/0.012 exactly; per-target means 0.304/0.410/0.465. Merged with runs.csv: Spearman(retained, common_checkpoint_update_capture) = 0.936 pooled over 390 runs (p=3e-178), 0.948 over 21 target x method points; per-target ordering of the three dictionaries by retained energy equals ordering by update capture in 6/7 targets; frozen field capture = retained energy - 0.018 (sd 0.033). Update-match rows of Fig 9C therefore ARE a readout of span geometry vs v0 — CONFIRMED. However, for panel B (held-out NMSE) the reviewer's claim is NOT supported: Spearman(retained energy, NMSE excess over exact) = -0.032 at run level (p=0.52) and -0.15 over 21 target x method points (p=0.51); full dictionary ordering by retained energy matches ordering by NMSE in only 26/130 runs, and the best-retained dictionary is the best-NMSE dictionary in only 45/130 runs. So panel B is not a retained-energy readout; the learning outcome is decoupled from the geometry by the nonnegative-conductance dynamics. The deeper point still holds for B: all three restricted rules deliver a response-independent fixed profile P_D v0 and the exact rule's trial-varying spatial content is ~2%, so B compares fixed anatomical profiles rather than testing alignment of a response-derived spatial field with routes. On the title: the section also contains an independent structure-function partial-correlation analysis (Fig 9C top rows, 69 partners / 13 scans; supplementary 3567-3601) that genuinely uses measured responses, so the title is not solely a span-geometry fact; but main.tex 391 acknowledges the near-rank-one field only for the major-branch surrogate (Fig S22) and nowhere states it for the complete-tree model, and line 392 interprets the 0.976 as "freezing transport explained little of the loss" instead of "the exact field is itself almost the fixed transfer vector".
- **Suggestion:** State that >95% of the exact field is the fixed transfer profile; retitle around the structure-function test; report retained-energy fractions beside Table S9; drop 9B's dictionary comparison from the main text or use a model with trial-dependent spatial structure.

#### M015 — The 'subtree' dictionary in the full-tree test is mostly single-site distal addresses with incomplete coverage; Fig 9A misrepresents it
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** main.tex:379-386, Fig 9A; supplementary.tex:3686-3690, 3732-3736; run_reconstructed_tree_task_learning.py build_passive_tree
- **Claim:** In the complete-tree measured-response test (main.tex 373-386, Fig 9A; supplementary.tex 3686-3690, 3732-3753, Table S9), the 'subtree/topology' dictionary is selected by beta*(inclusive descendant-site fraction), and because beta = g_i/g_total is near zero for thick proximal segments the rule picks thin distal segments: across 130 runs the four routes touch 1.31 sites each on average (max 2), 430/520 routes are the site's own contact segment, 40/52 distinct routes are leaves, 6/13 scans are entirely one-hot, and the realized dictionary covers only 48% of input sites on average (23-83% by scan), leaving ~52% of sites with zero credit under the frozen v_D = P_D v0. The within-span oracle equals the trialwise projection (0.2436) in every scan, confirming the span itself, not coefficient rigidity, is the limitation. Fig 9A draws four disjoint two-site bands with full coverage and says 'complete-arbor routes may overlap', which misrepresents this. The 'no preferential alignment with subtree routes' null therefore tests four single-site distal inhibitory addresses, not subtree-level ancestry routing; report support size/coverage (Table S9/Methods), redraw Fig 9A, and either relabel the condition or rerun with routes constrained to >=2 descendant sites (feasible: every scan has >=3 such candidates).
- **Evidence:** ancestry_matrix marks cursor==site first. Metadata: ancestry nonzeros mean 5.23 for 4 columns (1.31 sites/route, max 2); 6/13 scans all one-hot; coverage upper bound 0.58; retained v0 as low as 0.107. Within-span oracle equals trialwise projection for ancestry in every target (0.2436), implying disjoint supports.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.92):** CODE. code/reconstructed_tree/analyze_microns_morphology_credit.py:298-309 ancestry_matrix starts the cursor at the site's own segment and marks it before ascending (inclusive ancestry). scripts/run_reconstructed_tree_task_learning.py:334-345: beta = g_i/g_total per inhibitory segment; leverage = beta * ancestry.mean(axis=0); top-4 by leverage are the 'topology-matched routes'. Supplement 3689-3690 describes this rule correctly ('ranked by this fraction times their descendant-site fraction') but never reports the realized support.

METADATA (source_data/fulltree_boundary/output/dictionary_and_validation_metadata.jsonl, 130 records, 13 scans, used_channels=4 in all). Recomputed: topology-route nonzeros mean 5.231 over 4 columns = 1.31 sites/route, max 2.0 (reviewer's numbers reproduce exactly); site-shuffle 5.231 (value-preserving), random routes 7.05. 6/13 scans have nnz==4 (all four routes one-hot). 430/520 selected route segments are literally a site's own contact segment. Coverage upper bound nnz/n_sites mean 0.578 (matches reviewer). ||v_D||/||v0|| for topology: min 0.1074, mean 0.479 (matches reviewer's 0.107).

MECHANISM CHECK. I re-ran electrical_geometry/parent_map/ancestry_matrix on segment_metrics.csv for all 13 scans and reproduced the archived selected_route_segments exactly (assert passed). In every scan the highest-coverage candidates (depth 0-3, covering 100%/33-60% of sites) have beta 0.000-0.03, while the selected routes have beta 0.02-0.11 with descendant-site fraction 0.077-0.33; 40/52 selected routes are leaf segments. So beta dominates the product and the rule does favor thin distal single-site segments. Realized site coverage of the selected dictionary (fraction of site rows with any nonzero) = 0.48 mean across scans (range 0.235-0.83), i.e. ~52% of sites receive exactly zero credit under v_D = P_D v0 — slightly worse than the reviewer's ~40%. Every scan has >=3 multi-site candidate columns, so a >=2-descendant-site constraint is feasible.

ORACLE. source_data/fulltree_within_span_oracle/cell_metrics.csv and scan_metrics.csv: for topology-matched routes, trialwise_update_oracle == trialwise_field_projection to 12 digits in all 7 targets and all 13 scans (diff 0.0); condition_summary.csv gives 0.243581131344 for both, matching supplement 4550-4553 and main 386. Frozen-baseline 0.2322 matches Table S9.

FIGURE. figures/main/figure_09.pdf panel A and scripts/build_main_figure_09.py:491-527: ROUTE_SETS for 'subtree' = ((0,1),(2,3),(4,5),(6,7)) — four DISJOINT two-site bands covering all 8 sites (the builder comment calls them 'nested subtree' bands but they are drawn as siblings, so the reviewer's word 'nested' is inexact); annotation 'illustrative sites; complete-arbor routes may overlap' (line 543) implies multi-site overlapping routes, the opposite of the realized 1.31-site, ~48%-coverage dictionary. Main text 379-386 and section title (373) call these 'subtree routes'; Methods 1075-1077 says 'four ancestry ... routes'; Table S9 caption (4522-4525) says 'four-route span'. No line in main.tex or supplementary.tex reports route support size or site coverage.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Text checked: main.tex 375-386 (four-route dictionary D, P_D v0, "Subtree routes showed no reliable advantage") and caption 407 ("restricted rules ... one fixed profile projected into a four-route dictionary"); supplementary.tex 3686-3690 (routes "ranked by this fraction times their descendant-site fraction"), 3732-3736 ("conductance-weighted topology dictionary"), 3750-3792 (oracle 0.2322→0.2436 ancestry). Nowhere in main.tex or supplementary.tex is route support size or site coverage for this test reported (grep for one-hot/single site/support size/coverage in the R10/full-tree sections: none). Code checked: scripts/run_reconstructed_tree_task_learning.py build_passive_tree lines 334-346 (kernel = ancestry*beta, leverage = beta*ancestry.mean(0), argsort top-4) and code/reconstructed_tree/analyze_microns_morphology_credit.py ancestry_matrix lines 298-309 (cursor starts at the site itself, so a site's own inhibited segment is a one-site route). Data checked: source_data/fulltree_boundary/output/dictionary_and_validation_metadata.jsonl (13 scans): topology nonzeros 4,4,7,5,4,4,4,5,5,7,8,7,4 (mean 5.23 for K=4 -> 1.31 sites/route; 6/13 scans have exactly 4 nonzeros = all one-hot); retained ||P_D v0||/||v0|| for topology 0.583,0.288,0.644,0.957,0.107,0.500,0.117,0.319,0.353,0.570,0.794,0.367,0.631 (min 0.107 confirmed). I then rebuilt the actual dictionaries from drafts/dendritic-credit-routing/results/microns_morphology_credit/segment_metrics.csv with the run's config (e/i scale 0.35) via the same electrical_geometry/ancestry_matrix/leverage code: selected route segments reproduced exactly for all 13 scans. Results: 39/52 selected routes touch exactly one site (max support 3, not 2 as the finding said); 43/52 selected route segments are the site's own contact segment; 40/52 are leaf segments; mean selected-route path depth 124 um vs candidate-pool median 46 um; selected beta 0.03-0.11 vs candidate median 0.005-0.017 (beta dominates the ranking). True site coverage (union of supports / n_sites) = 0.44,0.50,0.40,0.45,0.40,0.50,0.31,0.25,0.24,0.83,0.67,0.80,0.44, mean 0.48 -> on average 52% of sites receive exactly zero credit under v_D (worse than the finding's ~40%; the finding's 0.58 was a nonzero-count upper bound). In every scan a candidate route covering all sites existed (best_single_route_cov = n_sites) but was never selected. Span is an exact coordinate subspace in 7/13 scans. source_data/fulltree_within_span_oracle/run_metrics.csv: trialwise_field_projection update_match == trialwise_update_oracle for topology in all 130 runs (max |diff| = 0.0), whereas random/shuffle differ by up to 0.238/0.175 -- consistent with (near-)disjoint single-site supports. Fig 9A (figures/main/figure_09.pdf; scripts/build_main_figure_09.py lines 494-521, 543): a hand-drawn definition with 8 terminals, subtree routes ((0,1),(2,3),(4,5),(6,7)) = four contiguous two-site chips covering all sites; only caveat is "illustrative sites; complete-arbor routes may overlap". Also note random-route controls sometimes contain larger supports (nonzeros 13, 11, 12 in three scans), i.e. some controls are more subtree-like than the "subtree" dictionary.
- **Suggestion:** Report route support size and site coverage in Table S9/Methods; constrain routes to ≥2 descendant sites or add a scalar/residual column and rerun, or relabel as 'four distal inhibitory single-site addresses'; redraw Fig 9A.

#### M018 — Synthetic noise-resilience task generator is never defined in main text or SI
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** reproducibility
- **Location:** main.tex:155, 595-606, 630-631; supplementary.tex:1959-2080, 3989
- **Claim:** The "synthetic noise-resilience task" behind the 480-fit depth-by-feedback cohort, Table S19/S20 rows, Fig S8, the spatial-map and fixed-budget cohorts, the 320-fit exact/BP rerun and the 120-checkpoint diagnostic is never defined in the main text, the SI or the methods fragments; only its name appears. Verification of the nested repository (commit 4f3612a5) and the cohort configs shows it is not a de-novo synthetic task at all: it is MNIST (784 inputs, 10 classes, 48k/12k/10k split, unnormalised) corrupted by fixed low-rank Gaussian channel noise (50-d latent, column-normalised random 784x50 projection with seed 0, per-split latent seeds 1/2/3, sigma 1.5) and then clamped to [0,1]. The manuscript's labels "synthetic", "randomly projected noise task" and "signed synthetic inputs" therefore misdescribe the task (the generator clamps inputs to be nonnegative, and the actual exclusion rule in the audit script keys on a null transfer-output activation, not on input sign). The generator code is absent from the reviewer software archive (no data_generation path among 3,203 checksummed files; the release loader imports an absent module and its tests skip it), so the 122 release configs naming noise_resilience cannot be executed from the archive alone. Fix: add a generator paragraph to Methods or SI S5 stating base dataset, projection construction, latent dimension, sigma, seeds, clamp and split, rename the task (e.g. "low-rank-noise MNIST"), reconcile the "signed synthetic inputs" rationale at main.tex:596-597 with the clamp and the transfer-activation rule, and include the nested data_generation module (or port load_noise_resilience_mnist into the core package) in the code archive.
- **Evidence:** grep 'noise-resilience|noise task|randomly projected' in SI returns mentions only; code absent from src/ (SI 2010: separate nested repository at commit 4f3612a5); scripts carry only the task key.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Snapshot text checked: main.tex:155 ("synthetic noise task"), 594-606 ("Signed synthetic inputs ... valid for raw-additive currents but excluded where they drove positive shunting conductances"), 630-631; supplementary.tex:1959-2080 (depth-by-feedback 480/640 runs, matched routing, inhibitory dose, 320-fit rerun citing nested synthetic-data repo commit 4f3612a5, spatial maps, fixed-budget cohort, 120-checkpoint diagnostic), captions at 2861, 2868, 3982, 3989, 3996, Tables at 4762-4770, 4793-4794, 4818-4819, 4836 (Table S20). grep over main.tex, supplementary.tex and the six *_methods.tex fragments for noise-resilien|noise task|randomly projected|corrupt|latent|channel noise|projected gaussian: every hit is a mention or label; no sentence anywhere states what the task is (base data, dimension, classes, noise model, SD, projection rank, seeds, clamp, split). Methods 554-590 (image-task cohorts) describes MNIST/Fashion/CIFAR only; the pointer at 589-590 to "SI S5 and Tables S10-S12" does not resolve to a task description. Code: live src/dendritic_modeling has no noise_resilience loader (grep returns only analysis/fmi/rank_estimator_comparison.py, unrelated); datasets/unified_options.py:60-63 delegates unknown names to an optional module experiments.data_generation.synthetic_datasets. That module exists only in an untracked nested git repo /n/holylabs/.../dendritic-modeling/experiments (.git HEAD = 4f3612a52756c3691e5bd269c6465c02f240c1e8, 2 commits, dated 2025-09-28), file experiments/experiments/data_generation/synthetic_datasets.py:469-537 load_noise_resilience_mnist and :1429-1445 dispatch. The actual generator: MNIST loaded via load_mnist_as_datasets (train_valid_split 0.8, normalize false, flatten true), a fixed 784x50 torch.randn projection with column normalisation seeded by projection_seed=0 (lines 434-444), independent N(0,1) 50-d latents per split with seeds 1/2/3, noise = 1.5 * latent @ P^T added to pixels, then clamp(0,1) (lines 447-466). The journal cohort configs confirm these exact parameters: submission/software_release/article_analysis/configs/prospective_learning_benefits/canary_noise_shunting_local3f.yaml lines 40-53 (dataset_name noise_resilience; sigma_task 1.5; noise_latent_dim 50; projection_seed 0; train/valid/test_noise_seed 1/2/3; clamp_inputs true), and this file differs from the MNIST twin only in those lines (diff). 122 release YAMLs reference noise_resilience (64 prospective_fixed_budget_depth, 16 prospective_learning_benefits, 40 prospective_shunting_topology, 2 regular_tree). The reviewer software archive at journal/submission/software_release does NOT contain the generator: SHA256SUMS.tsv (3,203 entries) has 0 paths containing data_generation; the release package's datasets/standard_datasets.py:984-1012 still imports the absent experiments module at runtime; release tests/test_datasets.py:1519,1527 skip the relevant tests ("requires the experiments/data_generation module which is not available in CI"); release article_analysis/scripts/collect_clean_exact_bp_rerun.py:38 hard-codes EXPECTED_EXPERIMENTS_COMMIT = 4f3612a5... and line 149 verifies the nested repo. README.md and METADATA.json contain no mention of the nested repo or the noise task. Source data CSVs (source_data/prospective_learning, prospective_followup, prospective_input_validity, spatial_topology_audit) carry only the key "noise_resilience"; none contains sigma_task/noise_latent_dim. Secondary observation: main.tex:596-597 attributes the shunting exclusion to "signed synthetic inputs", whereas the generator at the cited commit clamps inputs to [0,1], and the audit script journal/scripts/build_prospective_input_validity_audit.py:83-89 and source_data/prospective_input_validity/summary.json key the exclusion on a null transfer_output_activation for noise_resilience+dendritic_shunting, wording it "signed synthetic transfer output". A reader cannot reconcile this without the generator description.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** Snapshot text checked: main.tex:155 ("synthetic noise task"), 534 ("Separate 320-fit exact/BP rerun"), 554-606 (Methods image-task cohorts: MNIST/Fashion/CIFAR described; 596-601 "Signed synthetic inputs ... valid for raw-additive currents"; pointer to "SI S5 and Tables S10-S12"), 630-631; supplementary.tex:1718-1760 (S5 architecture: MNIST only), 1959-2080 (depth-by-feedback, matched routing, dose, 320-run rerun citing "nested synthetic-data repository ... commit 4f3612a5" at 2013-2014, spatial, fixed-budget, 120-checkpoint diagnostic), captions 2861/2868/3982/3989/3996, Tables at 4562-4630 (S10-S12: MNIST data spec only; S12 row 4618 cites nested commit 4f3612a5), 4762-4770 (S19), 4785-4800 (S17: noise rows 33.378/33.490 and 36.690/36.788), 4836-4850 (S20). The six *_methods.tex fragments mention noise only for the morphology tasks. grep for noise-resilien|noise task|randomly projected|sigma|latent|clamp|ten classes|three classes in main.tex+supplementary.tex: every hit is a label; no sentence defines the task. Code: src/dendritic_modeling has no noise_resilience loader; datasets/unified_options.py:60-63 and standard_datasets.py:1000-1020 delegate to an optional module experiments.data_generation.synthetic_datasets that lives only in an untracked nested git repo /n/holylabs/.../dendritic-modeling/experiments (2 commits; HEAD 4f3612a5..., 2025-09-28). The working-tree file there (git status " M", mtime 2026-04-10, 1620 lines) defines load_noise_resilience_mnist (lines 469-537): MNIST 48k/12k/10k, fixed 784x50 column-normalised randn projection (projection_seed 0), independent 50-d N(0,1) latents per split (seeds 1/2/3), noise = 1.5*latent@P^T, clamp(0,1); dispatch at 1429-1445 reads sigma_task/noise_latent_dim/... from dataset_params. Journal configs (scripts/generate_prospective_learning_sweeps.py:43-60; configs/prospective_fixed_budget_depth/*.yaml lines 32-45) pass exactly those parameters. BUT the committed file at the cited commit (git show 4f3612a5:experiments/data_generation/synthetic_datasets.py, 1269 lines) contains no load_noise_resilience_mnist and no dataset_params handling; its dispatcher (lines 1029-1039, 1114-1118) maps noise_resilience -> NoisyLineDataset(n_samples=8000+1000+1000, image_size=p.get("image_size",784), noise_level=0.2) with random_split: a 3-class (blank/row/column) task on 784x784 images. The clean checkout used for the 320-run rerun (/n/holylabs/.../dendritic-modeling-clean-exactbp-20260810: root 74792ca, experiments 4f3612a5, tracked-clean, file 45,704 bytes, 0 occurrences of load_noise_resilience_mnist; collector scripts/collect_clean_exact_bp_rerun.py:38,149-172 enforces this) therefore ran the NoisyLine task. Source data confirm: source_data/clean_exact_bp/run_outcomes.csv noise checkpoints are 1,258,832,202 bytes at depth 1 vs 1,622,346 for MNIST; (bytes-7458)/4 - 2314 non-input params, divided by 128 neurons x 2 compartments x 2 signs = 614,656.0 = 784^2 exactly; test categorical log-likelihood averages -1.120/-1.120/-1.116/-1.116 (additive d1-d4) and -1.099/-1.099/-1.094/-1.086 (shunting), i.e. -ln 3 = -1.0986 (MNIST rows average -0.101); accuracies 32.8-39.0% with additive best_epoch 1.5-3.0 (chance for 3 classes). By contrast source_data/prospective_learning/seed_outcomes.csv (depth-by-feedback cohort, Table S19) has noise-task parameter counts identical to MNIST (403,722 at d1; 15,360 active synapses => 784-dim input) and BP accuracies 93.4-95.0%, consistent with the uncommitted MNIST+projected-noise generator. Release: submission/software_release/SHA256SUMS.tsv has 0 paths containing data_generation or noise_resilience. Secondary: main.tex:596 says "signed synthetic inputs" but the intended generator clamps inputs to [0,1]; the audit keys the exclusion on the transfer-output activation.
- **Suggestion:** Add a generator paragraph to Methods or SI S5 (dimension, classes, signal/noise SD, projection, split sizes, signed-vs-ReLU convention); include the nested synthetic-data repository in the code archive (Code availability 1210-1218).

#### M019 — Supplementary Fig. S8F x-axis is misregistered by one depth
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** figure
- **Location:** figure_S08_panels_A-I.pdf F; supplementary.tex:3989; analyze_prospective_followup_results.py ~1195-1220
- **Claim:** Supplementary Fig. S8F (figure_S08_panels_A-I.pdf; caption at supplementary.tex:3986-3990) is misregistered by one depth: the local-minus-BP points are drawn at categorical x = 0..3 while the tick labels D1-D4 sit at 1..4, so the MW-scalar curve shows -9.2 pp left of D1, -14.2 at "D1", -13.9 at "D2", -15.8 at "D3" and nothing at D4, whereas the source CSV gives these values at depths 1, 2, 3, 4. Cause: rebuild_review_visual_corrections.py feeds plot_fixed_budget a read_csv contrasts table whose depth column is string-typed (because of the "4 - 2" routing-interaction row), and matplotlib treats the strings as categories; the neuron-specific and exact-path curves (all near 0) are shifted identically. Panels A-E and G-I are correctly registered and no text quotes per-depth panel-F values, so the qualitative conclusion (MW-scalar gap widens with depth; other fields near zero) is unaffected. Fix: cast depth to int (pd.to_numeric(part.depth)) before plotting in plot_fixed_budget, regenerate S8 via the rebuild script, confirm a point under D4, and rerun the figure lineage audit.

#### M069 — A single soma broadcast captures almost all of the rank-1 independent field; marginal value of subtree routes over the broadcast is never tested
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:327-329, Fig 7C; supplementary.tex:3227-3260; Table S26
- **Claim:** In the independent reciprocal-cable test (Fig 7C, Table tab:reciprocal_capacity), the K=1 "depth bin" is literally a constant vector (script depth_dictionary returns ones for count<=1) and the soma-broadcast direction alone captures 0.314 of weighted response energy, 96% of the rank-1 oracle (0.326), in all 8 cells (0.885-0.986 of oracle). Depth-bin dictionaries at every K span this constant while subtree, random and shuffled dictionaries never contain it, so the depth-vs-subtree comparison is confounded: with a budget-matched [constant, K-1 subtree routes] dictionary, subtrees exceed depth bins at K=2 (0.402 vs 0.330; 7/8 cells), K=4 (0.474 vs 0.374; 8/8), K=8 (0.585 vs 0.445; 8/8) and K=16 (0.750 vs 0.572; 8/8), reversing the main-text statements "Depth bins performed better at K<=4, were not reliably separated at K=8" (main.tex:329, 341) and the Table conclusion "did not reliably exceed depth bins" (supplementary.tex:3222). The headline "coarse" conclusion survives only in the sense that the broadcast is the dominant coarse mode; the specific depth-over-ancestry ordering is an artifact of dictionary construction. Fix: add the constant column to every family (or evaluate all families on the centered field, as the tree-Haar analysis at supplementary.tex:3161-3175 already does), report the broadcast share explicitly, and revise the K<=4/K=8 sentences and Table tab:reciprocal_capacity accordingly.
- **Evidence:** cell_method_capture.csv K=1: dense 0.326, depth 0.314, degree-depth 0.142, morphology 0.124, random 0.056, shuffle 0.036; tree-Haar analysis already centers.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.92):** Text checked: main.tex:327-329 ("Depth bins performed better at K<=4, were not reliably separated at K=8 ... Most capacity therefore arose from coarse geometry rather than fine branch identity") and Fig 7C caption main.tex:341 ("Depth bins lead at K<=4, are unseparated at K=8"); supplementary.tex:3200-3224 (reciprocal-operator paragraph: "did not reliably exceed depth bins") and Table \label{tab:reciprocal_capacity} at supplementary.tex:4973-5003 (depth bins 0.445 vs ancestry 0.458, 5/8 cells). Note the finding's supplement location (3227-3260) is actually the inhibitory-census section; the relevant text is 3200-3224. Nowhere is it stated that the K=1 depth bin is a constant vector.

Code checked: scripts/analyze_reciprocal_routing_controls.py lines ~198-203: depth_dictionary(depth, count) returns np.ones((n,1)) when count<=1, i.e. the K=1 "depth bin" IS the soma broadcast; and for K>=2 the one-hot bin indicators always span the constant. Subtree ("morphology paths"), random and shuffled dictionaries are columns of the ancestry*beta matrix and never contain a constant column (K=1 subtree has mean 3.75 nonzeros per cell_method_capture.csv wiring_nonzeros). capture() (lines ~181-195) is uncentered: denominator is total weighted energy including the mean mode.

Data recomputed from source_data/reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv (240 rows, 8 cells): K=1 means dense 0.3260, depth 0.3138, degree-depth 0.1416, subtree 0.1239, random 0.0560, shuffled 0.0364 — exactly the reviewer's numbers; depth/dense at K=1 per cell 0.885-0.986; depth > subtree at K=1 in 8/8 cells.

Independent recomputation (notes/verify-M069/augment.py, re-running exact_reciprocal_response on source_data/figure3/segment_metrics.csv with the script's defaults e=i=0.35, dose 1e-3): the constant direction alone holds 0.314 of weighted response energy (per cell 0.195-0.521), = 96% of the rank-1 oracle. Budget-matched augmented dictionary [constant, K-1 leverage-ranked subtree routes] vs the paper's depth bins: K=2 0.402 vs 0.330 (7/8 cells higher), K=4 0.474 vs 0.374 (8/8), K=8 0.585 vs 0.445 (8/8, min diff 0.099), K=16 0.750 vs 0.572 (8/8). On the centered field (constant removed from numerator and denominator), subtree+constant vs depth bins vs random+constant: K=4 0.275 / 0.091 / 0.169; K=8 0.457 / 0.197 / 0.312. So the reported ordering "depth bins lead at K<=4 and are unseparated at K=8" reverses once both families are given the same broadcast column; the degree-depth surrogate comparison (both without constant) is unaffected. The reviewer's remark that the tree-Haar analysis already projects out the weighted constant h0 is correct (supplementary.tex:3161-3175, Eq. s_irregular_wavelet_power), so the two structural analyses currently treat the broadcast inconsistently.
- **Suggestion:** Add augmented dictionaries [constant, K−1 routes] for each family on the centered field; state in the 7C caption that the K=1 depth bin is the soma broadcast at 96% of the oracle.

### Severity: medium

#### M001 — No one-sentence contribution; the abstract is an inventory, not an argument
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** structure
- **Location:** main.tex:51-52 (abstract); 69; 413
- **Claim:** The abstract (main.tex:52) states the open question (sentence 1) and then lists ten equal-weight result sentences with no "here we show" answer; its closing sentence is generic and makes no claim. The organizing one-sentence contribution requested in the authors' own review item 1 (COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md:26) is not present in the abstract despite the implementation log marking it done, although a thesis question does close the Introduction (line 67) and an organizing claim opens the Discussion (line 413). Of the ten Results sections, only R9 (focal shunting) falls outside the Introduction's three questions; R3 maps directly to question 1 and the questions already follow section order, so no reordering is needed. Fix: replace abstract sentence 2 or 13 with an explicit answer (e.g. morphology fixes a sparse spatial-credit dictionary; cross-subtree interactions, not task rank, determine which patterns a task needs; image tasks and measured cortical responses use only the coarsest pattern), demote some result sentences to a clause, and add shunting/conductance state to the third Introduction question.
- **Evidence:** 196-word abstract: 12 declarative result sentences, zero question/hypothesis/conclusion sentences. R3, R6, R9 belong to none of the three questions. Authors' own COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW item 1 requests this and it is unimplemented.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked main.tex line 52 (abstract, 196 words confirmed by wc -w; split into 13 sentences), lines 55-69 (Introduction; three questions at line 69), lines 73-410 (ten \subsection* headings via grep), line 413 (Discussion opening), and analysis/COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md line 26 (item 1) vs REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION_20260905.md line 32 and REVIEW_COMPLETION_20260905.md line 50 (both mark item 1 "Implemented/Addressed").

What holds: (1) The abstract has no headline answer. Sentences 3-12 are ten (not twelve) coordinate result statements with no weighting; sentence 13 ("These results connect task interactions, credit resolution and dendritic organization under explicit computational and learning constraints") is a conclusion sentence but asserts nothing falsifiable. (2) The organizing sentence proposed in review item 1 ("Dendritic morphology constrains a dictionary of spatial credit patterns, whose usefulness depends on ...") does not appear anywhere in main.tex (grep for "here we show", "we show that", "usefulness depends", "central" returns nothing), so the implementation logs' "Implemented" label is not borne out by the abstract text. (3) R9 (Focal shunting, line 344) is not covered by any of the three Introduction questions at line 69; it is motivated only by the conductance paragraph at line 65.

What is refuted or overstated: (a) "zero question/hypothesis/conclusion sentences" is wrong: sentence 1 states the open question ("when branching improves learning remains unclear") and sentence 13 is a conclusion, albeit vacuous. (b) "12 equal-weight results" is 10. (c) Sentence 7 is not "unexplained jargon": "each branch combines two inputs through linear and product terms ... interactions across subtree boundaries ... minimum sufficient depth" is plain-language, if vague ("a condition"). (d) R3 explicitly maps to question 1, which literally asks "when can restricting noisy feedback help?"; R6 (serial forward stages, physical depth) maps loosely to question 2's "groupings or depths". Only R9 is orphaned. (e) The three questions already follow section order (Q1 -> R1-R5, Q2 -> R6-R7, Q3 -> R8-R10), so "reorder the Introduction's three questions" is unnecessary. (f) The Introduction does end with a one-sentence framing question (line 67: "when does a morphology-constrained dictionary preserve the distinctions a task requires, and when is a simpler delivery rule sufficient?") and the Discussion opens with an organizing claim (line 413: "Task rank or fidelity to an instantaneous gradient alone does not specify an optimal morphology"), so the paper is not without a thesis; the thesis is just absent from the abstract.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.8):** Checked main.tex:52 (abstract), 69 (three questions), 73-373 (Results subsection headings), 413 and 425 (Discussion first/last paragraphs), supplementary.tex theorem environments, and analysis/COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md items 1/16 plus REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION_20260905.md:32,47 and REVIEW_COMPLETION_20260905.md:50,65.

CONFIRMED: (a) Abstract is 196 words (wc) and splits into 13 sentences (reviewer said 12; sentence 8 has a semicolon). Sentences 3-12 are ten parallel declarative result statements with no weighting; sentence 13 ("These results connect task interactions, credit resolution and dendritic organization under explicit computational and learning constraints") names topics but states no relation, so "inventory, not an argument" is fair. (b) Sentence 7 ("a condition on interactions across subtree boundaries determines exact representation and minimum sufficient depth") is the densest sentence; "exact representation" and "minimum sufficient depth" are undefined there. (c) R9 (Focal shunting, main.tex:344) is not covered by any of the three questions at line 69; it is only motivated by the conductance paragraph at line 65. (d) The one-sentence thesis exists but only at the very end of the Discussion (line 425: "task interactions supply a constructive link to morphology, while estimation error and credit delivery determine how much of that capacity learning realizes") and in line 413 ("Task rank or fidelity to an instantaneous gradient alone does not specify an optimal morphology"); neither appears in the abstract or the last Introduction paragraph, so the review log's "Implemented/Addressed" for item 1 (REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION:32, REVIEW_COMPLETION:50) is only partly true.

REFUTED/OVERSTATED: (1) "zero question/hypothesis/conclusion sentences" — sentence 1 frames the open question ("when branching improves learning remains unclear") and sentence 13 is a (weak) conclusion. (2) "R3 belongs to none of the three questions" — Question 1 at line 69 explicitly asks "when can restricting noisy feedback help?", which is exactly R3's heading (line 164). R6 (serial stages/physical depth) maps loosely to Question 2's "groupings or depths". Only R9 is orphaned. (3) "the one novel theorem" — the supplement has no theorem environments; it has 8 propositions and 2 corollaries (supplementary.tex:624-1188) covering credit capacity, update moments, coefficient risk, attenuation and no-sign-reversal, and the multi-affine cut criterion is stated as a bound (main.tex eq. morphologyselection, line ~286) and summarized in Table row 5203, so singling out sentence 7 as the sole theorem is inaccurate. (4) "Reorder the Introduction's three questions to match section order" — they already follow section order (Q1→R1-R5, Q2→R6-R7, Q3→R8,R10); only R9 lacks a home.
- **Suggestion:** Open with the question and a one-sentence answer (morphology fixes a sparse dictionary; cross-subtree interactions, not rank, set which patterns are needed; image tasks and measured responses use only the coarsest pattern), three supporting sentences, one limitation. Reorder the Introduction's three questions to match section order.
