#### M005 — The prospective failure of the paper's own update-utility selector is relabeled 'a linear control' and disclosed without numbers
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:306, 194, 857; supplementary 1370-1490, Fig S35
- **Claim:** Supplementary Fig. S35 is the original main-text prospective test of the paper's update-utility bound (the R3 bound at fixed step) as a morphology selector; it lost to the development-fixed maximum-budget tree and to rank-only in 20/20 seeds in both arms (regret 0.0806/0.1040 vs 0.0450/0.0427 and 0.0083/0.0082). The main text discloses the failure only qualitatively ("selected poor outcomes", line 306) or with the score's own fresh-cohort regret (0.0814/0.1007, line 857) but never says it lost to a fixed tree, never gives the fixed-tree regret, and never identifies the "initialization score" as the R3 utility; Results/Methods call it "a linear control" while the Methods heading and cohort table call it prospective; the abstract mentions only the successful interaction-estimator selection against fixed baselines. Not "disclosed without numbers" outright: Methods 857 has the score's regret.
- **Evidence:** SI 1466-1478: moment-policy regret 0.08055/0.1040 vs fixed 0.04495/0.04269, rank-only 0.00831, lost in every seed. MORPHOLOGY_REVISION_20260905.md: 'replaces the original initialization-only morphology-selection claim'. Cohort table line 539 calls it 'Prospective linear selection', contradicting 'control'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Numbers: recomputed from source_data/prospective_morphology_selection/policy_summary.csv, policy_seed_means.csv and paired_regret_summary.csv (live repo). Moment-selector regret 0.08055 (CI 0.07262-0.08781) feedback-only / 0.10404 (0.09975-0.10791) joint; fixed = maximum_budget 0.04495 / 0.04269; rank_only 0.00831 / 0.00821. Per-seed: moment selector worse than fixed/max-budget and rank-only in 20/20 seeds in both arms (paired_regret_summary positive_seeds = 0); it does beat random (20/20 vs uniform expectation, 20/20 and 13/20 vs sampled random). SI 1466-1470 states exactly these numbers; Fig S35 panels C,D show them graphically with no printed values. S35 caption (supp 4180) says "retained after replacing its main-text figure", confirming it was the original main-text prospective test; MORPHOLOGY_REVISION_20260905.md line 3 and 17 say the same. Main text: line 306 "A linear control exposed a different limitation: an initialization score predicted the first step but selected poor outcomes after 256 updates" - no numbers, no statement that it lost to a fixed tree; line 857 "A linear control showed how inaccurate learning forecasts..." DOES give numbers, but for the fresh 25,600-fit cohort (0.0814/0.1007; matches source_data/morphology_finite_horizon/REPORT.md 0.081429/0.100691), and omits the fixed-tree regret there (0.044693/0.042695 per REPORT.md), so a reader cannot see that the score lost to a fixed tree. Methods heading text line 839 ("The prospective linear-tree experiment") and cohort table line 539 ("Prospective linear selection") call it prospective, contradicting "control" at 306/857. Abstract line 52 says only "Estimates from limited noisy examples improve tree selection against fixed and short-training baselines". Main text never states that the "initialization score/utility" (lines 306, 848) is the R3 bound; strictly, SI 1433-1440 defines it as Eq. creditoperatorbound evaluated at fixed step eta=0.35, not the eta-optimized U of Eq. phaseutility (line 178) - the reviewer's "U (Eq. phaseutility)" is slightly imprecise. Line 196 does say "Neither analysis establishes prospective selection of a unique tree" and Discussion line ~419 mentions the initialization forecast being repaired, so the failure is disclosed qualitatively, just not with its most damaging comparison.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Recomputed from live source data: source_data/prospective_morphology_selection/policy_summary.csv and policy_seed_means.csv (20 seeds x 320 tasks per arm) give moment_selector regret 0.080551 (CI 0.07262-0.08781) feedback-only and 0.104039 (0.09975-0.10791) joint; development_best = maximum_budget 0.044949 / 0.042689; rank_only 0.008310 / 0.008209. paired_regret_summary.csv: positive_seeds = 0 of 20 for the moment selector vs development_best, maximum_budget and rank_only in both arms, i.e. it lost in every seed (it beat sampled random in 20/20 feedback-only and 13/20 joint). All numbers match SI supplementary.tex 1466-1472 exactly. Design status: SI 1370-1372 ("The prospective experiment..."), 1450-1456 (3,200 choices hashed 16:11:16 UTC 5 Sep 2026 before confirmation training), source README ("tests whether a calibrated initialization update-moment score selects useful route capacity and tree geometry"), cohort table main.tex:539 "Prospective linear selection", and S35 caption (supplementary.tex:4180) "retained after replacing its main-text figure" all describe a frozen prospective test, not a control. Main text: line 306 "A linear control exposed a different limitation: an initialization score predicted the first step but selected poor outcomes after 256 updates" and 857 "A linear control showed..." never say the score is the R3 bound, never mention the fixed/maximum-budget comparator, and give no S35 numbers at 306; the abstract (main.tex:50) is silent. OVERSTATEMENTS in the finding: (a) Methods 857 does give numbers (scalar regret 0.0814/0.1007 falling to 0.000064/0.000042; count baseline 0.000134/0.000197), which I traced to source_data/morphology_finite_horizon/summaries/fresh/policy_summary.csv rows original_scalar fixed_cache step 256 (0.081429 / 0.100691) - these are the fresh 25,600-fit cohort, not S35's original 12,800-fit cohort, so "without numbers" is only true of line 306 and of the fixed-tree comparison (fresh-cohort maximum_budget 0.044693 / 0.042695 is not reported anywhere in the main text). (b) The initialization score is Eq. creditoperatorbound evaluated at the fixed actual step eta=0.35 (SI 1433-1440), not the optimized-step U of Eq. phaseutility; a close relative, but the finding's "U (Eq. phaseutility)" is imprecise. (c) "Original" refers to a same-day intermediate draft: the last committed journal main.tex (560d5f2, 2026-09-05 01:06) contains no prospective linear-tree experiment or abstract claim about it, so no submitted or committed version ever carried the claim; the demotion is acknowledged in the S35 caption. (d) Main text already caveats: R3 line 194 "Neither analysis establishes prospective selection of a unique tree"; Discussion line 419 "propagating evolving weights and SGD noise repairs the initialization forecast, yet a cheap observed-context baseline explains most of the gain". Could not open figure_S35 PDF (tool hook timeout); relied on caption and CSVs.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Numbers recomputed from source_data/prospective_morphology_selection/policy_summary.csv and policy_seed_means.csv: moment_selector mean regret 0.08055 (CI 0.07262-0.08781) feedback-only and 0.10404 (0.09975-0.10791) joint; maximum_budget (= development_best) 0.04495 / 0.04269; rank_only 0.00831 / 0.00821. Per-seed: moment_selector regret > maximum_budget in 20/20 seeds and > rank_only in 20/20 seeds in both arms (paired_regret_summary.csv positive_seeds = 0 for both comparators). It beat uniform random expectation 20/20 and sampled random 20/20 (feedback-only) / 13/20 (joint). SI lines 1466-1478 and Fig S35 C-D (figure_S35_panels_A-F.png) match these numbers exactly. Framing: main.tex 306 'A linear control exposed a different limitation: an initialization score predicted the first step but selected poor outcomes after 256 updates'; 857 'A linear control showed...'; but Methods 837 calls the same experiment 'The prospective linear-tree experiment' and cohort table line 539 lists it as 'Prospective linear selection ... sealed choices'; SI heading 1371 'Prospective costed selection'; S35 caption (SI 4180) admits it is 'the unchanged original 20-seed confirmation experiment, retained after replacing its main-text figure'. Main text never cites Eq. phaseutility in 306 or 837-860; the only link is 'the bound at step 0.35' (847) and 'initialization utility' (848, 857). SI 1433-1440 shows the S35 score is the same descent bound evaluated at the fixed actual step eta=0.35, not the step-optimized U of Eq. phaseutility, so the reviewer's identification with U is close but not exact. Partly refuted: 'disclosed without numbers' is overstated - line 857 gives regret 0.0814/0.1007 for the initialization score (in the 25,600-fit follow-up) and Methods 851 says 'negative long-horizon result'; what is missing is the fixed-tree comparator (0.045/0.043), the all-20-seeds loss, and any regret in Results. Abstract (line 52) is silent on the failed selector but its selection sentence refers to the separate interaction-estimate selector (Fig 6C), not S35. R3 line 194 already says 'Neither analysis establishes prospective selection of a unique tree'.
- **Suggestion:** Add at end of R3 or at 306: 'Used prospectively among twenty costed trees, the update-utility score lost to a fixed maximum-budget tree in all 20 seeds (regret 0.081 vs 0.045; Fig S35); this motivated the interaction-structure approach.' Rename 'linear control' to 'original prospective test' at 306 and 857; add a clause to the abstract.

#### M008 — Tree-structured single-neuron computation precedents (Jones & Kording 2021; Poirazi & Mel 2001; Beniaguev 2021) are absent
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:262-276 (R6), 278-312 (R7); references.bib
- **Claim:** The manuscript does not cite the tree-structured single-neuron computation literature that directly precedes R7's binary-tree units with one scalar per branch (Jones & Kording 2021, Neural Computation; Poirazi & Mel 2001 and the two-layer subunit view of Poirazi, Brannon & Mel 2003, the latter cited only in the intro at main.tex:57) or the deep-network single-neuron capacity view (Beniaguev, Segev & London 2021). R7 (main.tex:280-284) cites only ML tensor/spectral theory for its tree-computation framing. R6's serial conductance ladder is more loosely related to these works than the finding states. A referee from this community would likely flag the omission; a one-sentence attribution at main.tex:280 plus bib entries, and optionally a mention in R6 or Discussion line 423, would resolve it.
- **Evidence:** grep Jones/Kording/Beniaguev: 0 hits; 'Mel' only in poirazi2003pyramidal. main.tex:284 'A branch must summarize its inputs in one scalar output' is their premise.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked references.bib in the snapshot (68 entries, listed all keys): no entry for Jones & Kording (2021 or 2022), Beniaguev et al. 2021, or Poirazi & Mel 2001; the only Mel/Poirazi entries are poirazi2003pyramidal (line 17) and chavlis2025dendrites (line 253). grep -i 'jones|kording|beniaguev' over main.tex, supplementary/supplementary.tex, the six live *_methods.tex fragments, and the live journal bib: 0 hits. poirazi2003pyramidal is cited once, main.tex:57 (intro, as evidence that dendritic locations are functionally distinct), not at the R7 framing sentence main.tex:280, which cites only cohen2016tensor, canatar2021spectral, lampinen2019generalization (ML theory, no single-neuron tree precedent). main.tex:284 'A branch must summarize its inputs in one scalar output' and main.tex:282 (7-unit binary tree, one multi-affine unit per node, root-only readout) do describe the same object class as Jones & Kording's k-tree units and the two-layer subunit model of Poirazi, Brannon & Mel 2003. The manuscript also uses MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/CIFAR image tasks (main.tex:143,160,580-641), the benchmarks Jones & Kording used, sharpening the relevance. Partial coverage exists: Discussion main.tex:423 cites dendritic ANN work (chavlis2025dendrites, sezener2021gated, iyer2022activedendrites, lv2025dendritic) and cable-learning (bicknell2021synaptic), and the authors' analysis/reference_audit.md 'Closest prior work added' table (lines 24-36) shows a deliberate prior-work audit that nonetheless omitted this lineage. Overstatement: R6 (main.tex:262-276) is a shunting-normalization conductance ladder with sensor placement, not a replication of Beniaguev's deep-CNN fit to an L5 neuron's temporal I/O; and R7's Fourier-cut representability theorem is a different analysis from J&K's empirical training, so 'replicate the object' is too strong for R6; it is accurate for R7's model class.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** Checked with grep over the snapshot main.tex, supplementary/supplementary.tex, references.bib and the six live methods fragments (/n/holylabs/.../journal/supplementary/*_methods.tex): zero hits for "Jones", "Kording", "Beniaguev". references.bib has 68 entries; the only Mel/Poirazi entries are poirazi2003pyramidal (bib line 17) and chavlis2025dendrites (bib line 252). Poirazi & Mel 2001 is absent. poirazi2003pyramidal is cited once, in the Introduction (main.tex:57) inside a seven-reference biophysics list; chavlis2025dendrites is cited once, in the Discussion (main.tex:423) as an "engineered gating architecture". Neither is cited in R6 (main.tex:262-276) or R7 (main.tex:278-308). R7's opening sentence (main.tex:280) frames precedent exclusively via cohen2016tensor, canatar2021spectral and lampinen2019generalization (ML references); main.tex:284 states "A branch must summarize its inputs in one scalar output", and main.tex:282 defines the node u=a+b u_L+c u_R+d u_L u_R with binary-tree children and root-only readout — i.e. the scalar-subunit binary tree of Jones & Kording (2021, Neural Comput.) and the two-layer subunit model of Poirazi, Brannon & Mel. R6's depth ladder D1-D4 (main.tex:266-275, "Depth benefits also saturated") and the SI depth sections (supplementary.tex:2424 "Nonlinear physical depth", Fig S31; supplementary.tex:3989 caption "Fixed terminal-branch and input-contact budgets do not make greater dendritic depth intrinsically beneficial") carry no citation to any prior single-neuron depth/capacity work (Jones & Kording's depth-vs-accuracy analysis; Beniaguev, Segev & London 2021 deep-network equivalence). Discussion main.tex:415 again attributes the hierarchical-computation precedent only to cohen2016tensor. Additional overlap the reviewer did not mention: R2 (main.tex:143-149) and SI Fig S4 (supplementary.tex:2915) evaluate tree units on flattened MNIST, Fashion-MNIST and CIFAR-10, the same benchmark set used by Jones & Kording 2021. No results or numbers are affected; the fix is one framing sentence at main.tex:280 (and a clause in R6) plus three bib entries.
- **Suggestion:** At 280 add: 'Tree-structured single-neuron models with one scalar per branch [poirazi2003, Jones & Kording 2021] and deep-network fits [Beniaguev 2021] establish what such trees can compute; here we ask which trees a given interaction structure requires and at what depth.' Add entries; cite Jones & Kording in R6.

#### M009 — The 47-cell and second-mouse 'structural checks' replicate only the route-generated positive control; the independent-field test exists only in the 8-cell proxy-labeled pilot
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:329-333, 421, Fig 7G caption 340; supplementary.tex:2960-2995, 3082-3120, 4000-4003, table 4975
- **Claim:** The independent-target (reciprocal-cable focal-shunt) capture test exists only for the initial eight proxy-labeled cells (source_data/reciprocal_routing, 8 root_ids); the 47-cell v661 and 10-cell Pinky replications evaluate only the route-generated positive control t=Gamma a (model-matched field). On the independent field, subtrees do not beat depth bins at K=8 (0.458 vs 0.445, 5/8 cells, Wilcoxon P=0.84) and lose at K=1,2,4 (0/8, 1/8, 2/8). The main text at line 333 does correctly scope the repetition to the "positive-control comparison", and the supplement (2993-2995, S27 caption) states the endpoint is model matched, so the finding is a clarity/overreach issue rather than a false statement: Discussion line 421 ("a second mouse supporting a structural check"), the Fig 7G title/caption (line 340), and the S10 caption headline do not say the replicated check is the favorable model-matched control and not the independent-field test. Fix: label panel G and the S10/S27 headlines as "route-generated field (positive control)", qualify line 421 accordingly, and either run the reciprocal operator on the 47-cell cohort (the focal solver already ran on 45 of them) or state explicitly that the independent-field test is limited to eight cells and that depth bins matched or exceeded subtrees there for K<=8.
- **Evidence:** reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv has eight root_ids only; microns_v661_replication/routing/ empty; supp_figure_routing_curves.csv has only model-field methods; S10B caption 'constructed target fields'; Pinky primary outcome on model-matched field. Per-cell subtree>depth on cable field: K=1 0/8, K=2 1/8, K=4 2/8, K=8 5/8, K=16 7/8. Main 331 itself calls the field 'deliberately favorable'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** DATA FACTS (all confirmed). (1) source_data/reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv: 240 rows, exactly 8 root_ids x 5 channel counts x 6 methods; summary.json "n_cells": 8; report.md K=8 depth-bin contrast 0.0137 [-0.037, 0.068], 5/8 cells, Wilcoxon p=0.84375 (my exhaustive 2^8 sign-flip on the same 8 differences gives P=0.68; either way not separated). Recomputed per-K subtree>depth counts from the CSV: K=1 0/8 (means 0.124 vs 0.314), K=2 1/8 (0.180 vs 0.330), K=4 2/8 (0.267 vs 0.374), K=8 5/8 (0.458 vs 0.445), K=16 7/8 (0.664 vs 0.572) - exactly as the reviewer stated. (2) The reviewer's claim that microns_v661_replication/routing/ is empty is WRONG: it holds cell_metrics.csv, feedback_compression_curves.csv, segment_metrics.csv, summary.json. But those files are the model-matched field: routing_capacity_20stream_summary.json "boundaries": "The perturbation covariance is generated from the same conductance-tree model"; methods present are only dense PCA oracle / morphology-aware paths / random paths / depth-only bins / shuffled ancestry; supp_figure_routing_curves.csv has the same five methods and no reciprocal/shunt-response field; replication_summary.json has routing_eight_channels and focal_primary_dose_one but no reciprocal-operator capture. (3) pinky_v185_replication/routing/: same feedback_compression_curves format, five model-field methods, summary.json compares to the "minnie65_k4_reference" 47-cell model-matched contrasts; no reciprocal operator. (4) Scripts: analyze_reciprocal_routing_controls.py writes only to source_data/reciprocal_routing (DEFAULT_OUTDIR, line 41); build_main_figure_07.py line 235 reads only reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv for panel C; nothing in scripts/ applies the reciprocal operator to v661 or Pinky cohorts. So the independent-field test exists only in the 8-cell cohort. TEXT FACTS (where the finding overstates). main.tex:333 does NOT say the stronger test was repeated: it reads "The predefined four-route positive-control comparison was repeated in 47 disjoint cells from the same mouse and in ten eligible cells from the independent Pinky mouse." supplementary.tex:2993-2995 says "The endpoint remains capture of a model-matched field"; supplementary.tex:3082-3131 describes the 47-cell result under fields "generated by multiplying independent route amplitudes by the kernel"; S27 caption (supplementary.tex:4122) ends "The target fields are model matched". main.tex:331 says "this deliberately favorable condition" of the t=Gamma a control, and main.tex:329-331 reports honestly that depth bins beat subtrees at K<=4 and are unseparated at K=8. Residual ambiguity that supports the reviewer: main.tex:421 "a second mouse supporting a structural check" does not say which test; Fig 7 panel G title "Disjoint and second-animal cohorts" and the caption at main.tex:340 ("control-minus-subtree weighted residual norm") never name the field, while panels C/D are explicitly labeled; S10 caption (supplementary.tex:4002) "Structural routing and focal shunting remain robust" describes "modeled field-energy capture" without saying model-matched; and the paragraph ordering at main.tex:329-333 (stronger test, then "repeated") invites conflation. The reviewer's sub-claim that the text "calls the target 'the stronger structural test' then says it was 'repeated'" is therefore inaccurate as a quotation of 333, which explicitly scopes the repetition to the positive control.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Data facts CONFIRMED. (1) source_data/reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv (live repo, 240 rows) contains exactly 8 root_ids x 5 K x 6 methods; operator_audit.csv lists the same 8 cells; summary.json n_cells=8, status complete. Recomputed per-cell morphology>control on this independent focal-shunt field: depth bins K=1 0/8 (0.124 vs 0.314), K=2 1/8 (0.180 vs 0.330), K=4 2/8 (0.267 vs 0.374), K=8 5/8 (0.458 vs 0.445, Wilcoxon p=0.84375 in summary.json), K=16 7/8 (0.664 vs 0.572); random 8/8 and shuffled 8/8 at K=8; degree-depth surrogate 6/8 at K=8 (p=0.078). These match supplementary.tex:4975-4989 (Table, 0.458/0.445/0.014/5/8) and main.tex:331. (2) The 47-cell and Pinky routing data are positive-control (t=Gamma a) only: microns_v661_replication/routing_capacity_20stream_summary.json boundaries[0] = 'The perturbation covariance is generated from the same conductance-tree model'; pinky_v185_replication/routing/ holds only feedback_compression_curves/k4_cross_animal_contrasts (route-generated residuals) and supplementary.tex:2995-2997 states 'The endpoint remains capture of a model-matched field'; grep for reciprocal/shunt-response across both directories returns nothing. scripts/build_main_figure_07.py:235 (panel C <- reciprocal_routing, 8 cells) and :281-282 (panel G <- pinky k4_cross_animal_contrasts.csv, minnie65 v661 n=47 + Pinky n=10) confirm Fig 7C is the 8-cell independent field and Fig 7G is the route-generated field. Minor error in the reviewer's evidence: microns_v661_replication/routing/ is NOT empty in the live repo (cell_metrics.csv, feedback_compression_curves.csv, summary.json etc.), but all are positive-control outputs, so the substance holds. Text claims PARTLY OVERSTATED. main.tex:333 reads 'The predefined four-route positive-control comparison was repeated in 47 disjoint cells... and in ten eligible cells from the independent Pinky mouse' -- the Results sentence already identifies what was replicated as the positive control, so the reviewer's reading that the text implies the 'stronger structural test' was repeated is not supported at line 333. main.tex:331 also honestly reports depth bins better at K<=4, unseparated at K=8. What remains unlabeled: main.tex:421 Discussion ('a second mouse supporting a structural check') does not say which field; Fig 7G caption (main.tex:340) says only 'control-minus-subtree weighted residual norm ... in a disjoint 47-cell ... cohort and ten qualifying mouse-2 ... cells' without naming the route-generated field, and the rendered panel G title is 'Disjoint and second-animal cohorts' whereas panels C/D carry explicit 'independent target' / 'positive control, t = Gamma a' tags (figure_07.pdf); supplementary.tex:4000 S10 caption title 'Structural routing and focal shunting remain robust in a non-overlapping public-v661 cell sample' and supplementary.tex:3104-3110 do not state that the 47-cell routing used the route-generated field (only routing_capacity summary JSON does). Supplementary.tex:5213 summary row correctly limits the independent-field claim to random/row-shuffled controls.
- **Suggestion:** Run the reciprocal-cable focal-shunt operator on the 47-cell cohort (focal solver already ran on 45) and, if feasible, Pinky; add to Table S26/Fig 7C. Otherwise rewrite 333, 340 and 421 to say replication covers only the route-generated control and that on the independent target depth bins matched or exceeded subtrees up to K=8.

#### M016 — Discussion is a section-by-section recital with the hedges repeated, not a synthesis
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** structure
- **Location:** main.tex:413-426
- **Claim:** The Discussion (main.tex 413-425, 594 words) is organized as a Results-order walk-through: 415 restates R7, 417 restates R2-R5, 419 restates R6 plus the linear control, 421 restates R8-R10, and the caveats from each Results block are repeated at a density of roughly one negation per 50 words. It does contain a thesis (413), per-paragraph synthesis sentences (417, 419), a biological-reach paragraph (421), a proposed experiment (423) and a closing synthesis (425), so it is not devoid of interpretation. What it lacks is (i) an explicit statement of which result carries the paper and which are secondary, (ii) a predicted sign for the proposed plasticity experiment in 423 (on-route vs electrotonically matched off-route difference present only in high-conductance states), (iii) a statement of what the cut-rank criterion adds relative to the tensor/hierarchical-computation analyses it is merely 'connected' to in 415, and (iv) a mechanism-level rather than list-level relation to the eight cited theories in 423. Recommended: tighten to four or five paragraphs led by the claim, move repeated caveats to a single limitations paragraph, and give the 423 experiment an expected outcome. The one negation not tied to a result is 413's 'Task rank or fidelity to an instantaneous gradient alone does not specify an optimal morphology', which the Introduction never sets up as a competing view.
- **Evidence:** 415 restates R7; 417 R2-R5; 419 R6/linear control; 421 R8-R10; 426 generic; theories at 423 only 'can be combined with'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.8):** Read main.tex lines 411-426 (Discussion; 7 paragraphs, 594 words by wc: 413=73, 415=102, 417=112, 419=78, 421=101, 423=75, 425=53). Ordering check: 415 covers R7 (multi-affine, cut ranks, calibration, conductance model); 417 covers R2-R5 (one signal per neuron, Boolean parity/XOR-of-AND, branch-conflict, ancestry, subtree profiles, noisy/delayed cues); 419 covers R6 and the linear-selection control; 421 covers R8-R10 (arbors, shunting, visual cohort, imposed alignment). So the Results-order recital is real. Hedge count in 413-425 (grep): 'does not' x5, 'do not' x1, 'limit(s)' x3, 'omit', 'unnecessary', 'no learning' -- roughly one negation per 50 words. However, the reviewer's stronger claims are overstated: (a) 413 opens with a thesis, not a recap ('Their interaction determines when a task-compatible tree is useful. Task rank or fidelity to an instantaneous gradient alone does not specify an optimal morphology'), and 425 closes with a synthesis sentence ('task interactions supply a constructive link to morphology, while estimation error and credit delivery determine how much of that capacity learning realizes'); paragraphs 417 and 419 each end with a 'therefore' synthesis sentence. (b) 'What to believe about real dendrites' is stated in 421 ('sparse, mostly coarse addresses'; shunting matters 'principally in high-conductance states'; the visual cohort 'does not favor the tested subtree rule') and in 423 a biological test is given ('match baseline somatic voltage, focal first-order current and global error, then compare plasticity below an inhibitory site with electrotonically matched off-route sites'), with a partial expected-outcome ('Its dependence on conductance state could distinguish transport from forward gain control'). What is genuinely absent: no explicit statement of which result is strongest or load-bearing; the biological test states no predicted sign/magnitude (e.g., plasticity difference on-route vs off-route should appear only in high-conductance states and vanish with matched local current); 415 only 'connects' to cohen2016tensor rather than saying what the cut-rank criterion adds beyond tensor/kernel analyses; 423 lists eight theories with 'can be combined with' and no mechanism. (c) 'Delete sentences negating claims never made': the five 'does not' sentences each report an actual result or model limitation (415 cut theorem/conductance ancestors; 417 subtree profiles; 421 attenuation, visual cohort), so they are not straw negations; only 413's 'Task rank ... does not specify an optimal morphology' negates a position the Introduction (lines 55-69) never attributes to anyone. WRITING_REVISION_20260905.md line 11 states the intended Discussion design ('separates what can be represented, estimated and learned, then develops the biological implications and limitations'), which matches what is on the page but not the reviewer's stronger charge.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Read main.tex 411-426 (Discussion; 7 paragraphs, 595 words: 413=73, 415=102, 417=112, 419=78, 421=101, 423=75, 425=53 words), the Introduction (55-70) and abstract (51-53), and analysis/WRITING_REVISION_20260905.md line 11. Confirmed: four of seven paragraphs are one-Results-block recitals with the Results hedges carried over -- 415 = R7 (multi-affine/cut rank, 'exponential feature dictionary limits scaling', 'cut theorem does not automatically extend'); 417 = R2-R5 + Boolean ('do not consistently beat alternative profiles; noisy coefficients and delayed cues further limit delivery'); 419 = R6 + linear-selection control; 421 = R8-R10 ('uneven inhibitory labels limiting', 'does not improve final loss', 'contains no learning measurements and does not favor'). Six 'does not/do not/no' clauses in 16 lines. No sentence ranks results or says which is strongest. 415 says the cut-rank result 'connects' to tensor descriptions (cohen2016tensor) but never says how it differs or what it predicts for real arbors. Refuted/overstated parts: the reviewer omits paragraph 413, which is a genuine synthesis opener ('separating three questions: what a tree can represent, what can be estimated from examples, and what a learning rule can acquire... Task rank or fidelity to an instantaneous gradient alone does not specify an optimal morphology'); 417 and 419 each close with a synthesizing sentence ('Spatial credit therefore has conditional advantages that are distinct from the representational benefit of grouping inputs'; 'Predicting learning accurately therefore provides a different test from showing that detailed branching is useful'); 425 closes with a one-sentence thesis restatement; and 423 does state a concrete biological test (match baseline somatic voltage, focal first-order current and global error; compare plasticity below an inhibitory site with electrotonically matched off-route sites; conductance-state dependence distinguishes transport from gain control) -- what is missing is only the expected sign/falsifying outcome. On 'sentences negating claims never made': the six negations are mostly reported outcomes or scope limits (e.g., alignment-cohort null, reliability-aligned shunting null), not straw men; the one exception is arguably 413's 'does not specify an optimal morphology', which is the paper's thesis rather than a negation. Structure judgment only; no numbers involved.
- **Suggestion:** Four paragraphs: the answer in the paper's terms; why cut-rank differs from prior tensor/kernel analyses and what it predicts for real arbors; the biological boundary with the 423 experiment made concrete with expected sign; limitations. Delete sentences negating claims never made.

Could not fetch the Nature Communications format page (idp.nature.com auth redirect), so the 3,000-word Methods guidance is from recollection, not verified here. The claim that ρ 0.937/0.916, 95/100 and 13/20 are 'found only here and S16 caption' is wrong: they are in the supplement body text at supplementary.tex 1341 (0.9373, 0.9157), 1359 (95 of 100), 893-896 (13 of 20, regret 1.94, 2.75). They are indeed absent from Results (grep of main.tex 71-410 finds none). Conversely 1,407, 0.369, 0.443, 319/420 (Methods 821, Results 292) and 0.0814/0.000064/0.000134 (Methods 857) do not appear anywhere in supplementary.tex, so some Methods-only outcomes have no SI home. 573-606 are matched-width-fallback and Table S20 exclusion statements — provenance/exclusion reporting that Nature journals require somewhere, so 'historical decisions to delete' overstates; moving to SI with a pointer is reasonable.

The reviewer's 7,971 lies between these, so "~8,000 words, ~2.7x" is fair (my per-subsection figures differ from theirs — Models 744 vs 1,413 — because of math handling, not substance). Guidance: Nature Communications submission guide (ncomms-submission-guide.pdf, extracted text lines 41-45) says "Methods should be written as concisely as possible and typically do not exceed 3,000 words but may be longer if necessary" — i.e. a soft guideline, not a hard limit; the same guide caps main text at 5,000 words, and Results alone (lines 71-410) is ~8,600 words, which is the larger compliance problem and is outside this finding. Duplications checked line by line: 663 restates 230 (crossings shift with branch count, five two-branch seeds, S29D-F) and adds cosine 0.987 -> -0.519 and 5e-11 replay; 710 restates 260 (7.16 points, 54.47 below oracle, S32) plus CI and 25.83%/64.03%/80.29%; 821 restates 292 (1,407/1,680, NMSE 0.369/0.443, S36) plus 319/420 and regret 0.0095; 900 restates 274 (10.96, 0.64, S28/S31) plus 13.47 (12.89-14.05); the 0.232->0.244/0.385/0.468/0.976 passage is at line 1096 (reviewer cited 1097-1100, off by one line) and restates 396. The reviewer's claim that they occur "only here and S16 caption" is inaccurate: supplementary.tex lines 893 (13 of 20, 1.94), 1341 and 4392 (rho 0.9373, Table), and 1359 (95 of 100) carry them in SI text and tables. Summing the eight outcome paragraphs (663, 710, 778-793, 821, 857, 900, 991, 1096) gives 1,326 words; with chronology ~1,570, consistent with the reviewer's ~1,700. The flagged Interaction paragraphs 819-832 are, on reading their full text, mostly procedural (fits, seeds, margins, SI pointers) with a single outcome sentence each, so they are not restatement.
- **Suggestion:** One what/how/n/estimand paragraph (~250 words) per Results subsection plus Statistics; move 778-793, 857, 900, 991, 1097-1100 to SI (mostly already there); delete restatements at 663, 710, 821; move fallback/exclusion history to SI S5 with a pointer; trim derivational prose duplicating SI S1-S2.

- **Status:** confirmed

That commit exists only in a temporary --shared clone created and discarded by the release workflow (build_final_release.py:50-69); it is unresolvable in the canonical paper repo (not in any ref, reflog, or as a dangling object), as are the three earlier snapshot hashes recorded today (4ea1665, 6a8757e, 77479f3). Scope limits: the implementation snapshot (commit 87c8486, reachable ancestor of HEAD, archive contents verified against it) is correctly anchored; the Code availability text in main.tex:1210-1219 cites no hash and is not itself wrong; the archives are internally consistent (SHA256SUMS) and match the manuscript within 6 lines, so scientific content is not compromised -- the defect is a false, unverifiable provenance anchor in the reviewer archive plus a workflow that will keep producing such anchors and has no test guarding against it.
- **Evidence:** git cat-file -t 5483817 fails; log --all/reflog 0 hits; recorded commit time equals deterministic_timestamp_epoch (build-time transient commit). build_software_release.py 1085-1094 requires clean repo yet git status shows 206 dirty entries; bundle_metadata.json: source_commit 560d5f2, source_worktree_clean false.

There: HEAD=560d5f2 (main); `git cat-file -t 5483817839c16a9d48c343062dee4f6340ed84cd` -> "could not get object info"; 0 hits in `git log --all`, `git rev-list --all`, `git reflog` (reflog shows HEAD never left 560d5f2 after 2026-09-05 01:06), and no dangling object in `git fsck --no-reflogs`. A separate local clone captures the current tracked source ... in its own clean commit"), :50-51 (git clone --shared into mkdtemp), :65-69 (git add -u; git add -f; commit as "Scientific release snapshot"), :124 (build from that commit); the temp clone is discarded, so the hash is unrecoverable. Paper repo `git status --porcelain=v1` = 206 entries (278 with untracked). bundle_metadata.json: source_commit 560d5f2b..., source_worktree_clean false, source_snapshot_method "current explicit allowlist". build_software_release.py:1088-1092 raises if the journal repo is dirty; METADATA.json says journal_repository_clean_at_build true and README.md:4-7,14,174-177 says "committed paper snapshot at 5483817..." / "clean paper repository Git HEAD". No test checks reachability (tests/test_software_release_repositories.py:25-60 only builds fixture repos; grep for journal_repository_commit/cat-file in tests returns nothing).
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.93):** Checked: (1) submission/software_release/METADATA.json records journal_repository_commit=5483817839c16a9d48c343062dee4f6340ed84cd, journal_repository_clean_at_build=true, journal_repository_commit_utc=2026-09-06T03:04:49Z and deterministic_timestamp_epoch=1788663889 (= 03:04:49Z, the same instant); the software zip mtime is Sep 5 23:06 EDT, i.e. ~1 min after that "commit". The reviewer's parent-repo check would miss it, so I re-checked in the nested repo: `git cat-file -t 5483817839c1...` -> "bad object"; `git log --all` 0 hits; reflog 0 hits; `git fsck --unreachable` lists 7 dangling commits, none is 5483817. So the archived "committed paper snapshot" is not recoverable from any reachable commit; consistent with a transient build-time commit later discarded (build_software_release.py:1085-1094 hard-fails on a dirty paper repo, so a commit must have been made and undone, evidently in a clone/worktree no longer present; no script in journal/ creates commits). (4) nature_communications_bundle/manifests/bundle_metadata.json: source_commit=560d5f2, source_worktree_clean=false — confirmed; build_submission_bundle.py:362-374 records this honestly. The reviewer's location "main.tex:1210-1219" and the phrase attributing the hash to the main text are therefore slightly overstated; the defect lives in the archive README/METADATA. #### M023 — Title and Discussion conflate three different 'dictionaries'; the route dictionary is absent from the strongest new results
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** main.tex:39, 52, 133, 300, 413, 415; supplementary.tex:4686 (Table S14)
- **Claim:** The word "dictionary" is used in two distinct senses (route/delivery-profile dictionary, lines 52/63 etc., and the multi-affine "exponential feature dictionary", lines 300/415), and the title's "morphology as a dictionary" is looser than the text's own definition, in which morphology constrains the dictionary (line 63). Discussion line 413 attributes the representability/estimation/learning separation to "the dictionary framework", but R7's representability (cut-rank theorem) and estimation (Fourier-feature Lasso) involve no route dictionary; the route-dictionary variants tested in R7 (one-profile, two-subtree, shuffled projections; lines 302, 308, 827, 829) show no consistent advantage, so the strongest new results concern forward grouping, not the route dictionary. Table S14 (supp. 4684-4716) lists five constructions and omits the R7 projection-profile constructions although main line 133 says it compares "the constructions used below"; this is an incompleteness, not a contradiction, since the manuscript never labels the R7 profiles a dictionary. The phrase "sparse Fourier dictionary" does not appear in the manuscript.
- **Evidence:** Table S14 rows: A_u, P_K, Φ^(K), A_K, D. Methods 825/829 describe one-profile, two-subtree, shuffled profiles not in the table; morphology_credit/README.md: two-subtree oracle profiles did not beat shuffled.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked in the snapshot (main.tex): line 39 title "Dendritic morphology as a dictionary for local credit assignment"; line 63 defines "route dictionary" as a set of spatial delivery patterns and says "Morphology constrains the available patterns" (i.e. morphology constrains the dictionary; it is not itself the dictionary, so the title metaphor is looser than the definition). Lines 300 and 415 use "exponential feature dictionary" for the 255-monomial Fourier feature set of the multi-affine estimator (line 298: "all 255 nonconstant Fourier features") -- a genuinely different object from the route dictionary. The exact phrase "sparse Fourier dictionary" quoted in the finding does NOT occur anywhere in main.tex or supplementary.tex (grep -i fourier | grep dictionar returns only main.tex:298, which says "Fourier features"); so the manuscript has two senses of "dictionary" plus a title metaphor, not three named dictionaries. Discussion line 413: "The dictionary framework links these constraints by separating three questions: what a tree can represent, what can be estimated from examples, and what a learning rule can acquire." R7 (lines 278-310) and Methods 819-825: representability = centered cut-rank theorem on the multi-affine forward tree; estimation = Lasso over Fourier features + dynamic program; neither involves any delivery-profile/route dictionary -- confirmed. Route-dictionary objects enter R7 only via the credit comparison (Methods line 827: "one-profile projection, two proximal-subtree profiles and size-matched shuffled profiles"; line 829: conductance one-profile/two-subtree projections), and there the result is null: main line 302 "Oracle two-subtree profiles did not consistently beat shuffled profiles", line 308 calibrated broadcast excess 0.000059 (95% CI -0.000008 to 0.000154); source_data/morphology_credit/README.md confirms "did not consistently outperform size-matched shuffled profiles; averaged under SGD, their performance nearly equaled broadcast". The strong R7 numbers (regret 0.1561->0.0274; NMSE 0.1246 vs 0.5837; conductance 0.000150 vs 0.028921) are all forward-grouping effects. Table S14: supplementary.tex lines 4684-4716 (13 table environments precede it, so it is S14 as cited at main line 133); rows are exactly A_u, P_K, Phi^(K), A_K, D; caption says "The five constructions"; forward models listed for A_u are "Directed conductance and raw-additive forward trees", so the multi-affine and 4-terminal conductance projection profiles of R7 are not covered, while main line 133 says "The constructions used below share this expansion principle ... Supplementary Table S14 compares them." Mitigating: the manuscript never itself calls the R7 projection profiles a "dictionary" (supplement lines 1718-2919 use the word only at 2342/2345 for the context-field task), so the Table S14 gap is an incompleteness relative to line 133's "constructions used below", not an internal contradiction.
- **Suggestion:** Reserve 'dictionary' for route dictionaries ('feature basis' for Fourier); rewrite 413 as constraining forward interaction structure and delivery patterns separately; add R7 dictionaries to Table S14 or fix 133; consider 'Dendritic morphology constrains route dictionaries for local credit assignment'.

#### M025 — Hedging density hides which results are decisive
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** writing
- **Location:** main.tex:104, 115, 131, 147, 151, 186-187, 196-197, 236-238, 260, 274-276, 296, 331, 335, 366-371, 402
- **Claim:** About 37% of Results paragraphs (30 of 81) end on a negated scope-limiting sentence, and Results contain roughly 80 negation tokens in 8,150 words; the same modeled-not-observed / no-learning-advantage disclaimers for the MICrONS route (§R8) and focal-shunt (§R9) analyses appear in Results text, figure captions (Fig. 7 line 340, Fig. 8 line 369), Methods (line 991) and Discussion (line 421). Because strong (Figs 6-7) and weak (Fig 5) sections close with the same kind of sentence, the closing lines do not signal which results are decisive; stating each scope limit once and phrasing conclusions positively with the scope in the same clause would fix this without changing content.
- **Evidence:** ~60 negations in ~8,400 words ('does not' 20, 'do not' 14, 'not a' 12, 'neither' 7); modeled-not-observed at 317-318, 340, 366-371, 421.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked main.tex in the snapshot with a script that strips LaTeX macros/math and counts words and negations by section, plus a paragraph-ending scan of Results (lines 71-410, figure environments excluded). Word counts: Intro 560, Results 8153, Discussion 592, Methods 7977. Negation tallies in Results alone: 'not' 66, 'does not' 13, 'do not' 8, 'not a' 11, 'without' 10, 'cannot' 4, 'neither' 2, 'rather than' 7; adding Intro+Discussion gives 'does not' 19, 'do not' 9, 'not a' 11, so the reviewer's headline counts (20/14/12) are approximately right, but 'neither' 7 is an overcount (2 in Results, 4 incl. captions at 147,155,188,194,264,369,407; 12 only if 'nor' is included across the whole file). Paragraph-ending scan: 30 of 81 Results paragraphs (37%) end with a sentence containing not/neither/without/cannot/no (e.g. lines 104, 115, 124, 131, 147, 151, 184, 186, 188, 196, 224, 245, 249, 251, 260, 266, 272, 282, 292, 317, 327, 346, 356, 394, 396). That is a high density but not 'almost every paragraph'. Every cited line in the finding exists and contains the claimed hedge (verified 104, 115, 131, 147, 151, 186, 196, 260, 274, 276, 296, 317, 331, 335, 340, 369, 402, 421). Cross-section repetition is confirmed concretely for two sections: (a) §R8 — line 335 'the reported perturbations are sensitivity tests, not calibrated uncertainty ... without establishing their use during learning', caption line 340 'All fields are modeled, not observed', Methods line 991 'These are controlled preprocessing sensitivities, not calibrated reconstruction uncertainties ... Whether neurons use them during learning remains untested', Discussion 421; (b) §R9 — line 346 'not a trained weight or measured plasticity event', line 356 'does not ... establish a beneficial learning update', caption 369 'These modeled sensitivities neither measure plasticity nor establish a learning advantage', Discussion 421. Strong-vs-weak parity: the closing sentences of §R5 (weak, Fig 5; lines 249 'conditional advantage ... without attributing', 260 'do not derive the context signal') and of §R7/§R8 (strong, Figs 6-7; line 296 'does not establish a learning advantage', line 335 'without establishing their use during learning') are structurally identical negations, so the 'read alike' point holds for the paragraph endings, though the body text of §R7/§R8 does report effect sizes and seed counts positively. No numbers or science are at stake; this is a style finding.
- **Suggestion:** State each scope limit once (Methods or a limitations paragraph); phrase results positively with scope in the same clause; delete negations answering unraised objections.

#### M026 — Revision archaeology leaks into the manuscript ('historical', repo directory names, tool credits)
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:145, 573, 591, 601, 770, 779, 785, 803, 1096, 1115, 1153, 1162-1165
- **Claim:** 'Historical' appears 13 times in the main text (lines 145, 502, 505, 573, 591, 601, 770, 779, 785, 1096, 1153, 1162-1163) without ever being defined, and within the subtree-utility Methods (769-785) it labels two different superseded objects (a PCA-for-derangement comparator and the hypothetical-minibatch score). Source-data directory names appear bare in Methods (review_evidence_reanalysis at 762, fulltree_within_span_oracle at 1093, credit_phase_plane/points.csv at 1128, generator hierarchical_gain_load at 864) without being identified as Source Data paths. The 'Detailed retrospective score comparisons' paragraph (778-793) reads as a version comparison; its substantive R5 numbers should be kept but the historical-vs-new framing condensed. The Codex disclosure (1165-1168) is required by journal policy and should stay.
- **Evidence:** 145 'The historical matched-width fallback retained neuron identity...' before a fallback is introduced; 601 'Historical S1/S3 aggregates ... excluded'; 803 'review_evidence_reanalysis document ...'; 1115 'fulltree_within_span_oracle'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked main.tex in the snapshot with grep/sed and the compiled main.pdf via pdftotext. (1) 'historical' word count in main.tex is 13, not 11, on lines 145, 502, 505, 573, 591, 601, 770, 779, 785, 1096, 1153, 1162, 1163 (all render in main.pdf, e.g. PDF lines 123, 627-629, 660, 671, 682, 809, 814, 817, 1123, 1162, 1167-1168). No definition anywhere: the Methods preamble (main.tex:429-435) defines 'reanalysis' but not 'historical'; grep for any definition of the term in main.tex and supplementary.tex returned nothing (supplement uses it 11 more times, e.g. 2879-2886, 3077, 3846, 4711, 4915, again undefined). (2) Line 145: the matched-width fallback is first named in passing one sentence earlier (144, 'matched-width scalar-fallback control') but only explained at 145/573-580 and 593-597; so the 'historical' qualifier is unexplained at first use, though 'before a fallback is introduced' is slightly overstated. (3) The term denotes two different objects within one subsection: 769-770 'historical utility comparator that substituted PCA for derangement' vs 779/785 'historical noisy utility' = the hypothetical-minibatch score defined at 756; a reader cannot tell these apart. (4) Directory names: the reviewer's line numbers are wrong. review\_evidence\_reanalysis is at main.tex:762 (not 803); fulltree\_within\_span\_oracle at 1093 (not 1115); additionally credit\_phase\_plane/points.csv at 1128 and generator name hierarchical\_gain\_load at 864. All three source-data names resolve to real directories under journal/source_data/ (ls confirmed), but the main text never labels them as Source Data directories (762 reads 'SI S4 and \texttt{review_evidence_reanalysis} document...'). The \texttt MICrONS table names (921-927, 1181-1186) are legitimate public dataset identifiers and not part of this problem. (5) Range '778-816': the retrospective score comparison actually spans 778-793; lines 795-816 describe unrelated positive-conductance and same-span experiments. The 778-793 paragraph does carry substantive numbers that back R5 (rho=0.937/0.916, 13/20 vs 8/20 seeds, regret 1.94 vs 2.75 points), so it is not pure change log and should be condensed rather than removed. (6) 'Tool credits': the only such item is the OpenAI Codex statement at main.tex:1165-1168; Nature Portfolio policy requires LLM use to be documented in Methods, so this is a required disclosure, not revision leakage. That part of the finding is refuted.
- **Suggestion:** Replace 'historical' with 'earlier variant (Supplementary Fig. Sx)' or drop; move exclusion chronology and superseded-score comparisons to SI reproducibility; cite SI sections instead of directory names.

#### M027 — Prior dendritic credit models are not placed as special cases of the route dictionary
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:63-67; supplementary.tex:4686-4720 (Table S14)
- **Claim:** The Introduction (main.tex:67) lists segregated-dendrite/apical-teaching and burst-based models but never places them in the route-dictionary taxonomy, even though in this paper's notation they deliver one signal per neuron (the K=1 neuron-specific rung of main.tex:124 and Fig. 2A), and Table S14 (supplementary.tex:4684-4714) contains only the paper's own constructions; Körding & König 2001 is not cited anywhere. Stating "these models occupy the K=1 rung; we ask when intermediate ranks 1<K<N_u are useful" (and optionally adding a prior-models row to Table S14) would sharpen novelty. The Gavish et al. 2010 point is weaker than claimed: the balanced-tree Haar basis used in the main text (main.tex:737) is classical, and Gavish 2010 is already cited where its unbalanced-tree extension is used (SI 3148); a main-text citation to tree-Haar/wavelet literature at line 737 is optional polish, not a novelty issue.
- **Evidence:** main.tex:63 defines dictionary with no citations; Table S14 lists only this paper's constructions; gavish2010wavelets cited once at SI 3148.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.8):** Checked snapshot text with grep/sed. (1) main.tex:63 defines "route dictionary" with no citation (confirmed). main.tex:67 lists urbanczik2014, schiess2016, guerguiev2017segregated, sacramento2018dendritic, payeur2021burst, ellenberger2026gle, richards2019dendritic and then says only "Our question concerns the resolution and geometry of feedback over a whole arbor"; it never states how those models map onto the K taxonomy. main.tex:124 and the Fig. 2 caption (main.tex:160) define K=1 as the all-ones broadcast "one coordinate per neuron repeated over its arbor". Discussion main.tex:423 says the framework "can be combined with" those models, i.e. treats them as complementary, not as special cases. The SI never cites guerguiev/sacramento/payeur/urbanczik at all (grep over supplementary.tex: zero hits), and SI §Local rules (lines 299-493) contains no prior-model mapping. (2) references.bib has no Körding & König 2001 entry (grep for kording/koenig/körding: none), so that origin of apical-feedback two-compartment learning is uncited anywhere. (3) Table S14 = supplementary.tex:4684-4714 (\thetable is S\arabic, first table at line 2206 is S1, so the 14th at 4684 is S14; pdftotext of supplementary.pdf confirms "Table S14: Dictionary constructions..."). Its five rows are all this paper's constructions; no prior-model row (confirmed). (4) gavish2010wavelets appears exactly once, supplementary.tex:3148, in "Irregular-tree wavelet scale analysis" (3139-3185); it is absent from main.tex (confirmed). However, the main-text Haar basis (main.tex:737: "Haar basis of a balanced binary tree", also SI 915-962, 1253, 1686) is the classical balanced-tree Haar construction; Gavish et al. 2010 is the unbalanced/hierarchical-tree generalization and is cited exactly where that generalization is used. So "the Haar basis comes from Gavish 2010" overstates. (5) The reviewer's mapping "apical-compartment teaching = K=1 with subtree support" is only approximately right: in Guerguiev 2017 / Sacramento 2018 / Payeur 2021 the apical compartment or burst fraction carries one scalar per neuron that drives plasticity at basal synapses, which in this manuscript's notation is the K=1 neuron-specific broadcast A_u=1 (main.tex:124), not a subtree-supported profile; a "subtree support" reading only applies if several plastic compartments exist and only one is taught.
- **Suggestion:** At 67: 'apical-compartment teaching models deliver one profile per neuron (K=1 with subtree support) and tree-wavelet bases [gavish2010] supply nested profiles; we ask when intermediate dictionaries are useful.' Add a prior-models row to Table S14.

#### M028 — Schiess et al. 2016 is mislabelled in the Introduction and neither it nor Bicknell & Häusser is cited where the tree gradient is derived
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:67; 84-104 (Eqs. 2-3)
- **Claim:** The Introduction (main.tex:67) does not mislabel Schiess et al. 2016 (the five-mechanism list is not mapped one-to-one onto its seven citation keys), but it omits the mechanism Schiess et al. contribute—error backpropagation through an active dendritic tree—which is the closest precedent to Eq. (3). Eqs. (2)-(3) (main.tex:84-104) and the full Supplementary Section S1 derivation carry no citation to Schiess et al. 2016 or Bicknell & Häusser 2021; the only attribution is in Methods (line 490) and Discussion (line 423). Körding & König 2001, the origin of two-site (basal/apical) teaching, is absent from the bibliography. Suggested fix: add 'error backpropagation in active dendrites' to the line-67 list, cite schiess2016 and bicknell2021 at Eq. (3) (and at the top of S1), and add Körding & König 2001 next to guerguiev2017.
- **Evidence:** Line 67 mechanism list vs keys urbanczik2014, schiess2016, guerguiev2017, sacramento2018, payeur2021, ellenberger2026; lines 84-104 have no \citep.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked main.tex lines 55-70, 80-110, 423, 485-491; supplementary.tex lines 58-299 (Section S1) plus a grep of all \citep/\citet in the supplement; references.bib (68 entries); the six *_methods.tex fragments. (1) 'Mislabelled' is NOT supported by the text: line 67 lists five mechanisms ('somatic prediction, segregated basal and apical teaching, interneuron-mediated errors, burst-dependent plasticity and prospective dynamics') against seven keys (urbanczik2014, schiess2016, guerguiev2017, sacramento2018, payeur2021, ellenberger2026, richards2019), so there is no one-to-one mapping and no reader can infer that schiess2016 = 'segregated teaching'; the reviewer inferred it from key order. Schiess et al. 2016 (bib line 451-459: 'Somato-dendritic synaptic plasticity and error-backpropagation in active dendrites') does also build on the somato-dendritic prediction idea, so grouping it with urbanczik2014 is defensible. What IS true is that the mechanism list omits the contribution Schiess et al. actually make (error backpropagation through an active dendritic tree), which is the closest precedent to Eq. (3). (2) Confirmed: lines 84-104 (Eqs. 2-3) contain no \citep, and Supplementary Section S1 (lines 58-299) cites only horn2013matrix (line 646) and nesterov2004convex (line 762) in the whole theory block; the only attributions of the tree-gradient/adjoint to prior work are Methods line 490 ('This established adjoint construction ... \citep{almeida1987asynchronous,pineda1987recurrent,schiess2016somatodendritic,bicknell2021synaptic}') and Discussion line 423. So the derivation itself is presented in Results and S1 without citing Schiess 2016 or Bicknell & Häusser 2021, though the paper does attribute the adjoint in Methods. (3) Confirmed: no Körding/König entry exists in references.bib, main.tex, supplementary.tex or the methods fragments, despite line 67 naming 'segregated basal and apical teaching' as a mechanism (guerguiev2017 descends from Körding & König 2001).
- **Suggestion:** Reword 67; at Eq. (3) add 'generalizes the dendritic error backpropagation of Schiess et al. 2016 and cable sensitivities of Bicknell & Häusser 2021'; add Körding & König 2001.

#### M029 — The ladder is rank-1 vs rank-10 feedback; 'neuron identity' over-attributes what DFA and derangement show is just feedback rank
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:137, 143, 151-153, 417; Fig 2 title; supplementary.tex:4143
- **Claim:** The MNIST feedback ladder compares a bandwidth-1 scalar with a per-neuron field whose rank is at most the output dimension (10) because the decoder is linear 128x10, and then with the full compartment field; the paper never states this rank, never places the ladder on its own K/r_eff evidence map, and headlines the result as "one signal per neuron" (main.tex:137, 160, 417). Fixed random DFA (readout-DFA 0.07/0.10 pp) and ownership derangement (0.38-0.57 pp of an ~11 pp effect) show that the specific neuron-to-coordinate assignment is nearly irrelevant, so the effect should be described as "a low-dimensional population error field distributed over neurons", not neuron identity per se. However, those controls hold rank fixed at 10 and do not establish that rank is the operative variable, and the intermediate-rank dependence is untested on MNIST; the only related data (CIFAR random rank-4 recovering ~30% of the gap; an archived, lineage-unresolved noise-task ladder at 45/63/76/80/85% for rank 1/2/4/8/exact) suggest a graded rank dependence rather than saturation above rank 1. Recommended fix: state the rank of each rung explicitly, avoid implying 128 independent signals, either add a rank-k random-field ladder on MNIST or say the intermediate-rank question is open, and cite the CIFAR rank-4 result in R2 — but do not adopt the wording "more than one shared coordinate captures most of the gain", which the existing data do not support.
- **Evidence:** local_learning_broadcast.py:66-81 delta.mean(dim=1); local_learning_signals.py:342-358 randn(soma_dim, output_dim). Factorial readout−DFA 0.10/0.07 pp; correct−deranged 0.38-0.57 pp. S4C/Table S19: random rank-4 CIFAR 35.0% vs 29.7% fallback vs 47.2% exact. Code has 'low_rank' mode (config/training.py:1032).
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** STRUCTURAL CLAIM (rank-1 vs rank-<=10) CONFIRMED. Strict scalar: main.tex:556-566 Eq. strictscalar = mean over the 128 somatic errors with largest-magnitude fallback; code src/.../local_learning_broadcast.py:66-81 (delta.mean(dim=1), argmax fallback) — one number per example. Neuron rung: supplementary.tex:1743 states "A linear 128x10 decoder produced the class scores", so the 128 somatic errors are W^T delta_out and lie in a <=10-dim subspace at every step. DFA: local_learning_signals.py:342-358 builds randn(soma_dim=128, output_dim=10) — also rank 10; main.tex:511-513 describes it. low_rank mode exists (config/training.py:1028-1032, broadcast_rank default 4; low_rank mixin projects the soma error through a random 128xk, kxN_comp map that scrambles both neuron and compartment assignment).

NUMBERS RECOMPUTED (all match text): mnist_feedback_ladder/paired_contrasts.csv: neuron-scalar 11.25 (shunting) / 7.81 (additive) pp; exact-neuron 0.04 / 0.19 pp (text line 143: 11.2, 7.8, <0.2). mnist_between_within_factorial/paired_contrasts.csv: DFA neuron-scalar 11.03 / 10.80 pp; readout-DFA at neuron rung 0.071 / 0.101 pp; exact path-neuron under DFA 0.03 / 0.09 pp (text line 151, S30 caption line 4143). Ownership (prospective_input_validity/routing_valid_paired_contrasts.csv): correct-deranged 0.568 / 0.507 pp at 2 stages, 0.380 / 0.385 pp at 4 stages, 10/10 seeds (text line 149). CIFAR S4C (regular_tree_regimes/cifar10_control_ladder_runs.csv, 5 seeds): scalar-fallback 29.67%, random rank-4 34.98%, exact path 47.19%, BP 49.52% (Table S19 line 4909).

WHAT THE PAPER ALREADY SAYS: it does not claim the neuron's OWN coordinate is needed — line 143 says "preserving distinct neuronal coordinates", line 149 explicitly separates identity from ownership and reports the ~0.5 pp derangement cost, line 151 reports DFA parity. So "which neuron receives which coordinate matters little" is already in the text; the reviewer's suggested rewording partly restates it.

WHAT IS MISSING (the valid core): nowhere in main.tex or supplementary.tex is the neuron rung described as a rank-<=10 (output-dimension) field; grep for "rank ten/10-dimensional/output dimension" finds only main.tex:513 (DFA scaling). The paper's own vocabulary treats "profile count, rank ... distinct resources" (main.tex:124) and Fig 3H plots K/r_eff, yet credit_phase_plane/points.csv contains no MNIST-ladder point, so the ladder's rungs are never characterized in that framework. A reader of the R2 title (line 137), Fig 2 title (line 160) and Discussion (line 417) would take "128 per-neuron signals" as the operative level, whereas the data are consistent with "a fixed 10-dim population field, assignment irrelevant".

WHERE THE REVIEWER OVERREACHES: (1) DFA and derangement both hold rank at 10 and only change the map; they show the exact assignment is unnecessary, not that "rank" is the operative variable — that needs the intermediate-rank ladder, which is untested on MNIST. (2) The reviewer's proposed rewording "feedback carrying more than one shared coordinate captures most of the gain" is contradicted by the available (caveated) evidence: CIFAR random rank-4 recovers only (35.0-29.7)/(47.2-29.7)=30% of the fallback-to-exact gap; and the archived noise-task bandwidth ladder (regular_tree_regimes/noise_feedback_ladder_summary.csv, plotted as archived panel H "Feedback bandwidth" in scripts/build_regular_tree_regime_figure.py:366-405, 5 seeds) is graded: rank 1 45.3%, rank 2 63.0%, rank 4 76.4%, rank 8 79.5%, exact 84.7% — excluded from the current supplement because "its shunting execution remains unresolved" (Table S19 footnote, supplementary.tex ~4919). Note low_rank random fields also scramble within-arbor addresses and rescale by 1/sqrt(k), so they are not a clean rank-only manipulation. (3) The strict scalar is a nonlinear (fallback) reduction, not strictly a rank-1 linear functional, though bandwidth-1 is the right description.
- **Suggestion:** Add a rank-k random-feedback ladder on MNIST (k=1,2,4,10; 15 seeds) or reword to 'feedback carrying more than one shared coordinate captures most of the gain; which neuron receives which coordinate matters little'; mention the CIFAR rank-4 result in R2.

#### M030 — 'Fallback' names two different things, and the strict-scalar substitution frequency is promised but never reported
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:139, 145, 556-566, 593; supplementary.tex:414-433
- **Claim:** "Fallback" is used for two different objects: the epsilon-guard inside the strict scalar (largest-magnitude coordinate substituted when |population mean| <= 1e-6; main.tex:139, 566) and the separate matched-width training condition (main.tex:145, 573, 593, 610; SI 414-441; Table S16). Moreover, supplementary.tex:431-433 states the epsilon-substitution count "was measured directly in the same-seed computational rerun", but no value appears in the main text, SI, tables, or source data; the code path (reduce_error_to_scalar) records no count, and the only same-seed rerun (320 exact/BP runs) does not use the scalar reduction at all, so the sentence is unsupported. Either report the rate (e.g. per-epoch fraction of examples in the 15-seed strict-scalar runs) in Methods or Table S16, or delete the claim and say the rate was not recorded; rename one of the two "fallback" uses.
- **Evidence:** 139 vs 145 wording; SI 431-433 'measured directly in the same-seed computational rerun'; grep across .tex, audit.json, strict_scalar_implementation_audit.csv finds no value.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** (1) Two meanings of "fallback", verified in the snapshot text. main.tex:139 "a largest-magnitude fallback when the mean nearly cancelled"; main.tex:566 "When the population mean nearly cancels, the fallback uses the largest-magnitude neuronal error" (the epsilon branch of Eq. strictscalar, lines 556-565). main.tex:145 "The historical matched-width fallback retained neuron identity for a small group of near-somatic parameters"; main.tex:573 "The historical matched-width fallback retained a 128-coordinate somatic vector"; main.tex:593 "The matched-width fallback requires separate interpretation"; main.tex:610 "matched-width fallback and neuron-specific updates were compared". SI supplementary.tex:414-441 uses "matched-width scalar-fallback field" for the 128-wide-stage condition and Table S16 (supplementary.tex:4744-4757, label tab:strict_scalar_audit) uses "Strict minus fallback" for the same condition, while SI 417-431 describes the epsilon branch without the word but the code names it `fallback` (src/dendritic_modeling/training/strategies/local_learning_parts/local_learning_broadcast.py:78). Same word, two distinct objects (an epsilon-guard inside the strict scalar vs a separate training condition). (2) Substitution count promised but never reported. supplementary.tex:431-433: "The replacement count was not recorded in the initial runs and was therefore measured directly in the same-seed computational rerun." Grepped main.tex and supplementary.tex for replacement/substitut/triggered/invoked/cancel/argmax: no numeric value anywhere. Source data: /journal/source_data/mnist_feedback_ladder/strict_scalar_implementation_audit.csv has 23 columns (architecture ... strict_minus_legacy), none a count; strict_scalar_paired_contrasts.csv (13 columns) and audit.json contain no fallback/replacement field. The only "same-seed computational rerun" in the manuscript is the 320-run exact-path-transport vs backpropagation rerun (supplementary.tex:2006-2010, 4618, 4627; main.tex:600), whose two arms never call the scalar reduction, so it could not have measured the epsilon-substitution rate; its summary (/journal/source_data/clean_exact_bp/summary.json line 39 "transport_shape_fallbacks": 0; README.md:33-34) refers to a different quantity (transport-shape fallback), not the |mean|<=1e-6 substitution. The implementation reduce_error_to_scalar (local_learning_broadcast.py:66-81) computes near_zero = |mean|<=eps and returns only the tensor; it neither logs nor counts substitutions, and no caller (local_rule_components.py:418, local_learning_broadcast_mixin.py:44) records the count. So the SI sentence asserts a measurement that has no reported value and no data artifact behind it.
- **Suggestion:** Rename the ε-branch 'largest-coordinate substitute' and the other 'matched-width partial-identity control'; report the substitution rate in Methods or Table S17; if ~zero, say so and drop ε from Eq. (strictscalar).

#### M031 — Line 143 misreports the exact-path rung: reliably positive (0.19 pp) in additive MNIST, reliably negative on CIFAR-10 (−0.86 pp) and Fashion shunting; 'reproduced the ordering' and '<0.2 points' are both wrong
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:143, 155, 160 (Fig 2G); supplementary.tex:1921-1933, 4910, 4918
- **Claim:** Line 143's "flattened CIFAR-10 reproduced it [the ordering]" is inaccurate for the top rung: on CIFAR-10 exact path transport was reliably 0.86 pp below neuron-specific feedback (t95 −1.17 to −0.55; 18/20 seeds; P≈1e-5), i.e. the oracle-level rung underperformed the neuron rung, and neuron-specific feedback also exceeded matched BP (+0.74 pp [0.42,1.06], 17/20) — both stated only in the SI (1924-1926, S4D caption) or not at all (neuron vs BP). The main text should say the neuron-identity effect replicated while exact transport was slightly worse than neuron-specific on CIFAR-10 (and within ±1 pp of BP). The MNIST "<0.2 points" statement is numerically correct (additive +0.19 pp, reliably positive 14/15; shunting +0.05 pp, n.s.) and could be phrased "at most 0.2 points, reliably positive only in additive trees"; no equivalence margin is needed because no equivalence is claimed. The Fashion effects (−0.11/+0.13 pp, sign-flip P=0.10/0.31) are not reliably signed and "reproduced the ordering" is fair there.
- **Evidence:** cifar10 confirmatory paired_contrasts.csv: exact−neuron −0.00859 [−0.0117,−0.0055], 2/20 positive, P=1.1e-5; neuron 50.87% vs BP 50.12%; exact−BP −0.00115 within ±1 pp TOST. fashion shunting −0.107 pp [−0.213,−0.006], additive +0.13. mnist additive +0.186 pp [0.117,0.256], 14/15, P=3.1e-4; shunting +0.045 [−0.018,0.102]. SI 1924-1928: 'every prespecified exact-path superiority criterion failed'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** main.tex:143 ("Exact path transport then changed accuracy by less than 0.2 points in either architecture ... Fashion-MNIST reproduced the ordering ... flattened CIFAR-10 reproduced it against a strict scalar; exact transport was equivalent to matched backpropagation within the predefined CIFAR-10 margin"); main.tex:149, 1142-1143 (equivalence policy); supplementary.tex:1880-1885 (Fashion), 1917-1930 (CIFAR: exact−neuron −0.00859, 2/20, "every prespecified exact-path superiority criterion failed"), 2915 (S4D caption: "0.859 points lower"), 4910 (table: neuron 50.87 vs BP 50.12). Recomputed from seed_outcomes.csv: CIFAR exact−neuron −0.859 pp, t95 [−1.171,−0.547], 2/20 positive, paired-t P=1.5e-5, sign-flip P<1e-4; CIFAR neuron−BP +0.743 pp [0.423,1.064], 17/20, P=1.1e-4 (not reported or tested anywhere in main or SI); CIFAR exact−BP −0.115 pp [−0.457,0.226]. Fashion shunting exact−neuron −0.107 pp, bootstrap [−0.213,−0.005] but t95 [−0.233,0.019], sign-flip P=0.096; additive +0.132 pp, P=0.31 — not reliably signed, so reviewer's "reliably negative" is overstated and "reproduced the ordering" is fair for Fashion. MNIST additive exact−neuron +0.186 pp [0.115,0.257], 14/15, P=3e-4; shunting +0.045 pp, P=0.19 — the "<0.2 points" point-estimate statement is numerically true and is a magnitude bound, not an equivalence claim, so no margin test is required; Fig 2G (figure_02.pdf, "path resolution (B)" strip) shows these contrasts. paired_contrasts.csv values match the SI text exactly.
- **Suggestion:** Rewrite 143: exact transport changed accuracy by at most 0.2 pp (reliably positive only in additive trees, 0.12-0.26), was neutral/slightly negative on Fashion, and reliably lower than neuron-specific on CIFAR-10 (−0.9 pp, 2/20) while within ±1 pp of matched BP, which neuron-specific slightly exceeded; replace 'reproduced the ordering' with 'reproduced the neuron-identity effect'; test the MNIST rung against the ±0.5 pp margin used elsewhere.

#### M032 — R2's headline is not related to Bicknell & Häusser's finding that a global-error local rule nearly matches the exact gradient
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:137-158, 423
- **Claim:** R2 (main.tex:137-158) reports that one neuron-level coordinate with local eligibility captures nearly all of the exact-transport benefit (exact adds <0.2 pp) without noting that Bicknell & Häusser 2021 reached the same conclusion for a single reconstructed neuron; the Introduction (line 67) gives only a question-level distinction, so the result-level delta (layer-shared scalar rung, network/DFA replication, ownership contrast, error-field decomposition) should be stated in R2 or at Discussion line 417.
- **Evidence:** bicknell2021synaptic cited at 67, 490, 423, never in R2.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Citation facts verified by grep on the snapshot: bicknell2021synaptic appears in main.tex only at lines 67 (Introduction), 423 (Discussion) and 490 (Methods, adjoint reference); it does not appear anywhere in supplementary/supplementary.tex or the six *_methods.tex fragments, nor in R2 (main.tex:137-158). R2 line 143 reports the headline: neuron-specific feedback adds 11.2/7.8 pp over the strict scalar, and exact path transport then changes accuracy by <0.2 pp (15 paired seeds); line 155 repeats this under DFA (<=0.09 pp). This is structurally the same conclusion Bicknell & Häusser 2021 (Neuron 109:4001, checked bib entry references.bib:334-343) drew for a single reconstructed L5 neuron: replacing the exact per-synapse somatic-impact gradient with one neuron-level error times a local voltage-dependent factor loses little performance. So the reviewer's premise is correct and R2 does not mention it. However, the claim that the delta 'is not stated as a delta' is overstated: main.tex:67 already writes 'Detailed cable-learning models use synaptic sensitivities and local approximations to optimize a reconstructed neuron's input--output function [bicknell2021synaptic]. Our question concerns the resolution and geometry of feedback over a whole arbor...', which is a question-level delta placed in the Introduction. What is missing is the result-level statement in R2 (and Discussion line 417 'One signal per neuron recovers most image-task performance') that the K=1 ~ exact finding reproduces the single-neuron result in a network setting and that the new content is the layer-scalar rung, the DFA replication (S30), the ownership contrast (Fig 2F-G) and the spatial error-field decomposition (Fig 2D-E). The authors' own novelty audit (analysis/DEEP_LITERATURE_NOVELTY_AUDIT_2026-08-23.md:166-169) recommended stating this delta explicitly near the result; the Sept-5 review (COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md:76) flagged the same point, and only the Introduction sentence was added.
- **Suggestion:** After 143 add: 'This agrees with the single-neuron finding that a local approximation driven by one neuronal error recovers most of the exact gradient [bicknell2021]; the network ladder additionally separates that coordinate from a layer-shared scalar and from ownership.'
