#### M034 — Panel 2C's 'matched-width fallback' rung is, on every plotted tensor, the strict scalar
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:147, 160, 608-613; supplementary.tex:439-446; Fig. 2C
- **Claim:** Because the diagnostic excludes the 384 soma-layer couplings (soma mask) and the 256 somatic reactivation parameters (component filter), and every retained tensor is driven only by its own stage's error, the 'matched-width fallback' field in Fig. 2C is identical to the strict scalar field on all plotted tensors; the C rung is therefore the strict-scalar cosine under a different name. The B/C tick mismatch ('strict scalar' vs 'matched-width fallback') and the '---' strict-scalar cosine entries in the SI table invite a redundant request for a strict-scalar diagnostic. Relabel the C tick/caption (e.g. 'scalar (= strict scalar on plotted tensors)') and fill or footnote the SI table entries.
- **Evidence:** SI 439-443 (2,408,448 feedforward parameters strict; exception 384+256); main 611-613 nonsomatic tensors, soma coupling excluded; broadcast_mixin.py 185-190, 240 expand delta_scalar in both modes. Unverified whether the 256 somatic gains are excluded by collect_feedback_gradient_rerun.py.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Code path (live repo, read-only). Panel 2C data come from source_data/figure2/feedback_gradient_runs.csv, whose conditions are produced by neurips/scripts/measure_layer_soma_factorial.py: 'scalar_fallback' = approx_direct_code_per_soma, 'neuron_wise' = approx_direct_blockwise_per_soma (collect_feedback_gradient_rerun.py:33-36). _code_per_soma_tree (measure_layer_soma_factorial.py:490-503) supplies the 128-wide somatic seed only at the record whose v_n width equals the seed width (the soma stage); every other stage receives helper._reduce_error_to_scalar(seed).expand(...), i.e. the same robust scalar reduction the trained 'scalar' broadcast mode uses (local_learning_broadcast_mixin.py:183-190 and the else-branch at 240: delta_scalar.expand). _alignment_rows (674-712) drops components decoder/other/reactivation, so the somatic gain/threshold parameters (named ...reactivation.* via branch_layer.py:688) never enter the rows; _branch_level_summary (822-880) masks branch_layer_index==max_stage & component=='dendritic_conductance', i.e. the 384 branches_to_output.log_weight couplings of the soma layer. Each layer's local gradient consumes only that layer's e_n (_apply_local_grads_from_errors 570-660; the coupling gradient in local_learning_dendritic_block_mixin.py:130-168 uses e_v from the same record plus children voltages x_blk). Arithmetic: 784x(1152+384)x2 = 2,408,448 equals SI:441's synapse count, so the soma stage has no pre_w synapses. Therefore on every tensor that enters branch_numel_weighted_cosine (2,408,448 synapses + 1,152 terminal-to-proximal couplings) the fallback field equals the strict scalar field, and the plotted 'matched-width fallback' cosines (0.012+/-0.059 shunting, -0.001+/-0.047 additive; per_soma_shared rows, recomputed from the CSV) are exactly the strict-scalar cosines. Text: main.tex:147 and caption at 160 call the C rung 'matched-width scalar-fallback'; ALIGNMENT_LADDER_TICKS (build_main_figure_02.py:141-143) prints 'matched-width fallback' in C while B prints 'strict scalar' (verified in figure_02.pdf); Methods 608-613 disclose the soma-coupling exclusion but not that the two scalar fields coincide on the retained tensors; SI Table at supplementary.tex:4728 and 4732 prints '---' for the strict-scalar gradient cosine, implying it was not measured. Not verified empirically: the per-parameter details CSV (layer_soma_factorial_details.csv / branch_gradient_checkpoint_summary.csv) is not present under journal/analysis, so the conclusion rests on the code path, not on a re-run.
- **Suggestion:** Relabel the C tick 'scalar (identical to strict scalar on plotted tensors)' with a caption clause; if any somatic tensor is included, drop it or run the strict-scalar diagnostic at the same checkpoints.

#### M039 — No explanation why the one-step reliability advantage vanishes over 40 steps; an undisclosed exploratory dose pilot exists
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:190, 421, 796-806; supplementary.tex:1605-1625, 1685-1700; Tables S4-S5; source_data/conservative_adaptive_shunting_pilot
- **Claim:** The one-step reliability-aligned gain (+0.0013, 33/50, P=0.003) and the null final-loss contrast (+0.0004, P=0.154), plus the adaptive-local final-loss deficit (+0.0043, P=1.8e-5), are all correctly reported (Tables S4-S5, Fig S13 D,F — not S24C), but neither main.tex 190/421 nor SI S4 (supplementary.tex 1614-1620, 1676-1680) offers any explanation for why a one-step advantage does not persist over 40 steps, even though the SI's own finite-horizon bias-variance risk (supplementary.tex 1051-1110) is the natural tool and is applied only to the same-span experiment. Separately, a prespecified 20-seed exploratory attenuation-dose scan (source_data/conservative_adaptive_shunting_pilot; best dose 0.1: +0.000154 loss vs no shunt, 10/20 improved; failed its own 15/20 & 0.001 gate) ships in Source Data with no mention in the text or provenance manifest. It is a negative result consistent with the manuscript's conclusion, and its 27 Aug protocol barred manuscript use, so the omission is not misleading; but it is inconsistent with the disclosure of the other excluded exploratory implementation at supplementary.tex 1595 and should be stated in one sentence in SI S4 (with the gate) so that the shipped directory has a textual anchor. The reviewer's "~S_b/N_b steps" crossover rule is an unverified heuristic and should not be adopted as stated; the authors should derive the horizon dependence from their own Eq. s_coefficient_finite_risk-style analysis.
- **Evidence:** Table S4 rows 2,6; S5 row 4; Fig S24C; pilot summary.json fields best_positive_dose 0.1, seeds_improved 10, minimum_improved_pairs 15.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** (1) Numbers: recomputed from /n/holylabs/.../journal/source_data/positive_conductance_reliability_step_consistent/seed_outcomes.csv at heterogeneity 2 (50 seeds): reliability_aligned_shunt minus noisy_no_shunt one-step decrease = 0.001339, 33/50 positive, Wilcoxon P=0.0030; final test loss +0.000425, 33/50, P=0.154; aligned minus best_global one-step 0.001817, 49/50, P=3.6e-15. These match supplementary.tex 1607-1616 and Table S4 (supplementary.tex 4409-4427) exactly. Table S5 row 4 (supplementary.tex 4437-4450; text 1670-1674): adaptive local minus no shunt final loss +0.004250, 11/50, P=1.78e-5 — text and table agree. (2) Horizon argument: main.tex 190 ("Positive shunts reproduced relative gain-ordering effects but did not improve final loss over the unshunted noisy rule"), main.tex 421 and supplementary.tex 1614-1620/1676-1680 state the null without any mechanistic or horizon explanation; grep for horizon/sustain/stale/drift in the S4 shunt subsections (supplementary.tex 1533-1680) finds none. However, the SI already contains the needed machinery: the finite-time bias-variance risk (Eq. s_coefficient_finite_risk, supplementary.tex 1051-1110, "finite-horizon regularization effect") — it is applied only to the same-span coefficient experiment, never to the shunt experiment. The reviewer's specific "~S_b/N_b steps" crossover formula is the reviewer's own heuristic; I did not verify it and no trajectory data exist in seed_outcomes.csv to test it. (3) Pilot: /journal/source_data/conservative_adaptive_shunting_pilot/{summary.json,dose_summary.csv,seed_outcomes.csv} (2,520 rows, 20 seeds 8300-8319, doses 0,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1). Recomputed at h=2: adaptive_local_shunt minus noisy_no_shunt final loss = +0.000154 (dose 0.1, 10/20 improved), +0.000418 (0.25, 11/20), +0.000902, +0.001512, +0.003161 (dose 1, 3/20); identical to dose_summary.csv and summary.json (best_positive_dose 0.1, seeds_improved 10, decision rule 15/20 and 0.001, eligible_for_fresh_seed_confirmation false). The pilot protocol /journal/analysis/CONSERVATIVE_ADAPTIVE_SHUNTING_PILOT_20260827.md was written 27 Aug 2026 before inspection and states "no result enters the manuscript without a separately frozen fresh-seed run". grep -i conservative over main.tex and supplementary.tex returns no reference to it; it is absent from source_data/provenance_manifest.tsv (1,574 entries), inferential_units.csv and README.md, yet the directory ships inside source_data. By contrast the other excluded exploratory implementation IS disclosed at supplementary.tex 1595-1597. Minor: the finding cites "Fig S24C"; the positive-conductance figure is S13 (supplementary.tex 4024, figure_S13_panels_A-F.pdf; the relevant panels are S13 D,F), main.tex 190 cites S13/S14/S24.
- **Suggestion:** Add the crossover-horizon argument to SI S4 with a clause at 190; disclose the negative dose pilot and its decision rule.

#### M040 — Fig 3G depends on a task introduced two sections later, and its correlation is immediately undercut
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** structure
- **Location:** main.tex:192-195; Fig 3G; R5 239-261
- **Claim:** Fig 3G (main.tex:192-194, caption 201) reports a 540-fit reanalysis of the eight-context task that is only defined two subsections later (R5, main.tex:239-243) and is never cited from R5, whose Fig 5 caption instead points to Supplementary Fig. S16D (the same analysis). The follow-up sentence at line 194 ("correlations mainly reflected the benefit of larger bandwidth: always choosing K=8 matched the score's selection success"; Methods line 789, verified 95/100 for both) conflates argmax-selection parity with the pooled correlation: budget K alone gives rho=0.76/0.80 vs utility's 0.92/0.95, and within-budget rho(U,P1) is 0.86-0.90 at K=1,2,4, so utility is not mainly bandwidth. Fix by either moving the reanalysis to the end of R5 (one sentence plus S16D/Fig 3G) or keeping 3G in R3 with a self-contained one-line description of the task, and rewriting line 194 to say that utility does not identify an interior accuracy optimum (selection parity) rather than that its correlation is mainly bandwidth.
- **Evidence:** Line 192; Fig 5 caption points back to S16; Methods 824-826: score selected K=8 in 95/100 curves, same as always choosing it.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.8):** Structural facts confirmed in the snapshot: main.tex:192 opens "We tested this account against the eight-context routing task introduced below, using 540 existing fits" while the task is defined only in R5 (main.tex:239-243), with R4 (205-237) in between; the Fig 3 caption (main.tex:201, panel G) forward-references Fig.~\ref{fig:subtreefactorial} (Fig 5). main.tex:194 then states "The correlations mainly reflected the benefit of larger bandwidth: always choosing K=8 matched the score's selection success." R5 body (239-257) never cites Fig 3G; the Fig 5 caption (main.tex:259) points to "Supplementary Fig.~S16" (supplementary.tex:4044-4047, panel D = the same 540-fit reanalysis). The "95/100 curves = always choosing K=8" statement is at Methods main.tex:789 (the reviewer's "824-826" is a mis-citation; 817-834 is the interaction-structure Methods). I recomputed from source_data/review_evidence_reanalysis/: pooled_correlations.csv gives deterministic utility vs heldout accuracy rho=0.9242 (0.9108-0.9401) and vs P1 rho=0.9509 (0.9486-0.9542), matching lines 192/201; within_family_prediction_summary.csv gives match counts 19+20+20+16+20 = 95/100 for both deterministic_maximum_bound_decrease and budget_k on heldout_accuracy, confirming line 789. moment_scores.csv (540 dendritic_tree rows, 214 with utility<=0, matching the panel-G annotation). Overstatement check: the reviewer's "neither motivates nor is motivated" is partly subjective, and panel G does serve R3's stated aim (testing Eq. 7 against trained fits). However, line 194's justification is a non sequitur: selection-success parity (an argmax-within-family statistic) does not show the pooled correlation is "mainly bandwidth". Recomputed: budget K alone gives rho=0.758 (acc) / 0.797 (P1) vs utility's 0.924/0.951; among the 326 positive-utility fits, within-budget rho(U,P1) = 0.860 (K=1, n=37), 0.897 (K=2, n=49), 0.861 (K=4, n=100) and rho(U,acc) = 0.31/0.72/0.84; at K=8 P1 is constant (=1) and rho(U,acc)=-0.24. So utility carries substantial information beyond K, and the self-undercutting sentence undersells the panel while the forward reference leaves it without a home.
- **Suggestion:** Move the reanalysis to the end of R5 as one sentence with a Fig 5 panel, or drop 3G to SI (S16 exists); keep Eqs 6-7 as scaffold.

#### M041 — 'Utility predicts the K=4 contrast' rests on a constant seed-level prediction and a post hoc score revision; the shipped interior_optimum directory contradicts the text
- **Status:** partially confirmed

Remaining fixes: say explicitly that 13/20 equals the constant-prediction base rate (or drop it as an agreement statistic), state that the earlier released score selected K=8, and regenerate or mark superseded the interior_optimum factorial files and the builder docstring.
- **Evidence:** ancestry_contrast_optimum_predictions.csv predicted_k {4:20}, observed_best_k=4 in 13. interior_optimum/report.md 'observed 4, predicted 8; 6/20'; factorial_contrast_summary.csv K=4 −0.1414. build_interior_optimum_figure.py 147-160 reads panel D from review_evidence_reanalysis (+0.2625, 20/20). SI 1324-1327 acknowledges the curvature change.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Data checks (live repo, journal/source_data): (1) review_evidence_reanalysis/ancestry_contrast_optimum_predictions.csv, recomputed per criterion: deterministic_maximum_bound_decrease predicted_k = {4: 20}, observed_best_k = {4: 13, 2: 4, 8: 3}, argmax_match 13/20, mean regret 1.936 pp; hypothetical_minibatch64 predicted_k = {8: 13, 4: 5, 2: 1, 1: 1}, match 8/20, regret 2.749 pp. So 13/20 is exactly the number of seeds whose observed best K is 4, i.e. the base rate of a constant prediction; the manuscript's numbers (13/20, 8/20, 1.94, 2.75) are reproduced. ancestry_utility_contrast_summary.csv: deterministic K=4 mean +0.2625 (CI 0.196-0.328, 20/20 positive), K=1 -0.586, K=2 -0.731, K=8 0; minibatch K=4 -0.100 (5/20 positive). Figure S16 panel D (figures/supplementary/figure_S16_panels_A-D.pdf) shows these values; build_interior_optimum_figure.py lines 147-150 read panel D from review_evidence_reanalysis, while lines 4-5 and 44 still declare source_data/interior_optimum as the data source. (2) source_data/interior_optimum/ (dated Aug 12 22:44) still ships report.md and summary.json stating group-level 'observed_peak_k 4, predicted_peak_k 8', per-seed agreement 6/20, K=4 mean_utility_contrast -0.1414 (with learned_rank_k_upper_bound/PCA oracle in the control set) and -0.1007 non-oracle; factorial_peak_seed.csv predicts 8 in 19/20 seeds. Nothing marks it superseded. (3) Text: main.tex:256 'Utility predicts the mean K=4 contrast retrospectively (Supplementary Fig. S16)'; main.tex:790 'selected K=4 as the contrast maximum in every seed'; main.tex:795 'matched the realized maximum in 13/20 seeds ... leaving seed-level and prospective prediction unresolved'; supplementary.tex:891-897 'contrast-maximizing budget was K=4 in all 20 seeds, matching ... in 13 of 20 ... reduced optimum agreement to 8 of 20'; supplementary.tex:1324-1327 discloses the curvature-scale change ('The original released score used the largest logistic Hessian eigenvalue ... The corrected analysis uses the global logistic bound'); supplementary.tex:1318-1322 explains why minibatch variance is disregarded; review_evidence_reanalysis/README.md discloses the PCA-for-derangement comparator substitution of the historical analysis. S16 caption (supplementary.tex:4045) cites both analyze_interior_optimum.py and analyze_review_evidence.py, which is correct because panels A-C (depth phase, 135/200, unchanged) do come from interior_optimum. What is NOT stated anywhere in main or SI: that a constant K=4 prediction makes 13/20 the trivial base rate, and that the originally released factorial score selected K=8 at the group level with 6/20 agreement.

#### M042 — Fig 3H's factorial 'alignment' is span capture of the exact gradient, not alignment of the delivered update; K=8 marker offset undisclosed
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** main.tex:201 (Fig 3H); supplementary.tex ~1395-1400; source_data/credit_phase_plane/points.csv
- **Claim:** Fig 3H's factorial x-coordinates (0.13, 0.27, 0.52, 1.00) are exact-gradient energy capture of the ancestry span, as SI S4 (supplementary.tex:1512-1514) states; this is a consistent span-based measure across families, not a quantity panel G 'rejects'. However, the same 540-fit reanalysis shows the ancestry update actually delivered at K=1 and K=2 is anti-aligned or orthogonal to the gradient (mean delivered cosine -0.951 and -0.030; retained inner product -2.13 and -0.011; utility 0 in all K=1 and most K=2 ancestry fits), and neither the main text nor the SI says so, so readers will misread the low-K losses in panel H as 'weak positive alignment' rather than 'ascent direction'. The caption should state that low-K ancestry losses coincide with nonpositive delivered alignment (or plot a second, delivered-update coordinate). Separately, the K=8 factorial marker (0.94) and credit-reversal marker (1.06) are fanned ±0.06 from their true x=1.0 by the source table and rendered so in figure_03 H with an unexplained dashed rule at x=1; the caption should say the three x=1 markers are fanned and that the dashed line marks alignment 1.
- **Evidence:** moment_scores.csv K=1 capture 0.133, retained inner product −2.10; points.csv x_plot offsets; build_credit_phase_plane_figure.py line 500.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Numbers recomputed from source_data/review_evidence_reanalysis/moment_scores.csv (2700 rows; dendritic_tree x correct_ancestry_subtrees, 20 seeds per K): mean initial_gradient_capture = 0.1313/0.2652/0.5229/1.000 for K=1/2/4/8 (matches points.csv x and condition_summary.csv mean_initial_gradient_capture); mean retained_signal_inner_product = -2.127/-0.011/+1.266/+2.105; delivered-update cosine (inner/(||Mg||·||g||), ||g||^2 = inner/retained_signal_fraction) = -0.951/-0.030/+0.976/+1.000 (reviewer's numbers confirmed); deterministic_maximum_bound_decrease = 0 for all K=1 ancestry fits and for 60 of the 100 K=2 ancestry fits across the five implementations; 214 of the 540 dendritic_tree fits have zero utility (caption G's 214/540 confirmed). Also: learned_rank_k_upper_bound at K=1 has capture 0.133 but accuracy 0.563 vs ancestry 0.187 at capture 0.131, so span capture alone does not separate these outcomes; the operator sign does. Dodge confirmed: points.csv x=1.0 -> x_plot=0.94 (factorial K=8) and 1.06 (credit reversal K=2); build_credit_phase_plane_figure.py lines 494-503 define dodge ±0.060; the rendered main figure_03 panel H is built by scripts/build_main_figure_03.py lines 614-623, which plots x_plot and adds an axvline at 1.0 with the code comment 'Three observations sit at alignment exactly 1 and are drawn fanned in x ... the rule marks where alignment 1 really is'; the 'routing required' label is anchored at xy=(1.06, ...) (line 645). Manuscript text: main.tex:201 caption H says only 'Horizontal coordinates are family-specific alignment measures' and never mentions the fan or the dashed rule; supplementary.tex:1495-1531 ('Coordinates of the conceptual evidence map') explicitly defines the factorial x as 'mean initial exact-gradient capture of the correct-ancestry span' but also says nothing about the ±0.06 display offset; grep for dodge/coincide/offset/x_plot in main.tex and supplementary.tex returns nothing relevant. Nature Source Data export submission/nature_source_data/Figure_3/Fig3h_phase_plane_points.csv carries both x and x_plot columns, so the offset is disclosed in data but not in text. Refuted part: the claim that H 'uses a quantity panel G rejects' overstates — H's x-axis is consistently a span-energy fraction across families (capture, imposed ρ, imposed a), the same f_sig-type quantity used in panels C–E, and SI S4 states this definition; G is a separate delivered-update utility analysis, not a rejection of capture. Nowhere in main.tex or supplementary.tex, however, is it stated that the K=1 and K=2 ancestry updates actually delivered are ascent/orthogonal directions (negative or ~zero inner product with the gradient), so a reader of panel H sees K=1 at 'alignment 0.13' and cannot tell that the operator delivered an anti-aligned update.
- **Suggestion:** Plot the factorial family at delivered-update cosine or state the quantity and sign discrepancy in the caption; mention the ±0.06 offsets.

#### M043 — Adjacent diverging heatmaps in Fig 3 use red for 'good' in C and red for 'bad' in E
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** figure
- **Location:** figure_03.pdf C, E; main.tex:201
- **Claim:** Fig. 3 panels C and E share the same blue–white–red diverging colormap, but positive/red means "routing advantage" (favorable) in C and "projection worse" (unfavorable, per the caption's "Negative values favor projection") in E, so a reader scanning the two stacked heatmaps must invert the color reading between them. Panel E directly below: colorbar "Δ loss (×10⁻²)" −45…45 with the identical blue-white-red map; red cells at f_noise=1.00 (38, 25, 13, 5) and blue at f_sig=1.00/f_noise=0.10 (−45). main.tex:201 caption E: "Analytic projected-minus-unprojected one-step loss ... Negative values favor projection" — so red in E = projection worse (unfavorable). #### M044 — Fig 3 caption says D/F 'follow the analytic construction' but they are realized 50-seed 95% intervals; F omits the unshunted reference
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** figure
- **Location:** main.tex:201; figure_03.pdf D, F; Methods 749-753
- **Claim:** The Fig 3 caption (main.tex:201) states that "lines/ribbons in D,F follow the specified analytic construction", but both panels plot realized 50-seed means with 95% percentile-bootstrap ribbons (20,000 resamples; build_main_figure_03.py 466-476 and 533-550 on ci95 columns from run_credit_phase_theory_experiment.py bootstrap()); only panel E is analytic. The interval type is stated in Supplementary S4 but not in the main caption. Panel F omits the "no attenuation" arm that the SI Methods lists as compared (unit gains; expected guaranteed decrease exactly 0 under the sum-S = sum-N = 8 normalization; realized mean 0.49 at h=2, 95% CI -0.04 to 0.98). The main text's "unshunted noisy rule" comparison refers to the separate positive-conductance experiment, not Fig 3F, so the omission is a completeness gap rather than an unsupported textual claim. Fix: reword the caption to "Ribbons in D,F: 95% bootstrap intervals over 50 paired seeds (20,000 resamples); E is analytic", and either draw the no-attenuation series in F or state that unit gains have zero expected decrease under the normalization.
- **Evidence:** build_main_figure_03.py 473-476, 519-550 fill_between ci95 columns (n_seeds=50); reliability_phase_summary.csv h=2 no_shunting 0.488 (CI −0.04-0.98), guarantee 0; run_credit_phase_theory_experiment.py 285-290 normalization.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.88):** Caption, main.tex:201 (last sentences): "Panels C,D,F: 50 paired seeds; lines/ribbons in D,F follow the specified analytic construction." Checked against the builder and data: scripts/build_main_figure_03.py lines 466-476 (panel D) plot depth_training_summary.csv mean_final_population_loss with fill_between(ci95_low/high_final_population_loss); lines 533-550 (panel F) plot reliability_phase_summary.csv mean_population_loss_decrease with fill_between(ci95_low/high_population_loss_decrease). Those ci95 columns are produced by run_credit_phase_theory_experiment.py bootstrap() lines 388-393: percentile [0.025,0.975] of resampled seed means (20,000 draws per configs/credit_phase_theory/confirmatory.json bootstrap_draws=20000). So D and F ribbons are realized 50-seed percentile-bootstrap intervals, not an analytic construction; the CSV does carry a separate analytic column (mean_expected_guaranteed_decrease) that F does not draw. Recomputed from reliability_phase_seed.csv, no_shunting at h=2: n=50, mean decrease 0.4877, bootstrap 95% [-0.047, 0.973] (CSV: 0.4877, -0.0419..0.9831), guarantee exactly 0. Zero guarantee follows from lines 286-289 (signal and noise_energy each renormalised to sum to 8) and lines 307-312: guaranteed = sum(a*S - 0.5 a^2 (S+N)) with a=1 gives 8 - 0.5*16 = 0. reliability_phase_summary.csv contains six methods (anti_aligned_shunting, best_global_gain, explicit_point_gate, no_shunting, reliability_aligned, shuffled_shunting); figure_03.pdf panel F legend shows only four (SNR-aligned, best global, shuffled, anti-aligned). Points where the finding overstates: (1) the interval type is not "undefined" in the manuscript, only in the main caption; supplementary.tex S4 (line ~1303) states "Intervals used 20,000 resamples of the 50 paired seeds"; (2) the main text does not compare Fig 3F against a no-shunting arm: main.tex:191 compares matched gains with "a common gain" (best global, which is drawn), and the sentence "did not improve final loss over the unshunted noisy rule" refers to the positive-conductance experiment (supplementary.tex figure caption at line 4024, panels C-F), not Fig 3F. The unshunted arm of the Fig 3F screen is however listed as compared in the SI Methods (supplementary.tex ~1301: "We compared no attenuation, the best common gain, ...") and is absent from the panel; the explicit_point_gate arm is also undrawn but is stated in the caption to coincide with SNR-aligned (confirmed: identical means in CSV).
- **Suggestion:** Caption: 'Ribbons in D,F: 95% [bootstrap/t] intervals over 50 paired seeds'; keep 'analytic' only for E; add the no-shunting series or state its zero expected decrease and the normalization.

#### M045 — Branch-specific learning-rule precedents (Legenstein & Maass 2011; Moldwin et al. 2021) missing
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:205-238, 73-116
- **Claim:** R4 (main.tex:205-238) presents branch-selective gating of plasticity ("branch-local eligibility gate", lines 224, 232, 235) without citing any prior branch-specific plasticity model; Legenstein & Maass 2011 (branch-strength potentiation) is closely related and uncited (Legenstein appears only as a bellec2020eprop coauthor), and the single-neuron gradient-rule literature at main.tex:67 and 423 cites only Bicknell & Häusser 2021, omitting Moldwin, Kalmenson & Segev 2021 (gradient clusteron). Both should be cited, with a one-clause statement of how R4's context-supplied selector differs from activity-gated branch plasticity.
- **Evidence:** 'Legenstein' only as bellec2020eprop coauthor; 'Moldwin', 'clusteron' 0 hits.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked the snapshot text and bibliography directly. (1) grep -i for "legenstein", "moldwin", "clusteron", "kastellakis", "kording" over main.tex, supplementary/supplementary.tex and references.bib: the only hit is references.bib:504, where Legenstein appears as a coauthor of bellec2020eprop; Moldwin/clusteron have zero hits anywhere, including the analysis/*.md revision logs. references.bib contains 68 entries in total. (2) Read main.tex:205-238 (R4) and 73-116 (R1): R4 contains no citations at all; it states at line 224 "The selector can be delivered in feedback or supplied by a branch-local eligibility gate; the resulting update is identical" and at line 232 "A dendritic branch is one possible site for this selection", and Fig 4 caption (235) repeats "A local eligibility gate gives the same update" — i.e. the paper explicitly frames branch-local gating of plasticity as an implementation, which is what Legenstein & Maass 2011 (branch-strength potentiation, J Neurosci) modeled. (3) The related-work sentences are main.tex:67 (cites urbanczik2014, schiess2016, guerguiev2017, sacramento2018, payeur2021, ellenberger2026, richards2019, then "Detailed cable-learning models ... \citep{bicknell2021synaptic}") and main.tex:423 (same set plus chavlis2025, sezener2021, iyer2022, lv2025); single-neuron gradient rules are represented only by bicknell2021synaptic. Moldwin, Kalmenson & Segev 2021 (gradient clusteron, PLoS Comput Biol) is the other standard single-neuron gradient-descent-with-dendritic-nonlinearities reference and is absent. (4) Caveat on the reviewer's wording: Legenstein & Maass gate plasticity by branch-local activity (NMDA-spike-like nonlinearity), whereas R4's selector is an externally supplied context; so it is closely related prior work on branch-gated plasticity rather than a "direct precedent" of the context-gated conflict experiment. The factual core (neither work cited; no citations in R4 at all) is fully confirmed.
- **Suggestion:** Cite Legenstein & Maass at 207 stating the delta (gate in feedback or eligibility); cite Moldwin 2021 with Bicknell & Häusser as single-neuron gradient rules.

#### M048 — The deranged control fails at every conflict dose, not 'at high conflict', and for a different reason
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** main.tex:228; Fig 4E
- **Claim:** The cyclic-derangement control in Fig. 4 is 25.1, 23.3 and 12.0 pp below correct selection at chi=0 for B=2, 4, 8 (means 0.549/0.563/0.674 vs 0.800/0.796/0.794; all 20 seeds per B), where no distractor inputs exist and the authors' own mean-field prediction assigns it full relative signal (1.0) with an initial gradient cosine of 0.98-0.99. Its failure at chi=0 is therefore a training-dynamics failure of misrouted credit (test loss 11.0 at B=2 vs 0.42 for correct), not a distractor effect; the deficit then grows to ~57 pp at chi=1. main.tex:228 ("failed at high conflict"), placed after "At zero conflict, selected and shared rules achieved similar accuracy", and panel E's chi=1-only slice with no deranged series in panel D, lead readers to infer the control is harmless without conflict. State that derangement fails at every dose and why; add the deranged curve to panel D or a chi=0 column to panel E.
- **Evidence:** path_necessity_fashion/seed_outcomes.csv within_neuron_deranged: B=2 0.549→0.224, B=4 0.563→0.224, B=8 0.674→0.225 across χ; all 20 B=2 seeds at χ=0 in 0.53-0.57.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** TEXT: main.tex:227-228 reads "At zero conflict, selected and shared rules achieved similar accuracy ... A cyclic reassignment sent every signal to the wrong branch at matched rank and sparsity and failed at high conflict." Nothing in main.tex (lines 205-236, Methods 638-655) or the supplement Fashion-MNIST section (supplementary.tex:2080-2180; prespecified criterion at 2100 is only "correct routing above derangement", no dose) reports the deranged rule at chi=0. DATA (journal/source_data/path_necessity_fashion/condition_summary.csv, recomputed from seed_outcomes.csv, n=20 each): within_neuron_deranged mean test accuracy at chi=0 is 0.549 (B=2), 0.563 (B=4), 0.674 (B=8) vs correct_path 0.800/0.796/0.794, i.e. deficits of 25.1, 23.3 and 12.0 pp (NOT 25-30 pp as the finding states; B=8 is 12 pp). At chi=1 the deranged means are 0.224/0.224/0.225 (deficits 57.6/57.3/56.8 pp). Per-seed B=2 chi=0 range is 0.534-0.567 (reviewer's "0.53-0.57" holds); B=4 0.530-0.607; B=8 0.601-0.750; every seed at every B is far below correct selection at chi=0. MECHANISM: at chi=0 all branches receive the identical image, so no distractor exists; the authors' own mean-field column theoretical_deranged_relative_signal = 1 at chi=0 and the measured initial gradient cosine is 0.991/0.987/0.983, yet trained test loss is 11.0/3.65/0.81 (correct: 0.42/0.43/0.45) and train accuracy 0.545/0.559/0.673 - the update is well aligned at initialization and then diverges, consistent with the reviewer's attribution to the cross-branch coupled dynamics (each branch's weights are driven by another branch's error) rather than to conflicting inputs. FIGURE: routing_figure_panels.py:281-304 (path_accuracy_facets, panel D) plots only correct_path and neuron_shared_k1; build_main_figure_04.py:220-260 (full_conflict_controls, panel E) slices summary at conflict_probability==1.0 only, and the docstring (lines 225-229) explicitly says "The chi = 0 null they used to state separately is the left edge of panel D" - which contains no deranged curve. Visually confirmed in figures/main/figure_04.pdf: D has two series (neuron-shared, branch-specific); E shows the deranged triangle only at full conflict.
- **Suggestion:** State failure at every dose with the cause; add the deranged curve to 4D or a χ=0 column to 4E.

#### M049 — Gradient-cosine in Methods is the common-exact-state value, negative for a trivial reason; own-state cosine is strongly positive
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** main.tex:663; supplementary.tex:2120-2126; Fig S29D-F
- **Claim:** The main text (main.tex:663) and supplement (supplementary.tex:2120-2126) quote only the common-exact-state cosine (0.987 -> -0.519 at B=4, 0.952 -> -0.701 at B=8, chi=0.5) and conclude that "the instantaneous descent boundary moves during learning" and that the mean-field boundary "need not describe trained states". The source data show the opposite for the shared rule's own trajectory: own-state cosine at epoch 250, chi=0.5 is +0.778 (B=4) and +0.817 (B=8); own-state cosine crosses zero at chi = 0.664 (B=4) and 0.569 (B=8), matching the mean-field chi_c = 0.667 and 0.571 to three decimals; and own-state cosine turns negative only at doses where accuracy has already collapsed (B=4 chi=0.75, acc 0.217; B=8 chi=0.6, acc 0.292). The common-state negativity is a real geometric fact (the exact-rule minimum is not a fixed point of the shared rule; exact gradient norm there is ~11% of initial, not zero), but it should be described as such, not as a moving descent boundary. Quote own-state cosines as primary, state that the own-state boundary at trained states coincides with chi_c (a stronger result than the text currently claims), and fix supplementary.tex:2120 which says D-F show common-state cosine when the caption and artwork also show own state.
- **Evidence:** review_branch_trajectories/condition_summary.csv: common −0.519/−0.701; own +0.778/+0.817; own negative at χ=0.75 (−0.828) B=4, χ=0.6 (−0.644) B=8.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Text checked: main.tex:663 reports only "At common exact-learning states with four branches and chi=0.5, mean gradient cosine changed from 0.987 at initialization to -0.519 at epoch 250 ... The instantaneous descent boundary therefore moves during learning." Own-state values appear nowhere in the main text (only a pointer at main.tex:723 "own-state gradient diagnostics"). supplementary.tex:2120-2126 says S29 D-F "displays common-state gradient cosine", quotes 0.987->-0.519 (B=4) and 0.952->-0.701 (B=8), and concludes the mean-field boundary "need not describe trained states". The S29 caption (supplementary.tex:4136) does say dashed curves are own state at epoch 250, and figure_S29_panels_A-C.png panels D-F do draw them (orange dashed).

Data recomputed from source_data/review_branch_trajectories/condition_summary.csv (condition neuron_shared_k1; "shared" is not a condition name): B=4 chi=0.5 epoch 250: common_exact_state -0.5194 (20/20 seeds negative inner product), own_state +0.7775 (0/20 negative); B=8 chi=0.5: common -0.7012, own +0.8170. Own-state epoch-250 cosine by dose, B=4: 0.854, 0.816, 0.778, 0.721, 0.654, -0.009 (chi=0.667, 11/20 negative), -0.828 (0.75), -0.965 (1.0); B=8: 0.913, 0.927, 0.817, -0.031 (chi=0.571, 11/20), -0.644 (0.6), -0.921, -0.959, -0.976. All reviewer numbers match exactly.

zero_alignment_crossings.csv, epoch 250, mean interpolated zero-cosine dose: own_state 0.6636 (B=4) and 0.5689 (B=8) versus mean-field chi_c = 0.6667 and 0.5714; common_exact_state 0.2758 (B=4), 0.3115 (B=8). At epoch 0 both are 0.6689/0.5729. So on the shared rule's own trajectory the descent boundary does NOT move; it stays at chi_c to about 3 decimals. Only the common-state boundary moves.

endpoint_replay.csv shared-rule mean test accuracy: B=4 chi=0.5 0.789 (BP 0.796), chi=0.667 0.506, chi=0.75 0.217; B=8 chi=0.5 0.772, chi=0.571 0.515, chi=0.6 0.292. Own-state cosine turns negative exactly where accuracy collapses, as claimed.

Metric definition verified in scripts/run_review_branch_trajectories.py:26-31,49-54: common state = weights of CONDITIONS[1] = correct_path (an exact-equivalent rule, run_path_necessity_fashion.py:39-48), cosine = <exact grad, delivered update>/(norms) at that state.

Where the reviewer overstates: "negative for a trivial reason ... exact gradient is ~0". From gradient_trajectories.csv the exact gradient norm^2 at the exact rule's epoch-250 state (B=4, chi=0.5) is 0.00272 vs 0.208 at epoch 0 (norm about 11% of initial), so it is small but not zero, and a cosine of -0.52 in ~3,100 dimensions (random-direction SD about 0.02) is a genuine geometric fact: the exact-rule minimum is not a stationary point of the shared rule (signed_signal_ratio -0.77). It is not an artifact, but it is not the fact the main-text sentence conveys.
- **Suggestion:** Quote own-state cosines as primary; describe common-state as how the shared update looks at the exact solution; rewrite 'the boundary moves' as 'remains a descent direction below χ_c and reverses above'.

#### M050 — The K-profile of the ancestry contrast is fixed by the generator's three coefficients; below-chance accuracies unexplained
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:239-251, 673-694; Fig 5E
- **Claim:** The K-profile of the ancestry-versus-control contrast in R5/Fig 5E-F is fully determined by the three generator coefficients: the net class signal delivered along ancestry routes is -3.05 (K=1), -0.05 (K=2), +0.85 (K=4) and +1 (K=8). Consequently K=1 converges to the sign-inverted classifier (0.187; per seed acc(K=1)+acc(K=8) ~ 1.00), K=2 is essentially null (0.449), K=8 ties every control by construction (rank-8 projector = identity, 20/20 ties), and at K=1 all sparse families are the same all-ones row. A positive ancestry-minus-best-control contrast could therefore only appear at K=4, and the S16 'utility predicts K=4' retrospective test is likewise implied by these coefficients. The manuscript discloses that the generator 'deliberately' favors hierarchical grouping (main 243, 682) and that full-rank ties are by construction (Table S1), but it never gives the net per-budget coefficients, never explains the far-below-chance accuracies visible in Fig 5E, and presents the intermediate-budget location as a finding. R5 and the Fig 5 caption should state the four net coefficients, label K=1 anti-aligned / K=2 null / K=4 aligned / K=8 tie, and explain the below-chance accuracy as sign-inverted convergence. (R4's shared-rule below-chance crossing already has an analytic explanation in Fig 4C, so that part of the suggestion is optional.)
- **Evidence:** Coefficients −0.15/−0.45/−0.75; seed_outcomes.csv ancestry 0.1866/0.4486/0.8010/0.8100; K=8 all controls 0.8100 with 20/20 ties (rank-8 projector = identity).
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Text checked: main.tex:241-247 (R5), 256 (Fig 5 caption), 673-694 (Methods generator: coefficients 1 / -0.15 / -0.45 / -0.75, ancestry routes Phi = sqrt(K/8)*1[same group]); supplementary.tex:2246-2296 (factorial section), 4349-4370 (Table S1: 0.1866/0.4486/0.8010/0.8100; 'full-rank conditions are exactly tied by construction'), 880-900 (utility 'recovers the mean intermediate-bandwidth ancestry peak', K=4 in 20/20 seeds). grep of main.tex and supplementary.tex for 'below chance|inverted|anti-aligned|net signal|net coefficient': no explanation of the factorial's below-chance accuracies anywhere; 'below-chance' appears only at supp 2232 for the two-stream deranged condition. Config: journal/configs/trained_subtree_address/full_factorial_confirmatory.json (sibling 0.15, same_half 0.45, opposite_half 0.75, noise 1.1). Code: journal/scripts/run_trained_subtree_address_full_factorial.py lines 75-80 (tree_relation), 110-118 (distractor amplitude = -coef * sign), 195-220 (grouped_routes: at budget 1 every sparse family is the all-ones row, so ancestry/derangement/depth-interleaved/random-sparse are identical at K=1), 223-229 (random_rank_routes: QR basis n x budget, projection = B B^T -> identity at K=8, so routes = exact). Recomputation: summing a(b,c) over contexts in block 0's ancestry group gives net coefficients -3.05 (K=1), -0.05 (K=2), +0.85 (K=4), +1.00 (K=8), exactly as the reviewer states; depth-interleaved nets are -0.95 (K=2) and +0.25 (K=4), explaining its 0.2018 and 0.7299. Source data source_data/trained_subtree_address_full_factorial/seed_outcomes.csv (dendritic_tree, n=20 per cell): correct ancestry 0.1866/0.4486/0.8010/0.8100; depth_interleaved 0.1866/0.2018/0.7299/0.8100; random_sparse 0.1866/0.2167/0.7634/0.8100; random_rank_k 0.5450/0.6026/0.7779/0.8100; derangement 0.1866/0.1847/0.1905/0.2513; at K=8 depth-interleaved, random-sparse, random-rank and learned-rank each tie ancestry in 20/20 seeds. Stronger than the reviewer's claim: per-seed acc(K=1)+acc(K=8) = 0.9967 on average (range 0.990-1.012; corr(acc_K1, 1-acc_K8)=0.89), i.e. the K=1 solution is the sign-inverted full-resolution classifier. Also noted: the reported initial gradient cosine at K=1 is +0.362 (per-example concatenated metric), which does not reveal the aggregate anti-alignment, so nothing in the reported diagnostics flags the sign inversion. Fig 5E (figures/main/figure_05.pdf) plots correct at ~0.19 (K=1) and ~0.45 (K=2) against a 0.5 chance line with no explanation.
- **Suggestion:** Give the four net coefficients in R5 and caption; state K=1 anti-aligned, K=2 null, K=4 aligned, K=8 tie; explain below-chance accuracy as sign-inverted convergence (also R4 shared rule).

#### M051 — The K=4 ancestry contrast is reported with three different tests/corrections (sign-flip Holm 0.00519, Wilcoxon Holm 0.0101, Wilcoxon BH 0.0064); low-budget losses unquantified
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** statistics
- **Location:** main.tex:247, 256 (Fig 5H), 771-773, 1148-1154; figure_05.pdf H; supplementary.tex:4366-4372 (Table S2)
- **Claim:** The single 1.27-pp K=4 ancestry-versus-best-control contrast is reported with three different adjusted P values that cannot be reconciled from Methods: sign-flip Holm P=0.00519 (main text line 247, SI Table S2), Holm-adjusted Wilcoxon P=0.0101 (Fig 5H artwork and caption), and BH-adjusted Wilcoxon P=0.0064 (Table S2), while inferential_units.csv lists BH-adjusted paired tests as the prespecified method and the sign-flip test is a 2026-09-05 post hoc addition. The Holm-Wilcoxon used in Fig 5H/I and the four-individual-control family of Fig 5I are declared only in the caption; the four-budget Holm family has two degenerate members (K=1: 0/20 positive, K=8: all exact ties), so the K=4 adjustment is effectively 2x raw. The caption phrase "exact test-count ties" is inaccurate: the 0.0048 Wilcoxon P is SciPy's asymptotic tie-corrected value (an exact Wilcoxon gives 0.00365), and "exact" refers only to restoring the 1/2048 accuracy grid. All three tests agree on significance (P<0.011), so the conclusion is not at risk, but the presentation is inconsistent. K=1/K=2 losses (-35.8 pp, 0/20; -15.4 pp, 5/20) are given in Table S2 but not numerically in the main text.
- **Evidence:** ancestry_k4_control_contrasts.csv: Wilcoxon 0.00482, sign-flip 0.002594, Holm 0.01014/0.005188; budget_scan_bh_correction.csv BH 0.006426; Fig 5H prints 'Holm P=0.0101'; recomputed bootstrap CI [0.591,1.953], 15/20 positive; K=1 −0.3583 (0/20), K=2 −0.1540 (5/20).
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.92):** Snapshot text: main.tex:247 reports "exact paired mean-sign-flip test P=0.00259, or P=0.00519 after Holm correction across four budgets"; main.tex:256 (Fig 5H caption) states "Displayed Holm-adjusted Wilcoxon P=0.0101 ... using exact test-count ties; the mean-sign-flip test reported in text is a separate test"; supplementary.tex:4366-4372 (Table S2 footnote) gives sign-flip P=0.00259 (Holm 0.00519) AND "archived two-sided Wilcoxon P=0.0048 (Benjamini-Hochberg-adjusted P=0.0064)". Fig 5 artwork (figure_05.pdf, panel H) prints "15/20 seeds positive; Holm P=0.0101"; panel I prints Holm P=0.0034/0.00018/7.6e-06/7.6e-06 for the four individual controls. Source data: review_evidence_reanalysis/ancestry_k4_control_contrasts.csv row best_matched_nonanatomical_oracle: wilcoxon 0.00481942, signflip 0.00259399, holm_four_budgets 0.01014043, signflip_holm 0.00518799; trained_subtree_address_full_factorial/budget_scan_bh_correction.csv K=4 row: wilcoxon 0.0048194216, bh_adjusted 0.006425895; inferential_units.csv line 5: "subtree_factorial ... paired seed bootstrap and BH-adjusted paired tests". Recomputation from ancestry_control_paired_differences.csv (20 seeds, K=4, best control): mean 1.26708984 pp, 15/20 positive, 0 zeros, differences all on the 1/2048 grid; scipy 1.15.3 wilcoxon(method="auto") = 0.004819 (identical to method="approx", i.e. asymptotic tie-corrected because |x| contains a duplicate 0.146484375 twice), whereas method="exact" = 0.003654 -> the caption phrase "exact test-count ties" does not describe the computed P; exhaustive 2^20 sign-flip on the mean = 2720/1048576 = 0.002594; 20,000-draw bootstrap CI [0.591, 1.953]; Holm across four sign-flip P values {K1: 1.9e-6 (0/20 positive), K2: 0.001465, K4: 0.002594, K8: 1.0 (20/20 exact ties)} gives K4 adj 0.005188 = 2x raw, confirming the K=1/K=8 members are degenerate. Methods declaration: main.tex:771-773 attaches Holm only to the sign-flip test and mentions "tied rank tests" without a correction; supplementary.tex:4278-4282 attaches BH to the "archived four-budget subtree scan" and Holm to the sign-flip reanalysis; no Methods/SI-S13 sentence declares a Holm-adjusted Wilcoxon across four budgets or the four-individual-control family of Fig 5I (grep for "individual control(s)" hits only the caption and main.tex:769). Scripts: build_main_figure_05.py:559-578 prints p_holm_four_budgets and p_holm_four_individual_controls_at_k4 from the Wilcoxon column; analyze_review_evidence.py:276-287 computes wilcoxon(x, method="auto") then Holm. Post-hoc timing: SI 4280 calls the sign-flip analysis "additional", main.tex:1153 "new"; analysis/REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION_20260905.md item 40 records the reanalysis. Refuted sub-claim: K=1/K=2 magnitudes ARE given in Table S2 (supplementary.tex:4361-4364: -0.3583 and -0.1540; footnote 4366-4367: 0/20 and 5/20 seeds); they are absent only from the main text (main.tex:247 says "worse ... at low budgets"; Fig 5F shows them graphically, no numbers).
- **Suggestion:** Declare one primary test (state which was prespecified) and print it identically in text, Fig 5H and Table S2; move the others to one SI/Methods sensitivity sentence noting post hoc addition and agreement; state degenerate Holm members and the Fig 5I family; give K=1/K=2 magnitudes; replace 'exact test-count ties' with 'accuracies restored to the 1/2048 grid before the tie-corrected test'.

#### M055 — The optimizer interaction is asserted from three of four cells and no recipe element is isolated
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:274, 893-900; supplementary.tex:2565-2590; Fig S28B, S31H
- **Claim:** The optimizer interaction (main.tex:274; Methods 893-900; SI 2565-2586; Figs S28, S31H) is supported by the source data, and the authors already concede it is "not a complete optimizer-by-rule factorial". Two things remain unsatisfying. (1) The optimizer change is a bundle — global Adam LR 0.003 -> 0.0008 together with split_params=true, which activates separate branch (0.0007), TopK (0.0015) and decoder (0.0015) rates — and no run varies one element alone; nor is full-BP autograd run under the LocalCA optimizer, so the exact-coordinate/LocalCA-optimizer cell is filled only by the LocalCA rule. Whether the 13.47-point drop reflects slower convergence at lower rates or a different fixed point cannot be determined (train and test accuracies move together; no epoch counts are logged), so the reviewer's assertion that "LR alone suffices to explain it" is speculation, not shown. (2) The main text reports only differences (10.96, 0.64, 13.47) and never the cell means or the 3.09-point BP-minus-exact-path contrast; a reader cannot see from main.tex that exact-path LocalCA (0.887) sits below BP-optimizer soma broadcast (0.912), i.e. that the eleven-point effect is a fall of the shared-coordinate cell under the LocalCA recipe rather than a rise of exact-path transport. Line 274's "explain why" overstates what the controls show relative to Methods line 900's "locate". Suggested fixes: give the four (five) D3 cell means and the 3.09 contrast in the Results paragraph; soften "explain why" to "locate the difference in the optimizer recipe"; and, if feasible, add the cheap one-factor diagnostic (soma-broadcast autograd at 0.0008 unsplit, and at 0.003 split; 60 fits each) or full-BP autograd under the split recipe.
- **Evidence:** point_dendrite_credit_controls/condition_summary.csv values; paired_contrasts full_bp_minus_local_path 0.03085; config lines 185-194 vs generate script line 159; SI acknowledges 'not a complete factorial'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Snapshot text: main.tex:274 ("Optimizer-matched controls explain why the same gradient comparison produced different endpoints"), main.tex:893-900 (recipes: step 0.003 BP vs 0.0008 LocalCA; matched broadcast "global 0.0008, branch weights 0.0007, decoder 0.0015"; "not a complete optimizer-by-rule factorial"; "These controls locate the eleven-point effect"), supplementary.tex:2565-2586 (0.64, 0.7771 vs 0.7778, -0.06, 13.47, 10.96, "full BP remained 3.09 points above exact-path LocalCA", "conditional on the split LocalCA optimizer"). grep of main.tex for "3.09"/"3.1 point": no hit — the BP-vs-exact-path-LocalCA contrast and all cell means are SI-only (Fig S28A/B builder build_supplementary_figure_s28_native.py:9-15 plots all five cells and five contrasts; Fig S31C shows serial BP 0.92 / exact-path 0.89 / shared-soma 0.78 but no soma-broadcast arm). Data recomputed from source_data/point_dendrite_credit_controls/condition_summary.csv and combined_seed_outcomes.csv (aligned, serial_tree, D3, n=10 each): full_bp test 0.9182 (train 0.9222), soma_broadcast_bp 0.9118 (0.9162), local_path 0.8873 (0.8924), local_shared 0.7778 (0.7836), soma_broadcast_matched 0.7771 (0.7832); paired_contrasts.csv: full_bp_minus_soma_broadcast__aligned__d3 0.00637 [0.00152,0.01168] 8/10, local_path_minus_local_shared 0.10957 [0.10342,0.11582] 10/10, bp_optimizer_broadcast_minus_matched_broadcast 0.13468 [0.12892,...], matched_broadcast_minus_local_shared -0.00063, full_bp_minus_local_path 0.03085 [0.02484,0.03788] 10/10 (SI writes 3.09, report.md writes 3.08; rounding only). Train≈test in every cell, so the gap is a fitting gap; the outcome CSV has no epoch/stopping columns, so the reviewer's "slower convergence in 180 epochs" mechanism is not checkable. Recipes: diff configs/nonlinear_physical_depth/confirmatory/aligned_shunting_bp.yaml vs aligned_shunting_local3f.yaml differs only in strategy, lr 0.003->0.0008, split_params false->true (plus sweep of error_broadcast_mode); generate_nonlinear_physical_depth_sweeps.py:158-166 sets lr and split_params by strategy with topk 0.0015/blocklinear 0.0007/decoder 0.0015 present in both (inert when split is off). generate_point_dendrite_credit_controls.py:103-110 copies only param_groups and optimizer into the matched arm; decoder_update_mode: local is identical in both configs, so the reviewer's separate "decoder path" factor is not a distinct config difference. audit.json: 200 new fits, seeds 10200-10209, all gates pass.
- **Suggestion:** Present the 2×2 cell means in 274 including the 3.09 cost; run a one-factor diagnostic (soma-broadcast autograd at LR 0.0008 without split; at 0.003 with split); replace 'explain why' with 'locate the difference in the recipe'.

#### M056 — Physical-depth Methods omit baseline rates/generator constants, misreport the LocalCA optimizer as a single rate, and the local-ratio family changes input statistics, not only ratio sharing
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** reproducibility
- **Location:** main.tex:270, 864-888, 895-898, 908-911; supplementary.tex:2436-2441, 2568-2575, 2796-2803
- **Claim:** The physical-depth Methods (main.tex 862-916) and SI S5 omit several cohort-wide constants of the primary hierarchical_gain_load runs: the distal excitatory baseline e_0 = 5.0, the inhibitory baseline 2.0, minibatch 256, early-stopping patience 30, weight decay 0.01 and gradient clip 5 (the 0.02 non-distal baseline, 0.08 noise SD, 21/22/21 tier widths and 10,000-example splits are stated in the SI). Main 885 reports LocalCA as a single Adam step 0.0008, but every LocalCA config in the cohort used a split recipe; the recipe given at 897 (global 0.0008, branch weights 0.0007, decoder 0.0015) omits the TopK synaptic-contact rate 0.0015 that governs the 14,336 active contacts, and 'post hoc' at 896 applies to the broadcast evaluation, not to the recipe. The local-ratio family (paired_cumulative) is not a minimal ratio-availability manipulation: besides the disclosed cumulative E/I gains, it sets all excitatory baselines to 5.0 (vs 0.02 in middle/proximal blocks for nested and flat) and multiplies non-distal excitation by gains, so the 29.12- and 39.38-point nested-minus-local-ratio contrasts (verified in paired_contrasts.csv) also reflect changed input statistics; this should be stated at main 270 / SI 2800 or addressed with a baseline-matched variant.
- **Evidence:** aligned_shunting_local3f.yaml 44-68, 185-194; synthetic_datasets.py _make_split factorized_sensors vs paired_cumulative; grep for baseline values in .tex returns nothing; SI 2568-2575 'found that the LocalCA reference used a different, split optimizer'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** TEXT CHECKED (snapshot): main.tex 264-276, 862-916; supplementary.tex 2425-2475, 2560-2585, 2790-2845, 4555-4600 (training-protocol tables cover only the regular-tree MNIST cohorts). CONFIG: journal/configs/nonlinear_physical_depth/confirmatory/aligned_shunting_local3f.yaml lines 44-69 (e_baseline 5.0, i_baseline 2.0, sensor_e_baseline 0.02, independent_noise_std 0.08, n_samples 10000), 169 (batch_size 256), 173 (patience 30), 180-181 (grad_clip 5.0, weight_decay_rate 0.01), 185-194 (param_groups lr 0.0008, split_params true, topk_lr 0.0015, blocklinear_lr 0.0007, decoder_lr 0.0015). aligned_shunting_bp.yaml 182-191: lr 0.003, split_params false. A loop over all 50 hierarchical_gain_load configs (confirmatory, task_family_alignment, physical_alignment_dose, physical_depth_h4_*) returned identical baseline/noise/batch values, so the constants are cohort-wide. task_family_alignment/nested_factor_serial_bp.yaml vs local_ratio_serial_bp.yaml differ only in nuisance_layout (factorized_sensors vs paired_cumulative); nested vs flat differ only in gain_structure. CODE: src/dendritic_modeling/datasets/synthetic_datasets.py 1957-1964: factorized_sensors sets E to sensor_e_baseline 0.02 everywhere and e_baseline 5.0 only where level_ids==0; paired_cumulative sets E to 5.0 in all three blocks. 1984-1996 + _hierarchical_log_field 1867-1889: paired_cumulative multiplies every E and I block by the cumulative hierarchical field (3 scales distal, 2 middle, 1 proximal), whereas _factorized_log_fields (1899-1940) applies gain only to the distal signal block of E and a single-factor field per I block. src/dendritic_modeling/models/base.py 190-225: with split_params the TopK synaptic-contact group gets topk_lr (0.0015), BlockLinear child coupling gets blocklinear_lr (0.0007), decoder 0.0015, and the 'global' lr 0.0008 reaches only a catch-all group. train_valid_split is only used by imagenet_splits.py, so the 0.9 value is inert here. SOURCE DATA: source_data/task_family_alignment/paired_contrasts.csv rows task_specificity_interaction nested_minus_local_ratio: bp 0.29118 (CI 0.2873-0.2954, 10/10), local3f 0.39378 (0.3658-0.4262, 10/10) — matches SI 2826 and 2836. WHAT THE REVIEWER GOT WRONG: SI 2440-2441 already states the 0.02 middle/proximal excitatory baseline and the 0.08 noise SD; SI 2452 and 2464 state the 21/22/21 tier widths and 4/2/2 inventories; main 909 and SI 2803 state 10,000 examples per split. Genuinely unstated anywhere in main or SI: distal excitatory baseline 5.0, inhibitory baseline 2.0, minibatch 256, patience 30, weight decay 0.01, gradient clip 5, and the TopK contact rate 0.0015 (main 897 lists only global/branch/decoder). Main 885 'step ... 0.0008 for LocalCA' is inaccurate for the primary cohort, whose LocalCA fits used the split recipe; SI 2568-2575 confirms the reference used a split optimizer. Main 270 and SI 2800-2802 do disclose that local-ratio uses cumulative gains on E and I at each level, but neither discloses that non-distal excitatory baselines rise from 0.02 to 5.0 and that non-distal E acquires gain variability it lacks in the nested/flat families.
- **Suggestion:** Give all generator constants and batch/split sizes at 872; rewrite 884-886 to state the split LocalCA recipe; state in 270/SI that local-ratio also raises non-distal baselines, or add a nested variant with matched baselines.

#### M057 — 'Depth benefits saturated' is BP-specific; under shared-soma LocalCA the H4 optimum is D4
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:276; supplementary.tex:2700-2716; Fig S31D/E
- **Claim:** Main text line 276 states "Depth benefits also saturated ... the four-factor task peaked at D3 rather than D4" without the BP qualifier, but in the same 360-fit H4 factorial the prespecified primary contrast for shared-soma LocalCA went the other way: D3 0.6610 -> D4 0.7137, +5.27 pp (95% CI 4.84-5.72; 10/10 seeds; q=0.0039), while exact-path LocalCA was flat (+0.60 pp, 7/10, gate failed) and BP fell (-1.46 pp, 0/10). The supplement (line 2732), Fig S31D (outlined shared-soma D4 cell) and Fig S31E ("Serial BP depth optimum", blank untested cells) already carry this nuance, so the defect is confined to the main-text sentence: it should say that saturation at D3 was observed for BP and exact-path LocalCA, whereas shared-soma LocalCA still improved at D4, and it should make clear that the H2 "favored D2" statement rests on a D1/D2-only ladder. Optionally note that the H4 D3 arm places the two coarsest tiers together in the proximal stage ([4],[2],[1+1]), so D3 and D4 differ in tier-to-stage assignment as well as depth.
- **Evidence:** physical_depth_h4_factorial: full_bp D3 0.8671, D4 0.8525; local_shared 0.6610→0.7137; paired depth__shared_local d4_d3 +5.27 (4.84-5.72).
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Main text: main.tex:276 reads "Depth benefits also saturated. The two-factor task favored D2, whereas the four-factor task peaked at D3 rather than D4." with no BP qualifier in that sentence (the BP-specific paragraph is the separate one at line 272). The +5.27 pp shared-soma result appears nowhere in main.tex (grep "5.27" -> no hits in main; only supplementary.tex:2732).

Numbers recomputed from source_data/physical_depth_h4_factorial/: condition_summary.csv aligned serial_tree shunting means are full_bp D1-D4 = 0.5962/0.7112/0.8671/0.8525; local_shared = 0.5968/0.6300/0.6610/0.7137; local_path = 0.5966/0.6764/0.7587/0.7647. From paired_contrasts_by_seed.csv (header-name lookup): depth__shared_local__aligned__d4_d3 mean = +5.270 pp, 10/10 positive; depth__path_local__aligned__d4_d3 = +0.602 pp, 7/10 positive; depth__serial_bp__aligned__d4_d3 = -1.458 pp, 0/10. paired_contrasts.csv gives shared_local CI 4.841-5.722, exact P=0.001953, positive_claim_gate=True, and this contrast is in the "primary" family (supplementary.tex:2697-2699, 2732-2733). RESULTS.md in the same folder states the same values.

H2 depths: supplementary.tex:2654-2657 ("D1 [8] and D2 [4,1]"), Fig S31 caption line 4115, and source_data/remaining_physical_experiments/condition_summary.csv rows with hierarchy=2 contain only depth 1 and 2 for all four regimes (aligned serial BP 0.6588 -> 0.9672; shared 0.6551 -> 0.8671; path 0.6547 -> 0.9686). So "favored D2" is a two-point ladder; no D3 arm exists for H2.

Tier grouping: supplementary.tex:2710-2713 states the explicit H4 D3 grouping [4],[2],[1+1] for inventory [4,2,1,1] (line 2695); with the soma-to-tips convention at 2461-2466 (D3 [2,1,2] = two proximal, two middle, four distal units; global tier goes to the proximal stage), the two coarsest one-group tiers share the two-compartment proximal stage in D3, whereas D4 [1,1,2,2] gives each tier its own stage. Reviewer's structural point is correct.

Mitigating facts the finding omits: (i) the supplement already reports the +5.27 pp shared-soma gain (line 2732) and Fig S31D (figures/supplementary/figure_S31_panels_A-G.pdf) draws an outline on the shared-soma D4 cell (0.71) and on serial-BP D3 (0.87); panel E is explicitly titled "Serial BP depth optimum" with blank cells for untested H2 D3/D4 and H3 D4; Supplementary Table row at supplementary.tex:5208 says "H4 saturates at D3 under BP". (ii) Exact-path LocalCA D4-D3 is +0.60 pp with 7/10 and fails the prespecified gate (supplementary.tex:2733-2734), so "saturation" does hold for two of the three serial regimes; only the shared-soma regime keeps improving. (iii) main.tex:276 ends by saying the experiments "do not establish a universal correspondence between task hierarchy and optimal depth."
- **Suggestion:** Rewrite 276: BP peaked at D3 (−1.46) whereas shared-soma LocalCA improved at D4 (+5.27); note H2 compared only D1/D2 and the [4],[2],[1+1] grouping.

#### M058 — R7 packs seven experiments and 31 decimals into the longest section; its length and ten SI figures are disproportionate to evidential weight
- **Status:** partially confirmed

The quoted phrase 'These signed algebraic nodes are not conductance compartments' is real and is in the SI methods fragment /n/holylabs/.../journal/supplementary/morphology_followup_methods.tex line 34 (input at supplementary.tex 1492), not the main text. 'No NMSE headline is identifiable' and 'the thread is lost at 296 and 306' are judgments; the headline numbers do exist at lines 292, 298 and 300 but are surrounded by 28 other decimals, and paragraphs 296 and 306 each introduce a negative/null sub-result (Boolean credit contrast; linear-control forecasting) without a linking sentence, so the readability concern is defensible though subjective.
