#### M059 — R7 is a forward-representability result in a different model; its link to the route-dictionary thesis is asserted, not shown
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** structure
- **Location:** main.tex:278-308, 413-415; SI note:interaction_structure
- **Claim:** R7's theorem, dynamic program, estimator and Fig 6B-D are forward-representability results in a scalar multi-affine model with no dictionary object in the cut criterion; the paper mostly frames them that way (main 69, 415, 417), but Discussion line 413 overstates by saying "the dictionary framework links these constraints". More substantively, the paper never explains why root broadcast fails on the multi-affine trees (Fig 6E; broadcast gradient cosine 0.30-0.57, S40D) but is nearly equivalent to exact credit in the conductance model (Fig 6F; excess 0.000059 [-0.000008, 0.000154]; calibrated-broadcast cosine 0.93-0.99, S42F). The available explanation — conductance path sensitivities rho_n are positive so a calibrated broadcast only rescales them, whereas the multi-affine path derivative q_v changes sign with sibling-branch state — is present in pieces (main 280, credit_methods q_v, SI 1161) but never assembled; it should be stated via the cosine contrast, with the caveat (main 296, S43F) that derivative sign alone does not predict broadcast failure.
- **Evidence:** Main 280 is the only bridge; SI 1381-1392 motivates the model from the linear experiment; S42F cosine >0.93, broadcast excess 0.000059.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.8):** Checked main.tex 278-312 (R7 + Fig 6 caption), 55-69 (Introduction), 411-419 (Discussion), 817-834 (Methods); SI fragments morphology_followup_methods.tex lines 1-64 (note:interaction_structure, \input at supplementary.tex:1492), morphology_credit_methods.tex lines 4-12, morphology_conductance_methods.tex (full); SI captions at supplementary.tex:4220 (S40) and 4237 (S42); SI 1161-1178 (no-sign-reversal proposition); figure_06.pdf; source_data/morphology_conductance/summaries/fresh/{paired_contrasts.csv,credit_geometry_summary.csv} and source_data/morphology_credit/figure_gradient_summary.csv.

CONFIRMED parts. (1) The cut theorem, DP, estimator and Fig 6B-D are purely representational: main 288-292 defines the criterion rank(C_S°)<=1 with no dictionary object, and the SI note states "These signed algebraic nodes are not conductance compartments" (followup_methods.tex:34). The note is motivated from the linear experiment ("The preceding linear experiment allowed a dense trainable input map...We therefore conducted a separate mechanism study", followup_methods.tex:4-16) — the reviewer's cite "SI 1381-1392" actually points at the prospective-selection note (note:prospective_selection), not this motivation, but the substance is right. (2) Discussion main 413 says "The dictionary framework links these constraints by separating three questions: what a tree can represent..."; representability is not a property of a route dictionary (defined at main 63 as spatial patterns for delivering learning signals), so that sentence does overreach. (3) The 6E-vs-6F mechanism is indeed unstated as an explanation. Numbers verified: conductance calibrated-broadcast-minus-exact (compatible, Adam) = 5.93e-5 [-8.2e-6, 1.54e-4] (paired_contrasts.csv), matching main 308; S42F calibrated-broadcast gradient cosine 0.9937 (step 0) to 0.9335 (step 1000) (credit_geometry_summary.csv), so ">0.93" holds. In the multi-affine model the compatible/Adam/broadcast cosine is only 0.304-0.571 across steps 0-1024 (figure_gradient_summary.csv), yet the two cosine panels (S40D, S42F) are never contrasted and no text says that the conductance path sensitivity rho_n=dS/dV_n is a positive quantity that a calibrated broadcast rescales without sign error, whereas q_v changes sign. Main 308 ends with "Here the grouping benefit required little improvement in credit detail" with no reason given.

REFUTED/OVERSTATED parts. (a) The paper does not present R7 as a dictionary result: the Introduction (main 69) poses representability/estimation as a separate second question; main 415 says "within a defined model class" and "the cut theorem does not automatically extend to conductance ancestors"; main 417 states spatial-credit advantages are "distinct from the representational benefit of grouping inputs". So "the link is asserted, not shown" reduces to one over-strong sentence at 413, not a structural mislabeling. (b) The sign-varying mechanism is partly stated: main 280 and Fig 6A caption ("credit multiplier 1-2v changes sign"), credit_methods.tex:6 defines q_v=du_root/du_v as the exact path credit, and SI 1161 proves no sign reversal for passive adjoints. (c) R7 does bring dictionaries into the same trees empirically (Fig 6E; S40 five rules; S42 four rules), so the representability/dictionary interaction is tested, not only asserted. (d) The reviewer's proposed unification via derivative sign is undercut by the authors' own Boolean control: aligned XOR-of-AND has the sign-varying 1-2v multiplier yet broadcast reached NMSE 0.00345 (main 296), and S43F caption says "No learning outcome is inferred from a derivative sign alone". The honest bridge is the gradient-cosine contrast (0.30-0.57 vs 0.93-0.99), not a derivative-sign law.
- **Suggestion:** Either reframe R7 as the forward-capacity complement and rewrite 413, or state the q_S sign-varying-multiplier mechanism to unify 6E and 6F.

#### M060 — The centered-cut proposition is a special case of the hierarchical-Tucker/tree-tensor-network rank characterization and should be cited as such
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:286-290, 415; SI note:centered_cut_theorem; references.bib
- **Claim:** The multi-affine tree (SI Eq. s_multiaffine_node) is a bond-dimension-2 tree tensor network (hierarchical Tucker format) with the constant pinned into every bond space. The unconstrained statement "representable iff every subtree matricization has rank ≤ bond dimension", the nestedness argument used for sufficiency, and the max-over-cuts singular-value-tail lower bound (SI Eq. s_centered_cut_bound; main Eq. eq:morphologyselection) are all standard hierarchical-Tucker results (Hackbusch & Kühn 2009; Grasedyck 2010; Hackbusch 2012), which the manuscript never names or cites—only Cohen et al. 2016 is cited (main.tex:280, 415; morphology_followup_methods.tex:59-61). The proposition is correct (worked examples recomputed), and the genuinely new content is the centered refinement rank(C_S°)≤1 obtained by pinning the constant, plus its application to morphology selection. The SI proposition (morphology_followup_methods.tex:65-113, compiled SI §4.14) and Discussion ("the cut theorem", "exact connection", main.tex:415) should state the tree-tensor-network identification, cite the HT sources, and present only the centered refinement as new.
- **Evidence:** SI 1455-1462 acknowledges the rank-2 form without naming it; grep Hackbusch/Grasedyck/'tensor network' returns nothing.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Text checked: main.tex:280 (cites cohen2016tensor only), 286-290 (rank(C_S°)≤1 necessary and sufficient "(Supplementary Section S4)", Eq. eq:morphologyselection), 415 (Discussion: "exact connection", "the cut theorem", cites only cohen2016tensor). The reviewer's "SI 1455-1462" is wrong for the snapshot (that region is the prospective linear-selection methods); the proposition actually lives in the \input fragment at supplementary.tex:1492 -> journal/supplementary/morphology_followup_methods.tex lines 59-61 (only citation: "Interaction-tensor descriptions have previously been used ... \citep{cohen2016tensor}. The centered constant-feature criterion below is derived for the specific local model"), 65-113 (Proposition prop:s_centered_tree_iff with full proof), 116-120 (Eq. s_centered_cut_bound), 93-94 ("Its full cut matrix has rank at most two"), 116-117 ("stronger than an uncentered rank-two constraint"); compiled SI §4.14 p.21. Grep of main.tex, supplementary/, all six *_methods.tex fragments and references.bib for hackbusch|grasedyck|tensor network|tucker|oseledets|bond dimension|tensor train: zero hits; references.bib:657 has only cohen2016tensor. Math check: node rule u=a+b u_L+c u_R+d u_L u_R (Eq. s_multiaffine_node) is exactly (1,u_v)=T_v[(1,u_L)⊗(1,u_R)] with T_v=[[1,0,0,0],[a,b,c,d]], i.e. a tree tensor network / hierarchical-Tucker format with bond dimension 2 whose bond space is pinned to contain the constant. Unconstrained HT rank theorem (Hackbusch & Kühn 2009; Grasedyck 2010) gives representability iff rank(C_S)≤2 at every dimension-tree node; the constant-pinning turns this into rank([e_∅|C_S])≤2 ⟺ rank(C_S°)≤1, which is the paper's proposition. The sufficiency step in the SI proof (h_P ∈ V_L⊗V_R) is the HT nestedness of minimal subspaces, and Eq. s_centered_cut_bound is the standard per-matricization Eckart–Young lower bound maximised over tree nodes. I recomputed the SI's two worked examples by enumerating all 256 inputs: matching (12)(34)(56)(78) at even–odd root split: full rank-2 tail 0.50, centered tail 0.75, centered rank 4; quartet 1234/5678 at cut 12345: full tail 0.00, centered tail 0.25 — both match the text, so the proposition and bound are correct; the issue is attribution only. Cohen et al. 2016 (cited) itself builds on HT decompositions, so the authors are one citation removed from the source, not silent.
- **Suggestion:** State the tree-tensor-network identification, cite the three works, present only the centered refinement as new.

#### M062 — The Boolean 'grouping' primary contrast is a bootstrap test of a proven inequality
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** statistics
- **Location:** main.tex:297, 855-856; Fig S44E; boolean_morphology_methods.tex
- **Claim:** The prespecified 'grouping' primary contrast (main.tex line 296: 0.5632, 97.5% CI 0.5491-0.5827; Methods line 832; Fig S44E) is almost entirely predetermined by the proposition in boolean_morphology_methods.tex lines 56-84: every crossed balanced tree has NMSE >= 8/15 = 0.5333 for all coefficients, so once the aligned learner reaches 0.00095 the contrast is at least 0.5324 and the 0.01 margin is met by construction; the observed 0.5632 exceeds the floor-implied value by only 0.031. The only non-trivial content is (i) the aligned tree is learned from label-independent initialization (0.00095) and (ii) crossed trees land within 0.0308 (5.8%) of the proven floor (0.5660/0.5623) -- the latter is stated nowhere in the manuscript (it appears only in the assistants' log). Fig 6F has the same structure: compatible bound is zero and every incompatible bound is positive (min 0.0054), so the 0.02877 contrast's sign is guaranteed given compatible learning; the informative fact, unreported, is that learned incompatible error (0.0289) is about 2.2x the mean bound (0.0132), with the caveat that the two use different normalizations. Recommend describing both grouping outcomes as attainment relative to the proven bound, drawing the 8/15 floor in S44E, and reserving confirmatory-test language for the credit contrast.
- **Evidence:** primary_contrasts.csv crossed_minus_compatible_exact 0.5632; bound 0.5333; interaction_bound/report.json mean 0.01322, min 0.00537; incompatible_minus_compatible 0.02877.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Snapshot main.tex line 296 (Boolean learning paragraph; the finding's '297' is a blank line), line 304 (conductance), line 311 Fig 6 caption panel F, Methods line 832 ('Only the grouping contrast passed') and 829; the cited lines 855-856 are the linear-tree forecast section and are irrelevant. Proposition in journal/supplementary/boolean_morphology_methods.tex lines 56-84: crossed balanced trees have NMSE >= 8/15 for every coefficient choice, aligned tree exact inside [-2,2]; lines 38 and 107 disclaim attainment. Source data source_data/boolean_morphology/primary_contrasts.csv: crossed_minus_compatible_exact 0.56317, 97.5% CI [0.54914, 0.58275], margin 0.01, passes=True. condition_summary.csv xor_of_ands Adam exact: balanced_ac_bd 0.56596, balanced_ad_bc 0.56228, balanced_ab_cd 0.000945 (broadcast crossed 0.5550/0.5629, also at the floor). Recomputed: crossed mean 0.5641, excess over 8/15 = 0.0308 (5.8%); floor-implied contrast 0.5324 vs observed 0.5632, so >94% of the confirmatory effect is the theorem. Conductance: source_data/morphology_conductance/interaction_bound/report.json mean incompatible bound 0.013222, min 0.005367, all compatible bounds zero; summaries/fresh/learning_summary.csv Adam exact step 1000 incompatible 0.028921 vs compatible 0.000150; paired_contrasts.csv 0.028771 [0.026919, 0.030725]; ratio learned/bound 2.19 (caveat: bound is population-normalized, learned error is split-variance-normalized, per supplementary.tex line 4237). Neither main.tex nor supplementary.tex reports learned crossed error against 8/15 or learned incompatible conductance error against 0.0132 (grep for 0.564/0.566/0.562/attain/above a bound returns nothing); only analysis/BOOLEAN_REVISION_20260905.md line 47 notes 'smallest margin above a bound was 0.000321828'. Fig S44E (figure_S44_panels_A-H.png) draws the 0.01 margin as a line at the foot of a 0-0.6 axis with no 8/15 floor. Nuance against the finding: the sign is guaranteed only conditional on the aligned learner attaining NMSE below 8/15 from label-independent init, which is exactly the motivation stated at line 296 ('Exact representability does not guarantee learning'); that condition is real but is fully captured by the aligned NMSE 0.00095, so the bootstrap interval and the 0.01 margin add nothing.
- **Suggestion:** Describe grouping outcomes as attainment relative to the proven bound; reserve confirmatory language for the credit contrast; for 6F report learned/bound ratio and whether the gap is optimization or looseness.

#### M063 — The 'two-sweep fitting pilot' is a weak baseline; a converged pilot might erase the estimator's advantage
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:298-299, abstract; Fig 6C; S41C,F
- **Claim:** The estimator's statistically established advantage over the training-based baseline is specific to the two-sweep, single-initialization pilot. Re-running selection on the sealed calibration samples with the same 192-fit/64-gate split but a converged pilot (32 ALS sweeps) shrinks the pilot-minus-estimated regret at the primary condition from +0.060 [0.027,0.094] to +0.030 [-0.001,+0.060] (1 init), +0.019 [-0.012,+0.047] (2 restarts) and +0.010 [-0.017,+0.036] (4 restarts), none of which passes the paper's own adjusted-lower-bound criterion; at 1,024 labels or noiseless calibration the converged pilot ties or beats the estimator. The manuscript labels the baseline honestly ("two-sweep pilot", Fig 6C; main.tex:298, 823; S41 caption), but the abstract's "short-training baselines" and the pooled superiority claim should be qualified: either add a converged-pilot control to S41 and state that the interaction estimator matches, rather than beats, a fully fitted pilot at equal label budget (its genuine advantage is compute and a regret floor near the oracle-cut value ~0.022 that is reached already at 64 labels), or restrict the claim to "a two-sweep pilot". Note also that the S36 zero-regret pilot is a different, privileged object (best of 4 restarts scored on exact population error, run.py:105), so main.tex:821-823 should not be read as a fair comparison between the two studies.
- **Evidence:** policy_summary.csv 256/0.5: estimated 0.0274, pilot 0.0872; 64: 0.0274 vs 0.1552; 1024: ~0.031 vs ~0.065; population pilot regret ~1e-17.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Text checked: main.tex:298 ("0.0872 for the two-sweep pilot to 0.0274"), main.tex:52 abstract ("against fixed and short-training baselines"), main.tex:311 Fig 6C caption ("fitting-pilot baselines", adjusted interval [0.0268,0.0939]), main.tex:821 (full-domain "pilot that briefly fitted every candidate for two sweeps had numerical-zero regret"), main.tex:823 (protocol), supplementary.tex:4229 S41 caption, journal/supplementary/morphology_calibration_methods.tex:10-24 (pilot = 2 ALS sweeps, one fixed init, 192 fit / 64 gate; final training = 32 sweeps x 2 restarts on 1,024 samples). Source data recomputed: source_data/morphology_calibration/policy_summary.csv rows "all": noise 0.5 estimated_cut regret 0.0274/0.0274/0.0334 at 64/256/1024 labels vs two_sweep_pilot 0.1552/0.0872/0.0641 (reviewer's numbers correct; estimator flat, pilot falling); primary_contrasts.csv pilot improvement 0.0599 CI97.5 [0.0268,0.0939]; calibration_timing.csv 256/0.5: estimator 0.0027 s + score 0.0436 s vs pilot 0.0661 s (reviewer's 0.045 vs 0.065 s correct); source_data/morphology_structure/policy_summary.csv two_sweep_pilot regret 2.9e-17 / 2.5e-17 (correct). Caveat on the reviewer's inference: scripts/morphology_structure/run.py:105 shows the S36 "two-sweep pilot" is the minimum over 4 restarts of the sweep-2 POPULATION NMSE on the exhaustive clean 256-point domain, i.e. a privileged object not comparable to the S41 pilot, so "zero regret in the full-domain study" is not by itself evidence about the S41 pilot. Decisive check: I re-ran selection on the sealed calibration samples (source_data/morphology_calibration/confirmatory/calibration_samples_*.npz, 20 seeds x 4 families) with the authors' own model.fit ALS (scripts/morphology_structure/model.py:183) and bridge.pilot logic (scripts/morphology_calibration/bridge.py:142-156), same 192/64 split and same 64-sample gate, scoring the chosen candidate by its validation-selected final test NMSE from candidate_outcomes.csv and bootstrapping 10,000 whole-seed draws. My 2-sweep/1-init pilot reproduces the published regrets exactly (0.0872, 0.0641, 0.0816), validating the reimplementation. At the primary condition (256 labels, noise 0.5): 32 sweeps x 1 init regret 0.0573 (pilot-minus-estimated +0.030, 95% CI [-0.001,+0.060]); 32 x 2 restarts (the reviewer's proposed control) 0.0460 (+0.019, CI [-0.012,+0.047]); 32 x 4 restarts 0.0372 (+0.010, CI [-0.017,+0.036]). All three converged pilots fail the paper's own superiority criterion (adjusted lower bound > 0) and the 4-restart one also fails the 0.01 margin. At 1024/0.5 the converged pilots tie or beat the estimator (0.0266-0.0343 vs 0.0334); at 256 noiseless the 4-restart pilot beats it (0.0099 vs 0.0232, CI [-0.030,+0.002]). Noiseless 2-sweep pilot regret 0.0816 vs noisy 0.0872 shows the pilot's deficit is almost entirely undertraining/single-init, not label noise. Converged-pilot compute is still ~2 s per task on one CPU. Notes and per-task rows saved under scratchpad/review_snapshot/notes/verify-M063/.
- **Suggestion:** Add a post hoc converged-pilot comparison (32 sweeps × 2 restarts on 192 samples) in S41; if the estimator loses, replace 'short-training baselines' with 'a two-sweep pilot'.

#### M064 — 'Root broadcast' is a different rule in Fig 6E (unit sensitivities) than in Fig 6F (calibrated), confounding the 'credit requirements depend on interaction order' conclusion
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** main.tex:300-305, 311; morphology_end_to_end_methods.tex; morphology_conductance_methods.tex
- **Claim:** The "broadcast" rule in the multi-affine experiments (Fig 6E, S40, S41G-H; unit sensitivity at all six nonroot units, Adam 0.003) and the "calibrated broadcast" in the conductance experiment (main.tex:304, S42; frozen mean initial path-sensitivity profile, all-positive with ~7-fold range, Adam 0.01) are different rules, and the main text (300 vs 304; Discussion 417) juxtaposes their outcomes (NMSE 0.7321 vs +0.000059 excess) without saying so. The distinction is not cosmetic: the calibrated profile has cosine only 0.76-0.88 with a unit vector in the conductance model, and in the multi-affine model a calibrated profile would have init field-cosine ~0.73 versus ~-0.10 for the unit rule (about half the nonroot units start with fixed negative path gain under the symmetric initialization). Part of the apparent multi-affine-vs-conductance contrast may therefore be rule definition; this is untested because neither a calibrated-broadcast arm in the multi-affine runs nor a unit-broadcast arm in the conductance runs exists. The paper does state that the XOR branch derivative changes sign (282, 311) but never uses it to explain the 6E/6F difference; the reviewer's suggested decisive mechanism (sign-varying vs always-positive path gain) should not be asserted either, since aligned XOR-of-AND learns with broadcast despite a sign-changing derivative (296). Fix: state explicitly that the rules differ, and add the missing arm (calibrated broadcast in the multi-affine experiment and/or unit broadcast in the conductance experiment).
- **Evidence:** S40E broadcast at 0.01/0.03 still ~0.78-0.88; S42F calibrated cosine >0.93; S40D unit-broadcast cosine ~0.3.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** RULE DEFINITIONS (confirmed). Multi-affine/Fig 6E: journal/supplementary/morphology_end_to_end_methods.tex lines 27-29 "trained with either exact path credit or unit root broadcast ... broadcast replaced the six other internal sensitivities by one"; morphology_credit_methods.tex line 6 "a unit root broadcast"; code scripts/morphology_credit/experiment.py:147 `if meta['rule']=='broadcast':routed[i,:6]=1.`; Adam 0.003 for broadcast at main.tex:311 and :825. Conductance/Fig 6F: morphology_conductance_methods.tex line 18 "A fixed calibrated broadcast supplied delta0*gamma_n, where gamma_n was the mean initial path sensitivity ... on the label-free calibration inputs. The profile was frozen"; scripts/morphology_conductance/model.py:49 calibrate_profiles; source_data/morphology_conductance/README.md. Main text names them "root-broadcast"/"broadcast" (main.tex:300, 311, 825) vs "Calibrated broadcast"/"fixed calibrated broadcast" (main.tex:304, 829) but nowhere states they are different rules; no unit-broadcast arm exists in the conductance runs (run.py:21 RULES) and no calibrated arm in the multi-affine runs (experiment.py:25 RULES). The supplement body (lines 3546-3920) never uses "broadcast"; only captions S40/S42 (supplementary.tex:4219, 4237) describe each rule separately.
NUMBERS (recomputed). source_data/morphology_credit/summaries/fresh/all_rate_sensitivity.csv, mean over 3 families x 2 structures: Adam broadcast 0.7755/0.7823/0.8871 at 0.003/0.01/0.03 (reviewer's 0.78-0.88 correct); compatible-only 0.596/0.597/0.700 vs exact 0.048/0.033/0.036. figure_gradient_summary.csv compatible,adam,broadcast cosine 0.304 (step 0) rising to 0.571 (step 1024) (reviewer's "~0.3" holds early). source_data/morphology_conductance/summaries/fresh/credit_geometry_summary.csv calibrated_broadcast Adam cosine 0.9937 (step 0) to 0.9335 (step 1000) (reviewer's ">0.93" correct); learning_summary.csv compatible Adam: exact 0.000150, calibrated_broadcast 0.000209, matching main.tex:304.
HOW DIFFERENT THE RULES ARE (my computation). All 180 fresh calibrated profiles (runs/fresh/seed_*_final_states.npz, task_*_initial_profiles) are strictly positive with max/min ratio ~6.9 (e.g. [0.234,0.144,0.079,0.059,0.657,0.255]) and cosine with the unit vector 0.756-0.878 (mean 0.827) — the conductance "broadcast" is not a unit broadcast. In the multi-affine model, using experiment.py's own forward/path-sensitivity recursion with the paper's N(0,0.5^2) zero-bias init on compatible trees (10 seeds x 3 families), the unit profile has field cosine -0.10 with the exact nonroot field at init, whereas a calibrated mean-initial profile would have cosine 0.73; ~51% of (unit,pattern) entries are negative and many units have a FIXED negative sign across all 256 patterns (init-dependent, capturable by calibration). So the rule difference is material, and the reviewer's "part of the 6E vs 6F difference is rule definition" is a live, untested confound.
OVERSTATED PARTS. (a) "the sign-varying q_S argument is never made": main.tex:282 and caption :311 explicitly state that the branch derivative 1-2v changes sign — but only for XOR-of-AND in panel A, never to reconcile 6E with 6F. (b) The reviewer's proposed decisive mechanism ("sign-varying vs always-positive path gain") is contradicted by the paper's own data: main.tex:296 aligned XOR-of-AND learns with broadcast (NMSE 0.00345, perfect classification) despite the sign-changing derivative; my init computation suggests fixed per-unit sign mismatch is at least as important. (c) The paper does not state a single "credit requirements depend on interaction order" conclusion from 6E vs 6F; Fig 6F plots only exact credit (figure_06.pdf panel F legend "Adam; exact path credit"), and the calibrated-broadcast number appears only in text (main.tex:304) and S42E. The confound is a text-level juxtaposition (line 300 "broadcast ... 0.7321" vs line 304 "Calibrated broadcast's compatible-tree excess was only 0.000059 ... grouping benefit required little improvement in credit detail") plus Discussion main.tex:417 lumping "broadcast" across experiments.
- **Suggestion:** Add a calibrated-broadcast arm to the multi-affine experiments, or state the rules differ and that the decisive difference is sign-varying vs always-positive path gain.

#### M068 — Proxy label contamination is not quantified; roughly half the 'inhibitory' contacts in the main-figure cohort may be excitatory
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:319-327, 335, 991; supplementary.tex:2939-2950, 3020-3030; Table S24; S33A; Fig 7A
- **Claim:** The 73.0% direct/proxy agreement quoted at main.tex 335 and supp 2949 is pooled over a jointly labeled subset that is 71.5% inhibitory (1439/2012), whereas the analysed population is 24% labelled-inhibitory; the per-class rates shown only in Fig S33A (E→I 14.7%, I→E 31.9%) imply, if they transfer to unlabeled contacts, a proxy-inhibitory precision of roughly 0.45-0.54 (0.50 at the self-consistent 17.5% true-I prior). This model-based contamination estimate is not stated anywhere, and it bears directly on β_k, the inhibitory-leverage route ranking (main.tex 328), and the E/I coloring of Fig 7A. The consequence for candidate-route ubiquity is smaller than claimed: candidates are inhibitory-bearing segments (not branch points as line 320 says), and even direct-only labels leave 75% of segments (61-100% per cell) as candidates, so ubiquity reflects inhibitory synapse density rather than contamination. Route-identity sensitivity to direct-only labels (Jaccard ≈0.24, capture 0.25 vs 0.33) is already reported in S33C/D and at main.tex 991. Suggested fix: state the class composition of the joint subset and a precision estimate under a stated prior in SI S6 and at line 335, note that direct-only labels carry the opposite (I-enriched, 4.7% coverage) bias rather than presenting them as a clean alternative, and correct "branch point" to "inhibitory-bearing segment" at line 320.
- **Evidence:** confusion_pooled.csv E/E 489, E/I 84, I/E 459, I/I 980; cell_metrics n_i/(n_e+n_i)=0.24; per-cell inhibitory-bearing segments 0.92-1.00; S33C direct-only Jaccard ~0.2.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked: main.tex 319-320, 328, 335, 341 (Fig 7 caption), 991; supplementary.tex 2939-2950, 3020-3030, 3033-3042, 3052-3053, Table S24 (4926-4948, tab:microns_cells), S33 caption 4164 and figure_S33_panels_A-F.pdf; source_data/review_morphology_uncertainty/{confusion_pooled,confusion_by_cell,mapping_radius_sensitivity}.csv; source_data/figure3/cell_metrics.csv; scripts/analyze_review_morphology_uncertainty.py lines 108-145; scripts/build_review_completion_figures.py 102-112.

Confirmed numbers: confusion_pooled.csv = E/E 489, E/I 84, I/E 459, I/I 980 (direct_class,proxy_class order per script line 111); agreement 1469/2012 = 73.01% (matches supp 3038). Row error rates: direct-E→proxy-I 84/573 = 14.7%; direct-I→proxy-E 459/1439 = 31.9%. The jointly labeled subset is 71.5% direct-I (1439/2012), whereas the full eight-cell population is 24.0% labelled-I (6888/28669, Table S24 totals; per cell 0.20-0.32 from cell_metrics n_i/(n_e+n_i)). So the quoted 73.0% is dominated by the inhibitory rows. If the row error rates transfer to the unlabeled population, proxy-I precision = p(1-0.319)/[p(0.681)+(1-p)0.147]: 0.45 at p=0.15, 0.54 at p=0.20; solving for the observed 24.0% proxy-I fraction gives p=0.175 and precision 0.50. Reviewer's "~55%" (4:1 prior) recomputes to 0.537 — consistent. Nothing in main.tex or supplementary.tex reports a precision/prior estimate (grep for precision/14.7/31.9 finds none in text), and main.tex 335 and supp 2949 quote only the pooled 73.0%.

However: (1) Supplementary Fig S33A DOES print the row percentages 85.3/14.7 and 31.9/68.1 (build_review_completion_figures.py 109-112; verified in the PDF), and the S33 caption says "counts and row percentages expose the asymmetric disagreement" — so per-class errors are reported graphically, just not in text nor converted to precision. (2) Candidate routes are segments with I_size>0 (script line 142), not branch points as main.tex 320 says: hybrid 5 µm candidates per cell = 138/146, 101/104, 40/40, 22/22, 76/78, 34/34, 123/128, 59/64 (593/616 = 96%; reviewer's 92-100% confirmed). But under direct-only labels the candidate pool is still 96, 73, 35, 22, 64, 33, 93, 46 (462/616 = 75%, per cell 61-100%), so candidate ubiquity is mostly inhibitory-synapse density (e.g. cell 1: 1378 proxy-I contacts over 6147 µm cable ≈ 1 per 4.5 µm vs 42 µm mean segment; even at 50% precision nearly every segment is I-bearing), not principally proxy contamination. The causal clause "so nearly every branch point is a candidate route" is overstated. (3) Route identity under direct-only labels: mean selected-route Jaccard vs nominal = 0.214/0.238/0.238 at 2/5/10 µm (reviewer's ~0.2 confirmed), fixed-field capture 0.254 vs 0.329 hybrid; this instability is already disclosed (S33C/D; main.tex 991 "Direct-only classification produced the largest change in selected routes"). (4) The reviewer's remedy (build Fig 7A-F from direct-only labels) trades one bias for another: direct labels come from proofread presynaptic axons that are I-enriched (joint subset 71.5% I; 47-cell cohort cell_metrics shows n_i>n_e, e.g. 105 vs 28, with 4.71% coverage), and the "direct" label is itself a coarse cell-type classifier output, not ground truth; the transfer of row error rates to unlabeled contacts is an assumption the paper explicitly declines (supp 3041-3042).
- **Suggestion:** Add per-class error rates and a precision estimate under a stated prior to SI S6 and 335; build Fig 7A-F from direct-only/47-cell cohorts or state segment coverage in the caption; report surviving candidate routes under direct-only labels.

#### M070 — Headline efficiency panels 7E/F are computed only on the route-generated field; independent-field numbers are available and smaller
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** structure
- **Location:** main.tex:331, Fig 7E-F (340); build_main_figure_07.py 252-269, 620-703; capture_per_wire/report.md
- **Claim:** Fig. 7E-F and the numbers at main.tex L331 (85% of oracle capture at ~7% of coefficients; capture-per-coefficient 14.2x vs dense and 2.8x vs density-matched shuffle) are computed exclusively on the route-generated positive-control field t=Gamma a (capture_per_wire is a reanalysis of figure3/routing_capacity_curves.csv.gz), a condition the authors themselves call deliberately favorable and whose report notes the normalization rewards sparse dictionaries by construction. On the independent focal-shunt field (reciprocal_routing, K=8) the same quantities are smaller: 65% of oracle capture at 6.35% density, capture-per-wire 7.9 (subtree) vs 3.6 (depth bins, density 1/K by construction) vs 4.1 (matched-density shuffle) vs 0.69 (dense), i.e. within-cell ratios ~11.4x vs dense, ~2.0x vs shuffle and ~2.3x vs depth bins (versus 14.2x, 2.8x and 5.3x on the favorable field). The text discloses the positive-control origin at L331, but the caption for E-F does not, and the independent-field efficiency is never reported. Report E/F on the independent field (or both, independent first), quote the independent ratios at L331, and state that depth-bin density is 1/K by construction.
- **Evidence:** capture_per_wire derived from figure3/routing_capacity_curves.csv.gz; recomputed on reciprocal_routing K=8: subtree density 6.35%, capture per wiring 7.9/3.6/0.69.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Provenance check: build_main_figure_07.py lines 252-253 (wire_efficiency_table reads source_data/capture_per_wire/cell_method_channel.csv) and 272-273 (per_wire_summary reads capture_per_wire/summary.csv) feed panels E and F; model_field_curves (line ~205) reads source_data/figure3/routing_capacity_curves.csv.gz for panel D; cable_field_curves reads source_data/reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv for panel C. capture_per_wire/report.md lines 3-4: "Secondary analysis of the frozen Figure 3 routing-capacity table (source_data/figure3/routing_capacity_curves.csv.gz)", and its Scope paragraph literally says "The normalization rewards sparse dictionaries by construction; the substantive comparison is against the equally sparse controls". analyze_capture_per_wire.py lines 30-31 and 47-52 confirm the input table and the anchors 0.487/0.0692/0.850. Supplement L3106-3109 and tab:capacity caption (L4951) identify that table as "fields generated from the ancestry-route kernel" (the t=Gamma a positive control). So 85%/7%/14.2/2.8 (main.tex L331 and caption L340 E,F) are all computed on the route-generated field. Builder docstring and section comment call F "the wiring-economy headline"/"the headline".

Recomputation on the independent (focal-shunt) field, reciprocal_routing/cell_method_capture.csv merged with operator_audit.csv n_e_segments, K=8, 8 cells: subtree (morphology paths) capture 0.4583, wiring density 0.0635 (6.35%), capture-per-wire 7.92; dense SVD oracle 0.6938 -> 0.69; depth bins 0.4446 at density 1/8 -> 3.56; row-shuffled 0.2493 at subtree-matched density -> 4.07. Within-cell mean ratios: subtree/dense 11.45 (route-generated field: 14.18), subtree/shuffled 1.98 (route-generated: 2.82), subtree/depth 2.32 (route-generated: 5.30). Oracle fraction on the independent field 0.654 versus 0.850 on the route-generated field. Reviewer's 6.35%, 7.9/3.6/0.69 and ~2.3x vs 5.3x all reproduce. Depth-bin density is 1/K by construction: figure3 summary.csv gives 1, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125 for K=1,2,4,8. Caveat: the reciprocal_routing table records wiring_nonzeros only for morphology paths (NaN for controls), so control densities on the independent field had to be taken by construction (depth 1/K; shuffle = matched to subtree, as stated at main.tex L327); random-route density on that field is not recorded.

Mitigating context: main.tex L331 does state these figures come from the "deliberately favorable condition" and that 85% is oracle-relative, and the numbers do not appear in the abstract or Discussion (grep hits only L331 and L340). But caption L340 for E and F does not say the field is the positive-control field, and the layout (F spans two rows) makes the favorable-field efficiency the visual headline of Fig. 7 while the independent-field efficiency numbers, which are materially smaller, are not reported anywhere.
- **Suggestion:** Report E/F on the independent field (or both, independent first), quote independent ratios at 331, demote positive-control efficiency to SI; state depth-bin density is 1/K by construction.

#### M071 — The 73.0% agreement sentence conflates the disjoint and initial cohorts
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:335
- **Claim:** main.tex:335 attaches the 73.0% direct/proxy agreement to the disjoint 47-cell cohort's direct labels, but the audit is a within-pilot comparison of direct presynaptic vs target-structure proxy labels on 2,012 jointly labeled contacts in the initial eight cells (report.json n_cells=8; SI 2947-2949, 3034-3038; Fig. S33A). The sentence should be split: direct labels covered 4.71% of inputs in the disjoint cohort; separately, in the initial eight cells direct and proxy labels agreed on 73.0% of 2,012 jointly labeled contacts with asymmetric errors (Supplementary Fig. S33A). Methods line 991 already phrases this correctly.
- **Evidence:** SI 2947-2949, 3020-3025; review_morphology_uncertainty/report.json n_cells 8, 2012 contacts, 0.7301.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** main.tex:335 reads "Direct E/I labels covered only 4.71% of inputs in the disjoint cohort, and agreed with the initial cohort's proxy labels on 73.0% of jointly labeled contacts" — the subject of "agreed" is the disjoint cohort's direct labels, implying a 47-cell-direct vs 8-cell-proxy comparison, which cannot exist because the cohorts share no cells/contacts. Source of the number: supplementary.tex:2947-2949 (subsection "Cohort and data sources", eight-cell pilot: "Proxy and direct presynaptic labels agreed for 73.0% of 2,012 jointly labeled contacts") and 3034-3038 ("all eight initial cells ... E/E 489, E/I 84, I/E 459, I/I 980: agreement was 73.01%"); S33 caption at 4164 ("All analyses use eight initial reconstructed cells"). source_data/review_morphology_uncertainty/report.json: n_cells=8, n_jointly_labeled_mapped_contacts=2012, direct_proxy_agreement=0.73012; recomputed (489+980)/2012=0.73012 and 489+84+459+980=2012. The 4.71% is the 47-cell v661 cohort (supplementary.tex:3133, 4003; main.tex:936). main.tex:991 (Methods) states the audit correctly as a within-initial-sample direct/proxy comparison, so only the Results sentence misattributes it.
- **Suggestion:** Split into two sentences: disjoint coverage 4.71%; in the initial eight cells direct and proxy agreed on 73.0% of 2,012 contacts with asymmetric errors (S33A).

#### M072 — The focal-shunt field used as the 'independent' target in Fig 7C is piecewise constant on the ancestry partition, so not independent of the subtree-route construction
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:329, 340 C, 960-967; Table S24 footnote
- **Claim:** The focal-shunt operator H used in Fig 7C / Table S24 is generated by a different operator (finite-differenced passive-cable Green's function) that is not fitted to the routes, but it is not structurally independent of them: for a passive tree the adjoint-ratio part of each column is exactly a combination of nested-subtree indicators along the shunted segment's ancestral path (residual 1e-23), and even including the driving-force term at most 3.6% (mean 2.3%) of H's energy in any of the eight cells lies outside that span. "Generated independently of the route construction" (main.tex:329), "generated independently of the route field" (main.tex:340), "independent response operator" (main.tex:961, supplementary.tex:3201) should be reworded to "generated by the cable physics rather than sampled from the tested dictionary", the ancestry-piecewise-constant structure should be stated, and the "17.6% outside direct ancestry support" statistic (supplementary.tex:3209-3210) should be qualified because that energy is itself constant on sibling subtrees. The reported capture ordering (subtree > shuffle, subtree ≈ depth bins) is correct and already limits the fine-topology claim.
- **Evidence:** Analytic identity (see M012); Methods 960-963 define H as the adjoint-ratio field; Fig 7C behaviour matches the prediction.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.92):** Text checked: main.tex:329 ("target generated independently of the route construction ... not synthesized from the selected routes"), main.tex:340 Fig 7C caption ("generated independently of the route field"), main.tex:961-963 (H = finite-differenced exact log-gradient after focal shunt, soma voltage restored), supplementary.tex:3200-3212 ("independent response operator ... placed 17.6% of its weighted energy ... outside direct ancestry support") and Table S24 footnote at supplementary.tex:4992-4994. No sentence anywhere acknowledges the ancestry-piecewise-constant structure (grep "piecewise" in main+supplement: no hits).

Code checked: journal/scripts/analyze_reciprocal_routing_controls.py lines 115-180 (exact_reciprocal_response) and journal/code/reconstructed_tree/run_focal_shunting_credit_perturbation.py lines 60-122 (conductance_system builds a symmetric tree Laplacian+diagonal M; exact_e_gradient returns adjoint_i * (E_e - V_i) with adjoint = M^{-T} e_soma * (V_soma - target)). Since V_soma is restored by solve_with_soma_clamp and target_output is fixed, column k of H = (1/dose)[log|G'_{soma,i}/G_{soma,i}| + log|(E_e-V'_i)/(E_e-V_i)|]. For a tree-structured symmetric M, Sherman-Morrison plus the separator identity G_{ab}=G_{ac}G_{cb}/G_cc gives G'_{soma,i}/G_{soma,i} = 1 - d G_kk/(1+d G_kk) for i in subtree(k) and 1 - d G_{ck}^2/(G_cc(1+d G_kk)) for i whose path diverges at ancestor c, i.e. exactly a combination of nested-subtree indicators along k's ancestral path.

Recomputation (notes/verify-M072/decompose_H.py, decomp.csv, capture_by_field.csv; I re-ran the authors' own functions on source_data/figure3/segment_metrics.csv with the script defaults e_scale=i_scale=0.35, dose=1e-3, E_e=1, E_I=-0.2, all 8 cells): (a) adjoint-ratio term HG has residual energy outside the span of nested-subtree indicators along the shunted segment's ancestral path of 1e-23 to 1e-22 in every cell (exact identity). (b) The driving-force term HD, which the finding excluded, is also nearly in that span (residual 0.2-3.5%). (c) The full H used in Fig 7C/Table S24 has 0.19%, 1.6%, 2.0%, 2.4%, 2.7%, 3.0%, 3.3%, 3.6% of energy outside that span (mean 2.3%, max 3.6%); restricting the basis to inhibitory-bearing ancestors (the dictionary's candidate columns) plus a constant gives the same medians. So the supplement's "17.6% outside direct ancestry support" understates the coupling: that off-support energy sits as constants on sibling subtrees, still inside the nested-indicator family the dictionary is built from. (d) Reproduced K=8 captures with the script's dictionaries: subtree 0.458, depth bins 0.445, random 0.364, shuffle 0.251 (matches Table S24 0.458/0.445/0.363/0.249), and the same ordering holds for HG and HD separately, confirming the finding's stated behaviour. Aside noted but not part of this finding: HG and HD have larger energy than H and a large negative cross term (HG 71.5, HD 30.1, H 19.6 in cell ...651419), i.e. the shunt-induced increase in (E_e - V) nearly cancels the adjoint attenuation.
- **Suggestion:** Replace with 'generated by a different operator (cable Green's function) not fitted to the routes' and note the ancestry-piecewise-constant structure; optionally add driving-force-only or non-branch-point shunt targets.

#### M073 — Standard-calibration numbers in Fig 8F/main text are at an unstated ~0.08 nS dose, an order of magnitude below one GABAergic synapse
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:360, 369 F, 1031-1038; Table S26
- **Claim:** Fig 8F and main.tex:360 report the standard-calibration (R_a=150, R_m=15,000) shunt contrast at a relative dose whose absolute magnitude is never stated: in the Fig 8F source data the unit relative-local dose is median 0.049 nS (original eight; IQR 0.02-0.08) and 0.055 nS (45-cell cohort), roughly 10-20x below a single GABAergic synapse, and because the dose is tied to local leak it spans ~0.02-2.7 nS (~100x) along F's axial/leak x-axis, so regime and absolute dose covary. The dose definition itself is stated (Methods 1006-1007, caption C), and the SI fixed-nS analysis (supplementary.tex:3486-3494; recomputed exactly) shows the R_m ordering survives at fixed 0.5 nS (0.0104/0.0073/-0.0039) and 5 nS (0.0974/0.0741/0.0061, CI includes zero), so the qualitative conclusion is robust; but these contrasts, and the fact that at ~20 nS (input-normalized dose 4, no background) the R_m=15,000 contrast returns (+0.066, 7/8 cells), appear only in the SI. Caption F and Table S28 (not S26) should state the nS dose per regime, and line 360 should quote the fixed-nS contrasts so the 'standard calibration' null is not read as a physiological-dose result.
- **Evidence:** physical_cable_sensitivity/summary.json focal_dose definition; site_outcomes relative_local dose 1 medians 0.076/1.14/3.81 nS; cell_electrotonic_ratios median_leak 0.066 nS.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Text checked: main.tex:360 (standard-calibration numbers -0.0019/+0.0024, "Lower membrane resistance increased the contrast by more than an order of magnitude (Fig 8F)"), main.tex:369 caption F (x-axis = cohort-median axial/leak ratio, curve labels = R_m; no dose or nS mentioned), caption C ("Passive dose is relative to baseline local conductance"), main.tex:1006-1007 ("Shunt doses were 0.25, 0.5, 1 and 2 times local membrane-plus-synaptic conductance"), main.tex:1031-1040 (nS calibration; "Fixed nS, local-loading and local-input-conductance doses ... are specified in SI S9"), supplementary.tex:3455-3494 and Table tab:physical_focal at supplementary.tex:5028-5054 (compiled as Table S28, not S26 — S26 is the ancestry-dictionary table; supp pdftotext lines 8062/8098). pdftotext of figure_08.pdf panel F: labels "Electrotonic boundary", "standard passive R_m = 15,000", curve labels 300...30,000; no nS. Source data recomputed: (1) Fig 8F dataset source_data/physical_cable_sensitivity/focal_localization.csv.gz, focal-shunt rows at dose 1: median delta_conductance = 0.0487 nS (original eight, Ra150_Rm15000; IQR 0.0225-0.0807, 10th-90th pct 0.012-0.128), 0.0545 nS (v661, Rm15000), 0.244 nS (Rm3000), 0.731 nS (Rm1000), 2.44/2.72 nS (Rm300), 0.024 nS (Rm30000): a ~100x nS range along F's x-axis. summary.json focal_dose = "one times local leak plus excitatory plus inhibitory conductance, excluding axial coupling". cell_electrotonic_ratios.csv cohort-median leak at Rm15000 = 0.0855 nS (orig eight), 0.0712 nS (v661); the reviewer's 0.066 nS is a single cell's row. (2) The reviewer's 0.076/1.14/3.81 nS come from source_data/focal_selectivity_phase1/site_outcomes.csv.gz relative_local dose 1 pooled over background multipliers 0/1/4 (I reproduce exactly 0.076/1.142/3.808 when pooling); with background 0 they are 0.037/0.549/1.83 nS. So the numbers are from the SI matrix, not the Fig 8F data, and overstate the Fig 8F dose ~1.5x, but the order of magnitude (~0.05 nS, i.e. 10-20x below a ~0.5-1 nS GABA-A synapse) holds. (3) Recomputed SI fixed-nS and input-normalized contrasts (cell means, 5000 cell bootstraps) match supplementary.tex:3486-3494 exactly: 0.5 nS -> 0.0104/0.0073/-0.0039; 5 nS -> 0.0974/0.0741/0.0061 [-0.0125, 0.0234]; unit input-conductance dose at Rm15000 -> -0.0004 (bg 0), 0.1210 (bg 1), 0.2587 (bg 4). Additionally, input-normalized dose 4 at Rm15000 with no background (median 20.7 nS) gives +0.0661 [0.030, 0.101], 7/8 cells positive — the standard-calibration effect is dose-conditional at large doses, which the main text does not say. Refuted sub-claim: the dose *definition* is stated (Methods 1006-1007, caption C, SI 3477-3479); only the resulting absolute nS magnitude and its covariation with R_m are unstated. The conclusion of F (R_m ordering, ~0 at R_m=15,000) survives at fixed 0.5 and 5 nS, so the confound does not overturn the result. Could not view the rendered figure image (Read tool hook timeout); relied on pdftotext of the panel.
- **Suggestion:** State dose definition and nS range in caption F and Table S26; quote the fixed-nS and input-normalized contrasts at 360; consider re-plotting F at matched κr_k.

#### M075 — Active-channel parameters promised in 'SI S9' are not in the SI
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** reproducibility
- **Location:** main.tex:1042-1051; supplementary.tex:3503-3512
- **Claim:** Methods (main.tex:1050-1051) promises that SI S9 gives the active-channel equations and density distributions, but SI S9 (supplementary.tex:3501-3523) states only the generic current form I_a = g_a h_a(V)(V−E_a) and three acceptance thresholds; the gating function h_a(V) = σ((V−V½)/s)^p, the per-family reversal potentials, half-activations, slopes, gate powers and median densities (Na 0.06×leak, E 1.70, V½ 0.43, s 0.085, p 3; K 0.10, −0.30, 0.50, 0.14, 4; Ca 0.025, 2.40, 0.57, 0.10, 2; HCN 0.05, 0.50, −0.21, −0.12, 1; NMDA 0.15×excitatory, 1.0, 0.20, 0.16, 1), the log-normal SDs (0.35 global, 0.25 segment) and the accepted voltage range (−0.75 to 1.75) exist only in configs/focal_selectivity/active_linearization_ensemble.json, which is in the reviewer software archive but is not named or cited in the text. Add an SI table (and the h_a form) and cite it from Methods.
- **Evidence:** Values only in configs/focal_selectivity/active_linearization_ensemble.json (Na 0.06×leak, E 1.70, V½ 0.43, slope 0.085, power 3; K, Ca, HCN, NMDA likewise; SDs 0.35/0.25).
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** main.tex:1042-1051 (Methods, active sensitivity): "Channel parameters were fixed in the protocol; lognormal global and segment multipliers varied conductance densities ... SI S9 gives channel equations, density distributions and acceptance criteria." SI section numbering checked: "Focal shunting perturbations" (supplementary.tex:3290) is the 9th numbered \section, so "S9" resolves correctly. supplementary.tex:3501-3512: Na/K/Ca/HCN/NMDA families "had fixed reversal potentials, half-activation voltages, slopes and gate powers specified in the predefined parameter file. Global and segment-level maximal conductances were drawn independently from prespecified log-normal distributions." No values follow. supplementary.tex:3515-3523 states acceptance criteria but only as "all voltages to lie in the specified range" (range never stated), residual < 1e-10, symmetrized-Jacobian eigenvalue > 1e-8, and gives only the generic form I_a = g_a h_a(V)(V−E_a); the gating function h_a(V) is never defined (grep for h_a/Boltzmann/v_half/V_{1/2} in main.tex and supplementary.tex returns only lines 3519-3522). Table tab:active_focal (supplementary.tex:4484-4509) is results only (localization/energy ratios), no parameters. No other SI table or the six \input methods fragments (grep half|reversal|lognormal|slope|power across journal/supplementary/*_methods.tex) contain them. The parameter file is not cited by path anywhere (contrast SI 1307 and 2385, which cite configs by path). Verified the reviewer's numbers against /n/holylabs/kempner_dev/Users/hsafaai/Code/dendritic-modeling/drafts/dendritic-local-learning/journal/configs/focal_selectivity/active_linearization_ensemble.json: Na {reference leak, median_scale 0.06, reversal 1.70, v_half 0.43, slope 0.085, power 3}; K {0.10, −0.30, 0.50, 0.14, 4}; Ca {0.025, 2.40, 0.57, 0.10, 2}; HCN {0.05, 0.50, −0.21, −0.12, 1}; NMDA {reference excitatory, 0.15, 1.0, 0.20, 0.16, 1}; channel_global_log_sd 0.35, channel_spatial_log_sd 0.25; solver minimum_voltage −0.75, maximum_voltage 1.75. Implementing code (journal/scripts/run_focal_selectivity_active_ensemble.py:43-49, 126-137) shows h_a(V) = [σ((V−V½)/s)]^p with the density referenced to local leak (or excitatory conductance for NMDA, floored at 0.05× a floor value) times exp(0.35 Z_global) × exp(0.25 Z_segment) — none of this functional form is in the manuscript. Mitigation: main.tex Code availability (~1212-1218) says the reviewer software archive contains "versioned configurations," and the file is present in journal/submission/software_release/article_analysis/configs/focal_selectivity/, so reviewers can in principle recover the values, but the manuscript text makes a specific promise ("SI S9 gives channel equations, density distributions") that S9 does not fulfil.
- **Suggestion:** Add an SI table of these values and cite it from Methods 1050.

#### M076 — n=7 percentile bootstrap drives 'lower than both controls' while exact Wilcoxon for subtree vs shuffle is P=0.11
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** statistics
- **Location:** main.tex:394, 396, 1096; supplementary.tex:3775-3781; Fig 9C
- **Claim:** At main.tex:394 and Fig 9C the frozen-profile "subtree update match was lower than both controls" rests, for the shuffle contrast, solely on a 7-target percentile/BCa bootstrap (CI [-0.37,-0.04]); the archived exact Wilcoxon (P=0.109, 5/7 negative), an exact sign-flip test (P=0.109) and a t-interval ([-0.43,+0.02]) do not exclude zero, and the subtree-random contrast is only marginal (6/7, P=0.047). This is inconsistent with Methods (main.tex:1139-1140) and supplement (3701-3702), which state paired comparisons used Wilcoxon tests, yet no P is reported for any Fig 9C contrast. The within-span-oracle contrasts at main.tex:1096 / supplementary.tex:3775-3781, however, are supported by the rank test (1/7 positive, exact Wilcoxon P=0.047 for both shuffle and random), so the reviewer's location list overreaches there. Fix: report exact Wilcoxon P and positive-target counts beside each seven-target interval in Fig 9C/Table S(functional_full_tree), and soften line 394 to "lower than random routes (6/7, P=0.047) and lower on average than shuffles (5/7, P=0.11)". The manuscript's overall conclusion (no subtree advantage) is unaffected.
- **Evidence:** fulltree_boundary summary.json: topology−shuffle −0.207, CI [−0.372,−0.041], Wilcoxon 0.109 (5/7); topology−random P 0.047 (6/7); NMSE topology−exact CI [0.013,0.080], P 0.078.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked main.tex:388-394 ("Subtree update match was lower than both controls"), main.tex:1096, main.tex:1139-1140 (Methods: "Wilcoxon signed-rank tests were used for paired small-cell cohorts"), supplementary.tex:3701-3702 ("Paired method comparisons used two-sided Wilcoxon tests"), 3760-3781 and the Fig 9C caption (main.tex:407: "diamonds/whiskers are means and 95% target-bootstrap intervals"). Source data: source_data/fulltree_boundary/output/summary.json gives update_capture topology-vs-shuffle mean -0.2070, percentile CI [-0.3717,-0.0408], negative_cells 5/7, wilcoxon_two_sided_p 0.109375; topology-vs-random -0.1262, 6/7, P 0.046875; NMSE topology-vs-exact CI [0.0126,0.0800], 5/7, P 0.078125 -- all matching the reviewer. Recomputed from cell_method_means.csv (7 targets): subtree-shuffle update-match diffs [-0.381,+0.004,-0.107,-0.483,-0.097,-0.489,+0.103]; 20,000-draw percentile CI [-0.373,-0.044]; BCa [-0.377,-0.051]; t-interval [-0.430,+0.016]; t-test P=0.064; exact Wilcoxon P=0.109; exact sign-flip P=0.109. Subtree-random: t-CI [-0.244,-0.008], Wilcoxon 0.047. NMSE subtree-exact: t-CI [-0.001,0.090], Wilcoxon 0.078. Fig 9C is drawn by scripts/build_main_figure_09.py: bootstrap() at lines 195-203 is np.quantile of resampled means (plain percentile); panel_native_effects (1515-1560) reads review_evidence_reanalysis/functional_native_contrasts.csv (shuffle row: -0.2070, CI [-0.371,-0.046], positive_targets 2) and draws no P values; pdftotext of figure_09.pdf shows no P annotations. No Wilcoxon P for any Fig 9C contrast appears in main.tex or supplementary.tex (grep for 0.109/5/7/six of seven). Refuting part: the numbers at main.tex:1096 and supplementary.tex:3775-3781 are the within-span ORACLE contrasts, not the frozen-profile ones; from fulltree_within_span_oracle/cell_metrics.csv (mode trialwise_update_oracle) subtree-shuffle diffs [-0.381,-0.041,-0.109,-0.521,-0.120,-0.495,+0.098], 1/7 positive, exact Wilcoxon P=0.047, t-CI [-0.447,-0.001]; subtree-random 1/7 positive, P=0.047, t-CI [-0.264,-0.018]. So the rank test does support those two sentences (marginally).
- **Suggestion:** Report exact Wilcoxon P beside each seven-target interval; soften to 'lower than random (6/7, P=0.047) and lower on average than shuffles (5/7, P=0.11)'; consider t/BCa intervals.

#### M078 — The imposed-alignment control is not a sensitivity analysis for the measured-response null; minimum detectable effect never stated
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:400-402, Fig 9D,E; supplementary.tex:3868-3915; Methods 1109-1116
- **Claim:** Fig 9D/E is an imposed-alignment sufficiency control (eight initial cells, eight-route dictionary, oracle coefficients; the subtree capture curve equals a by construction with zero-width bands in alignment_controlled_curves.csv), and the authors already label it as such (main.tex 400-402; SI 3905-3915). It is not, and is not claimed to be, a sensitivity analysis for the measured-response null. What is genuinely missing is a same-pipeline positive control: no synthetic subtree-confined target has been run through the frozen-profile complete-tree learning pipeline (SI 3700-3795; fulltree_boundary/report.md), and no minimum detectable effect is stated (main.tex 1160: no power calculation). The NMSE sensitivity is asymmetric: the subtree-vs-shuffle interval excludes advantages > 0.032 NMSE (~72% of the 0.044 exact-restricted gap), but the subtree-vs-random interval excludes > 0.011 (~25% of the gap), and the update-match endpoint does resolve dictionary differences of ~0.13 with intervals excluding zero (fulltree_within_span_oracle). Recommendation stands: add a synthetic subtree-aligned target through the same frozen-profile pipeline and state the smallest NMSE/update-match advantage the seven-target design would detect; moving D/E to SI (review item 33, recorded as "stays") is optional.
- **Evidence:** alignment_controlled_curves.csv credit_capture 0.2, 0.4,... zero-width; Table S9 topology-vs-shuffle CI [−0.0207,0.0321]; review item 33 recommended moving D/E to SI.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked in the snapshot: main.tex lines 400-402 (imposed-alignment paragraph: eight initial cells, eight-route dictionary, g(a)=sqrt(a)u_par+sqrt(1-a)u_perp, "Subtree capture equals a by construction", quadratic objective in Supp Fig S5, "controlled sufficiency result... not an empirical estimate of alignment"); Fig 9 caption line 406 (D/E: eight reconstructions, "equals a by construction", "use no visual responses"); Methods lines 1109-1116 (a in {0,.2,.4,.6,.8,1}, 40 streams x 256 fields, positive diagonal quadratic curvature 0.5-1.5, oracle projected descent); supplementary.tex 3868-3915 ("coefficients are oracle projections... conditional positive control, not independent evidence"). Fig 9 text layer (pdftotext) confirms D = "Imposed alignment", E = "Alignment manipulation check" plotting field-energy capture vs imposed a; build_main_figure_09.py line 228 reads alignment_controlled_curves.csv. Note: Fig 9E itself shows capture only; the diagonal quadratic objective enters only Supp Fig S5, not the main panel. CSV source_data/alignment_controlled/alignment_controlled_curves.csv rows "morphology-selected paths": capture 0.2 [0.19999999999999998, 0.20000000000000004], 0.4 [0.39999999999999997, 0.4000000000000001], ... 1.0 [1,1] -- zero-width bands confirmed. Frozen-profile pipeline: SI 3700-3795 and source_data/fulltree_boundary/report.md describe exact/topology/random/shuffle only; report.md states explicitly "This analysis does not reuse those [route-generated positive] fields"; grep of scripts/ and source_data/fulltree_* for "positive control|synthetic|planted" returned nothing. So no synthetic subtree-confined target was run through the complete-tree learning pipeline -- confirmed. The only route-generated positive control in the paper is the capture analysis of Fig 7D (main 331), a different pipeline. Minimum detectable effect: no statement anywhere; main.tex 1160 "No prospective parametric power calculation was performed"; main 398 and SI 3863-3864 "do not estimate power under unknown missing partners". Numbers: Table (tab:functional_full_tree, SI 4534-4557) exact 0.7877, topology 0.8321, random 0.8333, shuffle 0.8391; topology-vs-shuffle 0.0070 [-0.0207, 0.0321]; topology-vs-random 0.0013 [-0.0090, 0.0113]. Gap exact-topology = 0.0444; 0.0321/0.0444 = 0.72, so the reviewer's "~70%" holds for the shuffle contrast only. Against random routes the interval excludes NMSE advantages > 0.0113, i.e. ~25% of the gap -- the reviewer cited the wider of the two intervals. Also, the update-match endpoint is not underpowered: fulltree_within_span_oracle/paired_ancestry_contrasts.csv gives ancestry-minus-random -0.126 [-0.192, -0.027] (frozen) and -0.141 [-0.213, -0.039] (oracle), intervals excluding zero, so the pipeline demonstrably resolves dictionary differences of ~0.13 in update match. Review item 33 (COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md line 110) did recommend moving D/E to SI; REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION_20260905.md line 64 records "D/E alignment control stays"; REVIEW_COMPLETION line 82 marks item 33 "Addressed". Figure Read tool failed (hook timeout); artwork verified via text layer and builder script only.
- **Suggestion:** Move D/E to SI; add a same-pipeline positive control with synthetic subtree-confined trial-varying targets; state minimum detectable NMSE and update-match effects.

#### M079 — Francioni reanalysis: post hoc one-sided P values and inclusion in the evidence ladder overstate a re-expression of a published result
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** statistics
- **Location:** supplementary.tex:3918-3941, Table S33; main.tex:402, 1118-1124; Fig 9F
- **Claim:** The six-animal Francioni reanalysis re-tests the source paper's own Extended Data 13E per-animal contrasts (the 'signed separation' column is read directly from the source sheet and equals P+ minus P-), with a prediction the SI states was formulated after publication (supplementary.tex 3938-3939). Table S33 nonetheless reports one-sided exact sign-flip P values (0.0313, 0.0156, 0.0156; verified), two of which sit at the n=6 floor of 1/64 and therefore convey nothing beyond the 6/6 sign counts already in the table. Fig 9F places this reanalysis as the bottom rung of the paper's evidence ladder labeled only 'coordinate δu / retrospective (6 animals) / S17' (status: conditional), without indicating on the figure or in its caption that the underlying data are a published third-party experiment. The paper's text hedges appropriately ('re-expression', 'retrospective', 'does not demonstrate within-arbor teaching signals'), so the issue is presentation rather than a false claim: drop the P values or mark them descriptive, and attribute the 9F rung to 'published data (Francioni et al. 2026)'.
- **Evidence:** SI 3936-3940 'retrospective test'; Table S33 P 0.0313, 0.0156, 0.0156; sign/common decomposition is algebraic.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Snapshot supplementary.tex 3924 ("The following reanalysis tests that prediction"), 3935-3940 ("one-sided exact random-sign test enumerated all 2^6 sign assignments ... This is a retrospective test because the prediction was formulated after publication"); Table S33 at 5157-5176 (caption "Retrospective six-animal signed-credit reanalysis"; footnote "Exact one-sided random-sign permutation P values were 0.0313, 0.0156, and 0.0156"; 83.7%/16.3% energy). Recomputed from journal/source_data/animal_learning_francioni/animal_signed_contrasts.csv by full 2^6 enumeration: P+ = 0.03125 (5/6 positive), P- = 0.015625 (6/6), separation = 0.015625 (6/6); 0.015625 = 1/64 is the minimum attainable one-sided P for n=6, so two of three P values are saturated sign counts. Signed/common pooled energy recomputed = 0.8368/0.1632. The script scripts/analyze_francioni_signed_credit.py reads sheet 'Ext 13E' columns 0-2 and asserts col 2 == P+ - P- (my check: True), i.e. the 'signed separation' endpoint is the source paper's own Extended Data 13E column, not a new endpoint; the common/signed split is a 45-degree rotation of those two columns. Fig 9F (pdftotext of figures/main/figure_09.pdf; builder scripts/build_main_figure_09.py EVIDENCE_RUNGS) is a four-rung 'Evidence boundary' ladder whose bottom rung reads 'coordinate δu / retrospective (6 animals) / S17' with status 'conditional' (amber); the rung and the Fig 9 caption (main.tex 407) do not name Francioni et al.; attribution appears only in body text main.tex 402 and in SI captions 4052/5159. The Fig 3H conceptual evidence map (source_data/credit_phase_plane/points.csv) has no Francioni row. Mitigation: main.tex 402 calls it a 're-expression', main.tex 407 says 'Neither ... nor the retrospective animal analysis demonstrates within-arbor teaching signals', SI 4052 says 'retrospective external comparison ... consistent with'. I could not access the Francioni paper to check whether it already reports a per-animal statistic for Ext 13E.
- **Suggestion:** Drop the P values, present S33 as descriptive consistency; label the 9F rung 'published (Francioni et al. 2026)' or remove it.

#### M080 — Methods sentence attributes new-cohort regrets (0.0814/0.1007) to the original 12,800-fit experiment; values unlocatable in SI
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** main.tex:857
- **Claim:** In main.tex:857 the regrets 0.0814/0.1007 are the original scalar selector's fixed-cache, 256-update regrets re-evaluated in the new 20-seed, 25,600-fit cohort (fresh/policy_summary.csv original_scalar: 0.081429/0.100691), whereas the original 12,800-fit experiment cited in the preceding sentence gave 0.0806/0.1040 (SI lines 1466-1467). The main text does not say which cohort the 0.0814/0.1007 values come from, and these numbers appear nowhere in the SI text or tables (only as a plotted curve in Fig. S38A,B). The sentence should state the cohort explicitly ("On the twenty new seeds, the original score's fixed-cache regret was 0.0814/0.1007; the frozen moment forecast reduced it to 0.000064/0.000042") and the SI fragment (morphology_followup_methods.tex ~line 337) should quote the 0.0814/0.1007 values so the main-text numbers are locatable.
- **Evidence:** morphology_finite_horizon/summaries/fresh/policy_summary.csv original_scalar 0.081429/0.100691 (fixed_cache, 256); prospective_morphology_selection/policy_summary.csv 0.080551/0.104039; grep 0.0814/0.1007 in SI returns nothing.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** main.tex:857 reads "In the original 12,800-fit experiment, initialization utility ... selected poor 256-update endpoints (Supplementary Fig. S35). A forecast ... was fixed before 25,600 fresh fits. When training reused a fixed example cache, regret ... fell from 0.0814/0.1007 to 0.000064/0.000042". Recomputed from source_data/morphology_finite_horizon/summaries/fresh/policy_summary.csv (fresh cohort, regime fixed_cache, checkpoint 256, rank=all, 20 seed blocks / 320 tasks): original_scalar = 0.081429 (feedback_only) and 0.100691 (joint_forward_feedback); gaussian_plugin_sgd = 0.0000642/0.0000417; rank_only 0.00824/0.00820. So 0.0814/0.1007 are the original scalar selector re-scored on the NEW 25,600-fit cohort, not the original experiment. The original 12,800-fit experiment (source_data/prospective_morphology_selection/policy_summary.csv, moment_selector) gives 0.080551/0.104039, which is what the SI states at supplementary.tex:1466-1467 ("0.08055 ... 0.10404"). The fresh_iid regime gives yet another value (0.08160 feedback-only). Grep of supplementary.tex and all six *_methods.tex fragments for 0.0814/0.1007 (and 0.081/0.100) returns nothing; morphology_followup_methods.tex:337-340 quotes only 0.000064/0.000042 and 0.000134/0.000197 and refers to "the original scalar score" without a number. The only place the 0.0814/0.1007 values exist in the SI is graphically (Fig. S38A,B plots an 'original scalar' curve per rank; build_morphology_followup_figures.py METHODS list includes original_scalar), not as text. Nuance: the sentence does not literally say the 0.0814/0.1007 belong to the 12,800-fit run (the fixed-cache regime exists only in the new cohort), but the preceding sentence names the original experiment and no cohort is attached to the "fell from" values, so a reader cross-checking against SI line 1466 sees a 0.0806/0.1040 vs 0.0814/0.1007 mismatch with no explanation.
- **Suggestion:** Rewrite: 'On twenty new seeds (25,600 fits), the original score's fixed-cache regret was 0.0814/0.1007; the frozen moment forecast reduced it to 0.000064/0.000042'; cite the Source Data table in the SI fragment; cite 0.0806/0.1040 separately if wanted.

- **Status:** confirmed

- **Claim:** The Methods paragraph for the primary MNIST feedback ladder (main.tex 568-578) gives no training hyperparameters at all — no optimizer, learning rates (TopK 1.5e-3, coupling 7e-4, reactivation 5e-4, decoder 1.5e-3), batch size (256), epochs (180), gradient clip (5), contact counts (40 E / 20 I per compartment), input dimension (784 flattened, unnormalized), positivity transform (softplus), decoder (linear 128x10) or MNIST train/validation split (48,000/12,000); these appear only in SI S5 (lines 1729-1746, 1841-1851) and Tables S10-S11, to which the main text does explicitly point (588-591). The main text also describes reactivation only as "the identity when disabled" (476-477) and never states that the MNIST ladder used a learned [tanh(m(V-b))+1]/2 nonlinearity with architecture-specific initialization (SI 1819-1832, Table S10). The CIFAR-10 sentence (585-588) likewise omits its batch size (256). All values are confirmed against the live configs, so the fix is purely textual: add two sentences with these values to 568-578 and "batch 256" to the CIFAR sentence.
- **Evidence:** SI 1727-1746 (784 inputs, 40 E/20 I contacts, softplus, 128×10), 1840-1851 (batch 256, 180 epochs, Adam 1.5e-3/7e-4/5e-4/1.5e-3, clip 5), Table S10 reactivation form.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.92):** Snapshot main.tex 568-578 (primary MNIST ladder paragraph): states only "128-soma [3,3] shunting and raw-additive networks", "Architectures, optimizers, decoders, schedules and initialization were matched", validation-based checkpoint selection, the strict-scalar reduction and the 640/2,413,312 fallback exception. No optimizer name, learning rates, batch size, epoch count, contact counts, input dimension (784), positivity transform (softplus) or decoder size appear. A grep of main.tex for batch|epoch|softplus|784|contacts|learning rate returns no hit inside 554-600 except the CIFAR-10 sentence at 585-588 (Adam 1e-3, at most 200 epochs, patience 40; no batch size) and the Fashion-MNIST split at 580-582 (48,000/12,000) — the MNIST split itself is never given in the main text. Main 588-591 does cross-reference "SI S5 and Tables S10--S12" for "complete input transforms, local learning rates, seeds and selected configurations", so the omission is disclosed rather than hidden. Reactivation: main 476-477 says f_n "was the identity when disabled"; the only other main-text reactivation mentions are CIFAR "reactivation calibration" (586), "identity reactivation" for the physical-depth study (875) and the conductance toy (829). Nowhere does the main text say the MNIST ladder used a learned parametric tanh; that appears only in SI 1819-1832 (eq. s_parametric_tanh, analytical init for shunting, 0.1/0.9 quantile init for raw-additive) and Table S10 row "Reactivation" (SI 4573). All SI values cited by the reviewer check out: SI 1729-1746 (784 inputs, 48k/12k split, 128 neurons, [3,3], 40 E/20 I TopK contacts per compartment, softplus, linear 128x10 decoder, 2,414,602 params); SI 1841-1851 (batch 256, 180 epochs, Adam beta 0.9/0.999 eps 1e-8, no weight decay, LRs 1.5e-3/7e-4/5e-4/1.5e-3, clip 5); Table S11 (SI 4580-4600) same. Table numbering by \begin{table} order: SI 2206 = S1, 4559 = S10 (regular_architecture), 4580 = S11 (regular_training), so the reviewer's "Table S10" is correctly located. The suggestion to add batch 256 to the CIFAR sentence is also supported: SI 1910-1911 states "batch size 256" for the CIFAR-10 ladder and configs/cifar10_additive_feedback_ladder_confirmatory.yaml line 101 has batch_size: 256, while main 585-588 omits it.

#### M082 — Matched-width fallback described twice, and its parameter denominator is undefined in the main text
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:573-578, 593
- **Claim:** The matched-width fallback is described in two partially overlapping main-text paragraphs (main.tex:570-578 and 593), and the denominator "2,413,312 core parameters" (main.tex:575) is used but never defined in either the main text or the SI (supplementary.tex:444 also uses it without definition). The count is arithmetically correct: it equals Table S10's 2,414,602 total trainable parameters minus the 1,290-parameter 128x10 linear decoder (and equals the 2,408,448 candidate synaptic parameters plus 4,864 coupling and reactivation parameters). Fix by merging the two paragraphs and adding one clause such as "core (non-decoder) parameters; 2,414,602 including the decoder, Table S10".
- **Evidence:** Table S10: 2,408,448 candidate synaptic; 2,414,602 total trainable.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** main.tex lines 570-578 (primary MNIST ladder paragraph) and line 593 (standalone paragraph "The matched-width fallback requires separate interpretation") both state that the fallback retained the 128-coordinate somatic vector only at the 128-wide stage and both cite the 640-parameter exception; the second paragraph adds the interpretation (improved training, excluded from primary contrast) and the first adds the Table S16 equivalence-margin audit. So the overlap is real but partial, not a verbatim duplicate. On the number: "2,413,312 core parameters" (main.tex:575) appears nowhere else in main.tex and "core" is never defined there. In the SI it appears once, at supplementary.tex:444, and that sentence also uses the term without defining it (the reviewer's "defined only at SI 444" is slightly generous). Table S10 (supplementary.tex:4562-4576, confirmed as "Table S10" in the compiled PDF via pdftotext) gives 2,408,448 candidate synaptic and 2,414,602 total trainable; SI 1741-1746 repeats those. Recomputation: 2,414,602 - 2,413,312 = 1,290 = 128x10 + 10, exactly the linear decoder, so "core" = all trainable parameters excluding the decoder. Cross-check: 2,413,312 - 2,408,448 = 4,864 = 384 branch-to-soma couplings + 1,152 distal-to-proximal couplings (9x128) + 3,072 dendritic reactivation gain/threshold (12 compartments x 128 x 2) + 256 somatic gain/threshold, consistent with the architecture in Table S10. Table S16 = tab:strict_scalar_audit (supplementary.tex:4745; compiled PDF confirms "Table S16"), so the main-text pointer is correct. The number is therefore internally consistent but unexplained; a reader sees three different denominators (2,408,448 / 2,413,312 / 2,414,602) with no stated relation.
- **Suggestion:** Merge or move to SI with a pointer; define 'core parameters' or quote against 2,414,602.

#### M084 — Update-moment notation differs between main text and the supplement's proposition and notation table
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** consistency
- **Location:** main.tex:166-181, 728-742; supplementary.tex:721-745, 4636-4690 (Table S13)
- **Claim:** The update-moment notation is inconsistent across the two documents and within the main text: main.tex lines 166-178 and 729-732 write the exact gradient, delivered update and its covariance as bold μ, g̃ and Σ_route, whereas the SI proposition (supplementary.tex 723-753, 762-771) and Table S13 (4634-4690) write the same objects as bold g, d and V (with g_i reserved for synaptic conductance and V_n for voltage). Table S13, which main line 452 says "define[s] the notation", contains no entry for μ, g̃ or Σ_route, and none of those symbols occurs anywhere in the supplement. The main text additionally switches to bold g for the exact gradient at lines 779-782. The equations are structurally identical, so this is a readability/consistency defect rather than a scientific error; adopt one symbol set in both documents (or add a one-line mapping in Table S13 and at main 166).
- **Evidence:** Quoted definitions at main 166 and SI 725-729; Table S13 rows for d,m,V,U and w,g,ĝ.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** Main text (snapshot main.tex): line 166 defines "\bm\mu=\nabla\mathcal L(\bm w)" as the exact gradient, "\widetilde{\bm g}" as the delivered update estimator, "\Sigma_{\rm route}" as its covariance; Eqs. at 168-171 and 174-178 use \bm\mu, \bm m, \Sigma_{\rm route}. Methods lines 729-732 (eq:creditoperator) again use \widetilde{\bm g}=M(\bm\mu+\bm\xi), \Sigma_{\rm route}=M\Sigma M^T; Fig 3 caption line 201 uses \bm\mu+\bm\xi. Main line 452: "Supplementary Tables S13 and S14 define the notation and dictionary objects." Supplement (supplementary.tex): lines 723-729 define "\bm g=\nabla\mathcal L(\bm w)", "\bm d" routed update estimator, "V=E[(d-m)(d-m)^T]"; proposition Eqs. at 735-741 and 750-753 use \bm g, \bm m, tr(V); proof/special case at lines 762-771 uses \bm d=M(\bm g+\bm\xi), V=M\Sigma M^T. SI line 56: "Table~\ref{tab:notation} consolidates the notation". Table at 4634-4690: rows "$\bm w$, $\bm g$, $\widehat{\bm g}$ & Parameter vector, exact population gradient, and its unbiased stochastic estimate", "$\bm d$, $\bm m$, $V$, $U$ & Routed-update estimator, its conditional mean, its centered covariance...", "$M$, $\Sigma$, $U(M)$ ... special case $\bm d=M(\bm g+\bm\xi)$", and "$g_i$, $x_i$, $g_ix_i$ & Nonnegative maximal synaptic conductance". pdftotext of supplementary.pdf confirms the caption is numbered "Table S13". grep of the entire supplementary.tex for '\bm\mu', '\widetilde{\bm g}', 'Sigma_{\rm route}' and 'route}' returns zero hits, so none of the main-text symbols appear anywhere in the SI or in Table S13. Additional intra-main inconsistency found: main lines 779-782 (eq:normmatchedprogress) call the exact held-out gradient "\bm g" and the routed one "\widetilde{\bm g}_M", i.e. the main text itself switches from \bm\mu to \bm g for the same object between §R3/Methods (166-176, 729-732) and the retrospective-score paragraph (779-782). Reconciling note: none found (grep for 'main text' phrasing in SI only hits an unrelated line 573).
- **Suggestion:** Adopt one symbol set in both documents; add chosen symbols to Table S13 with a note for legacy Source Data names.
