#### M086 — Lv et al. 2025 (Dendritic Localized Learning) is a local credit-assignment algorithm, mis-grouped as an 'engineered gating architecture'
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:423
- **Claim:** In main.tex:423, Lv et al. 2025 (Dendritic Localized Learning) is a local, backprop-alternative learning algorithm (no weight symmetry, no global error signal, single-phase), not a "nonlinear or engineered gating architecture"; Chavlis & Poirazi 2025 is a structured dendritic ANN architecture trained with backprop, and only gated linear networks and active-dendrite networks fit the gating label. The single sentence should split these into (i) dendritic ANN architectures, (ii) gated/active-dendrite networks, and (iii) dendrite-inspired local learning algorithms, and optionally note where DLL sits relative to the paper's broadcast/K=1 versus exact-path rungs. Also verify that the bib key sezener2021gated (currently Veness et al. 2021, Gated Linear Networks) is the intended reference.
- **Evidence:** Lv et al. abstract (ICML 2025); Chavlis & Poirazi Nat Commun 16:943.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Snapshot main.tex:423 reads: "The framework can be combined with supervised, segregated, predictive and burst-based dendritic mechanisms \citep{urbanczik2014dendritic,...}, cable-learning methods \citep{bicknell2021synaptic} and nonlinear or engineered gating architectures \citep{chavlis2025dendrites,sezener2021gated,iyer2022activedendrites,lv2025dendritic}." This is the only citation of lv2025dendritic in main.tex, supplementary.tex and the six *_methods.tex fragments (grep). references.bib:262-272 identifies the entry as Lv et al., "Dendritic Localized Learning: Toward Biologically Plausible Algorithm", ICML 2025 (PMLR 267:41682-41700). I fetched https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/lv25c.html: the abstract states "we propose Dendritic Localized Learning (DLL), a novel learning algorithm designed to overcome these challenges" (weight symmetry, global error signals, dual-phase training) and evaluates it across MLPs, CNNs and RNNs -- i.e. it is a training rule applied to existing architectures, not an architecture and not a gating scheme. So grouping it under "nonlinear or engineered gating architectures" is inaccurate. Chavlis & Poirazi 2025 (references.bib:252-260, Nat Commun 16:943) is, to my knowledge, a sparse/structured dendritic ANN trained with standard backprop (the Nature page was behind a login redirect, so I could not re-fetch its abstract); "nonlinear ... architectures" fits it loosely, "gating" does not. Sezener/Veness (gated linear networks, references.bib:575-583) and Iyer et al. 2022 (active dendrites) are the only two that match "gating architectures". Minor side note found while checking: the bib key sezener2021gated resolves to Veness et al. 2021 (AAAI) with Sezener as a co-author, not to Sezener et al.'s dendritic gated network preprint -- the authors should confirm this is the intended reference. Impact is limited: a single Discussion clause, no result depends on it, and no other text in main or supplement characterizes DLL.
- **Suggestion:** Split the citation into dendritic ANN architectures, gated/active-dendrite networks, and dendrite-inspired local learning algorithms; optionally relate DLL to the K=1 vs exact-path rungs.

#### M088 — S2 caption contains a literal tab in place of \textbf and an unclosed quotation, rendering as 'extbfE'
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** writing
- **Location:** supplementary.tex:2868; supplementary.pdf p.52
- **Claim:** In the Supplementary Fig. S2 caption (supplementary.tex line 2868; supplementary.pdf p.52), `\textbf{E}` was corrupted into a tab followed by `extbf{E}`, so the PDF prints "The extbfE ordinate", and the opening `` before "additive (legacy)" is never closed with '', leaving an unbalanced open quotation mark in the rendered caption. Both defects are present in the live repository as well. Fix: replace the tab+`extbf{E}` with `\textbf{E}` and append '' after "additive (legacy)".
- **Evidence:** cat -A shows '^Iextbf{E}'; pdftotext confirms; same defect in live repo.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.98):** Snapshot supplementary.tex line 2868 (S2 caption, \label{fig:inherited_competence}): `cat -A` shows the literal text `...so the artwork uses ``additive (legacy). The ^Iextbf{E} ordinate uses the three-factor (3F) local rule...` — a raw tab (^I) precedes `extbf{E}` where `\textbf{E}` was intended, and the opening `` before "additive (legacy)" has no closing ''. `grep -nP '\t'` finds line 2868 as the only tab-containing line in the file. pdftotext of the snapshot supplementary.pdf page 52 prints `uses “additive (legacy). The extbfE ordinate uses the three-factor (3F) local rule` (1 match for 'extbfE' on p.52). The live repo file /n/holylabs/kempner_dev/Users/hsafaai/Code/dendritic-modeling/drafts/dendritic-local-learning/journal/supplementary/supplementary.tex has the identical defect at line 2868 (grep -P '\t' hits only that line; the string "additive (legacy)" appears once with no closing quote).
- **Suggestion:** Replace with '\textbf{E}' and close the quote; add a pre-commit check for raw tabs/unbalanced quotes in captions.

#### M089 — Figures S5-S44 are dumped in one block far from their text and not numbered in order of first citation
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** structure

- **Claim:** Supplementary Figs. S5-S44 are placed as one contiguous block (supplementary.tex 3913-4256; PDF pp.69-107) far from the text that discusses them (e.g. the Fig. S13 experiment is described on p.34 at tex lines 1533-1545 without any cross-reference, while the figure appears on p.77), and figure numbers follow include order rather than first citation: main-text first citations run S4, S30, S8, S13, S14, S24, S29, S16, S32, S31, S28, S43 ... S5, S17, with all 44 citations hard-coded rather than \ref'd. In addition, 22 of the 40 block figures are never cited in the supplement text, and S18 and S21 are cited nowhere at all. Nature Portfolio requires supplementary items numbered in order of first citation (cited as 'Supplementary Fig. 1', not 'S1'); renumber by first main-text citation, cite or drop S18/S21, and add cross-references from the relevant supplement subsections.
- **Evidence:** includegraphics line numbers; page mapping from pdftotext; grep of citation order.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked supplementary.tex \includegraphics/\label lines: S1-S3 at 2860-2876 and S4 at 2914 sit inside the Artificial-tree section (1718-2919); S5 at 3913 (Alignment-controlled section) and S6-S44 at 3974-4253 are all inside the 'External signed-credit' section (3918-4260), i.e. one terminal block. Numbering is by order of \includegraphics (\renewcommand{\thefigure}{S\arabic{figure}} at line 25), not by citation. pdftotext page map of supplementary.pdf (129 pages): captions S1-S4 on pp.51-55; S5-S44 on pp.69-107 contiguously (S13 on p.77). Fig. S13's subject, the state-matched positive-conductance reliability experiment (supplementary.tex 1533-1545), renders on p.34, and that text never \ref's fig:supp_positive_reliability (grep: label at 4025 has zero \ref in the supplement); its only citation is main.tex line 190. Main-text first-citation order (grep of 'Supplementary Fig(s). S..' in main.tex, unique): 4, 30, 8, 13, 14, 24, 29, 16, 32, 31, 28, 43, 36, 37, 44, 41, 40, 42, 35, 38, 39, 10, 27, 12, 20, 25, 33, 11, 22, 34, 5, 17 (lines 143-407), confirming the reported S4, S30, S8, S13, S29, S16 ... sequence. All 44 main-text citations are hard-coded numbers (0 \ref{fig:...} in main.tex), so renumbering must be done by hand. Additional facts found: 22 of S5-S44 (S12, S13, S14, S17, S18, S20, S21, S22, S24, S25, S27, S28, S30, S35-S39, S41-S44) are never cited anywhere in the supplement text, and S18 (label fig:supp_physical_depth_controls, line 4060) and S21 (fig:supp_focal_detail, 4081) are cited in neither the main text nor the supplement. The supplement also uses 'S' for three overlapping namespaces (sections S1-S13, tables S1-S35, figures S1-S44), so 'S13' on pp.3-4/108 is section S13 and on pp.116-117 is Table S13. The exact Nature Communications wording of the citation-order rule could not be fetched (nature.com pages 303-redirect to a cookie gate / 404), so that part rests on the well-known Nature Portfolio SI policy rather than a fetched quote; the structural facts above do not depend on it.
- **Suggestion:** Renumber by first main-text citation and place figures after their subsections or in a terminal ordered section; update inventory and filenames.

#### M091 — Interval ranges with negative endpoints render as '−0.022220–−0.018705'
- **Status:** confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** supplementary.tex:4420-4424, 4446-4449, 4469-4472; footnotes 4990-4992, 5040-5041
- **Claim:** In the supplement, seven bootstrap-interval cells with negative endpoints (Tables S4 lines 4418-4420, S5 lines 4444-4445, S6 lines 4468-4469, S28 line 5040) are typeset as `a--b` and render as "−0.022220–−0.018705" (en dash followed by minus), which is hard to parse; Table S28 (lines 5040-5041) mixes the dash form and the bracket form `[a, b]` in the same column; the footnote at lines 4993-4997 uses `−0.037–0.068`; while Tables S8, S31 and S33 use `[a, b]` and the prose uses "a to b". Three conventions coexist; the main text is unaffected. Adopt one convention (brackets or "to") for all supplementary tables and footnotes.
- **Evidence:** pdftotext lines 7544-7546, 7566-7567, 7582-7583; Tables S8, S31, S33 use brackets.
- **Verifier (confirmed, confidence 0.95):** Checked the tex source and a fresh pdftotext -layout of the snapshot supplementary.pdf. (1) Dash-with-negative-endpoint ranges: supplementary.tex 4418-4420 (Table S4, tab:positive_reliability) e.g. `$-0.022220$--$-0.018705$`; 4444-4445 (Table S5, tab:adaptive_reliability) `$-0.017913$--$-0.014501$`; 4468-4469 (Table S6, tab:same_span_learning) `$-0.3260$--$-0.2313$`; and 5040 (Table S28, tab:physical_focal) `$-0.0043$--0.0068`. Rendered text lines 7544, 7566, 7582 read "−0.022220–−0.018705", "−0.017913–−0.014501", "−0.3260–−0.2313" (en dash immediately followed by a minus sign) — exactly as the reviewer described. Seven occurrences of `--$-` in the supplement in total (grep count). (2) Mixed conventions in one column: Table S28 lines 5040-5041 put `$-0.0043$--0.0068` and `$[-0.00459,-3.47\times10^{-5}]$` in adjacent rows of the same interval column (rendered lines 8106-8107). (3) Other tables use brackets: S8 (4518-4520) `$[-0.2142,0.1466]$`, S31 (inhibitory census, e.g. `$[-21.460,-4.151]$`), S33 (animal credit, `$[-0.2054,-0.0866]$`). The prose uses a third convention, "to" (rendered line 1248: "−0.020404 (−0.022220 to −0.018705)"; line 1300 "−0.3260 to ..."). (4) Footnote at 4993-4997 (below tab:reciprocal_capacity) contains `$-0.037$--0.068`, rendered "−0.037–0.068" (line 8076) — readable but ambiguous with a subtraction; the reviewer's cited lines 4990-4992 are off by ~4 lines but the item exists. The main text (main.tex) has no such occurrences (grep empty), so the issue is confined to the supplement. Note on table numbering: reviewer's "Table S28" is correct — the 28th \label{tab:} in the file is tab:physical_focal at line 5031.
- **Suggestion:** One convention for all tables; never a dash when endpoints are signed; fix footnotes and inline ranges.

#### M095 — Most of the 34 new scripts and six SI methods fragments are outside the test and Makefile audit chain
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** reproducibility
- **Location:** journal/Makefile; journal/tests/; supplementary.tex:1492-1493; morphology_followup_methods.tex:214-218
- **Claim:** The S32–S44 and review-response apparatus (33 top-level scripts, 7 subpackages, six SI methods fragments, five tests and the S32–S44 PDFs) is currently untracked in the paper repository's working tree, and the software release's recorded journal commit (5483817) does not exist in the object database, so the released state cannot be reconstructed from git history. Execution of these scripts is not part of `make audit`: `figures` is not an audit prerequisite, only 4 of the 33 top-level scripts have tests under tests/, and the 36 passing subpackage tests are excluded by pytest.ini (`testpaths = tests`) and cannot even be collected together (three files named test_model.py). tests/test_manuscript_crossrefs.py reads only main.tex, and 11 of the 18 labels defined in the fragments are never referenced anywhere. However, the outputs of every S32–S44 script are hash-pinned in provenance_manifest.tsv and verified by audit_submission.py inside `make audit`, the release archive and Overleaf/submission bundles do include the scripts and fragments, and the manuscript makes no coverage claim, so this is an internal reproducibility-hygiene gap (commit the material, add the subpackage tests to testpaths with unique names, add a figures check to audit, expand \input in the crossref test, prune or reference the dead labels), not a reader-facing error.
- **Evidence:** Only 5 new scripts referenced by tests; analyze_review_evidence.py etc. tests=0, Makefile=0; `figures` target not a prerequisite of `audit`.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked in /n/holylabs/kempner_dev/Users/hsafaai/Code/dendritic-modeling/drafts/dendritic-local-learning/journal (paper repo HEAD 560d5f2 on main at time of check; the checkout is shared and had drifted from the snapshot's git status).

CONFIRMED PARTS. (1) Makefile: `audit: combined layout-audit test reproducibility format-audit citation-audit overlap-audit` then audit_figure_style_lineage.py + audit_submission.py; the `figures` target (`scripts/rebuild_final_publication_figures.py`) is not a prerequisite. (2) tests/test_manuscript_crossrefs.py reads only MAIN = journal/main.tex (three tests: panel order, panel-letter case, equation-order); it never opens supplementary.tex or any \input fragment. Supplementary.tex:1492-1493 inputs morphology_followup_methods and boolean_morphology_methods; morphology_followup_methods.tex:214-218 nests four more fragments. (3) Labels: the six fragments define 18 labels; grepping main.tex + supplementary.tex + all fragments for any occurrence other than the \label itself gives ZERO hits for 11 of them (eq:s_equal_input_sensitivity, eq:s_fresh_sgd_moments, eq:s_interaction_targets, eq:s_tree_minimax_dp, note:centered_cut_theorem, note:constructive_morphology, note:finite_calibration_morphology, note:finite_horizon_morphology, note:interaction_structure, prop:boolean_xor_and, sec:conductance_grouping_bridge) — the reviewer's "9 of 18" undercounts. (4) Git: `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` shows 33 untracked top-level scripts plus 63 untracked files in 7 subpackages (morphology_structure/dynamics/credit/calibration/conductance, boolean_morphology, boolean_theory), all six *_methods.tex fragments, 5 tests, and figures/supplementary/figure_S32..S44 PDFs. Only 4 of the 33 (analyze_fulltree_within_span_oracle, analyze_review_evidence, audit_review_curve_lineage, rebuild_review_visual_corrections) exist in any commit, and that commit (5f0e6cf "Freeze reviewed...") is reachable from no branch (only the .dendritic-review-snapshot worktree). The release archive's METADATA.json records journal_repository_commit 5483817839c1… with clean_at_build=True, but `git cat-file -t` returns "bad object" — that commit is not in the repository. (5) Per-script coverage of the 33: referenced by tests/ = 4 (analyze_review_morphology_uncertainty, combine_manuscript_pdfs, run_prospective_morphology_selection, run_review_coefficient_encoder; their 11 tests pass in 13 s); in Makefile = 2 (combine_manuscript_pdfs via `combined`, rebuild_final_publication_figures via `figures`); invoked by rebuild_final_publication_figures.py = 14. analyze_review_evidence.py, analyze_review_response_baselines.py, audit_review_response_baselines.py, run_review_branch_trajectories.py, run_review_coefficient_hard_readout.py, export_morphology_investigation_sources.py, audit_prospective_morphology_selection.py: tests=0, Makefile=0, rebuild=0. provenance_manifest.tsv names these as the source-data builders for figS32–figS39 (e.g., 122 rows run_prospective_morphology_selection.py, 275 rows export_morphology_investigation_sources.py, 19 rows analyze_review_evidence.py).

REFUTED / OVERSTATED PARTS. (a) "No test exercises": the seven subpackages contain 13 test files; run per directory they pass (structure 5, dynamics 4, credit 2, calibration 11, conductance 5, boolean_morphology 3, boolean_theory 6 = 36 passed). They are excluded from `make test` only because pytest.ini sets `testpaths = tests`, and they cannot be collected in one invocation because three are named test_model.py (pytest "import file mismatch" error). (b) "Outside the audit chain": the outputs of every S32–S44 script are hash-pinned — provenance_manifest.tsv (1574 rows) records builder + SHA-256 for each S32–S44 asset and source file, and `make audit` runs scripts/audit_submission.py --strict-pending whose audit_manifest_rows (lines 187-223) recomputes sha256 of every manifest source. So the products are audited; only execution is not. (c) The reviewer software archive (submission/Dendritic_credit_assignment_software.zip, built 2026-09-05 23:06) does contain the new scripts — verified analyze_review_evidence.py, run_prospective_morphology_selection.py, export_morphology_investigation_sources.py, build_morphology_followup_figures.py, boolean_morphology/experiment.py under both article_analysis/scripts and journal_package/journal/scripts; JOURNAL_SCRIPTS in build_software_release.py lists them and JOURNAL_DIRECTORIES (lines 107-113) includes the seven subpackages. build_overleaf_bundle.py (45-51) and build_submission_bundle.py (100-138) copy the fragments. (d) README.md lines 41-47 document `make figures` as a separate step before `make audit`, and rebuild_final_publication_figures.py lines 120-122 asserts that all 44 SI includes exist. (e) The manuscript text never claims test coverage for these scripts (the only test cited is tests/test_laminar_optimality.py at supplementary.tex:870-871, 970; no scripts/*.py path appears in main or SI), so no reader is misled.
- **Suggestion:** Add a figures-check target to audit; smoke tests for each analyze_review_*/run_review_* script; make crossref test expand \input and assert every SI label is referenced.

### Severity: low

#### M024 — Title promises a positive proposal; the decisive empirical results are boundary results
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex title (~40); 51-52; section titles 137, 344, 373
- **Claim:** The manuscript's biological sections (Figs 7–9) establish structural capacity, a state-conditional modeled transport effect, and a null for endogenous route use in measured responses, and the flagship image-task result (Fig 2) is that one signal per neuron captures nearly all the benefit; readers skimming the title "Dendritic morphology as a dictionary for local credit assignment" may expect a demonstrated biological mechanism. However, the abstract, Introduction and Discussion already disclose these boundaries, the title names a descriptive framework rather than a mechanism, and Figs 3–5, 6 and 8 supply positive conditional evidence (branch selection 35–58 pp; ancestry +1.27 pp at K=4; utility ρ=0.92; shunt contrast +0.067 at Rm=300 in 8/8 cells) — so "constructive evidence is Figs 6–7 only" is inaccurate and the proposed retitle would over-narrow the paper to §R7. At most, the abstract's first sentence or the cover letter could state up front that the biological analyses are capacity/boundary results; a retitle is not warranted.
- **Evidence:** paired_contrasts.csv exact−neuron 0.0004 (shunting)/0.0019 (additive); Ra150_Rm15000 shunt−current −0.00195 (8 cells)/+0.00242 (45); within-span oracle topology 0.232 vs random 0.385, shuffle 0.468.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.8):** Numbers in the finding recomputed and confirmed: (1) source_data/mnist_feedback_ladder/paired_contrasts.csv: exact path − neuron specific = 0.000447 (shunting, 10/15 seeds positive, P=0.186) and 0.001860 (additive, 14/15, P=0.0003); neuron − scalar = 0.1125 / 0.0781 — matches main.tex lines 143 ("11.2", "7.8", "<0.2 points"). (2) source_data/physical_cable_sensitivity/cell_primary_contrasts.csv, column `difference` (shunting − additive/current localization): original_eight Ra150_Rm15000 mean −0.00195 (2/8 positive); v661_disjoint Ra150_Rm15000 mean +0.00242 (45/45 positive) — matches main.tex line 358 (−0.0019, +0.0024) and supplementary.tex lines 3470–3489. Note the reviewer's paraphrase "zero at standard passive parameters" is slightly off: the effect is small, not zero, and 45/45 positive in the disjoint cohort; the paper itself says "small and opposite-signed". (3) source_data/fulltree_within_span_oracle/condition_summary.csv: topology-matched update match 0.2322 (frozen) → 0.2436 (oracle); random routes 0.3847; site-shuffle 0.4677; unprojected 0.9756 — matches main.tex lines 391–392. So the reviewer's factual evidence is correct. What does NOT hold: (a) "constructive evidence is Figs 6–7 only" is refuted by the snapshot text. Fig 3 (lines 164–199): utility score correlates with final accuracy ρ=0.924 and with one-step progress ρ=0.951 over 540 fits; resolution-matching interior optimum; reliability-weighted gains beat common gain. Fig 4 (lines 205–233): selected vs shared rule 35–58 pp at full conflict, 20 paired seeds, advantage increases in every seed. Fig 5 (lines 239–254): ancestry +1.27 pp at K=4 (CI 0.59–1.95, 15/20, P=0.0026, Holm P=0.0052); rewiring removes it. Fig 8 (lines 344–367; same CSV): shunt − current localization at Ra150_Rm300 = +0.0674 (8/8 cells, Wilcoxon P=0.0078) and +0.0808 (45/45 cells, v661); Rm1000 +0.0164 (8/8); active model positive in 8/8 cells at three doses (P=0.0078). These are positive, conditional results about when the dictionary matters, not boundary results. (b) The title does not "promise a mechanism": the Introduction (line 63) defines route dictionary as a descriptive device ("We describe this organization using a route dictionary…"), the abstract (lines 51–52) already reports the three boundary results the reviewer cites ("Image tasks benefit mainly from neuron-specific feedback"; "Measured visual responses, however, show no preferential alignment"), and the Discussion (lines 413, 421) opens with the framework and explicitly labels R8–R10 as defining "the biological reach". The authors' own internal review (analysis/COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md line 58) judged the title "concise and appropriately cautious". (c) The proposed retitle ("Task interaction structure, not task rank…") would describe only §R7 (lines 278–309) and misdescribe R1–R6 and R8–R10, which concern feedback resolution, transport and anatomy rather than interaction structure; restructuring to lead with Figs 6–7 would break the Results' explicit dependency order (R7 opens "The preceding experiments varied the resolution and placement of feedback. Choosing a morphology also requires…", line 280).
- **Suggestion:** Retitle (e.g. 'Task interaction structure, not task rank, sets when dendritic morphology matters for local credit assignment') or restructure with Figs 6-7 first and image/shunt/measured-response results as the boundary.

#### M037 — Update-utility bound presented without citation; it is the standard descent lemma for noisy gradient estimators
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** prior-work
- **Location:** main.tex:164-184, Eqs. 6-7
- **Claim:** Eqs. (6)-(7) (main.tex 166-180) are the standard L-smooth descent lemma applied to a stochastic update estimator and its step-size optimum (recomputed; identical to Bottou, Curtis & Nocedal 2018 Lemma 4.2 / Nesterov 2004). The Results text presents them with no citation and no pointer to SI S4; the only attribution is \citep{nesterov2004convex} at supplementary.tex:762, and Bottou et al. 2018 is absent from references.bib. The text does not assert novelty (it says 'which we call update utility', and Methods line 735 concedes both forms 'follow the smoothness inequality'), so this is an attribution-placement omission rather than a misleading novelty claim. Fix: after Eq. (6) add 'the standard smoothness bound for stochastic gradient estimators \citep{nesterov2004convex,bottou2018optimization}; its use here is to evaluate it for delivered fields whose moments depend on the dictionary (Supplementary Section S4)', and add the Bottou et al. bib entry.
- **Evidence:** No \citep in 164-184; nesterov2004convex cited only in SI; DEEP_LITERATURE_NOVELTY_AUDIT concedes the bound is elementary.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked snapshot main.tex lines 164-184 (Results, Eqs. 6-7): no \citep/\citet anywhere in that range (grep on 'cite[pt]?{' over lines 164-184 returns nothing); the compiled main.pdf text around 'which we call update utility' likewise carries no bracketed reference. Methods lines 727-735 (Eq. 8, fixed-operator case) also carry no citation, but line 735 does say 'Both formulations follow the smoothness inequality; the scientific question is which delivery rules produce favorable moments' -- i.e. the main text already acknowledges the inequality is generic, without attributing it. Citation counts: nesterov2004convex appears 0 times in main.tex and exactly once in supplementary.tex, at line 762 ('Apply the descent lemma \citep{nesterov2004convex} to -eta d ...') in the proof of the Routed-update moment bound proposition (SI Section S4, lines 714-790; S4 numbering verified from the \section list: S1 line 58, S2 299, S3 493, S4 714). references.bib contains werfel2005learning (line 440) and nesterov2004convex (line 640) but NO entry for Bottou/Curtis/Nocedal 2018 (grep for bottou|curtis|nocedal returns nothing), so the reviewer's suggested citation would need a new bib entry. werfel2005learning is cited in main.tex only at line 61 (intro), for the scalar-vs-neuron-specific feedback point, not for the bound. Recomputed the math: with w+ = w - eta*d and L-smoothness, L(w+) <= L(w) - eta*mu^T d + (L eta^2/2)||d||^2; taking expectations with E||d||^2 = ||m||^2 + tr(Sigma) gives Eq. 6 exactly; minimizing -eta a + (L b/2) eta^2 over eta>=0 gives eta* = [a]_+/(Lb) and value -[a]_+^2/(2Lb) = Eq. 7. This is the textbook descent lemma for a stochastic estimator (Nesterov 2004 Thm 2.1.5-style; Bottou, Curtis & Nocedal 2018 SIAM Rev. Lemma 4.2/Eq. 4.6 gives precisely the mean/second-moment form). Authors' own audit (analysis/DEEP_LITERATURE_NOVELTY_AUDIT_2026-08-23.md line 72-76) states 'The smoothness bound itself is elementary'. Overstated part of the finding: 'which we call update utility' (line 173) is a naming statement, and neither the Introduction (55-70) nor Discussion (411-427) uses 'utility' or claims the bound as novel; combined with Methods line 735 and the SI citation, a careful reader is not actively misled about novelty. What is missing is attribution at the point of first presentation in Results and a pointer from Results to SI S4 (line 178 points only to S3).
- **Suggestion:** After Eq. (6): 'the standard smoothness bound for stochastic gradient estimators [nesterov2004; Bottou 2018; cf. werfel2005]; its value here is evaluating it for delivered fields whose moments depend on the dictionary.'

#### M038 — Laminar/Ky-Fan prose overstates the spectral screen: the aligned endpoint violates the ordering hypothesis at K=1
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** supplementary.tex ~1150-1160; main.tex:186; Methods 742-748; Fig 3C
- **Claim:** The aligned endpoint of the spectral screen (level energies 0.15,0.35,0.30,0.20,0 with one mode each at levels 0 and 1) has per-mode eigenvalues 0.15, 0.35, 0.15, 0.15, 0.05x4, 0x8, so it does not satisfy the coarse-to-fine ordering hypothesis of the laminar proposition at K=1: ancestry captures 0.15 versus the Ky-Fan value 0.35 (regret 0.20), while it attains the bound exactly at K=2,4,8. The manuscript does not claim K=1 attainment - it explicitly restricts the attainment statement to K=4 (supplementary.tex:965-967) and the main-text statements are conditional - so "silently picks budgets" is overstated. However, the K=1 shortfall (present in spectral_bound_summary.csv) is never reported in text, table or Fig 3C, no ceiling is drawn in Fig 3C, and the juxtaposition of "ordering ... is not sufficient" with "the experiment realizes both regimes" (supplementary.tex:963-965) could lead a reader to assume the aligned endpoint is an instance of the ordered class at every budget. Suggested fix: add one sentence stating that ancestry is Ky-Fan-optimal at K>=2 with regret 0.20 at K=1 because level 1 carries more energy than the shared level-0 mode, and optionally add the dense ceiling to Fig 3C or Table tab:credit_phase.
- **Evidence:** spectral_phase_summary.csv alignment=1: K=1 0.150 vs 0.350 (regret 0.200); K=2/4/8 equal the bound. level_variances() divides level energy equally.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Numbers confirmed. (1) code/theory/credit_phase.py tree_haar_basis(16) gives level counts 1,1,2,4,8 (levels [0,1,2,2,3,3,3,3,4x8]); scripts/run_credit_phase_theory_experiment.py:59-65 level_variances() divides each level energy equally among its modes, and configs/credit_phase_theory/confirmatory.json:18 has level_energy [0.15,0.35,0.30,0.20,0.0]. Recomputed per-mode eigenvalues coarse-to-fine: 0.15, 0.35, 0.15, 0.15, 0.05x4, 0x8 -> ancestry/Ky-Fan: K=1 0.150/0.350 (regret 0.200), K=2 0.500/0.500, K=4 0.800/0.800, K=8 1.000/1.000. (2) source_data/credit_phase_theory/spectral_phase_summary.csv rows alignment=1: ancestry K=1 mean_spectral_capture 0.15, morphology_regret 0.2; dense_pca_upper_bound 0.35; K=2/4/8 ancestry regret ~1e-16. spectral_bound_summary.csv: mean_ky_fan_regret_at_rho1 = 0.2 at K=1, 0 at K=2,4,8. So the aligned endpoint violates the proposition's coarse-to-fine ordering hypothesis (lambda_1>=lambda_2) at the level-0/level-1 boundary, and ancestry is Ky-Fan-optimal only for K>=2. (3) What the text claims: supplementary.tex:922-931 proposition requires ordered eigenvalues along coarse-to-fine Haar order and then holds "for every admissible budget K"; supplementary.tex:964-967 says "The spectral-alignment experiment realizes both regimes: at full alignment and K=4 ancestry attained the rank-matched Ky-Fan bound to numerical precision, whereas at random alignment its advantage over sampled random routes vanished" - K=4 is named explicitly, not silently. spectral_bound_report.md likewise says "at K=4". main.tex:186 ("subtree routes reach the spectral ceiling when they span the dominant field patterns") and main.tex:748 ("attains the rank-constrained spectral ceiling when it contains a leading eigenspace") are conditional and correct. Methods main.tex:742 discloses the level energies (0.15,0.35,0.30,0.20,0), from which the non-monotone ordering can be derived, but no sentence says level 0 < level 1 or that the aligned endpoint lies outside the ordered class at K=1. (4) The K=1 regret of 0.20 is not reported anywhere in the manuscript: supplementary Table (label tab:credit_phase, supplementary.tex ~4380-4395) reports only the rho=1, K=4 ancestry-random contrast 0.5520 "Dense rank-K upper bound; full rank tied"; Fig 3C (figures/main/figure_03.pdf) shows only ancestry-minus-random (K=1, rho=1 cell = +8x10^-2 = 0.150-0.067) with no Ky-Fan/dense ceiling row. tests/test_laminar_optimality.py uses a different spectrum (4,2.5,1.8,...) and never tests the paper's level energies, so its "verified numerically" line (supplementary.tex:968-970) does not cover the experiment's aligned covariance.
- **Suggestion:** State ordering holds only from level 1 (ancestry Ky-Fan-optimal at K≥2, regret 0.20 at K=1), or move 0.35 to level 0 in a rerun; add the Ky-Fan regret/dense ceiling to Fig 3C.

#### M046 — R4 heading and Fig 4 title call distractor branches 'inactive', contradicting the mechanism
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:205, 235, 207, 230; 417
- **Claim:** The heading (205) and Fig 4 title (235) use 'inactive distractors' in a defined technical sense (forward-gated-off branches with zero exact gradient but nonzero local eligibility), but that definition is stated only in Methods (735) and in the Fig 4B label; at first reading of the heading, 'inactive' can be misread as 'receives no input', which line 207 and caption A explicitly deny. There is no factual contradiction with the mechanism, and the Discussion sentence at 417 is not contradicted by 228 because 'branch-conflict tasks' already presupposes conflict. Optional polish: replace 'inactive' with 'unselected' or 'gated-off' in the heading/title, and add 'under input conflict' at 417.
- **Evidence:** 207 'every branch received an image and formed an ungated local eligibility'; 228; 230.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked main.tex 205 (heading 'prevents updates from inactive distractors'), 207 ('every branch received an image and formed an ungated local eligibility'), 228 ('At zero conflict, selected and shared rules achieved similar accuracy'), 230 ('branches that received inputs but did not determine the output'), 235 (caption title 'inactive distractors'; caption A says 'every branch receives an image and forms an ungated eligibility ... dashed proximal paths are ... gated-off forward routes'; caption B says 'unselected exact derivatives are zero'), 417 ('Branch-conflict tasks require selector information in feedback or eligibility'). Also found main.tex 735 (Methods), which defines the term: 'context-gated tasks in which inactive branches have zero exact gradient but nonzero local eligibility', and figure_04.pdf panel B, whose labels read 'local eligibility: e_b != 0 for every branch' and 'inactive exact gradient = 0' (builder scripts/build_main_figure_04.py:186). So 'inactive' is used consistently as a defined term meaning forward-gated-off / zero exact gradient, not 'no input'; the definition, however, appears only in Methods (735) and the figure label, after both headings. Recomputed from source_data/path_necessity_fashion/condition_summary.csv: at chi=0, correct_path vs neuron_shared_k1 mean test accuracy = 0.7999/0.7991 (B=2), 0.7961/0.7961 (B=4), 0.7937/0.7953 (B=8) -> 'similar accuracy' at zero conflict is correct; at chi=1 gaps are 34.9, 58.2, 57.4 pp -> matches '35--58 percentage points'. The Discussion sentence at 417 refers to 'branch-conflict tasks', i.e. tasks with conflict present, so it does not contradict 228 (chi=0 is the no-conflict endpoint of the dose sweep); the sentence is compressed but not wrong.
- **Suggestion:** Use 'unselected' or 'gated-off' in heading/caption; Discussion 'Under input conflict, branch tasks require selector information'.

#### M047 — Fig 4 branch-conflict model has no dendrite; its role relative to Fig 5 is unexplained
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** structure
- **Location:** main.tex:205-238, 207-208, 222-224, 234
- **Claim:** R4 (Fig 4) and R5 (Fig 5) both use abstract feedback trees without dendritic biophysics, and the main text already says so upfront (lines 207-208, 235, 241, 251) and bridges the two sections explicitly (line 241). R4's headline (shared feedback is harmed by conflicting distractors) is anticipated by its setup, which the authors acknowledge (lines 217-218); its empirical content is the branch-count-dependent crossing and the gradient-alignment reversal. R4 and R5 test different questions (presence of selector information vs. support geometry at matched rank), so they are not a single point; at most, both sections could be tightened editorially, but the suggested merge into one two-row figure would discard the controls (4C,E; 5F,G,I) that carry the evidence.
- **Evidence:** Lines 207-208, 227-228, 238; R4 499 and R5 518 words.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked main.tex lines 55-72 (intro), 205-260 (R4, R5 and both captions), 417 (Discussion), 638-663 and 687-793 (Methods), and viewed figures/main/figure_04.pdf and figure_05.pdf. (1) "No dendrite" is true but explicitly disclosed, not hidden: line 207 "one logistic output unit with B in {2,4,8} parallel branch weight vectors and no internal forward compartments or conductance nonlinearity"; line 208 "This separates the information needed to select a branch from any specifically dendritic implementation"; line 232 "A dendritic branch is one possible site for this selection"; caption line 235 "there are no serial or conductance nonlinearities". R5 likewise: line 241 "Internal tree nodes defined feedback supports and did not perform serial forward computation"; line 251 "without attributing the effect to dendritic material". So the suggestion "state upfront both are abstract feedback trees" is already implemented in both sections. (2) "Role relative to Fig 5 is unexplained" is refuted: line 241 opens R5 with an explicit bridge, "Branch selection compares fully shared with fully resolved feedback. We next asked whether nested subtree routes offer a useful intermediate dictionary." The Discussion (line 417) also lists the two results as distinct claims. (3) "Harm follows from the setup" is acknowledged by the authors themselves (lines 217-218: "The exact derivative of every unselected branch is zero ... Increasing conflict therefore does not create opposing nonzero exact branch gradients"). The non-trivial empirical content is the branch-count-dependent chance crossing predicted by chi_c = B/[2(B-1)] = 1, 2/3, 4/7 (line 224; Methods line 661) and tested in Fig 4F (mean-curve crossings ~0.96, 0.67, 0.57 read from the figure), and the gradient-cosine reversal 0.987 -> -0.519 during training (line 663). (4) "One point": R4 contrasts presence vs absence of selector information (shared delta/B vs delta*1[b=b*]); R5 holds rank K, sparsity and row norm fixed and contrasts support geometry (ancestry vs deranged / depth-interleaved / random-sparse / dense controls, Fig 5F,I; degree-depth rewiring, Fig 5G), finding a small 1.27 pp effect at K=4 (line 247). All R5 controls at K=4 carry rank-4 selective information, so the reviewer's unifying title "restricted dictionaries help only when they carry selector information" misstates what R5 tests. Word counts (body text, LaTeX macros stripped): R4 ~504, R5 ~529, consistent with the reviewer's 499/518, but these are among the shorter Results sections (R3 ~877, R7 ~1198, R10 ~825), so "two full figures" is not disproportionate. The proposed merged figure (4A,D,F; 5E,H) would drop 4C (analytic boundary), 4E (BP/gated-point equivalence and derangement) and 5F,G,I (individual controls, rewiring), which are the controls that make the small R5 effect credible.
- **Suggestion:** Merge R4 and R5 into 'Restricted dictionaries help only when they carry task-selective information' with one two-row figure (4A,D,F; 5E,H); state upfront both are abstract feedback trees.

#### M054 — R6 has no main figure yet carries the paper's largest effect and its key optimizer caveat
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** figure
- **Location:** main.tex:262-277; Figs S28, S31
- **Claim:** Results subsection R6 (main.tex 262-276) is the only Results section without a main figure: its 30.9-pp depth benefit and its 10.96-vs-0.64-pp optimizer-dependent credit-resolution contrast (a caveat repeated in the Discussion) are supported only by Supplementary Figs S28 and S31, even though S31 already contains publication-quality panels (C, D/E, F/G, H) that map one-to-one onto those paragraphs, and the authors' assistant-written review (item 24) had recommended returning physical morphology to the main sequence before the authors chose to keep nine figures. The 30.9-pp effect is not the paper's largest (Fig 4 reports 35-58 pp), and the 13.47-pp optimizer effect is stated only in Methods and the S28 caption, not in R6. Promoting a compact version of S31 (C+H, or C/F/G/H) or trimming R6 to its two load-bearing claims is a reasonable optional improvement, not a defect.
- **Evidence:** No \begin{figure} or Fig.~\ref in 262-277; S31 already has publication-quality panels B, C, F/G, H mapping onto the paragraphs; REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION item 24 'Kept the nine-figure sequence'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Checked main.tex 262-276 (R6): six paragraphs, no \begin{figure} and no Fig.~\ref to a main figure; only "Supplementary Fig.~S31" (line 264) and "Supplementary Figs.~S28 and S31" (line 274) are cited. grep counts 9 \begin{figure} in main.tex, none in R6. Supplement figure ordering (grep begin{figure}) confirms S28 = figure_S28_panels_A-B.pdf (supplementary.tex 4126-4131) and S31 = figure_S31_panels_A-G.pdf (4147-4152); I viewed S31: it is a full-page, publication-quality figure that actually has panels A-H (file name says A-G, caption describes A-H), with C (D1-D3 ladder), D/E (H4 heatmap, depth optima), F/G (alignment dose, BP vs LocalCA) and H (0.637 vs 10.957 pp) mapping directly onto R6 paragraphs at lines 272, 274, 276. Numbers: 30.9 pp is consistent with S31C/E (0.92 - 0.61) and with source_data/physical_depth_clean_source_replication/paired_contrasts_by_seed.csv (e.g. 0.3099 for seed 10205); 10.96 (10.34-11.58), 0.64 (0.15-1.17) and 13.47 (12.89-14.05) match supplementary.tex 2570-2580 and the S28 caption at 4129, and main.tex Methods line 900. Review-log claim verified: analysis/COMPREHENSIVE_NATURE_COMMUNICATIONS_REVIEW_20260905.md line 80 (item 24, "restore physical morphology to the main sequence") and analysis/REVIEW_IMPLEMENTATION_20260905.md line 55 ("Revised decision | Kept the nine-figure sequence. Physical depth stays in S31..."); note this review was written by the authors' assistant, not by external referees. The Discussion (main.tex ~419) repeats the optimizer caveat ("their exact-credit advantage depends strongly on optimizer settings"), so the caveat is load-bearing. REFUTED sub-claims: (1) "the paper's largest effect" is wrong - main.tex line 228 (R4/Fig 4D) reports a 35-58 pp selected-vs-shared advantage at full conflict, and line 260 reports a 54.47-point gap to oracle coefficients; 30.9 pp is not the largest. (2) The 13.47-pp broadcast-optimizer effect does not appear in the R6 Results prose at all; it appears only in Methods (line 900), the S28 caption and SI S5 text, so it is not part of what R6 asserts. (3) "across four cohorts" is unsupported as stated; R6 covers D1-D4 morphologies and three task families, and the Methods table (line 543) lists six physical-depth cohorts. - **Suggestion:** Promote S31 B/C/F-G/H as a compact main figure or fold S31C+H into an existing one; otherwise move R6 to SI with one sentence (S28, S31) or cut it to its two load-bearing claims.

#### M061 — 'Require depth three' (and Boolean 'depth two') is vacuous: it is the leaf-count floor for any binary tree
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:294-296, abstract 52, 415, Methods 819; supplementary.tex:4197 (S37); Fig S37D, S43C-D
- **Claim:** In main.tex:295-296, Fig S37A/D and Fig S43C, "require depth three" (eight inputs) and "require depth two" (four inputs) are the ceil(log2 n) floors of any full binary tree — for eight leaves depth 3 even forces the complete balanced tree — so only the nested targets' extra level (bound 0.1464 at depth 3 for the 24 eight-input nested controls; 0.1255 for the Boolean nested target on balanced trees) is informative. The abstract, Discussion (415), Methods (819) and the S37 caption already use accurate wording ("minimum sufficient depth", "admit zero-bound trees at depth three"). Reword the two main-text sentences, the methods-fragment line 197 and the S37A/S43C panel titles to say the primary targets are representable at the minimum depth allowed by their leaf count (a balanced tree), whereas the nested targets need one further level.
- **Evidence:** adaptive_constructions.csv: 105+35 at depth 3, 24 nested at depth 4; depth certificate tabulates 3,4,5 only; boolean depth_summary.csv 2 for six families, 3 for nested.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Depth convention: S37C x-axis reads "maximum root-to-leaf edge depth" and boolean_morphology_methods.tex:31-32 states "three balanced trees have depth two, and twelve comb trees have depth three" for four inputs, so a full binary tree on n leaves has depth >= ceil(log2 n): 8 leaves -> 3, 4 leaves -> 2. Moreover, an 8-leaf tree of depth exactly 3 must be the complete balanced tree, so "minimum depth 3" for a primary target is equivalent to "some balanced labeled tree is exact", information already present in Fig 6B/S36. Source data: source_data/morphology_structure/design/constructive_depth_certificate.csv (492 rows) tabulates maximum_depth 3,4,5 only; best_centered_cut_bound = 0 for all 105 quadratic_matching and 35 quartic_partition tasks at depth 3; nested_prefix_control = 0.146446609407 at depth 3 (= (2-sqrt2)/4, recomputed 0.1464466094) and 0 at depths 4,5. adaptive_constructions.csv (both constructive/ and constructive_dp_v2/, 164 rows): depth 3 for 105 matching + 35 quartic, depth 4 for 24 nested. source_data/boolean_theory/depth_summary.csv: minimum_exact_depth 2 for and4, or4, parity4, or_of_ands, xor_of_ands, and_of_xors; 3 for nested (balanced lower bound 0.12546 = (22-2sqrt57)/55, recomputed 0.1254606). So the reviewer's numbers are all correct and the stated depths are indeed the leaf-count floors except for the nested targets. Where the finding overstates: of the cited locations, abstract (main.tex:52) and Discussion (main.tex:415) only say the cut criterion "determines ... minimum sufficient depth" (a correct description of what the DP computes), and Methods main.tex:819 contains no "depth three" statement at all (only "An independent depth-constrained recurrence checks the depth certificate"). The S37 caption (supplementary.tex:4196) already uses non-vacuous wording: "Both primary families admit zero-bound trees at depth three" and states the 0.146446609407 depth-3 bound for nested controls. The floor-sounding "require" phrasing is confined to main.tex:295 ("require depths two and three despite identical input and gate counts"), main.tex:296 ("the 140 primary and 24 nested targets require depths three and four, respectively"), morphology_followup_methods.tex:196-197 ("reconstruct at minimum depth three"), Fig S37A title "Parallel: minimum depth 3", S37D rows "depth 3", and S43C "minimum depth 2". Nothing is numerically wrong and the nested contrast is explicitly stated with its bound, so this is a wording issue, not a misleading result.
- **Suggestion:** Reword as 'representable at the minimum depth allowed by eight leaves (balanced tree), whereas every nested target needs a fourth level (bound 0.1464 at depth 3)'; same for S37 caption and Boolean sentence; qualify 'minimum sufficient depth' in the abstract.

#### M065 — NMSE denominators and noise floors differ across Fig. 6 panels but are defined once
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:290, 300, 304, 311, 829
- **Claim:** The denominators and noise floors do differ across Fig. 6 panels (B: exact full-domain variance, no noise; C: clean-test MSE over population variance 1; D/E: noisy-test NMSE with floor 0.0225; F: noiseless targets, MSE over evaluation-split variance, floor 0), and the numbers 0.000150 (F) and 0.024-0.027 (D) are correct. However, the D/E floor is already stated three times in the main text (lines 300, 311 caption, and by implication Methods 823) and C is labeled "clean-test" in the caption; the only genuinely missing statement is that panel F (and the conductance Methods paragraph at line 829) evaluates noiseless targets and normalizes by evaluation-split variance. Because D, E and F share the axis label "test NMSE" in the same figure, a reader can still misread F's 0.00015 as a better fit than D's near-floor 0.024; a one-sentence addition to the caption and to Methods line 829 ("F evaluates noiseless targets and normalizes by test-split variance, so its floor is zero; D and E evaluate noisy labels with floor 0.0225") resolves it.
- **Evidence:** conductance methods: no label noise, evaluation-split variance; S42 caption notes distinct denominators; end_to_end methods floor 0.0225; calibration methods clean target.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Panel-by-panel check of denominators and noise floors. (B) main.tex:819 "Fit NMSE was the best observed restart divided by known target variance"; Fourier/cut quantities on the full 256-input domain -> exact, noiseless, floor 0. (C) supplementary/morphology_calibration_methods.tex:20 "primary endpoint was MSE against the clean target, divided by its known population variance of one"; main Fig. 6 caption (main.tex:311) says "Clean-test regret" -> clean, floor 0, and this IS stated in the caption. (D,E) morphology_end_to_end_methods.tex:52-53 "primary endpoint was noisy-test NMSE ... expected noise floor 0.0225" (= 0.15^2 / population variance 1, consistent with lines 13 and 44-45); main.tex:300 "near the 0.0225 noise floor" and caption main.tex:311 "Dotted segments mark the label-noise floor 0.0225"; Methods main.tex:823 "4,096 independent noisy test examples defined the primary endpoint" -> D/E floor IS stated in text, caption and Methods, so the claim that this is "defined once" is overstated. (F) morphology_conductance_methods.tex:16 "there was no label noise ... Reported NMSE is MSE divided by the target variance of the evaluation split"; live code scripts/morphology_conductance/run.py:83 `return mse/np.var(y)` on the evaluation split, run.py:94 training loss normalized by training-split variance; source_data/morphology_conductance/summaries/fresh/learning_summary.csv row adam,0.01,exact_path,True,1000 = 0.0001496 [6.8e-5, 2.44e-4] and False = 0.028921 [0.027066, 0.030867], matching main.tex:304 -> F is noiseless with a split-variance denominator, floor 0. Main text: grep of main.tex for noiseless/no label noise/clean returned only lines 800, 823, 827, 832; the conductance Methods paragraph (main.tex:829) and the Fig. 6 caption (main.tex:311) never say F is noiseless or split-normalized. Supplementary Fig. S42 caption (supplementary.tex:4237) states "distinct denominators" only between its own panel B (population bound) and C-E (split variance), not relative to the main-text D/E noisy floor. Figure artwork (figures/main/figure_06.pdf): panels D, E and F all carry the identical y-label "test NMSE"; D shows matching/quartic/nested at ~0.024 (at the floor) while F shows compatible grouping at ~0.0002 on a 0-0.05 axis, so the visual juxtaposition invites the F-vs-D comparison the reviewer describes. Also noted: the dotted floor segments visible in the PDF appear only in panel E, although the caption says D,E.
- **Suggestion:** Add to caption and Methods 829: 'C and F evaluate noiseless targets (F normalizes by test-split variance); D and E evaluate noisy labels, so 0.0225 is their floor.'

#### M066 — Fig 6F and text overstate a 3%-of-variance conductance grouping effect whose size is set by the generator
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** figure
- **Location:** figure_06.pdf F; main.tex:52, 304-305, 311; Discussion 415
- **Claim:** Fig 6F shows the conductance grouping effect (0.0289 vs 0.00015 NMSE, ~200x, 20/20 seeds) on a linear 0-0.05 axis under the title "grouping controls learning", whereas incompatible trees still explain ~97% of target variance and the analytic floor (mean 0.0132, not stated in the main text) sits at roughly half the observed penalty. The numbers are correctly reported and NMSE is already a fraction of variance, but the panel title and expanded axis frame a 3%-of-variance residual as categorical; a reader comparing with panels D/E (0-1 axes) could misjudge the scale. The effect size is NOT set by the 0.25 log-conductance perturbation: the bound is 0.0136 at zero perturbation and decreases with larger perturbations; it is set by the nominal shunt/coupling operating point of the planted teacher. Suggested fix: soften the panel-F title (e.g. "wrong grouping leaves an irreducible residual"), state the 0.013 analytic floor and the 2.9%-of-variance size in the text or caption, and note that the magnitude depends on the chosen nominal conductances.
- **Evidence:** paired_contrasts 0.02877 [0.02692,0.03073], 20/20; interaction_bound mean 0.01322, min 0.0054; per-teacher bounds 0.010-0.014; S42C uses a log axis.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Descriptive facts check out. figure_06.pdf panel F: linear y-axis 0.00-0.05, title "Conductance: grouping controls learning" (builder scripts/build_main_figure_12.py lines 70, 81: ax.set_ylim(-.001,upper), title string). main.tex:304 reports "NMSE 0.000150 for compatible grouping versus 0.028921 otherwise"; source_data/morphology_conductance/summaries/fresh/learning_summary.csv exact_path step 1000: compatible 0.00014957, incompatible 0.0289207; paired_contrasts.csv: 0.028771 [0.026919, 0.030725], 20/20 positive seeds — all match. interaction_bound/report.json: mean incompatible NMSE bound 0.013222, min 0.005367 (Supp. S42B caption at supplementary.tex:4237 states 0.013222). S42 panels C/D do use log axes (10^-4..10^-1). 1-0.0289 = 97.1% of variance explained by incompatible trees: arithmetic correct. The 0.013 bound value is not stated anywhere in main.tex (grep for 0.0132/13222 returns nothing; line 304 only says "yielding an analytic population-error bound").

Two parts of the finding are wrong. (1) "the effect size follows the planted 0.25 log-conductance perturbation": I recomputed the analytic bound with the paper's exact quadrature formula (scripts/morphology_conductance/interaction_bound.py, model.py; perturbation SD 0.25 defined in model.teacher_parameters and supplementary/morphology_conductance_methods.tex line 14) as a function of teacher perturbation SD, notes/verify-M066/bound_vs_sigma.py. Reproduced the paper's 0.0132221 at SD 0.25. At SD 0 (nominal conductances, no perturbation at all) the bound is 0.01359; SD 0.1: 0.01349; SD 0.5: 0.01241; SD 1.0: 0.01000. The obstruction is structural (a terminal response is multiplied by the wrong proximal shunt attenuation) and its size is set by the nominal operating point (h=3, b=3, a=4, proximal input log-SD 1.2), not by the 0.25 perturbation; larger perturbations slightly lower the mean bound. (2) "per-teacher bounds 0.010-0.014" is wrong: per-seed mean incompatible bounds range 0.0088-0.0197 (min single pair 0.0054). Also, the suggestion to "report the penalty as a fraction of variance" is already met by definition: main.tex:289 defines NMSE as squared error divided by target variance, so 0.0289 NMSE is 2.9% of variance. The abstract sentence (main.tex:51, "Conductance-based trees also benefit from compatible grouping, even when detailed feedback adds little") and Discussion line 415 ("demonstrates a benefit of compatible grouping") make no magnitude claim.
- **Suggestion:** Use the S42C log axis; retitle 'wrong grouping leaves an irreducible residual'; report the penalty as a fraction of variance with the analytic bound and perturbation dependence; abstract 'a small but consistent benefit'.

#### M067 — Conductance experiment's two prespecified primary contrasts reported with unadjusted 95% intervals, unlike sibling experiments
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** statistics
- **Location:** main.tex:304, 311, 829; supplementary.tex:4238
- **Claim:** The conductance experiment prespecified two primary contrasts (fresh_protocol.json) but, unlike the Boolean and pipeline siblings, its protocol never prespecified a CI level, Bonferroni adjustment or improvement margin; the main text (main.tex:304, 311, 829) reports these contrasts with pointwise 95% intervals and never calls them primaries, while Fig 6 C-E use Bonferroni 97.5% intervals. The supplement fragment (morphology_conductance_methods.tex:25-27, in the compiled SI) does name the two primaries and does state that intervals are pointwise. Recomputed 97.5% intervals ([0.02665, 0.03105] and [-0.000014, 0.000170]) leave both conclusions unchanged, so this is a presentation inconsistency, not a statistical error: the main Methods should name the two primaries and say that this protocol used unadjusted pointwise 95% intervals with no margin (or report 97.5% intervals for consistency).
- **Evidence:** fresh_protocol.json primary_contrasts; paired_contrasts.csv ci95 only (0.026919-0.030725; −0.000008-0.000154); fragment says 'pointwise rather than simultaneous'.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked: main.tex:304 (broadcast excess 0.000059, "95% interval -0.000008 to 0.000154"), main.tex:311 Fig 6 caption (panel F "paired 95% interval [0.02692,0.03073]" while C-E say "Bonferroni-adjusted 97.5%"), main.tex:829 Methods ("pointwise seed-bootstrap intervals were retained"; the word "primary" is never used for the conductance experiment in the main text). Protocol: source_data/morphology_conductance/fresh_protocol.json and development_protocol.json both list primary_contrasts = ["Adam exact-path incompatible-group mean minus compatible group", "Adam compatible-group calibrated-broadcast minus exact path"]; development_protocol.json "uncertainty" field = "Twenty independent fresh paired seeds; 10000 seed-bootstrap draws; full conditions retained" - no CI level, no Bonferroni, no margin prespecified. By contrast source_data/boolean_morphology/protocol.json:77 prespecifies "two Bonferroni-adjusted two-sided 97.5% intervals; ... mean improvement>=0.01 NMSE". Reviewer's claim that "Methods never names the primaries or the lack of adjustment" is wrong for the supplement: supplementary/morphology_conductance_methods.tex:25-27 (reached via morphology_followup_methods.tex:218 \input, and present in the compiled supplementary.pdf, pdftotext lines 4967-4971) says "The two primary contrasts were the Adam exact-gradient grouping effect and the Adam calibrated-broadcast-minus-exact effect within compatible groupings" and "Intervals are pointwise rather than simultaneous". It is correct for the main-text Methods. Fig 6F is drawn by scripts/build_main_figure_12.py:68 from learning_summary.csv ci95_low/ci95_high; summaries/fresh/paired_contrasts.csv has only ci95 columns. Recomputation from summaries/fresh/paired_seed_contrasts.csv (n=20, 10,000 draws, percentile bootstrap): grouping contrast mean 0.028771, 95% [0.026922, 0.030754], 97.5% [0.026650, 0.031050], 20/20 seeds positive; broadcast contrast mean 0.000059, 95% [-0.000008, 0.000154], 97.5% [-0.000014, 0.000170], 10/20 seeds positive. Both conclusions (grouping effect clearly positive; broadcast excess inconclusive) are unchanged under Bonferroni adjustment, and the text already treats the broadcast contrast as non-significant, so no claim is inflated by the unadjusted interval.
- **Suggestion:** Name the two primaries and report Bonferroni 97.5% intervals, or state that this protocol prespecified pointwise 95% and no margin.

#### M074 — Fig 8F standard-calibration contrasts are called 'small/negligible' but are reliably nonzero with opposite signs in the two cohorts
- **Status:** partially confirmed
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:360, 369; supplementary.tex:3448-3452, 3468-3471; Table S28 line 5039
- **Claim:** The reported numbers are correct, but the main text (main.tex:360) and Fig 8F caption (main.tex:369) already state that the two Rm=15,000 contrasts are opposite-signed, and Table S28 already gives intervals and sign counts. The residual issue is narrower: supplementary.tex:3468-3471 describes the standard-calibration result as "negligible or small" without noting that the 45-cell v661 contrast is positive in every cell (+0.0024, [0.0018, 0.0030], Wilcoxon P = 5.7e-14) while the 8-cell contrast is negative in 6/8 cells and only marginally excludes zero (−0.0019, [−0.0046, −0.00003], Wilcoxon P = 0.148), and no sentence offers a plausible reason for the systematic cohort difference (e.g., the v661 cells have roughly twice the median axial/leak ratio, 137 vs 68, and a twofold larger permissive-proxy contrast, 0.138 vs 0.069). Suggest one SI sentence to that effect; do not claim the 8-cell interval "reliably" excludes zero, and do not add "whiskers exclude zero in both cohorts" to the Fig 8F caption, since the 8-cell bound (−3e-5) is invisible at the panel's axis scale.
- **Evidence:** physical_cable_sensitivity/cell_primary_contrasts.csv and summary.json; Ra100_Rm15000 8-cell Wilcoxon P=0.039; v661 permissive dose-1 0.138 vs 0.069.
- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.85):** Numbers: recomputed from source_data/physical_cable_sensitivity/cell_primary_contrasts.csv (199 rows) and summary.json. original_eight Ra150_Rm15000: mean −0.00195, cell-bootstrap 95% [−0.00459, −3.5e-5] (upper bound −3e-5 to −5e-5 across five seeds), 2/8 positive, per-cell values [−0.0102, −0.0039, −0.0011, −0.0008, −0.0007, −0.0006, +0.0005, +0.0012], exact Wilcoxon P = 0.148 (summary.json). v661_disjoint Ra150_Rm15000: +0.00242 [0.00184, 0.00305], 45/45 positive, Wilcoxon P = 5.7e-14. Ra100_Rm15000 (8-cell, not in Table S28 or Fig 8F, which draws Ra150 regimes only per build_main_figure_08.py:544-546): −0.00174, P = 0.039. Fixed 0.5 nS dose at Rm=15,000: −0.0039 (−0.0075 to −0.0004), supplementary.tex:3487-3489. Permissive unit-dose contrasts 0.069 (8-cell, line 3392) vs 0.138 (v661, line 3449). All numbers cited in the finding are correct. Text: main.tex:360 already says "small and opposite-signed ... −0.0019 and +0.0024", and the Fig 8 caption (main.tex:369) already says "small, opposite-signed shunt-minus-current contrasts"; Table S28 (supplementary.tex:5031-5046) already reports intervals and positive-cell counts for every regime. So the sign difference and intervals are NOT hidden; the reviewer's "reporting means only" is wrong for main.tex:360/369 and Table S28. What is missing: supplementary.tex:3468-3471 calls the Rm=15,000 result "negligible or small" without noting that the v661 contrast is positive in all 45 cells (P = 5.7e-14) whereas the 8-cell contrast is negative in 6/8 and only marginally non-zero (bootstrap bound −3e-5; Wilcoxon P = 0.148); no sentence explains the cohort discrepancy (v661 median axial/leak is 137 vs 68 for the 8 cells, and the v661 permissive contrast is already 2x larger, 0.138 vs 0.069). The reviewer's "reliably nonzero" is an overstatement for the 8-cell cohort. Fig 8F artwork could not be inspected (Read tool blocked by hook timeout); from the builder, the y-axis spans −0.0175 to 0.106 (build_main_figure_08.py:592), so the 8-cell whisker top at −3e-5 is visually indistinguishable from the zero line, making the suggested caption "whiskers exclude zero in both cohorts" technically true but unverifiable by eye and fragile for the 8-cell cohort.
- **Suggestion:** Give intervals and sign counts, state the sign differs between cohorts and is an order of magnitude below the low-Rm regime, add a plausible-cause sentence; say 'small and slightly reversed (interval excludes zero)' in SI; caption: whiskers exclude zero in both cohorts.

Fix: add the mapping to both captions (or a small legend in S36B and S37C) and say in the S37 caption that the two primary-family curves overlap at zero.

- **Verifier (partially-confirmed, confidence 0.9):** Checked: (1) snapshot captions supplementary.tex:4188 (S36) and :4196 (S37); (2) text spans with fill colours extracted from the snapshot PDFs figures/supplementary/figure_S36_panels_A-D.pdf and figure_S37_panels_A-D.pdf with PyMuPDF; (3) the builder that produced these figures, journal/scripts/build_morphology_followup_figures.py s36() (lines 51-95) and s37() (118-140) plus journal_style.style_direct_color_labels (84-104); (4) source_data/morphology_structure/design/constructive_depth_certificate.csv.
S36: the only family key is coloured text in panel A ('All 105 perfect matchings' fill #348559 green; 'All 35 four-plus-four partitions' fill #8a63c6 purple). Panels B and D additionally encode family by marker (filled circle = matching, open triangle = partition; builder lines 66 and 88) and C by bar colour only; no legend text exists in B, C or D (the only legend-like text is '◇ mean centered-cut bound' in C), and caption line 4188 contains no colour, circle, triangle or family-marker mapping. So for S36 the claim holds exactly; the marker code is also undocumented.
S37: panel C has no legend (builder lines 126-131 draw three family curves with markers o/s/^ and no legend call; PDF text in C is only 'Zero bound ⇔ exact fit in this multi-affine class'). However the colour key IS on the same page: panel D lists '105 matching targets' (green #348559), '35 quartic targets' (purple #8a63c6), '24 nested controls' (orange-brown #9a6f2b), and A/B carry 'root readout' in green/orange. Also, per the depth certificate CSV the matching and partition curves coincide exactly (both 0.000 at depths 3,4,5; nested control 0.146447 at 3, 0 at 4,5), so only two traces are visually distinguishable, not three. Caption line 4196 says 'Curves coincide within family' but never maps colour to family. Note the older scripts/morphology_structure/plot_results.py (lines 87-88) and plot_constructive.py (line 105) do emit legends, but they are not the builder of the snapshot figures (their legend labels 'Quadratic matchings (105)' etc. are absent from the snapshot PDFs).
- **Suggestion:** Add legends to S36B/C and S37C and state the mapping in captions.
