## Completeness critic (unverified)

As completeness critic I looked for what the 18-reviewer panel did not cover. The panel was thorough on numbers, statistics, figures and framing, and I confirmed that the ethics/data-reuse, author-contribution, competing-interest and data-availability statements are present and adequate. What nobody examined is the experimental scope behind the two headline claims. First, every image-task result (MNIST, Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, ownership, DFA) uses a single layer of dendritic neurons under a linear or small-MLP readout, so the "neuron-specific" rung is the exact somatic error and no result speaks to networks where neuron-level credit must itself be transported; the main text never says "one layer". Second, the paper's central object, an intermediate within-arbor dictionary (K=3 subtrees in the [3,3] arbor), is never trained on any image task: the ladder jumps from K=1 to the full field, and Fig. 1E only reports projection capture. Third, the primary ladder uses one learning-rate recipe for all rungs with no per-rung sensitivity and no decoder-only baseline, whereas the multi-affine credit study did tune rates per rule. Fourth, the interaction estimator is developed and confirmed inside the same four-family mixture whose generator matches the estimator's sparse-Walsh prior, and this scope is stated only in an SI fragment. Fifth, the cut-rank/depth theory is never brought into contact with the reconstructed arbors. These are scope and missing-analysis gaps rather than errors; the paper's candour elsewhere makes them fixable by text plus a few modest reruns.

#### C01 — All image-task conclusions come from a single dendritic layer; the main text never says so
- **Severity (unverified):** medium
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:137-158 (R2), 141, 568-588 (Methods); supplementary.tex:1731-1745, 4569 (Table S10)
- **Claim:** Every image-task cohort (MNIST ladder, Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, ownership [2,2]/[2,2,2,2], DFA factorial) uses one layer of dendritic neurons under a linear 128x10 decoder (or a 20-32-16-10 MLP readout for CIFAR). With a linear readout the 'neuron-specific' rung is the exact somatic gradient, so the ladder tests only within-arbor resolution downstream of an exact neuron error; whether one signal per neuron still 'captures most of the benefit' when dendritic neurons sit in hidden layers whose own errors must be transported through other arbors is untested. The main text describes the network only as '128 directed [3,3] trees' (line 141) and never states that there is a single neuronal layer, so a reader will assume a deeper network than was run.
- **Evidence:** supplementary.tex 1731-1745: 'The core contained 128 modeled excitatory neurons ... A linear 128x10 decoder produced the class scores'; Table S10 row (4569): 'Modeled population: One layer of 128 excitatory modeled neurons'; main.tex 585-586: CIFAR used 'one 20-soma [3,3,3,3] population ... and a 20-32-16-10 decoder'; main.tex 160 caption calls the ownership cohorts 'two-stage'/'four-stage' where stage means tree depth, not neuronal layers. grep for 'hidden layer|single.layer|one.layer|multi.layer' in main.tex returns nothing outside the MLP control of R6. No panel finding addresses this.
- **Suggestion:** State in R2 (line 141) and Methods (568) that all image cohorts use a single layer of dendritic neurons with an exact readout error at each soma, and add this to the Table S34 boundary row. Ideally add one two-layer dendritic cohort (e.g. two stacked 64-neuron [3,3] layers on MNIST, 10-15 seeds) comparing neuron-specific vs exact-path feedback in the hidden layer; otherwise write explicitly in Discussion that the sufficiency of one signal per neuron is established only for a single dendritic layer.

#### C02 — The intermediate route dictionary (K=3 subtrees) is never trained on an image task
- **Severity (unverified):** medium
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:133, 137-158, Fig 1E (120), Fig 2A/B (160); supplementary.tex:438-630, 4220 (S40 caption)
- **Claim:** The paper's thesis is that morphology-constrained dictionaries of intermediate rank (1 < K < N_u) are the interesting object, and Fig. 1D/E shows that three subtree profiles capture 63-72% of the exact field in the MNIST trees. Yet the feedback ladder trains only K=1 (neuron broadcast) and K=12 (exact field); no K=3 subtree rung is ever trained on MNIST, Fashion-MNIST or CIFAR-10. Intermediate-K training exists only in the synthetic two-branch and eight-context tasks (Fig. 5) and the multi-affine trees (S40). A referee will ask whether the 'less than 0.2 pp' exact-path gain is already obtained at K=3, which would be the direct image-task test of the route-dictionary idea.
- **Evidence:** grep -i 'subtree|K=3' across supplementary.tex lines 1718-2919 (SI S5, artificial-tree experiments) finds subtree/ancestry training only in the two-branch subtree-address experiment (438-527) and the eight-context factorial (528-630); no MNIST/Fashion/CIFAR cohort in Table 1 (main.tex 523-552) or Tables S15-S19 has a subtree-feedback condition. Fig 1E (route_dictionary_atlas/capture_summary.csv) reports K=3 capture 0.7176/0.6253 as projection capacity only ('a learning rule must supply its own coefficients', caption 120).
- **Suggestion:** Add a K=3 subtree rung to the MNIST ladder at the same 15 seeds: coefficients either from the per-subtree mean of the exact field (oracle-projection rule, matching Fig. 1E) or from the trained three-route rule used in Fig. 5. Report it beside the 11.2/<0.2 pp contrasts in Fig. 2B/G. If it is not run, state in R2 and Discussion that intermediate within-arbor dictionaries were trained only on controlled tasks.

#### C03 — Primary ladder has no per-rung learning-rate sensitivity and no decoder-only baseline
- **Severity (unverified):** medium
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:141-143, 568-591; supplementary.tex:1841-1849, 4220 (S40 caption), 4583-4606 (Table S11)
- **Claim:** The strict-scalar, neuron-specific and exact-path rungs were all trained with one Adam recipe (TopK 1.5e-3, coupling 7e-4, reactivation 5e-4, decoder 1.5e-3) with no evidence that the rates were tuned for, or are robust across, feedback rungs; the strict scalar reaches only 85.9% (shunting) / 89.3% (additive) on MNIST. Adam removes the gross scale mismatch of a population-mean signal, but the standard referee demand for local-rule vs. richer-feedback comparisons is a per-rule rate sweep, which the authors themselves applied in the multi-affine credit study ('Stars mark rates selected separately for each rule and optimizer using only development seeds', S40 caption) but not here. There is also no decoder-only (frozen dendritic core) baseline, so the reader cannot tell how much the scalar rung adds over training the exactly-supervised linear readout on random dendritic features.
- **Evidence:** supplementary.tex 1841-1846 gives a single rate set for 'the primary feedback-definition experiment'; no rate screen exists among the 79 source_data directories (ls source_data | grep -i 'lr|rate|fair|sweep' is empty); grep for 'decoder-only|frozen core|random feature' in main.tex and supplementary.tex returns nothing; mnist_feedback_ladder/condition_summary.csv gives scalar 0.8588 vs neuron-specific 0.9713 (shunting). The ladder's decoder is 'locally updated' with exact errors in all rungs (main.tex 139, SI 1841).
- **Suggestion:** Run a small rate sweep (e.g. 3 rates x 3 rungs x 5 seeds) for the strict scalar and neuron-specific rungs in one architecture and report that the ordering is rate-insensitive; add a decoder-only control (frozen core, exactly trained readout) as a floor in Fig. 2B or Table S15. If the sweep is not run, state in Methods that rates were inherited from the neuron-specific development runs and not re-tuned per rung.

#### C04 — Interaction estimator is confirmed only inside the mixture it was developed on, with a prior matched to the generator; scope stated only in an SI fragment
- **Severity (unverified):** medium
- **Category:** overclaim
- **Location:** main.tex:52 (abstract), 298-302, 823-825, 415; Fig 6C-D; supplementary/morphology_calibration_methods.tex:6-8, 26
- **Claim:** Abstract and R7 claim that 'estimates from limited noisy examples improve tree selection' and that estimated trees 'support accurate learning on structured targets', but development seeds fixed the Lasso policy on the same four task families used for confirmation, and three of the four families (matchings, quartics, nested products) plus the fourth ('four distinct nonconstant monomials') are all exactly sparse in the 255-monomial Walsh dictionary that the estimator receives as its prior. No family outside the sparse-monomial class, no continuous-input target and no dense low-degree target is tested; the one within-mixture family without a compatible tree (random) is where the estimated tree fails (NMSE ~0.42 vs 0.03 elsewhere), and the main text summarises this as 'three of four families'. The only scope statement is in the SI fragment.
- **Evidence:** morphology_calibration_methods.tex line 6: 'Tasks were drawn from four equally weighted families ... a random-interaction control ... selected four distinct nonconstant monomials'; line 8: estimator 'prior information was ... a sparse multilinear dictionary'; line 26: 'it does not establish transfer to unknown task families'. main.tex 823: 'four fresh interaction families'; 825: 'eighty fresh functions from the same four-family mixture'. Fig 6D (figure_06.pdf) shows the random-family estimated tree at ~0.42 with a wide interval. Neither abstract (52) nor Discussion (415) carries the within-mixture scope.
- **Suggestion:** Add one held-out family outside the sparse-Walsh class (e.g. a target generated by a random depth-3 multi-affine tree with dense coefficients, or a smooth function of Gaussian inputs) evaluated with the frozen estimator and report regret as a secondary result; and move the SI scope sentence into R7 (after line 298) and the abstract ('within a prespecified family mixture').

#### C05 — The cut-rank/depth theory is never applied to, or related to, the reconstructed arbors
- **Severity (unverified):** medium
- **Category:** missing-analysis
- **Location:** main.tex:39 (title), 278-307 (R7), 315-337 (R8), 413-415 (Discussion)
- **Claim:** The constructive half of the paper (representability iff centered cut rank <= 1; minimum sufficient depth by dynamic program) is proved for eight-leaf binary trees with one scalar per branch, while the anatomical half analyses 65 reconstructed neurons with hundreds of branch points, non-binary branching and thousands of contacts. No analysis connects them: there is no census of branch order/depth or subtree-size structure in the MICrONS cells, no statement of which interaction structures those arbors could represent under the theorem, and no discussion of how the theorem's binary, one-input-per-leaf assumption maps onto real morphology. The Discussion says only that 'the cut theorem does not automatically extend to conductance ancestors' (415). Given a title that names morphology, a referee will ask what the theory predicts for an actual dendritic tree.
- **Evidence:** R7 text (278-307) and its Methods (817-861) mention only eight- and four-input trees; R8 (315-337) uses routes and capture with no cut-rank quantity; grep of main.tex for 'binary' returns only synthetic-task lines (650, 673); the Discussion's sole bridge sentence is at 415. The Pinky cells are described as having '5-13 excitatory segments' (caption 340), i.e. sizes at which the eight-leaf theory could be evaluated directly, but this is not done.
- **Suggestion:** At minimum add a Discussion paragraph stating explicitly that the representability theory has not been evaluated on reconstructed morphologies and what would be needed (leaf grouping by input identity, non-binary nodes). Better: for the 10 small Pinky cells and the eight pilot cells, report the binary-tree depth and subtree-size profile and the class of eight-input interaction structures they could host under the centered-cut criterion, as one SI figure.

#### C06 — Software versions are not stated anywhere in the manuscript
- **Severity (unverified):** low
- **Category:** reproducibility
- **Location:** main.tex:611, 1212-1219; supplementary.tex:1849, 4336-4344, 4608-4630 (Table S12)
- **Claim:** The Nature Portfolio reporting summary and Methods guidance require software names with versions. The manuscript names PyTorch (main 611; SI 1849 'deterministic PyTorch settings') but never gives a PyTorch, NumPy, SciPy, scikit-learn or CUDA version; Table S12 gives Python 3.10.13 and git commits for one cohort and 'not recorded' for others. The software archive does pin torch==2.9.1+cu128 in a lock file, but the text does not cite it.
- **Evidence:** grep -i 'torch [0-9]|torch==|numpy|scipy|version [0-9]' on main.tex and supplementary.tex returns no version numbers; submission/software_release contains a lock with 'torch==2.9.1+cu128' and pyproject 'torch>=2.0.0,!=2.6.0,<3.0.0'. SI 4336-4338 acknowledges missing package locks for the June jobs and the measured-response analysis.
- **Suggestion:** Add one Methods sentence listing Python, PyTorch (2.9.1+cu128 for the current cohorts), NumPy/SciPy/scikit-learn (for the Lasso and Wilcoxon tests) and CAVEclient/meshparty versions, pointing to the archive lock file, and repeat the versions in the reporting summary.

#### C07 — Measured-response signal is raw fluorescence; main Methods do not say so
- **Severity (unverified):** low
- **Category:** clarity
- **Location:** main.tex:1053-1062; supplementary.tex:3604-3616, 3636-3640
- **Claim:** Main Methods describe the functional data only as 'Similarity was correlation of condition-mean responses' (1061) and 'standardized target responses' (1064). The SI reveals that responses are mean raw RoiResponseSeries fluorescence over stimulus windows (3608), i.e. no deconvolution, neuropil correction or dF/F normalisation is applied. A referee comparing structure to function will want this stated in the main Methods with the reason, since raw fluorescence correlations between neighbouring ROIs can be inflated by shared neuropil, which bears on the ancestry-response partial correlations in Fig. 9C.
- **Evidence:** supplementary.tex 3608: 'mean raw RoiResponseSeries fluorescence over samples whose timestamps ...'; 3615: 'vectors of mean raw fluorescence over the 136 condition identities'; main.tex 1053-1075 contains no mention of fluorescence, calcium, deconvolution or neuropil.
- **Suggestion:** State in main Methods (after 1061) that responses are condition-mean raw fluorescence without deconvolution or neuropil subtraction, and add a sensitivity row (e.g. dF/F-normalised or neuropil-subtracted responses where available in the NWB files) to Table S(functional_targets) or note that this was not possible.

Nothing I found contradicts the panel's verdict that the numbers are sound, but before submission the authors should close the scope gaps that referees will raise about the two headline results: state explicitly (and ideally test with one two-layer cohort) that the image-task sufficiency of one signal per neuron was shown only in a single dendritic layer with an exact readout error; train the K=3 subtree rung on MNIST so the route-dictionary object is tested on the flagship task; add a modest per-rung learning-rate sweep and a decoder-only floor for the ladder; and carry the SI's within-mixture scope statement for the interaction estimator into the abstract and R7, preferably with one out-of-class held-out family. A Discussion paragraph relating the cut-rank theory to real arbors, plus software versions and the raw-fluorescence statement, are quick text additions.
